### Insurance-pricing axiomatics (premium-principle characterizations: Wang–Young–Panjer, Wang 1996, Yaari, Schmeidler/Denneberg, Goovaerts–De Vylder–Haezendonck, Gerber, Deprez–Gerber, Furman–Zitikis, Haezendonck–Goovaerts/Orlicz premium and Bellini–Rosazza Gianin). ACCESS CAVEAT: in this run WebSearch was disabled by the organization proxy (HTTP 403 "feature not enabled"), curl to any scholarly API was refused (CONNECT 403), and WebFetch of the primary sources was blocked (sciencedirect.com, doi.org/10.1016/S0167-6687(97)00031-0, cambridge.org ASTIN page, arXiv search/author listings, Wikipedia: robots/provenance/rate-limit refusals; only two unrelated arXiv abs pages resolved). No theorem below was read online during this session; every statement is reconstructed from memory of the papers with a located citation, so confidence is capped at "medium" throughout and a few are "low". The panel must verify exact wording against the sources before quoting.
KEY INSIGHT: The insurance-pricing literature contains exactly two families of genuine iff theorems: (I) comonotonic-additivity theorems (Schmeidler 1986 / Denneberg 1994 / Yaari 1987 / Wang–Young–Panjer 1997, and their signed, non-monotone versions Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida 1994 and Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020) and (II) independence-additivity theorems (Gerber 1974, Gerber–Goovaerts 1981, Deprez–Gerber 1985). For the loss ratio only family (I) is usable, because family (II) collapses any comonotone-additive premium to the expectation (Goovaerts–Kaas–Laeven 2010) and would force LR ≡ const. Within family (I) the decisive observation is that monotonicity is used ONLY to make the representing set function monotone; the representation itself (Choquet integral w.r.t. a set function of bounded chain variation, law-invariant version: signed distortion of bounded variation) needs only comonotonic additivity + sup-norm boundedness/uniform continuity (+ law invariance). The non-monotone associated family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) (F2) therefore IS covered — it is a signed Choquet integral (F7) — provided one axiomatises the ASSOCIATED FAMILY, not LR alone, exactly as the Haezendonck–Goovaerts/Orlicz premium is axiomatised through its level-crossing equation E[Φ(X/h)] = 1−α (Bellini–Rosazza Gianin 2008, 2012): an implicitly defined functional inherits scale-invariance from the conic structure of the sublevel sets and cash-additivity from the cash-additive hull (Assa Prop. 1 is the loss-ratio copy of BRG's Thm 2). What must be assumed on the pair/family: (a) affinity of β ↦ ρ_β(X) (otherwise the association only says LR = inf{β : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0}, a Cherny–Madan/Penot–Volle quasi-convex representation with non-monotone cones, with no ratio structure), (b) comonotonic additivity of ρ_0 and ρ_1 (equivalently of ρ and Π), (c) monotonicity of Π (to get g non-decreasing; without it the premium is also only a signed distortion), (d) continuity. What is obtainable on LR alone: law invariance, scale invariance, translation-to-break-even, the comonotone mediant property, and — if one accepts the premium Π as part of the data — the sharper 'premium-weighted comonotone averaging' LR(X+Y) = (Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y))/(Π(X)+Π(Y)), which (Furman–Zitikis weighted-premium form; Wang 1996 layer-premium-density form LR = premium-weighted mean of the layer loss ratio h/g) is equivalent to comonotonic additivity of ρ = LR·Π. The nested-layer proof of F2 is literally Wang's layer argument/Furman–Zitikis likelihood-ratio ordering, and Wang–Young–Panjer's compound-risk axiom (A5) is likewise incompatible with a non-constant LR. Honest conclusion for the panel: an iff theorem with hypotheses on LR alone can only characterise 'scale-invariant lsc functionals with nested conic sublevel sets'; the distortion-ratio form requires the association with a family of signed Choquet integrals, i.e. hypotheses on (LR, Π) or on {ρ_β}. (All statements reconstructed from memory because WebSearch was disabled by the organisation and primary-source fetches were blocked; verify wording before quoting.)
CANDIDATE IFF: THEOREM (candidate, 'Haezendonck–Goovaerts-type characterisation of the loss ratio'). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, X := L^∞_+ \ {0}, and let LR: X → (0,∞). Suppose LR is in association (Assa 2024) with a family {ρ_β}_{β ≥ 0}, ρ_β: L^∞ → ℝ, i.e. LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0, and the family satisfies: (H1) [affine identification] for every X, β ↦ ρ_β(X) is affine and strictly decreasing: ρ_β(X) = ρ_0(X) − β Π(X) with Π(X) := ρ_0(X) − ρ_1(X) > 0 on X; (H2) [comonotonic additivity] ρ_0 and ρ_1 are comonotonic additive on L^∞; (H3) [law invariance] ρ_0, ρ_1 are law-invariant; (H4) [continuity] ρ_0, ρ_1 are uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞ (equivalently bounded on the unit ball); (H5) [premium monotonicity and normalisation] Π is monotone and Π(1_Ω) = 1. THEN there exist a unique non-decreasing g: [0,1] → [0,1], g(0)=0, g(1)=1, and a unique h ∈ BV[0,1], h(0)=0, with LR(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>t)) dt / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>t)) dt for all X ∈ X, and ρ_β = C_h − β C_g = signed Choquet integral with distortion h − βg. CONVERSELY, for any such (h,g) the functional LR := C_h/C_g with ρ_β := C_h − βC_g satisfies the association and (H1)–(H5). SUPPLEMENTS (each an iff on the pair): h non-decreasing ⟺ ρ_0 monotone; h concave ⟺ ρ_0 subadditive; g concave ⟺ Π subadditive; h,g continuous ⟺ ρ_β continuous along bounded a.s. convergence; h = g ⟺ LR ≡ 1 ⟺ LR is pointwise monotone (F2). INTRINSIC CONSEQUENCES on LR alone (necessary, not sufficient): scale invariance, law invariance, translation-to-break-even, the comonotone mediant property, and quasi-convexity under (h concave, g convex) or (h = id, g concave). Proof route: (H1) ⇒ LR = ρ_0/Π; (H2)–(H4) ⇒ by Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida 1994 / Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Thm 1, ρ_0 = C_{k_0}, ρ_1 = C_{k_1} with k_i ∈ BV, hence Π = C_{k_0 − k_1}; (H5) ⇒ by Wang–Wei–Willmot (monotone ⟺ non-decreasing distortion) g := k_0 − k_1 is non-decreasing with g(1) = Π(1) = 1; set h := k_0; uniqueness by F3; converse by direct verification (Assa Prop. 1). The theorem is honest about F2: no monotonicity is assumed or obtained on the loss-ratio family beyond the premium.
THEOREMS:
  [1] Wang, S.S., Young, V.R., Panjer, H.H. (1997). Axiomatic characterization of insurance prices. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 21(2), 173–183. Theorems 2–3 (Choquet representation) and Theorem 4 (power/PH distortion). (confidence: medium (statement reconstructed from memory of the paper and of its abstract: 'four axioms ... Choquet integral representation with respect to a distorted probability (Yaari 1987) ... an additional axiom for reducing compound risks ... determines that the distortion function is a power function'); citation located; not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: probability space (Ω,F,P); χ = set of non-negative insurance risks X (random variables X ≥ 0, assumed to contain all non-negative bounded r.v.s and to be closed under comonotone sums, truncation min(X,d) and layer-shifts (X−d)_+); price functional H: χ → [0,∞]. Axioms: (A1) Conditional state independence (law invariance given the market/economic state): if X and Y have the same (conditional) distribution then H[X]=H[Y]; (A2) Monotonicity: X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ H[X] ≤ H[Y]; (A3) Comonotonic additivity: X,Y comonotone ⇒ H[X+Y]=H[X]+H[Y]; (A4) Continuity: lim_{d↓0} H[(X−d)_+] = H[X] and lim_{d→∞} H[min(X,d)] = H[X] (plus the normalisation H[1_Ω]=1, equivalently no unjustified loading on the sure loss). THEOREM: H satisfies (A1)–(A4) ⟺ ∃ a non-decreasing g:[0,1]→[0,1], g(0)=0, g(1)=1, such that H[X] = ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t)) dt, S_X(t)=P(X>t), for all X∈χ (Choquet integral w.r.t. the distorted probability g∘P). Additional (A5) 'Reduction of compound risks' (the price of a Bernoulli-compound/mixture risk I·X with I~Bernoulli independent of X is priced consistently as a compound: H[I·X] = H[I·H[X]]-type consistency): (A1)–(A5) ⟺ g(p) = p^{1/ρ} for some ρ ≥ 1, i.e. H is Wang's proportional-hazards (PH) transform premium.
      USE: This is the template theorem for the denominator Π_g and, when ρ is monotone, for the numerator ρ_h of LR = ρ_h/Π_g. It also pins down the exact role of monotonicity: (A2)+(A3) give a monotone set function (Schmeidler/Denneberg), then (A1) turns it into g∘P. For the loss ratio, (A2) is precisely what fails for the associated family ρ_β = (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) (fact F2), so WYP cannot be applied to ρ_β verbatim; but (A1),(A3),(A4) survive for ρ_β (differences of comonotone-additive, law-invariant, continuous functionals keep all three), which is exactly the hypothesis set of the signed-Choquet characterisations (Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida; Wang–Wei–Willmot) listed below. Axiom (A5) suggests an analogous 'compound-risk' axiom on LR: LR(I·X) = LR(X) for an independent Bernoulli I iff h and g are both power functions with the same exponent ratio structure (LR(I·X) = ∫h(qS_X)/∫g(qS_X) = LR(X) for all q ⟺ h(qp)/g(qp) = h(p)/g(p) up to the normalisation, forcing h/g constant, i.e. h = c·g, LR ≡ c): so compound-reduction is incompatible with a non-constant LR, a rigidity result of F2-type worth recording.
  [2] Yaari, M.E. (1987). The dual theory of choice under risk. Econometrica 55(1), 95–115, Theorem 1 (and Theorem 2 for risk aversion ⟺ convexity of the dual distortion). Primary source cited by Wang–Young–Panjer. (confidence: medium (classical theorem, reconstructed from memory; citation located; not read online in this session).)
      STATEMENT: Setting: V = set of random variables with values in [0,1] (identified with their distribution functions / decumulative distribution functions G_v(t)=P(v>t)); a preference relation ≽ on V. Axioms: (Y1) weak order (complete, transitive); (Y2) continuity (in the topology of weak convergence/ L^1 of DDFs); (Y3) monotonicity w.r.t. first-order stochastic dominance: G_u ≥ G_v pointwise ⇒ u ≽ v; (Y4) 'dual independence' (comonotonic independence): for u,v,w ∈ V pairwise comonotone and α∈[0,1], u ≽ v ⇒ αu+(1−α)w ≽ αv+(1−α)w (mixtures of payments, not of probabilities). THEOREM 1: ≽ satisfies (Y1)–(Y4) ⟺ ∃ a continuous non-decreasing f:[0,1]→[0,1], f(0)=0, f(1)=1, such that u ≽ v ⟺ ∫_0^1 f(G_u(t)) dt ≥ ∫_0^1 f(G_v(t)) dt; f is unique. THEOREM 2: the agent is risk averse (prefers E[v] to v / respects mean-preserving spreads) ⟺ f is convex (for payoffs; for losses the distortion is concave).
      USE: Gives the preference-theoretic (ordinal) version of the Choquet representation: monotonicity enters as FSD-monotonicity of a PREFERENCE, not of a functional. This is a possible route around F2: instead of asking the non-monotone ρ_β to be monotone, one asks only that the ORDER induced by LR (X ≼_LR Y iff LR(X) ≤ LR(Y)) be representable; Yaari's comonotone-mixture independence applied to the sublevel sets {LR ≤ β} = {ρ_β ≤ 0} (which are cones, F5, and comonotone-convex when ρ_β is comonotone additive) is exactly the 'comonotone intermediate value / mediant' property of F1. Yaari's Theorem 2 also explains F4: LR ≤ 1 with g concave (actuarial picture) corresponds to a risk-averse dual agent in the denominator.
  [3] Schmeidler, D. (1986). Integral representation without additivity. Proceedings of the AMS 97(2), 255–261, Theorem (main); Denneberg, D. (1994). Non-Additive Measure and Integral, Kluwer, Chapter 13 (Proposition 13.? 'representation of comonotonic additive functionals'); non-monotone extension: Murofushi, T., Sugeno, M., Machida, M. (1994). Non-monotonic fuzzy measures and the Choquet integral. Fuzzy Sets and Systems 64, 73–86. (confidence: medium for Schmeidler/Denneberg (classical, reconstructed from memory); low–medium for the exact Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida wording (bounded chain variation ⟺ bounded comonotonic-additive functional is how I recall their main theorem). Not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: SCHMEIDLER: Let B = B(Ω,Σ) be the Banach space of bounded Σ-measurable real functions, I: B → ℝ with I(1_Ω)=1. Then I is (i) comonotonic additive (a,b comonotone ⇒ I(a+b)=I(a)+I(b)) and (ii) monotone (a ≥ b ⇒ I(a) ≥ I(b)) ⟺ ∃ a unique monotone set function ν: Σ→[0,1], ν(∅)=0, ν(Ω)=1, with I(a) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (ν({a ≥ t}) − 1) dt + ∫_0^∞ ν({a ≥ t}) dt (Choquet integral), and then ν(A)=I(1_A). DENNEBERG (Ch.13): same statement on a Stone-vector-lattice / comonotone-closed cone of bounded functions, with uniqueness of ν on the generated σ-algebra. MUROFUSHI–SUGENO–MACHIDA (non-monotone version): I: B → ℝ is comonotonic additive and bounded (sup{|I(a)| : ‖a‖_∞ ≤ 1} < ∞, equivalently ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz) ⟺ ∃ a unique set function μ: Σ → ℝ, μ(∅)=0, of bounded chain variation (sup over chains ∅=A_0⊂A_1⊂…⊂A_n=Ω of Σ|μ(A_i)−μ(A_{i−1})| < ∞) such that I(a) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (μ({a ≥ t}) − μ(Ω)) dt + ∫_0^∞ μ({a ≥ t}) dt; monotonicity of I ⟺ μ monotone.
      USE: This is the exact tool that neutralises F2. The associated family ρ_β = (C_h − βC_g)/(1−β) is comonotonic additive and sup-norm Lipschitz (constant (‖h‖_BV + β‖g‖_BV)/|1−β|) but NOT monotone; Schmeidler/Denneberg do not apply, Murofushi–Sugeno–Machida does, and yields ρ_β as a Choquet integral w.r.t. the SIGNED set function μ_β = (h − βg)/(1−β) ∘ P (fact F7) with no monotonicity assumption at all. Hence an iff theorem for the loss ratio can replace 'monotone' by 'comonotonic additive + bounded' on the associated family and still get a Choquet (distortion) representation. It also shows what is lost: without monotonicity the distortions are only of bounded variation, so the characterised class is 'LR = C_h/C_g with h ∈ BV[0,1], h(0)=0, g non-decreasing' (g monotone because the premium Π must be monotone), i.e. the numerator risk measure need not be monotone unless one adds it as an extra axiom.
  [4] Wang, R., Wei, Y., Willmot, G.E. (2020). Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals. Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993–1015 (SSRN 3177712, 2018). Theorem 1 (characterisation on L^∞), Theorem 2 (on L^q / with Lebesgue-type continuity) and the propositions on monotonicity, convexity and Hölder continuity. (confidence: medium (I recall the structure 'comonotonic additive + law-invariant + continuity ⟺ signed Choquet integral' and the BV/TV Lipschitz result precisely, but the exact continuity hypothesis in Theorem 1 (uniform continuity vs. Lebesgue continuity, L^∞ vs L^q) must be checked); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless probability space (Ω,F,P); X = L^∞ (or L^q, q ≥ 1). A signed Choquet integral is I_h(X) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x)) dx with distortion h:[0,1]→ℝ, h(0)=0, of bounded variation (not necessarily monotone), so that I_h is finite on L^∞. THEOREM 1 (as I recall it): a functional I: L^∞ → ℝ is comonotonic additive, law-invariant and uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞ (equivalently: ‖·‖_∞-Lipschitz/bounded on the unit ball) ⟺ I = I_h for a unique h ∈ BV[0,1] with h(0)=0. Supplements: (a) I_h is monotone ⟺ h non-decreasing; (b) I_h is convex (⟺ subadditive, given positive homogeneity) ⟺ h concave; (c) I_h is continuous w.r.t. L^q-convergence/Lebesgue-continuous ⟺ h is Hölder/absolutely continuous-type conditions stated in the paper; (d) the sup-norm Lipschitz constant equals the total variation ‖h‖_TV; (e) every I_h is a difference of two (monotone) distortion risk measures, I_h = C_{h_1} − C_{h_2} with h_1,h_2 non-decreasing (Jordan decomposition of h).
      USE: Direct, law-invariant version of the previous entry and the natural home of the loss-ratio associated family: ρ_β = (C_h − βC_g)/(1−β) is exactly a signed Choquet integral with distortion (h−βg)/(1−β) (F7). The iff direction of Theorem 1 therefore gives: {ρ_β} is a family of law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, ‖·‖_∞-uniformly-continuous functionals, AFFINE in β ⟺ ρ_β = C_{k_0} − β(C_{k_0} − C_{k_1}) = I_{k_0 − β(k_0−k_1)} for BV distortions k_0, k_1, whence LR = I_{k_0}/I_{k_0−k_1} is a ratio of two signed Choquet integrals. Supplement (e) is the honest answer to 'what is needed on the pair vs on LR': without a monotonicity axiom on ρ, the numerator is only a difference of two distortion risk measures. Supplement (a) applied to Π = I_{k_0−k_1} shows that requiring the premium to be monotone forces g = k_0−k_1 non-decreasing, while h = k_0 stays BV.
  [5] Haezendonck, J., Goovaerts, M. (1982). A new premium calculation principle based on Orlicz norms. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 1, 41–53; Goovaerts, M.J., Kaas, R., Dhaene, J., Tang, Q. (2004). Some new classes of consistent risk measures. IME 34, 505–516 (Theorems 1–3 on the Haezendonck risk measure); Bellini, F., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2008). On Haezendonck risk measures. Journal of Banking & Finance 32(6), 986–994 (Theorems 2–4: coherence, attainment, dual representation); Bellini, F., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2012). Haezendonck–Goovaerts risk measures and Orlicz quantiles. IME 51(1), 107–114 (Theorem on Orlicz quantiles as minimisers). (confidence: medium (definitions and the coherence/ES-special-case results are certain; the exact dual-set description and the first-order condition in BRG 2012 are reconstructed from memory); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: Φ a (normalised) Young function: Φ:[0,∞)→[0,∞] convex, non-decreasing, Φ(0)=0, Φ(1)=1, Φ(x)→∞; Orlicz heart/space L^Φ; level α ∈ [0,1) (Haezendonck–Goovaerts write 1−α =: level). ORLICZ PREMIUM (implicit, level-crossing definition): for X ∈ L^Φ, X ≥ 0, X ≢ 0, H_α(X) := the unique h > 0 solving E[Φ(X/h)] = 1−α (existence/uniqueness because h ↦ E[Φ(X/h)] is continuous, strictly decreasing from +∞ (or Φ-limit) to 0 on the relevant range); H_α(0)=0. Equivalently H_α(X) = inf{h>0 : E[Φ(X/h)] ≤ 1−α} = inf{λ>0 : X/λ ∈ A_α}, A_α = {Y : E[Φ(Y)] ≤ 1−α} a convex, monotone acceptance set (a 'return risk measure'). PROPERTIES (H&G 1982, GKDT 2004 Thm 1): H_α is monotone, positively homogeneous (H_α(λX)=λH_α(X), λ>0), subadditive, law-invariant, H_α(c)=c/Φ^{-1}(1−α) for constants, NOT cash-additive. HAEZENDONCK–GOOVAERTS RISK MEASURE (GKDT 2004; BRG 2008 Def. 3): π_α(X) := inf_{x∈ℝ} { x + H_α((X−x)_+) }, defined via the level-crossing equation E[Φ((X−x)_+/(π−x))] = 1−α for the optimal x. THEOREM (BRG 2008, Thm 2–4): for every Young Φ and α∈[0,1), (i) π_α is a law-invariant coherent risk measure on L^Φ (monotone, cash-additive π_α(X+c)=π_α(X)+c, positively homogeneous, subadditive); (ii) the infimum is attained, and for Φ strictly convex the minimiser x*_α(X) is unique (BRG 2012: it is the 'Orlicz quantile', the solution of the first-order condition E[Φ'((X−x)_+/(π−x))·1_{X>x}]·(π−x)^{-1}… = 1−α -type equation); (iii) dual representation π_α(X) = sup_{Q ∈ M_α} E_Q[X] with M_α = {Q ≪ P : the Orlicz-type (conjugate) norm constraint ‖dQ/dP‖ ≤ 1/(1−α)-type bound stated via the Young conjugate Φ*}; (iv) for Φ(x)=x: H_α(X) = E[X]/(1−α) and π_α = ES_α (Expected Shortfall) exactly; for Φ(x)=x^p: H_α(X) = ‖X‖_p/(1−α)^{1/p}.
      USE: This is the direct ancestor of Assa's implicit association LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0: the Orlicz premium is defined only through an identification equation V(h,X) := E[Φ(X/h)] − (1−α) = 0, monotone and continuous in the scalar argument h, exactly as V(β,X)=ρ_β(X) is monotone (affine) in β (F6). Three transferable lessons: (1) existence/uniqueness of the implicitly defined functional requires only strict monotonicity + continuity of the scalar map and the right boundary behaviour (for LR: β ↦ ρ_β(X) strictly decreasing with ρ_0(X) > 0 > ρ_β(X) for β large, i.e. Π(X) > 0); (2) the functional defined implicitly inherits properties from the family: H_α is positively homogeneous because A_α is a set and X/λ-scaling acts on the parameter; analogously LR inherits scale-invariance because each sublevel set {ρ_β ≤ 0} is a cone (F5, Assa's Prop. 1 'ρ_β positively homogeneous ⟺ LR(λX)=LR(X)'); (3) the step 'inf over x of x + H((X−x)_+)' is the cash-additive hull of a non-cash-additive scale functional, which is how Haezendonck–Goovaerts obtain coherence; the loss-ratio analogue is Assa's 'translation to break-even' m = −ρ_β(X) (F1): LR(X − m) = β is solved by the cash-additive hull of ρ_β. This suggests restating the LR characterisation as: ρ_β are the (unique) cash-additive, comonotonic-additive signed functionals whose zero sets are the level sets of LR — identical in architecture to BRG 2008 Thm 2.
  [6] Deprez, O., Gerber, H.U. (1985). On convex principles of premium calculation. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 4, 179–189 (Theorems 1–3 and the Esscher characterisation; see also Gerber (1979) An Introduction to Mathematical Risk Theory, Ch. 5). (confidence: medium–low (the convexity/marginal-density theorem and the Esscher-as-derivative-of-exponential fact are certain; the exact numbering and the precise hypotheses of the Pareto-optimality theorem are reconstructed from memory); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: risks X in a linear space of random variables with all exponential moments (or L^∞); a premium principle H: X → ℝ. Definitions: H is convex if H(λX+(1−λ)Y) ≤ λH(X)+(1−λ)H(Y), λ∈[0,1]; translation invariant if H(X+c)=H(X)+c; Gateaux differentiable if H'(X;Y) := d/dε H(X+εY)|_{ε=0} exists and is linear in Y, so that H'(X;Y)=E[Y·ψ_X] for a 'marginal premium density' ψ_X. THEOREM 1 (convexity ⟹ supporting-hyperplane inequality): if H is convex and Gateaux differentiable then H(Y) ≥ H(X) + E[(Y−X)ψ_X] for all X,Y; if moreover H is translation invariant then E[ψ_X]=1 (ψ_X is a probability density when ψ_X ≥ 0, which holds iff H is also monotone). THEOREM 2 (Pareto-optimal risk exchange): with convex, differentiable principles H_1,…,H_n, a risk allocation is Pareto-optimal ⟺ the marginal densities ψ coincide (ψ_{X_1}=…=ψ_{X_n}); THEOREM 3 (Esscher/exponential rigidity): a convex, translation-invariant, differentiable principle that is additive for independent risks is the exponential principle H(X)=(1/a)log E[e^{aX}] (a ≥ 0, a=0 the net premium); and the Esscher principle E[Xe^{aX}]/E[e^{aX}] is the Gateaux derivative H'(X;X) of the exponential principle, i.e. ψ_X = e^{aX}/E[e^{aX}].
      USE: Deprez–Gerber is the actuarial ancestor of convex risk measures and gives the differential/duality route to iff theorems: a convex premium is the supremum of its tangent affine minorants. For the loss ratio this is the right language for F4/F5: 1/LR = sup{x : xρ(X) − Π(X) ≤ 0} is the quasi-convex 'sublevel-set' representation, and if ρ, Π are Gateaux differentiable (distortion C_h, C_g with differentiable h,g: C_h'(X;Y) = E[Y h'(S_X(X))]) then LR'(X;Y) = (ρ'(X;Y) − LR(X)Π'(X;Y))/Π(X) = E[Y·(ψ^ρ_X − LR(X)ψ^Π_X)]/Π(X): the marginal loss-ratio density is the SIGNED density ψ^ρ − β ψ^Π with β = LR(X), i.e. the derivative of ρ_β — a differential form of F7. Theorem 1 then gives a first-order characterisation of quasi-convexity of LR: LR is quasi-convex ⟺ for all X,Y with LR(Y) ≤ LR(X), E[(Y−X)(ψ^ρ_X − LR(X)ψ^Π_X)] ≤ 0, which is the 'conic picture' of F4 and fails in general exactly because the signed density ψ^ρ − βψ^Π is not non-negative (same obstruction as F2). Theorem 3 (independence additivity ⟹ exponential) also shows that any 'additivity for independent risks' axiom on ρ_β is far too strong (it would force LR constant).
  [7] Gerber, H.U. (1974). On additive premium calculation principles. ASTIN Bulletin 7(3), 215–222; Gerber, H.U., Goovaerts, M.J. (1981). On the representation of additive principles of premium calculation. Scandinavian Actuarial Journal 1981(4), 221–227; collected in Goovaerts, M.J., De Vylder, F., Haezendonck, J. (1984). Insurance Premiums: Theory and Applications, North-Holland, Chapter 2 (characterisation theorems) and Chapter 3 (properties table); also Kaas, Goovaerts, Dhaene, Denuit (2008). Modern Actuarial Risk Theory, 2nd ed., Springer, Section 5.4 'Characterizations of premium principles' (Theorems 5.4.1–5.4.4). (confidence: medium for Gerber 1974 and the mixed-exponential representation (classical, reconstructed from memory); low for the exact wording of the KGDD theorem numbers and of the Goovaerts–Kaas–Laeven 2010 note; not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: premium principle π defined on all bounded risks (or risks with all exponential moments). (GERBER 1974) THEOREM: π satisfies (i) π(X) ≥ E[X] (non-negative loading), (ii) π(X) ≤ ess sup X (no rip-off), (iii) π(X+c) = π(X)+c, (iv) additivity for independent risks π(X+Y)=π(X)+π(Y) when X ⟂ Y, and (v) π is a zero-utility principle (u(w)=E[u(w+π(X)−X)]) ⟺ π is the exponential principle π_a(X) = (1/a) log E[e^{aX}] for some a ≥ 0 (a=0 gives the net premium). (GERBER–GOOVAERTS 1981 / GDH 1984 Ch. 2) THEOREM: π is additive for independent risks, satisfies (i)–(iii) and a continuity/'consistency' condition (continuity under weak convergence of bounded risks with uniform bounds) ⟺ π is a mixture of exponential principles: π(X) = ∫_{[0,∞)} (1/a) log E[e^{aX}] dF(a) for a probability measure F on [0,∞) (with the a=0 term read as E[X]). (KGDD 2008, Thm 5.4.x / GDH 1984) THEOREM: a zero-utility premium principle is iterative (π(X) = π(π(X|Y)) for all Y) ⟺ u is linear or exponential, i.e. π is the net or the exponential principle. (GOOVAERTS, KAAS, LAEVEN 2010, IME 47:187–189, 'A note on additive risk measures in rank-dependent utility') THEOREM: a law-invariant functional that is additive both for comonotonic risks and for independent risks (and continuous/monotone) is the expectation E[X].
      USE: These are the 'additivity for independent risks' characterisations; their relevance to the loss ratio is as rigidity/exclusion results and as a template for 'how an iff theorem is proved by a functional equation': (a) Goovaerts–Kaas–Laeven shows that if the premium Π is both comonotone-additive and independent-additive it collapses to E, so the only loss ratios with an 'independence-additive' premium are of the form ρ/E (actuarial picture of F4); (b) the mixed-exponential theorem is a model for a 'Kusuoka-type' statement: a loss ratio built from independent-additive pairs would be LR = ∫(1/a)log E[e^{aX}]dF_1(a) / ∫(1/a)log E[e^{aX}]dF_2(a), the exponential analogue of the distortion ratio, and the same functional-equation technique (Cauchy's equation on log-moment-generating functions) proves it; (c) the iterativity axiom (conditioning consistency) is a candidate intrinsic axiom for LR: 'LR(X) = LR applied to the conditional-premium-weighted ...' — I expect it to force the net/E structure as in (a), another F2-type rigidity.
  [8] Furman, E., Zitikis, R. (2008). Weighted premium calculation principles. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 42(1), 459–465 (Definition 1, Theorems 1–3); Furman, E., Zitikis, R. (2009). Weighted pricing functionals with applications to insurance: an overview. North American Actuarial Journal 13(4), 483–496. (confidence: medium for the definition, Theorem 1 (covariance argument), the special cases and the distortion-as-weighted-premium identity; low for the exact numbering/wording of Theorems 2–3 and the homogeneity-iff-power claim (I believe it is stated in FZ 2009 or in Furman–Zitikis's 'Weighted risk capital allocations' IME 2008); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: non-negative risks X with E[X] < ∞ and E[w(X)] ∈ (0,∞); weight function w:[0,∞)→[0,∞) Borel. DEFINITION: the weighted premium π_w(X) := E[X w(X)] / E[w(X)] (the mean of the size-biased/weighted distribution dF_w = w dF / E[w(X)]). THEOREM 1 (non-negative loading): if w is non-decreasing then π_w(X) ≥ E[X], with equality iff w(X) is a.s. constant or X degenerate (proof: Cov(X, w(X)) ≥ 0 for non-decreasing w); if w is non-increasing then π_w(X) ≤ E[X]. THEOREM 2 (no rip-off, no unjustified loading): π_w(X) ≤ ess sup X and π_w(c)=c for constants. THEOREM 3 (monotonicity in the weight / ordering): if w_1/w_2 is non-decreasing then π_{w_1}(X) ≥ π_{w_2}(X) (likelihood-ratio ordering of the weighted distributions); and π_w preserves the likelihood-ratio (and hence first-order stochastic) order among risks when w is non-decreasing. SPECIAL CASES: w ≡ 1 net premium; w(x)=x gives E[X²]/E[X] = E[X] + Var(X)/E[X] (modified-variance principle, mean of the equilibrium/size-biased distribution); w(x)=e^{ax} Esscher principle; w(x)=1_{x>VaR_α} gives the tail conditional expectation; w(x) = x^{−1}... ; and (FZ 2009) every distortion premium with absolutely continuous g is a 'weighted' premium with rank-dependent weight: C_g(X) = E[X g'(S_X(X))], E[g'(S_X(X))]=1 (on atomless spaces). The weighted premium is in general neither cash-additive nor positively homogeneous (it is positively homogeneous iff w is a power function w(x)=x^γ, and then π_w(X)=E[X^{γ+1}]/E[X^γ]).
      USE: The weighted premium is itself a RATIO of two expectations defined through one function, so its theory is the closest existing 'ratio-functional' theory in actuarial pricing. Two uses: (1) the loss ratio of a distortion pair with absolutely continuous h,g is a Furman–Zitikis weighted average: LR(X) = ∫h(S_X)dt / ∫g(S_X)dt = E_{Q_g}[X · r(S_X(X))]/E_{Q_g}[X] where Q_g = g'(S_X(X))·P and r = h'/g' is the LAYER loss ratio; i.e. LR is the premium-weighted (size-biased under Q_g) average of the layer loss ratio r. This is exactly the structure behind the comonotone mediant property (F1) and gives an intrinsic reformulation: LR(X+Y) = [Π(X)LR(X)+Π(Y)LR(Y)]/(Π(X)+Π(Y)) for comonotone X,Y ('premium-weighted comonotone averaging'), which is stronger than the mediant inequality and, with Π comonotone additive, is EQUIVALENT to ρ = LR·Π being comonotone additive. (2) Theorem 3 (ordering by w_1/w_2 monotone) is the weighted-premium form of F2: LR = π-ratio is monotone in the risk only if the ratio of the two weights is monotone in the right direction, and the nested-layer argument of F2 (D ⊂ B) is literally the likelihood-ratio ordering argument with w_1 = h', w_2 = g'. Also, positive homogeneity of π_w ⟺ power weight is the FZ analogue of WYP's axiom (A5)/PH transform.
  [9] Wang, S. (1996). Premium calculation by transforming the layer premium density. ASTIN Bulletin 26(1), 71–92 (Sections 2–4: Definition of the layer premium density, Theorem on properties of the transform H_g, and the PH-transform results); companion: Wang, S., Dhaene, J. (1998). Comonotonicity, correlation order and premium principles. IME 22, 235–242 (Theorem 3: subadditivity ⟺ concave g); Young, V.R. (2004). Premium principles. In: Encyclopedia of Actuarial Science, Wiley (survey listing properties and the characterisation theorems). (confidence: medium (the layer-density definition, properties (i)–(vi) and Wang–Dhaene's subadditivity-iff-concavity are certain; the exact formulation of the PH-uniqueness statement (vii) in Wang 1996 is reconstructed and may be phrased differently there); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: non-negative risk X with decumulative distribution function S_X(t)=P(X>t); the net premium of the layer (a,a+h] is ∫_a^{a+h} S_X(t) dt, so S_X(t) is the 'layer net premium density'. Wang's principle: for a distortion g:[0,1]→[0,1] non-decreasing, g(0)=0, g(1)=1, H_g(X) := ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t)) dt; g(S_X(t)) is the LAYER PREMIUM DENSITY and the premium of the layer (a,b] is ∫_a^b g(S_X(t))dt. THEOREM (properties, Wang 1996 §3; Wang–Dhaene 1998): for all such g, (i) E[X] ≤ H_g(X) ≤ ess sup X iff g(p) ≥ p; (ii) H_g(λX) = λH_g(X) for λ ≥ 0 and H_g(X+c) = H_g(X)+c; (iii) layer additivity: H_g(X) = Σ_i H_g(layer_i(X)) for any partition of [0,∞) into layers (comonotonic additivity restricted to layers), (iv) H_g preserves first-order stochastic dominance; if g is concave H_g preserves second-order (stop-loss) dominance; (v) H_g is subadditive, H_g(X+Y) ≤ H_g(X)+H_g(Y) for ALL X,Y, ⟺ g is concave (Wang–Dhaene 1998, Thm 3; the 'if' via comonotone bound H_g(X+Y) ≤ H_g(X^c+Y^c) = H_g(X)+H_g(Y)); (vi) comonotonic additivity: H_g(X+Y)=H_g(X)+H_g(Y) for comonotone X,Y; (vii) PH transform g(p)=p^{1/ρ}: H_ρ is the unique distortion premium such that the relative loading on any layer (H_ρ(layer) − E[layer])/E[layer] is a function of the layer's net premium density alone through a power law, and H_ρ is 'scale-equivariant in the layer density' H_ρ applied to the exponential-tail family keeps the family. Young (2004) lists the resulting properties table (Esscher, Wang, PH, exponential, standard deviation…) and quotes WYP's representation theorem and Gerber's additive characterisation as the two existing iff results for premium principles.
      USE: The layer premium density is the natural object for an INTRINSIC axiomatisation of the loss ratio: for a distortion pair, the loss ratio of the infinitesimal layer at level t is r(S_X(t)) with r = h/g (not h'/g'), depending on the layer only through its 'net premium density' p = S_X(t), and LR(X) = ∫ r(S_X(t)) g(S_X(t)) dt / ∫ g(S_X(t)) dt is the premium-weighted average of layer loss ratios. Hence a 'layer loss ratio' axiom — (LL) for every t the loss ratio of the layer X∧(t+dt) − X∧t depends on X only through S_X(t) — together with (vi) comonotonic additivity of the premium is what produces the ratio-of-Choquet-integrals form, and property (iv)/(F2) shows the layer loss ratio r = h/g must be non-increasing for LR to be FSD-monotone, forcing r ≥ 1 ≡ constant: the F2 proof is exactly Wang's layer argument. Property (v) explains F4: g concave (subadditive, bid-type premium) and h concave give the 'conic picture'.
  [10] Bellini, F., Laeven, R.J.A., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2018). Robust return risk measures. Mathematics and Financial Economics 12, 5–32 (Theorems 2.1–2.3: representation of return risk measures); Bellini, F., Laeven, R.J.A., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2021). Dynamic robust Orlicz premia and Haezendonck–Goovaerts risk measures. European Journal of Operational Research 291, 438–446; and Gao, N., Munari, C., Xanthos, F. (2020). Stability properties of Haezendonck–Goovaerts premium principles. IME 94, 94–99. (confidence: medium–low (the return-risk-measure definition, the log bijection with monetary risk measures and the Orlicz premium as a return risk measure are certain; the exact theorem numbers/hypotheses (lsc vs. Fatou, L^∞_+ vs. L^∞) and the content of Gao–Munari–Xanthos are reconstructed from memory); not read online in this session.)
      STATEMENT: Setting: L^∞_+ (or L^∞) on a probability space; an acceptance set A ⊂ L^∞_+ with 1 ∈ A, A monotone (Y ≤ Z ∈ A ⇒ Y ∈ A) and star-shaped/closed. RETURN RISK MEASURE: ρ̃_A(X) := inf{λ > 0 : X/λ ∈ A} (positively homogeneous: ρ̃(λX)=λρ̃(X)). THEOREM (BLRG 2018): a functional ρ̃: L^∞_+ → [0,∞] is a return risk measure (monotone, positively homogeneous, normalised ρ̃(1)=1, lsc) ⟺ ρ̃ = ρ̃_A for the acceptance set A = {X : ρ̃(X) ≤ 1}; moreover (i) ρ̃ is 'multiplicatively convex'/quasi-convex ⟺ A is convex, and then ρ̃(X) = sup_{Q} E_Q[X]/α(Q)-type robust representation; (ii) the map ρ(X) := log ρ̃(e^X) is a monetary (cash-additive) risk measure on L^∞, and ρ̃ is convex iff ρ is convex — a bijection between return risk measures and monetary risk measures. COROLLARY: the Orlicz premium H_α is the return risk measure of A_α = {Y ≥ 0: E[Φ(Y)] ≤ 1−α}; the 'robust Orlicz premium' sup_Q H_α^Q and its dynamic version are again return risk measures and satisfy the level-crossing characterisation sup_Q E_Q[Φ(X/h)] = 1−α at h = H(X). GAO–MUNARI–XANTHOS: the Haezendonck–Goovaerts premium π_α is Lipschitz-continuous/stable on the Orlicz heart under Φ-norm perturbations and the minimiser map is continuous (stability of the implicit definition).
      USE: Shows how an implicitly-defined, scale-COVARIANT functional (degree-1 homogeneous) is characterised by a single acceptance set and a scalar level-crossing. The loss ratio is the scale-INVARIANT (degree-0) relative: 1/LR(X) = sup{x : x·ρ(X) ≤ Π(X)} = inf over the ratio structure, and LR(X) = inf{β : X ∈ A_β}, A_β = {X : ρ(X) ≤ βΠ(X)}, a one-parameter family of CONES that is increasing in β (F5), i.e. a Cherny–Madan-type index whose cones are not monotone. The BLRG log-transform trick suggests a loss-ratio analogue worth testing: log LR(X) = log ρ(X) − log Π(X) is a DIFFERENCE of two 'log-return' functionals, each of which becomes a monetary risk measure under X ↦ e^X; hence LR characterisation ⟺ characterisation of pairs of monetary risk measures (ρ∘log, Π∘log) — again a pair statement, confirming that scale-invariance alone cannot deliver an LR-only theorem. Monotonicity is used in BLRG only to make A monotone; dropping it (as F2 forces) leaves the Penot–Volle/quasi-convex sublevel-set representation LR(X) = inf{β : X ∈ A_β} with A_β arbitrary nested cones, which is the honest 'LR-alone' iff statement: LR is scale-invariant and lsc ⟺ LR(X)=inf{β : X ∈ A_β} for a nested family of closed cones A_β; adding law-invariance and comonotone-convexity of each A_β is what can be stated on LR alone.


### Choquet/Schmeidler scholar (comonotonic additivity, Choquet and signed Choquet integrals, Greco/Denneberg/Yaari representations). NOTE ON SOURCES: WebSearch is disabled for this organization (HTTP 403 on every query) and several hosts were blocked by the fetch proxy (ams.org Schmeidler PDF: provenance block; api.openalex.org, core.ac.uk: provenance block; sciencedirect.com, scholar.google.com, bing.com, duckduckgo, api.semanticscholar.org, export.arxiv.org: robots/403). Primary sources that WERE read in full: Wang-Wei-Willmot (2020, MOR) PDF; Wang-Wang-Wei (2020, ASTIN) PDF; Liu-Cai-Lemieux-Wang (2020, IME) PDF; Cerreia-Vioglio-Maccheroni-Marinacci-Montrucchio IGIER WP 366 (2010; = JMAA 2012) and WP 433 (2012; = Trans. AMS 2015, which restates Schmeidler 1986 verbatim as its Theorem 2); Guo-Wang-Xia-Yang (arXiv 2506.13435); Wang-Zitikis (2021, Mgmt Sci); Higher-order Gini indices (arXiv 2508.10663). Yaari (1987), Wang-Young-Panjer (1997), Greco (1982)/Denneberg (1994) and Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida (1994) are quoted from memory and marked accordingly.
KEY INSIGHT: The non-monotonicity of ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) (F2) is NOT an obstacle to a Choquet-type iff theorem, because the modern comonotonic-additivity representation theorems do not need monotonicity: Schmeidler's hypothesis 'monotone' can be replaced by 'of bounded variation' (Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida 1994 Thm 4.2; Cerreia-Vioglio-Maccheroni-Marinacci-Montrucchio 2012 Thm 13/Cor. 18, 2015 Thm 6/Lemma 18) or, under law invariance on an atomless L^∞, by 'uniformly sup-norm continuous' (Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020 Thm 1), and the representing object is then a Choquet integral w.r.t. a signed set function of bounded variation, i.e. a DIFFERENCE of two Choquet integrals — which is exactly the loss-ratio structure. Bounded variation (equivalently, being a difference of two monotone comonotonic additive functionals, CMMM Lemma 18) is the intrinsic substitute for monotonicity, and it is automatically satisfied by the canonical family. Monotonicity is then pushed to the two endpoints of the family (β=0 gives ρ, β→∞ gives Π) and converts, via Lemma 2(i) of Wang-Wei-Willmot, into 'h and g nondecreasing'. Two further consequences: (i) because ρ_β is cash-additive with constant (1−β) ≠ 0, every sublevel set of ρ_β is a translate of {ρ_β ≤ 0} = {LR ≤ β}, so WWW Theorem 3 (convex ⇔ quasi-convex for signed Choquet integrals) decides convexity of the iso-ratio sublevel sets: {LR ≤ β} is convex iff h − βg is concave, which turns F4 into an iff inside the distortion class; (ii) a characterization stated on LR alone is not available from this strand — LR is invariant under (ρ,Π) → (φρ, φΠ) for any positive φ, and the comonotone 'mediant' property only recovers Π up to scale on comonotone pairs with distinct ratios — so the honest theorem is on the pair (LR, {ρ_β}) linked by Assa's association, with all axioms placed on the family, and the family axioms reduce (by affinity in β) to axioms on the pair (ρ, Π) = (ρ_0, Π). What is on LR alone: necessary conditions (scale invariance, law invariance, sup-norm continuity, comonotone weighted mediant with weights Π(X)/(Π(X)+Π(Y)), closure of each {LR ≤ β} under comonotone sums and positive scaling), plus F3 identifiability.
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (signed-Choquet family characterization of the loss ratio). Setting: (Ω,F,P) atomless; L^∞ = L^∞(Ω,F,P); 𝒳 := {X ∈ L^∞ : X ≥ 0, P(X>0) > 0}; B := [0,∞) \\ {1}. Let LR: 𝒳 → [0,∞) and let {ρ_β}_{β ∈ B} be functionals L^∞ → R such that (ASSOC) for all X ∈ 𝒳 and β ∈ B: LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0, and LR(X) = 1 ⇔ ρ_0(X) = Π(X); (AFF) β ↦ (1−β)ρ_β(X) is affine on B for every X ∈ L^∞, and Π := (ρ_0 − (1−β)ρ_β)/β (independent of β ∈ B\\{0} by (AFF)). Then the following are equivalent: (A) [axioms on the family] (A1) every ρ_β is law-invariant; (A2) every ρ_β is comonotonic additive; (A3) every ρ_β is uniformly L^∞-norm continuous [equivalently, by (A2): Lipschitz; equivalently, by CMMM 2012/2015: of bounded variation]; (A4) ρ_0 and Π are monotone, Π(X) > 0 for all X ∈ 𝒳, and ρ_0(1) = Π(1) = 1. (B) [representation] there exist nondecreasing h, g: [0,1] → [0,1] with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, g(t) > 0 for t > 0, such that ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (signed Choquet integral, survival form) for every β ∈ B, and LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx for every X ∈ 𝒳. Moreover (h,g) is unique, and LR alone determines (h,g) unless LR ≡ 1 (F3). PROOF SKETCH. (A)⇒(B): by Wang-Wei-Willmot (2020) Theorem 1 each ρ_β = I_{h_β} with a unique h_β ∈ H; since h ↦ I_h is linear and injective and (1−β)ρ_β = ρ_0 − βΠ, we get ρ_0 = I_h, Π = I_g with h := h_0, g := (h − (1−β)h_β)/β ∈ H, and (1−β)h_β = h − βg; (A4) and Lemma 2(i) give h, g nondecreasing, h(1)=g(1)=1, and g(p) = Π(1_B) > 0 for p = P(B) > 0 (atomless). For X ∈ 𝒳 and β ∈ B: LR(X)=β ⇔ I_{h−βg}(X)=0 ⇔ C_h(X) = βC_g(X) ⇔ β = C_h(X)/C_g(X) (C_g(X) > 0); the case β = 1 is the extra clause of (ASSOC); since LR(X) has exactly one value, LR = C_h/C_g. (B)⇒(A): I_{h_β} is law-invariant, comonotonic additive and Lipschitz with constant TV(h_β) ≤ (TV(h)+βTV(g))/|1−β| (Wang-Wang-Wei 2020 Remark 2), monotone for β=0 and for Π = I_g by Lemma 2(i), and (ASSOC)/(AFF) hold by the same equivalences. HONEST ACCOUNTING: (A1)-(A3) for all β are equivalent, via (AFF), to (A1)-(A3) for the pair (ρ_0, Π) alone; the only genuinely 'LR-level' ingredient is (ASSOC). Necessary conditions on LR alone (not shown sufficient): positive scale invariance, law invariance, sup-norm continuity, and for comonotonic X, Y ∈ 𝒳 the weighted mediant LR(X+Y) = wLR(X) + (1−w)LR(Y) with w = C_g(X)/(C_g(X)+C_g(Y)). VARIANTS: (i) drop (A1) and replace (A3) by 'of bounded variation and outer continuous' on B(Σ): CMMM 2012 Corollary 18 gives LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for two capacities ν_1 = ν_{ρ_0}, ν_2 = ν_Π (no probability measure); (ii) replace (A2)-(A3) by 'convex, positively homogeneous, L^p-continuous' on L^p: Liu-Cai-Lemieux-Wang 2020 Theorem 2.4 gives ρ_β = sup of concave-distortion signed Choquet integrals (Kusuoka-type pair, numerator a sup of ES-mixtures, denominator an inf); (iii) ADD-ON (sketch, domain subtlety at β = 1 and at the boundary of 𝒳 to be checked): under (B), for β ∈ B the sublevel set {LR ≤ β} is convex iff h − βg is concave (WWW Theorem 3 (iv)⇔(v) plus the cash shift ρ_β(X+m) = ρ_β(X) + m); hence LR is quasi-convex on {LR < 1} iff h and h − g are concave, and {LR ≥ β} is convex for β > 1 iff βg − h is concave, sharpening F4 to an iff within the distortion class.
THEOREMS:
  [1] D. Schmeidler (1986), "Integral representation without additivity", Proc. Amer. Math. Soc. 97(2), 255-261. Statement read verbatim as Theorem 2 of Cerreia-Vioglio, Maccheroni, Marinacci, Montrucchio, "Choquet integration on Riesz spaces and dual comonotonicity", IGIER WP 433 (2012) [= Trans. AMS 367 (2015)], https://repec.unibocconi.it/igier/igi/wp/2012/433.pdf (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let X = B(Ω,F) be the space of bounded F-measurable real functions on a measurable space (Ω,F) and V: X → R. Two functions f,g are comonotonic iff (f(ω)−f(ω'))(g(ω)−g(ω')) ≥ 0 for all ω,ω'. V is comonotonic additive iff V(f+g)=V(f)+V(g) whenever f,g are comonotonic; V is monotone iff f ≥ g ⇒ V(f) ≥ V(g). THEOREM: the following are equivalent: (i) V is monotone and comonotonic additive; (ii) there exists a unique capacity μ: F → [0,∞) (μ(∅)=0, monotone) such that V(x) = ∫ x dμ for all x ∈ X, where ∫ x dμ = ∫_0^∞ μ(x ≥ t) dt + ∫_{−∞}^0 [μ(x ≥ t) − μ(Ω)] dt (Choquet integral). μ(A) = V(1_A). No continuity hypothesis is needed on B(Ω,F); the integral is a Riemann integral. (Consequences, standard: V is then positively homogeneous, translation invariant with V(x+c)=V(x)+cμ(Ω), and sup-norm Lipschitz with constant μ(Ω).)
      USE: Baseline for the premium Π and the risk measure ρ themselves (β=0 and β→∞ endpoints of the canonical family): a monotone, comonotonic-additive premium/risk functional on bounded losses IS a Choquet integral w.r.t. a capacity, with no continuity assumption. It does NOT apply to ρ_β = (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β), which is comonotonic additive but not monotone (F2). The fix is to replace monotonicity by bounded variation (Theorem 2 below) or, under law invariance, by uniform norm continuity (Theorem 4 below).
  [2] S. Cerreia-Vioglio, F. Maccheroni, M. Marinacci, L. Montrucchio (2012), "Signed integral representations of comonotonic additive functionals", J. Math. Anal. Appl. 385, 895-912; read as IGIER WP 366 (2010), https://repec.unibocconi.it/igier/igi/wp/2010/366.pdf (Theorem 13, Lemma 14, Corollary 18, Corollary 19, Theorem 22). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let L be a comonotonic Stone lattice of functions Ω→R (a Stone lattice that is comonotonically dense in a Stone vector lattice; B(Σ), the bounded Σ-measurable functions, is a Stone vector lattice). A functional V: L → R is OF BOUNDED VARIATION if for every f ∈ L_+ the total variation T(0,f) := sup{Σ_{i=1}^n |V(f_i) − V(f_{i−1})| : 0 = f_0 ≤ f_1 ≤ … ≤ f_n = f, n ∈ N} is finite. V is outer (inner) continuous if V(f_n) → V(f) along decreasing (increasing) sequences f_n ↓ f (f_n ↑ f) in L; pointwise continuous if V(f_n)→V(f) for uniformly bounded pointwise convergent sequences. THEOREM 13: for V: L → R the following are equivalent: (1) V is comonotonic additive, of bounded variation, and outer continuous; (2) there exists an outer continuous set function μ of bounded variation on the set system generated by L such that V(f) = ∫_0^∞ μ(f ≥ t) dt + ∫_{−∞}^0 [μ(f ≥ t) − μ(S)] dt for all f ∈ L (Riemann integrals); (3) there exist two outer continuous capacities ν_1, ν_2 such that V(f) = ∫ f dν_1 − ∫ f dν_2 (difference of two Choquet integrals) for all f ∈ L. Moreover the outer continuous μ in (2) is unique; μ is a capacity iff V is monotone; μ is supermodular iff V is supermodular. LEMMA 14(iii): every comonotonic additive functional of bounded variation on a Stone lattice is Lipschitz continuous (sup-norm), translation invariant and positively homogeneous. COROLLARY 18 (L = B(Σ)): V is comonotonic additive, of bounded variation and outer (resp. inner) continuous iff V is the Choquet integral w.r.t. an outer (resp. inner) continuous ν ∈ bv(Σ) (signed set functions of bounded variation in the Aumann-Shapley variation norm), the unique such ν being ν(A)=V(1_A). COROLLARY 19 (L = C(S), S compact): outer continuity can be dropped (Dini). THEOREM 22 (Stone vector lattice): V is comonotonic additive, supermodular, pointwise continuous (equivalently: pointwise continuous at 0) and of bounded variation iff V(f) = ∫ f dμ for a unique continuous supermodular μ of bounded variation on the σ-algebra generated by L; V monotone iff μ is a capacity. The paper explicitly says it "extends to the nonmonotone case the integral representation results of Schmeidler [1986] and Zhou [1998]" and the B(Σ) case of Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida (1994).

It removes monotonicity exactly: a non-monotone comonotonic additive functional is representable iff it has bounded variation (+ outer continuity on B(Σ)). The canonical ρ_β = (C_h − βC_g)/(1−β) is a difference of two monotone comonotonic additive functionals, hence of bounded variation, hence covered. Conversely, if an associated family {ρ_β} is assumed comonotonic additive, of bounded variation and outer continuous (NO law invariance), Corollary 18 gives ρ_β = Choquet w.r.t. ν_β ∈ bv(Σ) and LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for the capacities ν_1=ν_{ρ_0}, ν_2=ν_Π. Lemma 14(iii) also tells us bounded variation is the right substitute for monotonicity to get Lipschitz/positive homogeneity/translation invariance, i.e. all of Assa's Prop 1 properties for free.

Soc. 367 (2015); read as IGIER WP 433 (2012), https://repec.unibocconi.it/igier/igi/wp/2012/433.pdf (Theorem 6, Lemma 18, Theorem 19, Proposition 16). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let X be an Archimedean Riesz space with unit e (e.g. L^∞(Ω,F,P)), E the set of extreme points of the positive part of the unit ball of the order dual, ⟨x,φ⟩ the evaluation. x,y ∈ X are DUALLY COMONOTONIC iff (⟨x,φ⟩−⟨x,φ'⟩)(⟨y,φ⟩−⟨y,φ'⟩) ≥ 0 for all φ,φ' ∈ E. V: X→R is unit-additive iff V(x+αe)=V(x)+V(αe) for all x, α ≥ 0; unit-modular iff V(x ∧ αe)+V(x ∨ αe)=V(x)+V(αe); of bounded variation iff sup{Σ|V(x_i)−V(x_{i−1})| : 0=x_0 ≤ … ≤ x_n = x} < ∞ for all x ∈ X_+. THEOREM 6: for V: X → R the following are equivalent: (i) V is dual comonotonic additive and of bounded variation; (ii) V is unit-additive, unit-modular and of bounded variation; (iii) there exists an outer continuous set function μ: U(E) → R of bounded variation such that V(x) = ∫_E ⟨x,φ⟩ dμ(φ) (Choquet integral on the Stone space) for all x ∈ X. μ is unique and V is monotone iff μ is monotone. THEOREM 19 (Stone vector lattice L of functions, no monotonicity assumed): if V is comonotonic additive and of bounded variation then there is a unique μ ∈ bv(L) with V(f)=∫ f dμ; V monotone iff μ is a capacity. LEMMA 18: V is the difference V = V_1 − V_2 of two monotone, unit-additive, unit-modular functionals iff V is unit-additive, unit-modular and of bounded variation; such V are Lipschitz. PROPOSITION 16(iii): if V is monotone and unit-additive then V(α)=αV(1) and V is Lipschitz continuous. In the proof of Theorem 6 the authors show a (dual) comonotonic additive functional of bounded variation is V_1 − V_2 with V_1,V_2 monotone and (dual) comonotonic additive.
      USE: Gives the L^∞(P) version (an Archimedean Riesz space with unit) of the signed representation without passing through pointwise-defined functions, and the clean equivalence 'bounded variation ⇔ difference of two monotone comonotonic additive functionals' (Lemma 18 / proof of Thm 6). This is exactly the structure of ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β): the natural axiom to put on the associated family instead of monotonicity is 'bounded variation', and it is automatically satisfied iff ρ_β splits as a difference of two monotone comonotonic additive pieces, which is precisely the loss-ratio structure (numerator ρ, denominator Π).

      STATEMENT: Let (Ω,A,P) be an ATOMLESS probability space and L^∞ the essentially bounded random variables. H := {h: [0,1] → R : h of bounded variation, h(0)=0}. DEFINITION 1: for h ∈ H the signed Choquet integral I_h: L^∞ → R is I_h(X) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X ≥ x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X ≥ x)) dx; h is called the distortion function of I_h (and is unique). ρ: L^∞ → R is law-invariant if ρ(X)=ρ(Y) whenever X,Y have the same distribution; comonotonic-additive if ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for all comonotonic X,Y ∈ L^∞; (uniformly) norm-continuous if (uniformly) continuous w.r.t. the L^∞-norm. THEOREM 1: a functional I: L^∞ → R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous IF AND ONLY IF I is a signed Choquet integral (I = I_h for some h ∈ H). REMARK 3: if uniform norm-continuity is weakened to norm-continuity, a representation of the same form holds with h not necessarily of bounded variation (so not a signed Choquet integral in their sense); for positively homogeneous functionals uniform continuity is equivalent to Lipschitz continuity. LEMMA 1: for h_1,h_2 ∈ H with h_1(1)=h_2(1): h_1 ≤ h_2 on [0,1] ⇔ I_{h_1} ≤ I_{h_2} on L^∞. LEMMA 2(i): I_h is increasing (resp. decreasing) iff h is increasing (resp. decreasing). JORDAN DECOMPOSITION (Sec. 2.2): I_h = I_{h+} − I_{h−} with h± ∈ H increasing, h = h+ − h−, i.e. every signed Choquet integral is a difference of two (monotone) Choquet integrals. THEOREM 2: for X,Y ∈ L^∞, X ≤_cx Y iff I_h(X) ≤ I_h(Y) for all concave h ∈ H. THEOREM 3: for h ∈ H the following are equivalent: (i) h is concave; (ii) I_h is convex-order consistent; (iii) I_h is subadditive; (iv) I_h is convex; (v) I_h is quasi-convex; (vi) I_h is concave on mixtures. Also cited there: Theorem 4.2 of Murofushi-Sugeno-Machida (1994) characterizes signed Choquet integrals without law invariance; Prop. 4.11 of Marinacci-Montrucchio (2004) has analogues of Lemma 2(i)-(iii).
      USE: The central tool. (a) Theorem 1 characterizes EXACTLY the class to which the canonical ρ_β belongs (F7), with hypotheses that do not include monotonicity: law invariance + comonotonic additivity + uniform sup-norm continuity. So an iff theorem for LR can assume these three on the associated family and get ρ_β = I_{h_β}. (b) Uniqueness of h plus linearity of h ↦ I_h turns the affine structure (1−β)ρ_β = ρ_0 − βΠ into (1−β)h_β = h − βg. (c) Lemma 2(i) converts 'ρ and Π monotone' into 'h,g nondecreasing', and explains F2: ρ_β monotone iff h − βg monotone, which fails in general. (d) Theorem 3 (iv)⇔(v) (convex ⇔ quasi-convex for signed Choquet integrals) is the key to deciding convexity of the sublevel sets {LR ≤ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0}: for β ≠ 1 the functional I_{h−βg} is cash-additive with constant (1−β) ≠ 0, so all its sublevel sets are translates of {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0}; hence {LR ≤ β} convex (on L^∞ or on an increasing union of shifted positive cones) ⇔ I_{h−βg} quasi-convex ⇔ h − βg concave. This upgrades F4 to an iff within the distortion class: LR is quasi-convex on all its sublevel sets with β<1 iff h and h−g are concave (equivalently h−βg concave for all β∈[0,1]); superlevel sets {LR ≥ β}, β>1, convex iff βg − h concave. (e) Lemma 1 gives the ordering of ratios: with h(1)=g(1), h ≤ g ⇔ C_h ≤ C_g ⇔ LR ≤ 1 everywhere.

      STATEMENT: Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, 𝒳 a law-invariant convex cone of random variables with L^∞ ⊂ 𝒳, H = {h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0}. ρ_h(X) := ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X ≥ x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X ≥ x)) dx, defined when both integrals are finite; ρ_h is called a distortion riskmetric; h is unique. THEOREM 1 (general domain): ρ: 𝒳 → R is a distortion riskmetric IF AND ONLY IF it satisfies (a) law-invariance, (b) comonotonic-additivity, (c) continuity at infinity: ρ((X ∧ M) ∨ (−M)) → ρ(X) as M → ∞, (d) uniform sup-continuity: ∀ε>0 ∃δ>0: ess sup|X−Y| < δ ⇒ |ρ(X)−ρ(Y)| < ε. (Builds on Schmeidler 1986.) REMARK 2: ρ_h is Lipschitz w.r.t. the L^∞-norm with constant TV_h (total variation of h on [0,1]). PROPOSITION 2(ii): ρ_h is increasing (decreasing) iff h is increasing (decreasing). COROLLARY 1 (𝒳 ⊂ L^1): ρ is law-invariant, increasing, cash-invariant, continuous at infinity and comonotonic-additive iff ρ = ρ_h for an increasing h ∈ H with h(1)=1. THEOREM 3 (𝒳 ⊂ L^1): h concave ⇔ ρ_h convex-order consistent ⇔ subadditive ⇔ convex ⇔ quasi-convex ⇔ concave on mixtures. THEOREM 5 (𝒳 ⊂ L^1): ρ is a convex distortion riskmetric iff ρ(X) = ∫_{[0,1]} ES_α(X) dμ_1(α) + ∫_{[0,1]} ES_α(−X) dμ_2(α) for finite Borel measures μ_1, μ_2 on [0,1]; if ρ is increasing μ_2 can be taken to be 0. PROPOSITION 4: for p ∈ [1,∞) and continuous h ∈ H, ρ_h is L^p-continuous on L^p if h ∈ H_q (h' ∈ L^q near 0 and 1, q conjugate of p); convex distortion riskmetrics on L^p are L^p-continuous.
      USE: (1) Extends the L^∞ characterization to unbounded losses (L^p or any law-invariant convex cone ⊃ L^∞) at the price of the explicit 'continuity at infinity' axiom (c): this is the exact continuity hypothesis the strand asked about for 'L^∞ vs. general spaces'. (2) Corollary 1 is the monotone/cash-invariant special case (classical distortion risk measure) and, importantly, needs NO uniform continuity: for monotone comonotonic additive functionals, continuity at infinity suffices. So in the LR theorem the endpoints ρ, Π only need (law inv., monotone, cash-invariant, comonotonic additive, continuity at infinity), while the non-monotone ρ_β need uniform sup-continuity or bounded variation. (3) Theorem 5 gives a Kusuoka-type (ES-mixture) representation for the CONVEX signed case, i.e. for the ρ_β in the 'conic picture' of F4: ρ_β = (h−βg)/(1−β)-distortion with h concave, g convex is a mixture of ES_α(X) and ES_α(−X). This is the 'Kusuoka-type pair' description of the goal: numerator a mixture of ES_α(X), denominator a mixture of ES_α(−X) with a sign flip, i.e. Π(X) = −∫ ES_α(−X) dμ_2 is a 'bid price'. (4) Remark 2 supplies the explicit Lipschitz constant TV(h−βg)/|1−β| needed in the sufficiency direction.

Wang (2020), "Convex risk functionals: Representation and applications", Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 90, 66-79; read at https://sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2019Liu-Cai-Lemieux-Wang-IME.pdf (Definition 1.1, Remark 1.1, Lemma 2.1, Theorem 2.2, Theorem 2.4).       STATEMENT: Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless and p ∈ [1,∞]. ρ: L^p → R is a LAW-INVARIANT CONVEX RISK FUNCTIONAL if (B1) translation invariance: ρ(X+m) = ρ(X) + c·m for all m ∈ R, where c := ρ(1) − ρ(0) (c is NOT required to be 1); (B2) convexity: ρ(λX+(1−λ)Y) ≤ λρ(X)+(1−λ)ρ(Y), λ ∈ [0,1]; (B3) continuity w.r.t. the L^p-norm; (B4) law invariance. Remark 1.1: such ρ are not necessarily monotone and (B3) is essential in that case. Φ_c := {h: [0,1] → R concave, h(0)=0, h(1)=c}, Φ_c^p the subset giving finite signed Choquet integrals on L^p. THEOREM 2.2: ρ is a law-invariant convex risk functional on L^p iff there exist c ∈ R and β: Φ_c^p → (−∞,∞] such that ρ(X) = sup_{h ∈ Φ_c^p} { ∫_0^1 VaR_α(X) dh(α) − β(h) } for all X ∈ L^p. THEOREM 2.4: if in addition ρ is positively homogeneous (A2), then there exist c ∈ R and Ψ_c^p ⊂ Φ_c^p such that ρ(X) = sup_{h ∈ Ψ_c^p} ∫_0^1 VaR_α(X) dh(α), i.e. ρ is a supremum of convex signed Choquet integrals (distortion riskmetrics with concave h). LEMMA 2.1: every signed Choquet integral with concave h is a law-invariant convex risk functional; Gini mean difference, range, Gini shortfall, spectral measures and Wang's premium principle are examples.
      USE: A NON-MONOTONE Kusuoka/Frittelli-Rosazza Gianin representation. It covers the sublevel-set functionals x·ρ − Π of F5 and the ρ_β when these are convex but not comonotonic additive (e.g. ρ coherent law-invariant, Π concave law-invariant), giving the 'coherent/concave pair' version of the goal: if ρ_β is law-invariant, translation-invariant with constant 1 (Assa Prop 1), convex, positively homogeneous and L^p-continuous, then ρ_β = sup of signed Choquet integrals with concave distortions, without ever invoking monotonicity. The price: the representation is a sup (a family of distortions per β), so the pair (ρ,Π) is recovered only as (sup of Choquet, inf of Choquet) rather than as a single Choquet pair; this is the Kusuoka-type candidate class of the goal.
  [7] N. Guo, R. Wang, C. Xia, J. Yang (2025), "Rating criteria, risk measures, and risk consistency", arXiv:2506.13435 (Section 2.1.1 after Definition 3; Proposition 2(i), attributed to Guo et al. (2025, Theorem 7 and Corollary 1)); read at https://arxiv.org/pdf/2506.13435 (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: On L^∞ of an atomless probability space, a Choquet risk measure is ρ(X) = ∫ X dν for a capacity ν with ν(Ω)=1 (survival form). The paper states: 'Following from the main result of Schmeidler (1986), a mapping ρ: L^∞ → R is a Choquet risk measure if it satisfies (i) monotonicity, (v) normalization ρ(1)=1, and (vi) comonotonic additivity ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) whenever X and Y are comonotonic'. PROPOSITION 2(i): a Choquet risk measure ρ is law-invariant IF AND ONLY IF ρ(X) = ∫ X d(h∘P) for some increasing h: [0,1] → [0,1] with h(0)=0, h(1)=1 (i.e. ρ(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x))−1)dx, a distortion risk measure).
      USE: Closes the gap between Schmeidler's capacity ν and the distortion form h∘P used in Assa's setting: on an atomless space, law invariance of a Choquet integral is equivalent to ν = h∘P. Together with Theorem 1 this gives, for the monotone endpoints ρ = ρ_0 and Π: 'monotone + comonotonic additive + law-invariant + normalized ⇔ ρ = C_h, Π = C_g with h,g nondecreasing distortions' and no continuity hypothesis at all (continuity of h is NOT implied; h may jump, e.g. VaR).
  [8] M. E. Yaari (1987), "The dual theory of choice under risk", Econometrica 55(1), 95-115, Theorem 1 (from memory; Econometrica/JSTOR not fetchable here). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let Γ be the set of random variables with values in [0,1] (equivalently their distribution functions F, with decumulative functions G=1−F). A preference ≽ on Γ satisfies (A1) neutrality: identically distributed variables are indifferent (law invariance); (A2) completeness and transitivity; (A3) continuity w.r.t. the topology of weak convergence (L^1-convergence of decumulative distribution functions); (A4) monotonicity w.r.t. first-order stochastic dominance; (A5) dual independence: for all u,v,w ∈ Γ and α ∈ [0,1], u ≽ v implies α u ⊕ (1−α) w ≽ α v ⊕ (1−α) w, where ⊕ denotes the 'harmonic/comonotone' mixture of inverse distribution functions (αF_u^{-1} + (1−α)F_w^{-1}, i.e. the mixture is taken on comonotonic versions). THEOREM 1: ≽ satisfies (A1)-(A5) IF AND ONLY IF there exists a continuous nondecreasing f: [0,1] → [0,1], f(0)=0, f(1)=1, such that u ≽ v ⇔ ∫_0^1 f(G_u(t)) dt ≥ ∫_0^1 f(G_v(t)) dt; f is unique.
      USE: The law-invariant (distribution-based) counterpart of Schmeidler: it is where 'distortion of the survival function' enters, and it shows that, on the level of preferences over distributions, comonotonic mixing (dual independence) forces the Choquet/distortion form with CONTINUOUS f (continuity in weak topology ⇒ f continuous, unlike the L^∞ theorems where h may jump). For the loss ratio: LR = C_h/C_g compares two Yaari dual utilities; the ordering of losses by LR is NOT a Yaari preference (not monotone, F2), but each iso-ratio set {LR = β} is the indifference set at 0 of the signed dual functional I_{h−βg}. Yaari's continuity axiom suggests that if one wants continuity of LR under weak convergence (not only sup-norm), one must impose it on the family and will then get continuous h, g (F1's continuity assumption).
  [9] S. Wang, V. R. Young, H. H. Panjer (1997), "Axiomatic characterization of insurance prices", Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 21, 173-183, Theorem 3 (from memory; ScienceDirect not fetchable here). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let 𝒳 be the set of nonnegative bounded risks on an atomless probability space and H: 𝒳 → [0,∞) a price (premium) functional. Axioms: (1) conditional state independence / law invariance: H(X) depends only on the distribution of X; (2) monotonicity: X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ H(X) ≤ H(Y); (3) comonotonic additivity: H(X+Y) = H(X) + H(Y) for comonotonic X,Y; (4) continuity: lim_{d→0+} H((X−d)^+) = H(X) and lim_{d→∞} H(X ∧ d) = H(X) (Greco-type truncation continuity), plus lim_{d→0} H(X ∧ d)=0 in some versions. THEOREM: H satisfies (1)-(4) IF AND ONLY IF there exists a nondecreasing g: [0,1] → [0,1] with g(0)=0, g(1)=1 such that H(X) = ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t)) dt, S_X(t) = P(X > t) (Choquet integral w.r.t. the distorted probability g∘P). Adding the axiom H(X) ≤ sup X and H(X) ≥ E[X] gives g(p) ≥ p (safety loading).
      USE: This is the insurance-premium version of Schmeidler/Greco stated directly on the domain of NONNEGATIVE bounded losses, which is the domain of LR. Its continuity axioms are of Greco type (truncation continuity) rather than sup-norm continuity, so for the monotone endpoints ρ and Π of the LR family one can use these weaker, domain-intrinsic axioms. Its proof (via Greco's theorem) shows how to represent a functional defined only on L^∞_+ without extending to L^∞; for the non-monotone ρ_β no Greco-type theorem is available, so one either extends ρ_β to L^∞ by cash additivity (ρ_β(X) := ρ_β(X+M) − M ρ_β(1), well defined by comonotonic additivity) and applies Wang-Wei-Willmot Theorem 1, or uses the Jordan decomposition ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) and represents the two monotone pieces by Wang-Young-Panjer.
  [10] G. H. Greco (1982), "Sulla rappresentazione di funzionali mediante integrali", Rend. Sem. Mat. Univ. Padova 66, 21-42; as stated in D. Denneberg (1994), Non-Additive Measure and Integral, Kluwer, Theorem 13.2 (Greco's theorem) and Chapter 13 (from memory; book not fetchable). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let 𝓕 be a class of functions f: Ω → [0,∞] closed under: positive scalar multiples, f ∧ g, f ∨ g, truncation f ∧ a and (f − a)^+ for a ≥ 0 (a 'Stone-type' cone of nonnegative functions). Let Γ: 𝓕 → [0,∞] be (i) monotone, (ii) comonotonic additive, and satisfying the two truncation-continuity conditions (iii) lim_{a→0+} Γ((f − a)^+) = Γ(f) and (iv) lim_{a→∞} Γ(f ∧ a) = Γ(f) for all f ∈ 𝓕 (for bounded f, (iv) is automatic). THEOREM: then there exists a monotone set function μ on the set system {{f > a}: f ∈ 𝓕, a > 0} (and extensions to the generated σ-ring) such that Γ(f) = ∫ f dμ = ∫_0^∞ μ(f > t) dt for all f ∈ 𝓕; conversely every such Choquet integral satisfies (i)-(iv). The representing μ is not unique in general; it can be chosen as μ(A) = sup{Γ(f): f ≤ 1_A} or inf{Γ(f): f ≥ 1_A}. Denneberg (Prop. 13.? ) adds: on bounded functions, monotone + comonotonic additive already implies positive homogeneity and sup-norm Lipschitz continuity with constant Γ(1).
      USE: Greco's theorem is the version of Schmeidler on cones of NONNEGATIVE functions (the natural domain of insurance losses) and with the weakest known continuity (truncation continuity rather than sup-norm). It is what Wang-Young-Panjer use. For the LR problem it shows that the monotone endpoints can be axiomatized on L^∞_+ alone. It also highlights the obstruction for the family: Greco requires monotonicity, and I am not aware of a Greco-type (nonnegative-cone, truncation-continuity) theorem for non-monotone comonotonic additive functionals; the signed results (CMMM 2012, Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020) all work on vector lattices and need bounded variation or uniform continuity. Hence the honest hypothesis on ρ_β must be either bounded variation/uniform sup-continuity, or the Jordan-type decomposition into monotone pieces.
  [11] T. Murofushi, M. Sugeno, M. Machida (1994), "Non-monotonic fuzzy measures and the Choquet integral", Fuzzy Sets and Systems 64, 73-86, Theorem 4.2 (cited as the non-law-invariant characterization of signed Choquet integrals by Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020 and as the B(Σ) special case by Cerreia-Vioglio et al. 2012; statement reconstructed from memory). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let (Ω,Σ) be a measurable space and B(Σ) the bounded Σ-measurable functions. A non-monotonic fuzzy measure is a set function μ: Σ → R with μ(∅)=0; it is of bounded variation if its Jordan total variation |μ|(Ω) = sup Σ_i |μ(A_i)−μ(A_{i−1})| over chains is finite, and then μ = μ^+ − μ^- with μ^± monotone. The Choquet integral of f ∈ B(Σ) w.r.t. such μ is ∫ f dμ = ∫_0^∞ μ(f ≥ t) dt + ∫_{−∞}^0 [μ(f ≥ t) − μ(Ω)] dt = ∫ f dμ^+ − ∫ f dμ^-. THEOREM 4.2: a functional I: B(Σ) → R is the Choquet integral w.r.t. a non-monotonic fuzzy measure of bounded variation IF AND ONLY IF I is comonotonically additive and 'of bounded variation' in the sense that sup{Σ_i |I(f_i) − I(f_{i−1})| : 0 = f_0 ≤ f_1 ≤ … ≤ f_n = f} < ∞ for every f ∈ B(Σ)_+ (some versions state the hypothesis as: I comonotonically additive and bounded on {|f| ≤ 1}); μ(A) = I(1_A).
      USE: The earliest 'Schmeidler without monotonicity' statement, explicitly on B(Σ): monotonicity is replaced by bounded variation of the functional, and the representing set function is a difference of two capacities. This is the direct ancestor of CMMM Corollary 18 and of the signed Choquet integrals of F7, and it gives a non-law-invariant version of the candidate iff: an associated family {ρ_β} of comonotonic additive functionals of bounded variation on B(Σ) ⇔ LR = ∫X dν_1 / ∫X dν_2 for two capacities (no probability measure, no law invariance).
  [12] Higher-order Gini indices: An axiomatic approach (2025), arXiv:2508.10663 (co-authored by R. Wang; read at https://arxiv.org/pdf/2508.10663), Theorem 1 and the definition GC_n = GD_n/E[X]. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let 𝒳 = L^q, q ≥ 1, on an atomless probability space. The n-th order Gini deviation GD_n is a signed Choquet integral (law-invariant, comonotonic additive, non-monotone deviation measure), and the n-th order Gini coefficient is the RATIO GC_n(X) := GD_n(X)/E[X] on L^1_+. THEOREM 1: a mapping ρ: 𝒳 → R satisfies [A1] sample representability, [A2] symmetry (law invariance across permutations/i.i.d. samples), [A3] comonotonic additivity and [A4] uniform norm continuity IF AND ONLY IF there exist n ∈ N and weights a_1,…,a_n such that ρ = Σ_{i=1}^n a_i GD_i. In the proof, law invariance + [A3] + [A4] are used to invoke Wang-Wei-Willmot (2020) and conclude that ρ 'can be characterized as a signed Choquet integral with an associated distortion function'. The paper gives sharp bounds for ratios GD_n/GD_m of two signed Choquet integrals (Proposition 3, eq. (8)) but no characterization of such ratios.
      USE: This is the closest existing object to the loss ratio in the recent literature: a ratio (non-monotone signed Choquet integral)/(Choquet integral = mean), i.e. LR with Π = E and a deviation-type numerator. It confirms the methodological template of the candidate theorem: axiomatize numerator and denominator (or the associated family) via law invariance + comonotonic additivity + uniform norm continuity, obtain signed Choquet integrals, then study the ratio. It also confirms that NO axiomatization of a ratio of two Choquet integrals stated purely on the ratio exists in this literature, which supports stating the LR theorem on (LR, {ρ_β}) rather than on LR alone.


### Signed Choquet / Ruodu Wang strand (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 MOR; Wang–Wei 2020 MF; Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN; Wang–Ziegel 2015; Li–Wang 2023; Fiori–Rosazza Gianin 2023; Wang–Bignozzi–Tsanakas 2015; plus Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang 2020, Han–Lin–Wang 2025, Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei 2022, Wang–Zitikis 2021, Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei 2026). METHOD NOTE: WebSearch was disabled by the organization proxy (HTTP 403) and arXiv/SSRN full texts were blocked; every 'high' item below was read from the author-posted PDF at https://sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/ (links given in each citation). Fiori–Rosazza Gianin could only be read at abstract level (SSRN 3949592).
KEY INSIGHT: The monotonicity obstruction (F2) is not an obstruction at all in this strand: Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020, MOR) Theorem 1 characterizes signed (non-monotone) Choquet integrals on L^∞ by law invariance + comonotonic additivity + uniform sup-norm continuity, and Wang–Wei (2020, MF) Prop. 5.2 does the same for the PAIR (ρ,Π) on distributions. Uniform continuity — the price paid for dropping monotonicity — is FREE for the loss ratio, because each ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) is a fixed linear combination of two monotone cash-additive (hence 1-Lipschitz) functionals. Two further structural facts make an intrinsic iff theorem possible. (1) Cash-additivity identifies the family: if two cash-additive families are both in association with LR they coincide (ρ'_β(X)=ρ'_β(X−ρ_β(X))+ρ_β(X)=ρ_β(X)); and LR has a cash-additive associated family iff for every X the cash profile c ↦ LR(X+c) is the Möbius map (c+ρ(X))/(c+Π(X)) (this is Assa's Prop. 1 read backwards), so ρ(X) and Π(X) are recovered intrinsically as minus the zero and minus the pole of the profile, and the lemma LR(X)=β ⇔ ρ_β(X)=0 is then automatic. (2) Because h ↦ I_h is linear, the associated family is signed-Choquet iff the two endpoints ρ=ρ_0 and Π=lim_{β→∞}ρ_β are, and the distortions k_β=g+(h−g)/(1−β) lie on an affine line in H; WWW Lemma 1 / ASTIN Prop. 2(i) give uniqueness of (h,g) (F3) and the ordering iff 'LR ≤ 1 on L^∞_+ ⇔ h ≤ g'. WWW Theorem 3 (six equivalences) then converts shape questions into iffs: for β≠1, {LR ≤ β} is convex iff h−βg is concave, so LR is quasi-convex on bounded non-negative losses iff h is concave and g is convex (the 'conic picture' of F4 becomes an iff), and LR is quasi-concave iff h convex and g concave — which CORRECTS F4's 'actuarial picture' (ρ=E, g concave gives a quasi-CONCAVE, not quasi-convex, loss ratio, since I_g with g concave is convex/subadditive). The level-search form LR(X)=inf{β: I_{h−βg}(X) ≤ 0} on {Π>0} is a Han–Lin–Wang DQ / Cherny–Madan index built on a DECREASING family of signed Choquet integrals (use V_β=ρ−βΠ, not the normalized ρ_β which has a pole at β=1), and their Theorem 1 provides the proof architecture for recovering the family from the index. Finally, Wang–Wei Thm 3.2 / Wang–Ziegel show that LR has convex level sets (hence can be identifiable/elicitable) only in degenerate VaR-type or ess-sup/ess-inf cases, so F6's identification is a zero-set identification only; and Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Thm 3 shows the margin D=ρ−Π=I_{h−g} (a location-invariant signed Choquet 'variability' functional) already has an iff axiomatization template.
CANDIDATE IFF: THEOREM (candidate, signed-Choquet strand). Let (Ω,A,P) be atomless. Let LR be defined on a set D ⊂ L^∞ with values in R, such that for every X ∈ L^∞ the set {c ∈ R : X + c ∉ D} is a singleton {p(X)} (the 'pole', interpreted as the cash level at which the premium vanishes). The following are equivalent. (I) [representation] There exist h, g : [0,1] → R of bounded variation with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, such that D = {X : I_g(X) ≠ 0} and LR(X) = I_h(X)/I_g(X) for X ∈ D, where I_h, I_g are signed Choquet integrals; equivalently (via ρ_β := I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}), LR is in association with the unique cash-additive family of signed Choquet integrals whose distortions lie on the affine line g + (h−g)/(1−β). (II) [intrinsic axioms on LR] (A0) Möbius cash profile: for every X ∈ L^∞ there is a real D(X) with LR(X + c) = 1 + D(X)/(c − p(X)) for all c ≠ p(X) [equivalently: LR admits a cash-additive family in association, which is then unique; define Π(X) := −p(X), ρ(X) := Π(X) + D(X), so that LR = ρ/Π]; (A1) law invariance: LR(X)=LR(Y) and p(X)=p(Y) whenever X =_d Y; (A2) scale invariance: LR(λX)=LR(X) and p(λX)=λp(X) for λ>0; (A3) comonotonic additivity of break-even cash amounts: p(X+Y)=p(X)+p(Y) and p(X+Y)−D(X+Y) = (p(X)−D(X)) + (p(Y)−D(Y)) for comonotonic X, Y [equivalently: for β ∈ {0, ∞} the level sets of LR are closed under comonotonic sums, where LR=∞ means X ∉ D]; (A4) uniform sup-norm continuity of X ↦ p(X) and X ↦ D(X). Moreover: (a) (h,g) is unique (WWW Lemma 1); (b) in (II), axiom (A4) can be REPLACED by (A4') monotonicity of the two endpoints, X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ p(X) ≥ p(Y) and p(X)−D(X) ≥ p(Y)−D(Y) [i.e. ρ and Π monotone], in which case h and g are increasing distortion functions, the pair (ρ,Π) is a pair of Yaari/Denneberg distortion risk measures, every ρ_β is Lipschitz with constant (1+|β|)/|1−β|, and ρ_β is monotone iff h−βg is monotone (WWW Lemma 2(i)); (c) LR ≤ 1 on L^∞_+ iff h ≤ g (ASTIN Prop. 2(i)); (d) for β ≠ 1 the sublevel set {LR ≤ β} ∩ {Π>0} is convex iff h − βg is concave iff ρ_β is quasi-convex iff ρ_β is subadditive (WWW Thm 3 + Liu et al. Prop. 2.7), hence LR is quasi-convex on L^∞_+ ∩ D iff h is concave and g is convex, and quasi-concave iff h is convex and g is concave; (e) LR has convex level sets on finitely supported distributions (necessary for identifiability/elicitability) only if h − βg ∈ H* (Wang–Wei Thm 3.2) for all β in the range, which for h ≠ g forces {h,g} ⊂ H1* or {h,g} ⊂ H3* with a common level α (sufficiency proved; necessity conjectured, a special case of Wang–Wei's open problem on pairs (I_h, I_g) with CxLS). Proof of (I)⇔(II): (A0) ⇒ ρ, Π well-defined and cash-additive and LR=ρ/Π; (A1)-(A4) are, by direct translation through the zero and pole of the Möbius profile, exactly law invariance, positive homogeneity, comonotonic additivity and uniform continuity of ρ and Π (positive homogeneity being implied by the other three for signed Choquet integrals, WWW Lemma 2(iii)); WWW Theorem 1 (or Wang–Wei Prop. 5.2 for the pair) gives ρ=I_h, Π=I_g with h(1)=ρ(1)=1, g(1)=Π(1)=1; conversely WWW Lemma 2 gives all properties. What is needed on LR alone: (A0)-(A4) [or (A0)-(A3)+(A4')]; what is needed on the pair beyond that: nothing — the pair is recovered from LR, and additional pair-level properties (coherence, ordering, convexity of members of the family) correspond one-to-one to (c)-(d).

      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,A,P); space L^∞ of essentially bounded r.v.'s; H = {h:[0,1]→R : h of bounded variation, h(0)=0} (no monotonicity, no normalization). Signed Choquet integral I_h(X) = ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X≥x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X≥x)) dx (Remark 1: P(X>x) may replace P(X≥x)). Definitions: comonotonic-additive: ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for all comonotonic X,Y∈L^∞; law-invariant: ρ(X)=ρ(Y) whenever X =_d Y; uniformly norm-continuous: uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞. THEOREM 1: A functional I : L^∞ → R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous IF AND ONLY IF I is a signed Choquet integral I_h for some h ∈ H. LEMMA 1: for h1,h2 ∈ H with h1(1)=h2(1): h1=h2 iff I_{h1}=I_{h2} on L^∞ (uniqueness of h). LEMMA 2: (i) I_h is increasing (resp. decreasing) iff h is increasing (resp. decreasing); (ii) I_h(X+c)=I_h(X)+c·h(1); (iii) I_h(λX)=λI_h(X), λ>0; Lemma 4: |I_h(X)−I_h(Y)| ≤ TV(h)‖X−Y‖_∞. Jordan decomposition: I_h = I_{h+} − I_{h−} with h± increasing. Remark 2/3: uniform norm-continuity is used only to force bounded variation of h; for positively homogeneous functionals uniform continuity ⇔ Lipschitz continuity; with mere norm-continuity the representation holds with h not necessarily of bounded variation.
      USE: This is THE theorem that removes the monotonicity obstruction (F2). Assa's canonical family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) is a non-monotone functional; Theorem 1 characterizes exactly such functionals by law invariance + comonotonic additivity + uniform sup-norm continuity, with NO monotonicity. Crucially, if ρ=I_h and Π=I_g are monotone and cash-additive (h,g increasing, h(1)=g(1)=1) then each ρ_β is automatically Lipschitz with constant (1+|β|)/|1−β| in ‖·‖_∞, so the uniform-continuity hypothesis is FREE in the loss-ratio setting; conversely for an abstract family one must assume it (or assume monotonicity of the two endpoints ρ=ρ_0 and Π=lim_{β→∞}ρ_β). Since h ↦ I_h is linear and H is a vector space, {ρ_β} consists of signed Choquet integrals iff ρ and Π do, with ρ_β = I_{k_β}, k_β=(h−βg)/(1−β)=g+(h−g)/(1−β), i.e. the distortions of the associated family lie on an affine line in H through g with direction h−g; Lemma 1 gives uniqueness of (h,g) given LR and the normalization h(1)=g(1)=1 (recovers F3). Lemma 2(i) gives: ρ_β monotone iff h−βg is monotone (for β<1), which fails for large β unless h'≥βg' — a precise quantification of F2.
