      STATEMENT: Setting as in Theorem 1 (L^∞, atomless, h ∈ H of bounded variation, h(0)=0). THEOREM 2: for X,Y ∈ L^∞, X ≤_cx Y iff I_h(X) ≤ I_h(Y) for all concave h ∈ H. THEOREM 3: for h ∈ H the following are equivalent: (i) h is concave; (ii) I_h is convex-order consistent (X ≤_cx Y ⇒ I_h(X) ≤ I_h(Y)); (iii) I_h is subadditive; (iv) I_h is convex; (v) I_h is quasi-convex; (vi) I_h is concave on mixtures (i.e. F ↦ I_h(F) concave in the distribution). THEOREM 5: the extreme-aggregation measure Γ_{I_h}(X)=limsup_n (1/n) sup{I_h(S): S a sum of n copies of X in law} equals I_{h*}, h* = inf{g concave on [0,1], g ≥ h} the concave envelope, and I_{h*} is the smallest law-invariant convex functional on L^∞ dominating I_h.
      USE: Gives an EXACT iff for the shape of the loss-ratio sublevel sets. For β ≠ 1 and Π>0, {LR ≤ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0}; since (h−βg)(1)=1−β≠0, translation by constants shows {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} is convex iff ALL sublevel sets of I_{h−βg} are convex iff (Thm 3 (v)⇔(i)) h−βg is concave. Hence: on bounded non-negative losses (range of LR ⊂ [0,∞)), LR=I_h/I_g is quasi-convex iff h−βg is concave for every β≥0 iff h concave AND g convex (β=0 gives h concave; β→∞ gives g convex) — i.e. exactly the 'conic picture' of F4 (ρ convex/subadditive, Π a concave 'bid price', LR ≥ 1), now as an iff. Symmetrically {LR ≥ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≥ 0} is convex iff h−βg convex, so LR is quasi-CONCAVE iff h convex and g concave. CORRECTION TO F4: the 'actuarial picture' ρ=E (h(t)=t), g concave gives t−βg CONVEX, hence E/I_g is quasi-CONCAVE, not quasi-convex (I_g with g concave is convex/subadditive, so {βI_g − E ≥ 0} is a superlevel set of a convex functional). Also: ρ_β is subadditive/convex/quasi-convex/mixture-concave iff (h−βg)/(1−β) is concave — gives the family-level 'coherence' conditions needed to mimic Cherny–Madan. Theorem 5 yields the worst-case loss ratio under dependence uncertainty: Γ_ρ/Γ_Π = I_{h*}/I_{g*}.

      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,A,P); domain X a law-invariant convex cone with L^∞ ⊂ X ⊂ L^0 closed under addition and positive scaling; H = {h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0}; distortion riskmetric ρ_h(X)=∫X d(h∘P) (same formula as above) defined where both integrals are finite. THEOREM 1: ρ : X → R is a distortion riskmetric (ρ=ρ_h, h ∈ H) IF AND ONLY IF ρ is (a) law-invariant, (b) comonotonic-additive, (c) continuous at infinity: ρ((X∧M)∨(−M)) → ρ(X) as M→∞, and (d) uniformly sup-continuous (ε-δ in ess sup|X−Y|). PROPOSITION 2: (i) if h1(1)=h2(1) then h1 ≤ h2 on [0,1] iff ρ_{h1} ≤ ρ_{h2} on X (so h1=h2 iff ρ_{h1}=ρ_{h2}); (ii) ρ_h increasing iff h increasing; (iii) ρ_h(X+c)=ρ_h(X)+c h(1); (iv) positive homogeneity; (v) ρ_h(−X)=ρ_{ĥ}(X), ĥ(x)=h(1−x)−h(1). COROLLARY 1 (monotone case): on X ⊂ L^1 a functional is law-invariant, increasing, cash-invariant, continuous at infinity and comonotonic-additive IF AND ONLY IF it is ρ_h with h increasing and h(1)=1; and it is then subadditive/convex/quasi-convex/cx-consistent/mixture-concave iff h is concave (then coherent). THEOREM 3: same six equivalences as WWW Theorem 3 on X ⊂ L^1. THEOREM 5: on X ⊂ L^1, ρ is a convex distortion riskmetric iff ρ(X)=∫ES_α(X)dμ(α)+∫ES_α(−X)dν(α) for finite Borel measures μ,ν on [0,1]; ν=0 iff ρ increasing.
      USE: Extends the characterization beyond bounded losses (needed if the panel wants LR on L^1 or L^p losses: the continuity-at-infinity axiom replaces boundedness). Proposition 2(i) is the 'ordering' tool: with h(1)=g(1)=1, ρ_h ≤ ρ_g (i.e. LR ≤ 1 on non-negative losses) iff h ≤ g pointwise — gives an iff for the sign of the underwriting margin Π−ρ and the solvency-type statement LR ≤ 1 ⇔ h ≤ g. Corollary 1 is the monotone (Yaari/Kusuoka/Schmeidler) version: once the loss-ratio axioms force the two 'endpoints' ρ=ρ_0 and Π=lim_{β→∞}ρ_β to be monotone, cash-invariant, law-invariant and comonotonic-additive, they are distortion risk measures with increasing h,g and the continuity hypothesis is not needed. Theorem 5 gives a Kusuoka-type mixture form for the convex members of the associated family: ρ_β = ∫ES_α dμ_β + ∫ES_α(−·) dν_β.

      STATEMENT: Setting: functionals on sets M of Borel distributions on R (M_dis = finitely supported, M_∞ = bounded, M_con etc.), ρ̂(X)=ρ(F_X). CxLS (Def. 1.1): ρ has convex level sets on M if ρ(F)=ρ(G) ⇒ ρ(λF+(1−λ)G)=ρ(F) for all λ∈[0,1] with the mixture in M. THEOREM 3.2: Let M be convex and contain all three-point distributions and h ∈ H (BV, h(0)=0). Then I_h has CxLS on M IF AND ONLY IF h ∈ H* = H1* ∪ H2* ∪ H3*, where H1*: h = c·h(1)1_{(0,1)} + h(1)1_{{1}}, c∈[0,1] (I_h = h(1)(c·ess sup + (1−c)·ess inf)); H2*: h(t)=t·h(1) (I_h=h(1)E); H3*: h = c·h(1)1_{{α}} + h(1)1_{(α,1]}, α∈(0,1), c∈[0,1] (I_h = h(1)·VaR^c_{1−α}, a mixture of left/right quantiles). COROLLARY 3.3: if I_h has CxLS on M_dis then I_h is monotone. PROPOSITION 4.1: an injective function of functionals with CxLS has CxLS. PROPOSITION 4.2: if ρ2 has CxLS on M then (ρ1,ρ2) has CxLS on M iff ρ1 has CxLS on every level set M(r)={F: ρ2(F)=r}. PROPOSITION 5.2: a map I : M_∞ → R^d is comonotonic-additive and uniformly continuous w.r.t. the Wasserstein-L^∞ metric w(F,G)=sup_t |F^{-1}(t)−G^{-1}(t)| IF AND ONLY IF I = (I_{h1},…,I_{hd}) is a d-dimensional signed Choquet integral. THEOREM 5.3: for p∈(0,1), h∈H, (I_h, VaR_p) has CxLS on M_dis iff one of: (i) I_h = a VaR_p + I_{h*}, h*∈H*; (ii) I_h = a VaR_p + bE + c ES_p; (iii) I_h = aVaR_p + bVaR_p^+ + c ess sup, bc>0; (iv) I_h = aVaR_p + bVaR_p^+ + c ess inf, bc<0. PROPOSITION 6.4: elicitability or identifiability implies CxLS. Section 7 (open): characterize all two-dimensional signed Choquet integrals (I_h,I_g) with CxLS; characterize elicitable/identifiable d-dimensional signed Choquet integrals.
      USE: Proposition 5.2 applies DIRECTLY to the pair (ρ,Π): the vector (ρ,Π)=(I_h,I_g) is characterized as comonotonic-additive + w-uniformly continuous (no monotonicity), on distributions, which is the natural home of a law-invariant LR. Theorem 3.2 settles elicitability/identifiability questions for LR (F6): the level set {LR=β} is the zero set of I_{h−βg}; it is convex whenever h−βg ∈ H*, which (for h≠g, both increasing, h(1)=g(1)=1) happens for all β only when h,g both lie in H1* or both in H3* with the same α (e.g. LR = VaR^{c1}_{1−α}/VaR^{c2}_{1−α}, or (c1 ess sup+(1−c1) ess inf)/(c2 ess sup+(1−c2) ess inf)); since identifiability ⇒ CxLS (Prop. 6.4), LR with e.g. ρ=VaR_p, Π=E, or ρ=ES, is NOT identifiable in the Nolde–Ziegel/Fissler sense — the 'identification function' V(β,X)=ρ_β(X) of F6 is a functional identification (zero-set) only, not of the form ∫V(β,y)dF(y) unless h−βg is linear. A full iff for 'LR has CxLS' is a special case of the open problem in Section 7; the conjecture is: LR=I_h/I_g (h≠g increasing, normalized) has CxLS on M_dis iff {h,g} ⊂ H1* or {h,g} ⊂ H3* with common α (sufficiency proved by the zero-set argument; necessity would use Kou–Peng three-point distributions). Prop. 4.2 is the tool: (LR,Π) has CxLS iff LR has CxLS on each level set of Π.

      STATEMENT: Setting: standard atomless probability space; D = distribution functions on R; H = {h:[0,1]→[0,1] non-decreasing, h(0)=0, h(1)=1}; distortion risk measure ρ_h(F)=∫ h(1−F(x))dx-type Choquet integral; D* = distributions with finite support. Elicitability (Def. 2.1): ρ is elicitable if there is a strictly consistent scoring function S with E S(t,X) ≤ E S(x,X) for all t∈ρ(F), x∈R, equality only if x∈ρ(F). THEOREM 3.1: if ρ_h (h ∈ H) restricted to D* has convex level sets, then h is one of: (i) h(t)=t (mean); (ii) for some α∈(0,1), c∈[0,1]: h=0 on [0,α), h(α)=c, h=1 on (α,1] (quantile/VaR type, mixture of left and right α-quantiles); (iii) for some c∈[0,1]: h(0)=0, h=c on (0,1), h(1)=1 (mixture of ess inf and ess sup). Combined with Osband's necessity of CxLS for elicitability and the known elicitability of mean and quantiles: an elicitable distortion risk measure is either the mean or a quantile (with the caveat that mixtures c·VaR^-+(1−c)VaR^+ are not strictly elicitable on D*). Remark 3.1: three-point distributions suffice, so any distortion risk measure other than mean/VaR is not elicitable even w.r.t. bounded discrete distributions.

The three-point-distribution technique (Remark 3.1) is the proof device for necessity statements about LR on M_dis (and matches the Bernoulli / two-layer identification of F3).

DQ (Def. 1): DQ^ρ_α(X)=α*/α, α* = inf{β∈I : ρ_β(ΣX_i) ≤ Σρ_α(X_i)} (inf ∅ = ᾱ). MCP = monotone [M], constant-additive [CA] ϕ(X+c)=ϕ(X)+c, positively homogeneous [PH]. Axioms on an index D:(L^∞)^n→R with reference MCP risk measure ϕ (X ≃_m Y means ϕ(X_i)=ϕ(Y_i) ∀i): [+] D ≥ 0; [LI] D(X+c)=D(X), c∈R^n; [SI] D(λX)=D(X), λ>0; [R]_ϕ D(X) ≤ D(Y) whenever X ≃_m Y and ΣX_i ≤ ΣY_i; [N]_ϕ D(0)=0, D ≤ 1 on duplicate portfolios, D ≥ 1 on portfolios worse than duplicate; [C]_ϕ if Y^k ≃_m X and (ΣX_i − ΣY^k_i)_+ → 0 in L^∞ then (D(X)−D(Y^k))_+ → 0; [PC] for all X, d, {w∈Δ_n : D(w⊙X) ≤ d} is convex. THEOREM 1: D satisfies [+],[LI],[SI],[R]_ϕ,[N]_ϕ,[C]_ϕ for some MCP ϕ IF AND ONLY IF D = DQ^ρ_α for some α and some decreasing class ρ of MCP risk measures (and one may take ρ_α=ϕ). THEOREM 2: n ≥ 4, ϕ non-linear coherent: D satisfies the six axioms plus [PC] iff D = DQ^ρ_α for a decreasing class of coherent risk measures with ρ_α=ϕ. THEOREM 3(ii): diversification benefit exists iff DQ<1; (iv) if ρ_α comonotonic-additive and X comonotonic then DQ=1. THEOREM 4: DQ^{VaR}_α(X) = (1/α) P(ΣX_i > ΣVaR_α(X_i)); DQ^{ES}_α(X) = (1/α) min_{r>0} E[(rΣ(X_i − ES_α(X_i)) + 1)_+] when P(ΣX_i > ΣES_α(X_i))>0. What transfers is the PROOF ARCHITECTURE: recover the family from the index by solving for the cash amount at which the index hits a level, use [SI] for homogeneity and [LI] for cash-additivity, use a continuity axiom only through the auxiliary map, and let the normalization axiom pin the reference member. Remark 3 formalizes the F5 link to Cherny–Madan.

Φ^p_c = {h concave on [0,1], h(0)=0, h(1)=c, ‖h'‖_q<∞}, q conjugate to p. THEOREM 2.2: ρ : L^p → R is a law-invariant convex risk functional IF AND ONLY IF there exist c ∈ R and β : Φ^p_c → (−∞,∞] with ρ(X) = sup_{h∈Φ^p_c} {∫_0^1 VaR_α(X) dh(α) − β(h)} for all X ∈ L^p (β may be taken convex, Remark 2.3). THEOREM 2.4: ρ is additionally positively homogeneous iff ρ(X) = sup_{h∈Ψ} ∫_0^1 VaR_α(X) dh(α) for some Ψ ⊂ Φ^p_c. PROPOSITION 2.7: if (B1) holds with ρ(1)−ρ(0) ≠ 0 then convexity (B2) and quasi-convexity are equivalent; Example 2.5: fails when ρ(1)−ρ(0)=0.

Proposition 2.7 is directly relevant to the loss ratio: ρ_β satisfies (B1) with ρ_β(1)−ρ_β(0)=1 ≠ 0, so for the associated family quasi-convexity and convexity COINCIDE — hence quasi-convexity of ρ_β (the natural Cherny–Madan-type requirement) is equivalent to h−βg concave and to subadditivity of ρ_β. The difference D=ρ−Π=I_{h−g} has c=0, where the equivalence breaks (Example 2.5), explaining why quasi-convexity of LR itself (F4, 'open in general') is delicate at β=1.

      STATEMENT: Setting: X ∈ L^1 (atomless space), ε ∈ (0,1). PELVE: Π_ε(X) = inf{c ∈ [1,1/ε] : ES_{1−cε}(X) ≤ VaR_{1−ε}(X)} (inf ∅ = ∞), i.e. the level c solving ES_{1−cε}(X)=VaR_{1−ε}(X). PROPOSITION 1 (existence): equivalent are (i) some c∈[1,1/ε] solves ES_{1−cε}(X)=VaR_{1−ε}(X); (ii) Π_ε(X)∈[1,1/ε] and solves it; (iii) E[X] ≤ VaR_{1−ε}(X); (iv) Π_ε(X)<∞. PROPOSITION 2 (uniqueness): if E[X] ≤ VaR_{1−ε}(X) and VaR_p(X) is not constant on [1−ε,1) then the solution is unique. THEOREM 1: (i) Π_ε(λX+a)=Π_ε(X), λ>0, a∈R (location-scale invariance); (ii) Π_ε(f(X)) ≤ Π_ε(X) for increasing concave f; (iii) Π_ε(g(X)) ≥ Π_ε(X) for strictly increasing convex g; (iv) for comonotonic mixtures of increasing transforms, Π_ε lies between the two values (quasi-convex and quasi-concave along comonotonic directions). Remark 5: Π_ε is in general neither quasi-convex nor quasi-concave for non-comonotonic variables. Remark 3: Π_ε equals the bPOE ratio c_x(X)/P(X>x) when P(X>x)=ε. Assa–Lin–Wang Prop. 4(i): the PELVE curve ε ↦ Π_X(ε) is increasing (decreasing) iff the dual PELVE curve is; Thm 2: constant dual PELVE 1/c forces the quantile function to be f(y)=C1+C2 y^α+O(y^ζ) with (α+1)^{−1/α}=c; whether the PELVE curve determines the law up to location-scale is left open.
      USE: PELVE is the prototype of 'a level in association with a family': Π_ε(X)=c ⇔ ES_{1−cε}(X) − VaR_{1−ε}(X) = 0, i.e. the zero set of a DIFFERENCE of two Choquet integrals (a signed Choquet integral with distortion min{t/(cε),1} − 1_{t>ε}, non-monotone), exactly as in F7. The existence/uniqueness pattern (Prop. 1-2: existence iff the two risk measures are ordered at the extreme level; uniqueness iff a non-flatness condition on the quantile) is what the loss-ratio cash-solvability axiom must replicate: for LR, c ↦ LR(X+c) is a Möbius map (c+ρ(X))/(c+Π(X)), so existence/uniqueness of the break-even cash amount for level β≠1 holds iff ρ(X)≠Π(X). Theorem 1(iv)/Remark 5 show the realistic shape-regularity for such level functionals: comonotonic intermediate-value (mediant) property (F1) but no global quasi-convexity — supporting the choice of the mediant property as an LR axiom.

Its hypotheses (monotone families, ordering at endpoints, strict monotonicity in the level) are the analogue of the loss-ratio requirement that the Möbius cash profile be non-degenerate (ρ(X)≠Π(X)).

      STATEMENT: Setting: X = L^0 r.v.'s bounded below; distortion risk measure ρ_h(X_F)=∫_0^1 F^{-1}(t) dh(t) with h increasing, right-continuous, h(0)=h(0+)=0, h(1)=h(1−)=1 (note: this paper's h is the DUAL of the survival-form distortion; convexity here = concavity of the survival-form distortion). Diversification ratio (2.2): ρ(X_1+…+X_n)/(ρ(X_1)+…+ρ(X_n)); n-superadditivity ratio Δ^n_F(ρ)= sup{ρ(S): S∈S_n(F)}/(nρ(X_F)); extreme-aggregation measure ρ̄(X_F)=limsup_n (1/n) sup{ρ(S) : S ∈ S_n(F)}; (2.5): sup_n Δ^n_F(ρ) = ρ̄(X_F)/ρ(X_F). THEOREM 3.2: for a distortion risk measure ρ_h, ρ̄ = ρ_{h*} where h* is the largest convex minorant of h; ρ_{h*} is the smallest coherent risk measure dominating ρ_h (e.g. VaR_p ↦ ES_p). LEMMA 2.2: if ρ is subadditive then ρ̄ ≤ ρ; under positive homogeneity, comonotonic additivity, or convexity with ρ(0)=0, ρ̄ ≥ ρ. THEOREM 4.1: for convex ρ with ρ(0)=0, ρ̄ is the smallest coherent risk measure dominating ρ. PROPOSITION 4.3: for shortfall risk measures with loss function ℓ, ρ̄ is the expectile e_{p_ℓ}, p_ℓ = a/(a+b).
      USE: The only place in this strand where a RATIO of risk-measure values is itself the object (the superadditivity/diversification ratio), and the result is a sup-formula, not an axiomatization — evidence that ratio functionals have been treated via level/sup constructions rather than via iff axioms. For the loss ratio it yields the dependence-uncertainty bound sup over dependence of LR(S_n)/LR-type quantities: by WWW Thm 5, lim_n sup_S I_h(S)/sup_S I_g(S) = I_{h*}/I_{g*} (concave envelopes), i.e. the worst-case loss ratio of a large homogeneous pool is the loss ratio of the coherent envelopes; and Lemma 2.2 shows comonotonic additivity makes the ratio ≥ its marginal value — the comonotonic direction is the 'no diversification' direction for LR (consistent with F1's mediant property, which gives equality LR(X+Y)=LR(X) for comonotone copies).

      STATEMENT: Definitions: inter-quantile difference ΔQ_p = Q_p − Q^-_{1−p} on L^0 (p∈[1/2,1)); inter-ES difference ΔES_p = ES_p − ES^-_{1−p} on L^1 (p∈(0,1)), equivalently ΔES_p(X)=ES_p(X)+ES_p(−X); inter-expectile difference Δex_p = ex_p − ex_{1−p}. THEOREM 3 (characterization): for a variability measure ν : X → [0,∞] the following are equivalent: (i) ν satisfies (B1)-(B8); (ii) ν satisfies (B1)-(B4) and one of (B5)-(B6); (iii) ν(X)=∫_0^1 ΔES_p(X) dμ(p) for a finite Borel measure μ≠0 on (0,1]. Here (B4) = comonotonic additivity, (B5) = convex-order consistency, (B6) = convexity, (B7) = mixture concavity, (B8) = location invariance (with (B1)-(B3) the basic normalization/standardization/positive-homogeneity type properties, exact wording not fully read). Proposition 1: (1−p)ΔES_p = pΔES_{1−p} and ΔES_p = (1/(1−p))∫_p^1 ΔQ_q dq. The introduction notes ΔES_p is Example 4 (inter-ES range) of Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020), a signed Choquet integral with concave h, h(1)=0.
      USE: This is an existing IFF AXIOMATIZATION OF A DIFFERENCE OF TWO CHOQUET INTEGRALS (ES_p − ES^-_{1−p}), proved precisely by the signed-Choquet route: location invariance makes the distortion satisfy k(1)=0, comonotonic additivity + law invariance give the signed Choquet form (WWW Thm 1), and convex-order consistency/convexity forces k concave (WWW Thm 3). The loss-ratio analogue is the underwriting margin D = ρ − Π = I_{h−g}, with (h−g)(1)=0: D is a 'distortion variability measure' (law-invariant, location-invariant, comonotonic-additive, positively homogeneous), and LR = 1 + D/Π. Hence an axiomatization of LR can be split as: axiomatize the pair (Π, D) = (premium, margin) where Π is a monotone cash-additive distortion risk measure and D a signed Choquet variability functional — the Bellini et al. template gives the exact extra axioms (B4-B6) under which D is a mixture of inter-ES differences, i.e. h−g concave, i.e. (by WWW Thm 3) all ρ_β with β<1 convex.

Zitikis (2021), 'An axiomatic foundation for the Expected Shortfall', Management Science 67(3), 1413-1429, Axioms M, LI, P, NRC, Theorem 1, Theorem 2, Proposition 4, Lemma A.2; and T. Fissler, F. Liu, R. Wang, L. Wei (2026), 'Elicitability and identifiability of tail risk measures', Mathematical Finance 36(3), 451-464, Def. 3.1, Def. 3.7, Section 3, Remark 4.8. PDFs: https://sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2020Wang-Zitikis-MS.pdf ; https://sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2025Fissler-Liu-Wang-Wei-MF.pdf (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Wang–Zitikis, domain L^1 (also any L^q, q∈(1,∞], same proof): Axiom M: ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y) whenever X ≤ Y; Axiom LI: ρ(X)=ρ(Y) whenever X =_d Y; Axiom P (prudence = lower semicontinuity): lim_k ρ(ξ_k) ≥ ρ(X) whenever ξ_k → X pointwise and the limit exists; Axiom NRC: there exists an event A with ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for all X,Y sharing the tail event A. THEOREM 1: ρ : L^1 → R with ρ(1)=1 satisfies M, LI, P, NRC IF AND ONLY IF ρ = ES_p for some p∈(0,1), with p = 1 − P(A); without ρ(1)=1 the conclusion is ρ = λES_p, λ>0. THEOREM 2: for q∈[0,1), ρ : L^q → R satisfies M, LI, P, NRC iff ρ ≡ 0. PROPOSITION 4: under LI, NRC ⇔ p-additivity for some p. LEMMA A.2: a monotone ρ : L^1 → R is law-invariant and p-additive iff ρ = aE + bES_p for some a,b∈R. Fissler et al.: V : R^k×R → R^k is a strict M-identification function for T if T(F) = {x : ∫V(x,y)dF(y)=0} for all F∈M; T is M-identifiable if such V exists; CxLS (Def. 3.7): T(F0)∩T(F1) ⊂ T((1−λ)F0+λF1); Section 3: identifiability ⇒ CxLS, elicitability ⇒ CxLS*; Remark 4.8 (under continuity/richness assumptions, citing Steinwart et al.): ρ* is M-identifiable iff it has CxLS on M iff M-elicitable.
      USE: Wang–Zitikis Lemma A.2 is an iff that replaces comonotonic additivity by the much weaker 'p-additivity' (additivity on pairs sharing a tail event) and still lands in the two-dimensional span {aE + bES_p}: applied to the loss ratio it suggests a tail-event version of the characterization: if ρ and Π are monotone, law-invariant and additive on pairs sharing a common tail event A, then LR = (aE + bES_p)/(a'E + b'ES_p) — a 'mean/ES' loss ratio, which also appears as case (ii) of Wang–Wei Theorem 5.3 (the (I_h,VaR_p)-pairs with CxLS). Theorem 2 warns that such characterizations are domain-sensitive (nothing survives on L^q, q<1), so any L^p extension of the LR theorem must carry Wang–Zitikis/ASTIN-type integrability or continuity-at-infinity axioms. Fissler et al. make precise the sense in which F6 holds: V(β,X)=ρ_β(X) is a zero-set ('functional') identification, but LR is M-identifiable in the strict sense only if it has CxLS, which (via Wang–Wei Thm 3.2) fails outside the VaR-type/extremal cases.


### Law-invariant coherent/convex risk measures (Kusuoka / JST / Föllmer–Schied / Delbaen / Acerbi / signed-Choquet strand)
KEY INSIGHT: Every classical law-invariant representation theorem (Kusuoka 2001 Thm 4/7; Frittelli–Rosazza Gianin 2005; JST 2006 Thm 2.1/2.3; Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.62/4.93; Acerbi Thm 4.1; Shapiro Thm 3.1) assumes MONOTONICITY, which the associated loss-ratio family ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) violates (F2). But the two deepest results in this strand do not need it: (a) Schmeidler's 1986 Prop. 2 (= Thm 7 in Wang–Wei–Willmot) and its law-invariant form, Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Thm 1: on atomless L^∞, 'law-invariant + comonotone-additive + uniformly norm-continuous ⟺ signed Choquet integral I_h, h of bounded variation', with monotonicity ⟺ h increasing (Lemma 2(i)) and convex ⟺ subadditive ⟺ quasi-convex ⟺ h concave (Thm 3); (b) JST 2006 Thm 2.2 (concave + law-invariant + norm-usc ⟹ weak*-usc, no monotonicity) together with Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang 2020 Thm 2.2/2.4 (law-invariant, convex, norm-continuous, translation-covariant with constant c — no monotonicity — ⟺ sup of signed Choquet integrals with concave h minus penalty). Hence the right way to get an iff theorem for LR is to impose the axioms on the associated family ρ_β (which Assa's Prop. 1 makes intrinsic to LR via ρ_β(X)=−m where LR(X−m)=β) and to use the signed/non-monotone theorems; monotonicity of ρ and Π individually is then a separate, optional axiom (h,g increasing). Because ρ_β is cash-additive with constant 1−β≠0 (Lemma 2(ii)), its sublevel sets are translates of {ρ_β ≤ 0}={LR ≤ β}, so WWW Thm 3 applies level-set-wise and gives, within the distortion class, LR quasi-convex on L^∞ ⟺ h−βg concave ∀β ≥ 0 ⟺ h concave and g convex (and quasi-concave ⟺ h convex, g concave), closing F4; Shapiro's uniqueness (Thm 3.1(ii)) plus h(t)=ρ(1_A), P(A)=t, gives the identifiability F3 for free. Caveats: all equivalences are proved on L^∞ (signed) of an atomless space; on L^∞_+ only the '⟸' directions are immediate and the quasi-convexity '⟹' needs the WWW test variables to be nonnegative (plausible by translation, to be checked); Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 and Chen–Gao–Leung–Li 2022 / Filipović–Svindland 2012 supply the extension to L^p and Orlicz spaces, and Shapiro warns that comonotonic ⟺ single-generator fails at p=∞.
CANDIDATE IFF: Candidate Theorem (signed-Choquet characterization of the loss ratio). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless and let LR be defined on D={X∈L^∞: Π(X)≠0} (bounded losses, possibly after cash shifts) with values in [0,∞). Define, whenever it exists, the associated family ρ_β(X) := −m_β(X), where m_β(X) is the unique solution of LR(X−m)=β (β≠1). Then the following are equivalent: (I) LR satisfies (A1) law invariance; (A2) scale invariance LR(λX)=LR(X), λ>0; (A3) break-even translation: for every X the map c ↦ LR(X+c) is continuous and strictly monotone with limit 1 as c→+∞ (so m_β(X) exists uniquely for every β in the range of LR); (A4) for two distinct values β_1,β_2∉{1} (equivalently for all β≠1) the functional ρ_{β_i} is comonotone-additive [ρ_{β}(X+Y)=ρ_β(X)+ρ_β(Y) for comonotone X,Y]; (A5) each ρ_{β_i} is uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous. (II) There exist h,g: [0,1]→R of bounded variation with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1 such that LR(X)=I_h(X)/I_g(X), I_h(X)=∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X ≥ x))−1)dx+∫_0^∞ h(P(X ≥ x))dx (signed Choquet integrals, Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 Thm 1 applied to ρ=ρ_0 and Π=(ρ_0−(1−β)ρ_β)/β), in which case ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} for every β≠1 and the pair (h,g) is unique unless LR ≡ 1. Supplements, all 'iff' within (II): ρ and Π are monotone ⟺ h and g are increasing (Lemma 2(i)); ρ_β is convex (⟺ subadditive ⟺ quasi-convex) ⟺ h−βg is concave (Thm 3); LR is quasi-convex on L^∞ ⟺ h concave and g convex (then LR ≥ 1, the 'conic/bid-price picture'), and LR is quasi-concave ⟺ h convex and g concave (then LR ≤ 1, the 'actuarial picture'); ρ (resp. Π) is coherent ⟺ h (resp. g) is concave and increasing (Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.93 / JST Thm 2.3 / Acerbi Thm 4.1), in which case ρ=∫ES_α dμ_ρ, Π=∫ES_α dμ_Π with unique μ's (Shapiro Thm 3.1) and ρ_β=(1−β)^{-1}(∫ES_α dμ_ρ − β∫ES_α dμ_Π) is a signed Kusuoka mixture; if one drops (A4) and assumes instead that ρ_β is convex and norm-continuous for the relevant β, then (Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang Thm 2.2, JST Thm 2.2) LR(X) ≤ β ⟺ sup_{k∈Φ_1} (∫VaR_α(X)dk(α) − β_β(k)) ≤ 0 with concave, not necessarily increasing, k, and the sublevel cones {LR ≤ β} are automatically σ(L^∞,L^1)-closed (no Fatou axiom on LR is needed). What is needed on the pair vs. on LR alone: (A1),(A2),(A3) are axioms on LR itself; (A4),(A5) are axioms on two members of the associated family, which (A3) makes functions of LR alone; monotonicity and concavity are additional axioms on ρ and Π separately and are NOT consequences of any axiom on LR (F2).
THEOREMS:
  [1] S. Kusuoka (2001), "On law invariant coherent risk measures", Advances in Mathematical Economics 3, 83–95 (DOI 10.1007/978-4-431-67891-5_4), Theorem 4; restated verbatim as eq. (1.10) in A. Shapiro (2013), "On Kusuoka representation of law invariant risk measures", Math. Oper. Res. 38(1), 142–152 (Optimization Online 2011/09/3164) and as Theorem 1.1 / Theorem 2.1 of Jouini–Schachermayer–Touzi (2006). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let (Ω,F,P) be a standard (in particular atomless) probability space and ρ: L^∞(Ω,F,P) → R. Then ρ is a law-invariant coherent risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, subadditive, positively homogeneous) with the Fatou property [ρ(X) ≤ liminf ρ(X_n) whenever sup_n‖X_n‖_∞<∞, X_n→X in probability]  ⟺  there is a set M of probability measures on (0,1] such that ρ(X) = sup_{m∈M} ∫_{(0,1]} ES_α(X) m(dα) for all X ∈ L^∞, where ES_α(X) = α^{-1}∫_0^α VaR_{s}(X) ds (upper-tail convention for losses). (Shapiro's L^p version, p∈[1,∞): the same for real-valued ρ: L^p → R law-invariant coherent, with M supported on [0,1), no Fatou/lsc needed because finite convex functionals on L^p are norm-continuous.) By JST (2006) Thm 2.1 the Fatou hypothesis is redundant on L^∞ of a standard space.
      USE: Gives the target 'Kusuoka-type' class for ρ and Π separately when they are coherent. For the loss ratio: LR ≤ β ⟺ ρ_β(X) ≤ 0 with ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) a DIFFERENCE of two Kusuoka suprema – not itself coherent, so this theorem characterizes the ingredients (ρ,Π), not the family {ρ_β}. It is the right tool only for the 'pair' side of the iff.
  [2] S. Kusuoka (2001), op. cit., Theorem 7 (comonotonic case); verbatim modern form: E. Jouini, W. Schachermayer, N. Touzi (2006), "Law invariant risk measures have the Fatou property", Advances in Mathematical Economics 9, 49–71, Theorem 2.3 (pdf: mat.univie.ac.at/~schachermayer/pubs/preprnts/prpr0123.pdf), who write 'this latter result is known and may be found in [FS 04], Theorem 4.87' (= Föllmer–Schied 2nd ed.; = Thm 4.93 in the 4th ed. 2016, as cited by Wang–Wang–Wei 2020 ASTIN for 'the classic ES-based representation of coherent distortion risk measures').       STATEMENT: JST Thm 2.3 (read verbatim): Let (Ω,F,P) be a standard probability space and U: L^∞ → R. The following are equivalent: (i) U is a comonotone, law-invariant monetary utility function [U(X+c)=U(X)+c, U non-decreasing, U concave, U(X_1+X_2)=U(X_1)+U(X_2) for comonotone X_1,X_2 – which implies positive homogeneity]; (ii) there exists a probability measure m on [0,1] such that U(X) = ∫_0^1 U_α(X) dm(α) for all X ∈ L^∞, where U_α(X)=α^{-1}∫_0^α q_X(β)dβ for α∈(0,1), U_0=ess inf, U_1=E. No Fatou property is assumed (JST: 'in Theorem 7 of [K 01] the assumption of the Fatou property may also be dropped'). Equivalently (Föllmer–Schied 4th ed. Thm 4.93 / Sect. 4.6): ρ=−U is a Choquet integral ∫ X d(ψ∘P) with ψ a concave distortion function (ψ(0)=0, ψ(1)=1), ψ'(t)= ∫_{(t,1]} s^{-1} m(ds) a.e.
      USE: THIS is the exact citation for 'coherent & law-invariant & comonotone-additive ⟺ concave distortion'. For LR it characterizes the admissible monotone ingredients ρ=C_h, Π=C_g (h,g concave if both coherent). But ρ_β = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} is not monotone, so this theorem cannot be applied to the family; one needs the signed version (Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 1 below).
  [3] E. Jouini, W. Schachermayer, N. Touzi (2006), Adv. Math. Econ. 9, 49–71, Theorem 2.1 and Theorem 2.2 (read verbatim from the preprint pdf). The convex-case representation itself is attributed by JST to Frittelli & Rosazza Gianin (2005), "Law invariant convex risk measures", Adv. Math. Econ. 7, 33–46 (DOI 10.1007/4-431-27233-x_2) [FRG assume Fatou; JST remove it]. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Thm 2.1: Let (Ω,F,P) be standard and U: L^∞ → R. Equivalent: (i) U is a law-invariant monetary utility function [concave, non-decreasing, U(X+c)=U(X)+c]; (ii) there is a law-invariant, lower-semicontinuous, convex V: L^1 → [0,∞] with dom(V) ⊆ P(Ω,F,P) (densities) such that U(X)=inf_{Y∈L^1}{E[XY]+V(Y)}; (iii) there is a convex v: P([0,1]) → [0,∞] with U(X)=inf_{m∈P([0,1])}[∫_0^1 U_α(X) dm(α) + v(m)]. If any of these holds, U satisfies the Fatou property. Thm 2.2 (the 'automatic Fatou' mechanism): Let U: L^∞ → R∪{−∞} be concave, law invariant and u.s.c. for ‖·‖_∞. Then U is u.s.c. with respect to σ(L^∞,L^1). Thm 2.4: U law-invariant monetary with the Lebesgue property [U(X_n)→U(X) for uniformly bounded a.s.-convergent sequences] ⟺ representation (iii) with {v ≤ c} relatively compact in the Prokhorov topology on P(]0,1]) for each c>0.
      USE: Thm 2.2 needs only concavity + law invariance + norm-u.s.c. – NOT monotonicity and NOT cash-additivity. Hence it applies to the non-monotone family ρ_β whenever ρ_β is convex and norm-continuous: its sublevel sets {ρ_β ≤ 0} = {LR ≤ β} are then automatically σ(L^∞,L^1)-closed, so no Fatou-type axiom need be imposed on LR. Thm 2.4 (Lebesgue property) is the abstract form of F1's 'continuity along bounded a.s. convergence'.
  [4] F. Delbaen (2002), "Coherent risk measures on general probability spaces", in: Advances in Finance and Stochastics (Sandmann & Schönbucher eds.), Springer, 1–37, Theorem 3.2 (read verbatim from people.math.ethz.ch/~delbaen/ftp/preprints/RiskMeasuresGeneralSpaces.pdf). Same structure in Delbaen (2012), Monetary Utility Functions, Osaka Univ. Press, Ch. 4–5 (notes Handai_final.pdf, Thm 9 of Sect. 4.2 for the ba-representation; Ch. 5 'Law determined utilities' not readable online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let φ: L^∞(Ω,F,P) → R be translation invariant [φ(X+a)=φ(X)+a] and supermodular (coherent utility: monotone, superadditive, positively homogeneous), ρ=−φ. The following are equivalent: (1) there is an L^1(P)-closed convex set P_σ of probability measures absolutely continuous w.r.t. P with φ(X)=inf_{Q∈P_σ} E_Q[X] for all X∈L^∞; (2) the convex cone C={X: φ(X) ≥ 0} is σ(L^∞,L^1)-closed; (3) φ has the Fatou property [φ(X) ≥ limsup φ(X_n) for ‖X_n‖_∞ ≤ 1, X_n→X in probability]; (4) if X_n is uniformly bounded and decreases a.s. to X then φ(X_n)→φ(X). (Without Fatou one only gets a representation over finitely additive measures in ba.)
      USE: Shows that the acceptance cone of a coherent functional being weak*-closed is the same as a sequential continuity axiom. For LR, the cone K_β={LR ≤ β}={ρ−βΠ ≤ 0} is the acceptance cone of the non-coherent ρ_β; Delbaen's equivalence (2)⟺(3) survives for any cash-additive functional whose cone is convex, which suggests stating a continuity axiom on LR via its level cones.
  [5] H. Föllmer & A. Schied, Stochastic Finance, 4th ed. (De Gruyter 2016): Theorem 4.62 (law-invariant convex ⟺ Kusuoka-type representation with penalty; = Thm 4.59 in 3rd ed. 2011 as cited by Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang 2020), Theorem 4.67 (ES is the smallest law-invariant convex risk measure dominating VaR; cited as such by Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 and Wang–Wang–Wei 2020), Theorem 4.93 (comonotonic law-invariant coherent ⟺ ∫AV@R dμ ⟺ concave distortion; = Thm 4.87 in 2nd ed. 2004 as cited by JST 2006). Theorem numbers confirmed only through these citing papers; the book itself was not accessible online. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Thm 4.62 (4th ed.): (Ω,F,P) atomless, ρ: L^∞ → R convex risk measure. ρ is law-invariant ⟺ ρ(X)=sup_{μ∈M_1((0,1])} ( ∫_{(0,1]} AV@R_λ(X) μ(dλ) − β_min(μ) ), β_min(μ)=sup_{X∈A_ρ} ∫ AV@R_λ(X) μ(dλ); coherent case: ρ(X)=sup_{μ∈M} ∫ AV@R_λ(X) μ(dλ) with M ⊆ M_1((0,1]). Thm 4.93 (4th ed.): for a monetary risk measure ρ on L^∞ of an atomless space the following are equivalent: (a) ρ is law-invariant, comonotonic and convex (equivalently coherent); (b) ρ(X)=∫_{[0,1]} AV@R_λ(X) μ(dλ) for a μ∈M_1([0,1]); (c) ρ(X)=∫ (−X) d(ψ∘P) is a Choquet integral w.r.t. a concave distortion ψ: [0,1]→[0,1], ψ(0)=0, ψ(1)=1. [Statement reconstructed from the citing papers and memory; edition numbering as above.]
      USE: Supplies the 'concave distortion' side of the pair characterization in the sign convention of the project (survival-form Choquet integral C_g). Also provides, through Thm 4.62, the penalty-function form β_min(μ) that would be needed if ρ_β were merely convex rather than comonotone additive — but Thm 4.62 requires monotonicity, so for the non-monotone family the Liu–Cai–Lemieux–Wang version must be used instead.
  [6] C. Acerbi (2002), "Spectral measures of risk: a coherent representation of subjective risk aversion", J. Banking & Finance 26(7), 1505–1518; arXiv:cond-mat/0107190 (title there: 'Risk aversion and coherent risk measures: a spectral representation theorem'), Definition 2.3–2.4, Theorem 4.1 (read verbatim). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let X be a P/L random variable on (Ω,Σ,P) with E[X^+],E[X^−]<∞, φ ∈ L^1([0,1]) bounded, and M_φ(X) := −∫_0^1 F_X^{←}(p) φ(p) dp. Theorem 4.1: M_φ is a coherent risk measure (monotone, subadditive, positively homogeneous, translation invariant) ⟺ φ is an 'admissible risk spectrum', i.e. (i) positive: ∫_I φ ≥ 0 for every interval I ⊆ [0,1]; (ii) decreasing: ∫_{q−ε}^{q} φ ≥ ∫_q^{q+ε} φ for all q∈(0,1), ε>0 with [q−ε,q+ε]⊆[0,1]; (iii) normalized: ∫_0^1 |φ(p)| dp = 1. Sufficiency via M_φ = ∫ ES_α dμ(α); necessity by explicit counterexamples to monotonicity / subadditivity / translation invariance.
      USE: Translates 'concave distortion' into a weight function φ = −ψ' on quantiles. For LR = C_h/C_g with h,g absolutely continuous: ρ_β has spectrum φ_β = (φ_h − βφ_g)/(1−β), a SIGNED, non-monotone spectrum; Acerbi's necessity counterexamples are exactly the ones showing that ρ_β is not coherent (consistent with F2), and his Remark 2.6 domain V_φ shows how to extend LR beyond L^∞.
  [7] D. Schmeidler (1986), "Integral representation without additivity", Proc. AMS 97, 255–261, Proposition 2; quoted verbatim as Theorem 7 (Appendix A.1) of R. Wang, Y. Wei, G. Willmot (2020), "Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals", Math. Oper. Res. 45(3), 993–1015 (pdf: sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2019Wang-Wei-Willmot-MOR.pdf). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let S be a set, Σ a non-empty algebra of subsets of S, B the space of bounded real Σ-measurable functions with the sup norm. Suppose I: B → R is comonotonic additive [I(a+b)=I(a)+I(b) whenever (a(s)−a(t))(b(s)−b(t)) ≥ 0 ∀s,t] and continuous with respect to the supremum norm. Then, defining v(E)=I(1_E) on Σ, for all a∈B: I(a) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (v(a ≥ α) − v(S)) dα + ∫_0^∞ v(a ≥ α) dα. NO monotonicity is assumed; v is merely a set function (if I is monotone, v is a capacity and I is the classical Choquet integral).
      USE: The only classical representation theorem in this strand that does not need monotonicity. It is exactly what is needed for the non-monotone associated family ρ_β (F2, F7): comonotone additivity + sup-norm continuity ⟹ ρ_β is a (signed) Choquet integral w.r.t. v_β(E)=ρ_β(1_E).
  [8] R. Wang, Y. Wei, G. Willmot (2020), Math. Oper. Res. 45(3), 993–1015, Definition 1, Theorem 1, Lemma 2, Lemma 4, Theorem 3 (all read verbatim from the authors' pdf). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Standing assumption: (Ω,A,P) atomless; H := {h: [0,1]→R, h of bounded variation, h(0)=0}; I_h(X) := ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X ≥ x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X ≥ x)) dx, X∈L^∞. Theorem 1: a functional I: L^∞ → R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous (w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞) ⟺ I is a signed Choquet integral I_h for some h∈H. Lemma 2: for h∈H, (i) I_h is increasing (resp. decreasing) ⟺ h is increasing (resp. decreasing); (ii) I_h(X+c)=I_h(X)+c·h(1); (iii) I_h(λX)=λ I_h(X), λ>0; (iv) I_h(−X)=I_{ĥ}(X), ĥ(x)=h(1−x)−h(1). Lemma 4: |I_h(X)−I_h(Y)| ≤ TV(h)‖X−Y‖_∞. Theorem 3: for h∈H the following are equivalent: (i) h concave; (ii) I_h is convex-order consistent [X ≤_cx Y ⟹ I_h(X) ≤ I_h(Y)]; (iii) I_h subadditive; (iv) I_h convex; (v) I_h quasi-convex; (vi) I_h concave on mixtures (ρ̃(F)=I_h(X), X∼F, concave in F). Also I_h = I_{h^+} − I_{h^−} with h^± increasing.
      USE: The central theorem for the project. With ρ=C_h, Π=C_g one has ρ_β = I_{k_β}, k_β=(h−βg)/(1−β), k_β(1)=1 (cash-additive), non-monotone. Theorem 1 characterizes exactly such ρ_β (law-invariant + comonotone additive + Lipschitz, NO monotonicity), resolving the obstruction F2. Theorem 3 + Lemma 2(ii) give: {LR ≤ β}={I_{k_β} ≤ 0} is convex ⟺ I_{k_β} quasi-convex ⟺ h−βg concave; letting β run over [0,∞) yields LR quasi-convex on L^∞ ⟺ h concave and g convex (and quasi-concave ⟺ h convex, g concave), which settles F4 inside the distortion class. Lemma 2(i) shows monotonicity of ρ and Π separately ⟺ h,g increasing.
  [9] R. Wang, Q. Wang, Y. Wei (2020), "Distortion riskmetrics on general spaces", ASTIN Bulletin 50(3), 827–851, Definition 1, Proposition 1, Theorem 1, Theorem 2, Theorem 3, Theorem 5, Corollary 1 (read verbatim from sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2020Wang-Wang-Wei-ASTIN.pdf). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: (Ω,F,P) atomless; X a law-invariant convex cone with L^∞ ⊆ X ⊆ L^0; ρ_h(X)=∫ X d(h∘P) (signed Choquet integral), h∈H. Theorem 1: ρ: X → R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, continuous at infinity [lim_{M→∞} ρ((X∧M)∨(−M))=ρ(X)] and uniformly sup-continuous [∀ε ∃δ: ess sup|X−Y|<δ ⟹ |ρ(X)−ρ(Y)|<ε] ⟺ ρ is a distortion riskmetric ρ_h. Proposition 1: ρ_h finite on L^p if h∈H_q (‖h'‖_q<∞, 1/p+1/q=1); for concave h finiteness on L^p ⟹ h∈H_r ∀r<q. Theorem 2: for X,Y∈L^1, X ≤_cx Y ⟺ ρ_h(X) ≤ ρ_h(Y) for all concave h∈H with X,Y in dom ρ_h. Theorem 3 (X ⊆ L^1): h concave ⟺ ρ_h convex-order consistent ⟺ subadditive ⟺ convex ⟺ quasi-convex ⟺ concave on mixtures. Theorem 5 (X ⊆ L^1): ρ: X → R is a convex distortion riskmetric ⟺ there exist finite Borel measures μ,ν on [0,1] with ρ(X)=∫_0^1 ES_α(X) dμ(α) + ∫_0^1 ES_α(−X) dν(α); if ρ is increasing one can take ν=0. Corollary 1: ρ_h is a coherent risk measure ⟺ h concave (and increasing with h(1)=1).
      USE: Extends the signed-Choquet characterization from L^∞ to L^p / Orlicz-type spaces (unbounded losses), replacing 'uniformly norm-continuous' by 'uniformly sup-continuous + continuous at infinity'. Theorem 5 is the Kusuoka-type representation for a NON-monotone convex ρ_β: ρ_β(X)=∫ES_α(X)dμ_β + ∫ES_α(−X)dν_β, i.e. the loss-ratio family is a signed mixture of ES's of X and of −X – a 'two-sided Kusuoka representation' that makes the association LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0 explicit in ES terms.
  [10] P. Liu, J. Cai, C. Lemieux, R. Wang (2020), "Convex risk functionals: representation and applications", Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 90, 66–79, Definition 1.1, Theorem 2.2, Theorem 2.4, Example 2.3, Remark 2.3/2.5 (read from sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2019Liu-Cai-Lemieux-Wang-IME.pdf; the statements were paraphrased by the fetch tool because of quote-length limits). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: (Ω,F,P) atomless, p∈[1,∞]. ρ: L^p → R is a law-invariant convex risk functional if (B1) ρ(X+m)=ρ(X)+c·m for all m∈R, where c=ρ(1)−ρ(0) (c=1: risk measures; c=0: generalized deviations); (B2) convex; (B3) ‖·‖_p-continuous; (B4) law-invariant. NO monotonicity. Theorem 2.2: ρ is a law-invariant convex risk functional ⟺ there exist c∈R and β: Φ^p_c → (−∞,∞] such that ρ(X)=sup_{h∈Φ^p_c} ( ∫_0^1 VaR_α(X) dh(α) − β(h) ), where Φ^p_c={h concave on [0,1]: h(0)=0, h(1)=c, ‖h'‖_q<∞}, q=(1−1/p)^{−1} (for p=∞ the norm condition is dropped); ∫VaR dh is the signed Choquet integral ρ_h; β may be taken convex. Theorem 2.4: ρ is additionally positively homogeneous ⟺ ρ(X)=sup_{h∈Ψ^p_c} ∫VaR_α(X)dh(α) for some Ψ^p_c ⊆ Φ^p_c (β∈{0,∞}). Example 2.3: adding monotonicity ⟺ c=1 and β(h)=∞ for every non-increasing h (recovering Föllmer–Schied Thm 4.59, 3rd ed.).
      USE: The exact Kusuoka-type theorem for the non-monotone family: ρ_β = (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) satisfies (B1) with c=1 and is law-invariant; if ρ_β is convex and norm-continuous, Theorem 2.2/2.4 represent it as a supremum of signed Choquet integrals with concave (not nec. increasing) distortions, giving LR(X) ≤ β ⟺ sup_h{ρ_h(X) − β_β(h)} ≤ 0. This is the 'convex' route (beyond comonotone additivity) to an iff theorem for LR, and the constant c=ρ_β(1)−ρ_β(0)=1 is precisely Assa's cash-additivity axiom (Prop. 1).
  [11] A. Shapiro (2013), "On Kusuoka representation of law invariant risk measures", Math. Oper. Res. 38(1), 142–152, Theorem 3.1 (read verbatim from the Optimization Online pdf 2011/09/3164); complemented by A. Pichler & A. Shapiro (2015), "Minimal representation of insurance prices", Insurance: Math. Econ. 62, 184–193 (Optimization Online 2012/10/3660), Thm 2.2 and Prop. 3.1 (existence/uniqueness of a minimal Kusuoka set; Kusuoka representation ⟺ 'regularity' on spaces with atoms). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let Z=L^p(Ω,F,P), p∈[1,∞), (Ω,F,P) nonatomic, ρ: Z → R a law-invariant coherent risk measure with dual set A ⊆ Z* (ρ(Z)=sup_{ζ∈A}⟨ζ,Z⟩). Then: (i) A is generated by a single element η∈Z* [i.e. ρ(Z)=sup_{ζ∈O(η)}⟨ζ,Z⟩, O(η) the set of ζ distributionally equivalent to η] ⟺ there is a probability measure μ on [0,1) with ρ(Z)=∫_0^1 AV@R_α(Z) dμ(α) ∀Z∈Z; (ii) the μ in this representation is unique; (iii) ρ is comonotonic [ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for (X,Y) =_d (F_X^{−1}(U),F_Y^{−1}(U))] ⟺ A is generated by a single η; (iv) ρ comonotonic ⟺ the extreme points of A consist of a single class O(η). 'It is essential in (i),(iii),(iv) that p<∞.'
      USE: Gives UNIQUENESS of the spectral/Kusuoka measure for comonotonic coherent functionals on L^p, which is what underlies identifiability (F3): if LR = ρ/Π with both comonotonic coherent, then μ_ρ, μ_Π are unique, and LR determines (μ_ρ,μ_Π) up to the degenerate case LR≡1. Also warns that the comonotonic ⟺ single-generator equivalence fails on L^∞ (p=∞), so an iff theorem for LR on unbounded losses should be stated on L^p, p<∞.
  [12] F. Delbaen, F. Bellini, V. Bignozzi, J. Ziegel (2016), "Risk measures with convex level sets", Finance & Stochastics 20, 433–453, Theorem 3.9 (and Sect. 2.1 standing assumptions), read from people.math.ethz.ch/~delbaen/ftp/preprints/Convex_Level_Sets_final.pdf. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: (Ω,F,P) atomless; u: L^∞ → R a monetary (u(X+c)=u(X)+c, monotone, u(0)=0), concave, law-determined utility (Fatou automatic by JST). If u has convex level sets, CxLS: u(F)=u(G)=γ ⟹ u(λF+(1−λ)G)=γ for λ∈(0,1) (level sets of the induced functional on distributions are convex), then there exists a concave φ: R → R∪{−∞} such that u(ξ) ≥ 0 ⟺ E[φ(ξ)] ≥ 0 (shortfall-type acceptance set). Coherent corollary: the only coherent law-determined risk measures with CxLS (hence the only elicitable ones) are the expectiles e_α, α ≥ 1/2 (loss ℓ_α(x)=αx^+−(1−α)x^−). Companion result (Wang & Wei 2020, Math. Finance 30, 1337–1367, Thm 3.2): a signed Choquet integral I_h has CxLS on a convex set of distributions containing all three-point laws ⟺ h∈H*= {mixtures of ess sup/ess inf} ∪ {h(t)=t·h(1), the mean} ∪ {one-step h, i.e. a (mixed) quantile}; CxLS on discrete laws forces monotonicity (Cor. 3.3).
      USE: Relevant to F6: LR is not elicitable, but V(β,X)=ρ_β(X) identifies it. CxLS/elicitability theorems show that the only comonotone-additive LR with convex level sets in distribution would have both ρ and Π in H* (mean, quantile, extremes) – e.g. LR = VaR_p/E or E/VaR_p – so a 'mixture-convex-level-set' axiom on LR pins down the degenerate members of the distortion class and cannot be a general axiom.
  [13] S. Chen, N. Gao, D. H. Leung, L. Li (2022), "Automatic Fatou property of law-invariant risk measures", Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 105, 41–53 (arXiv:2107.08109), main theorem (abstract read verbatim); earlier D. Filipović & G. Svindland (2012), "The canonical model space for law-invariant convex risk measures is L^1", Math. Finance 22, 585–589 (from memory). Also F. Bellini, P. Koch-Medina, C. Munari, G. Svindland (2021), "Law-invariant functionals on general spaces of random variables", SIAM J. Financial Math. 12, 318–341 (arXiv:1808.00821), Prop. B.3–B.4. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Chen–Gao–Leung–Li: Let X be a rearrangement-invariant (law-invariant Banach lattice) function space over an atomless probability space. Every real-valued, law-invariant, coherent risk measure on X has the Fatou property at every X∈X with lim_n ‖X 1_{\{X ≤ −n\}}‖=0 ⟺ X has the AOCEA property (the distance from the convex hull of all equidistributed rearrangements of each nonnegative X∈X to the almost-order-continuous part X_a is zero); Orlicz spaces satisfy AOCEA. Filipović–Svindland: every law-invariant convex risk measure on L^∞ (atomless) extends uniquely to a law-invariant convex lsc risk measure on L^1 (its canonical model space). Bellini et al. Prop. B.3: for a convex σ(X,X*)-lsc functional π on a law-invariant subspace L^∞ ⊆ X ⊆ L^1, law invariance of π ⟺ Schur-convexity of π (monotonicity w.r.t. the convex order); Prop. B.4: such π is uniquely determined by its restriction to L^∞ and extends to L^1.
      USE: Justifies working on bounded losses (as in Assa 2024) without loss of generality: any law-invariant convex candidate ρ_β defined on L^∞ extends canonically to L^1/Orlicz spaces, and the Fatou/continuity axioms need not be imposed on LR. Schur-convexity ⟺ law invariance is the mechanism behind WWW/WWW-ASTIN Theorem 2–3 and hence behind the quasi-convexity answer for LR.
  [14] Y. Song & J.-A. Yan (2009), "Risk measures with comonotonic subadditivity or convexity and respecting stochastic orders", Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 45(3), 459–465 (DOI 10.1016/j.insmatheco.2009.09.011; page could not be opened – proxy rate limit – statement from memory); see also Song & Yan (2006), 'The representations of two types of functionals on L^∞(Ω,F) and L^∞(Ω,F,P)', Science in China Ser. A 49. (confidence: low)
      STATEMENT: (Ω,F,P) atomless, ρ: L^∞ → R monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous and law-invariant. (a) ρ is comonotonically subadditive [ρ(X+Y) ≤ ρ(X)+ρ(Y) for comonotone X,Y] and respects first-order stochastic dominance ⟺ ρ(X)=sup_{g∈G} ∫ X d(g∘P) for a set G of (not nec. concave) distortion functions g; (b) ρ is comonotonically convex and respects stop-loss (second-order) order ⟺ ρ(X)=sup_{g∈G_c} ∫ X d(g∘P) over a set G_c of CONCAVE distortions (i.e. ρ is a supremum of coherent distortion risk measures). Conversely such suprema have the stated properties.
      USE: Intermediate class between 'comonotone additive' (single distortion) and 'convex' (Kusuoka with penalty). If one only assumes LR's associated family is comonotonically subadditive and law invariant (not monotone), the analogous signed result is Corollary 2 of Wang–Wei–Willmot: ρ_β=sup_{h∈H_0} I_h, with Γρ_β = sup I_{h*} its convex envelope.
