KEY INSIGHT: Every classical acceptability-index theorem (Cherny-Madan; Biagini-Bion-Nadal; Bielecki et al.) is the composition of two independent steps: (1) index <-> nested family of level sets, which needs NO topology and NO monotonicity (Drapeau-Kupper Thm 1.10 / BCDK Thm 2.4: quasiconcavity <-> convexity of the level sets, scale invariance <-> level sets are cones); (2) level set -> dual/robust representation by the bipolar theorem, which needs lower semicontinuity but again NOT monotonicity (Volle 1998 Thm 3.4 = Frittelli-Maggis Thm 1; Drapeau-Kupper Thm 2.7 with the TRIVIAL preorder K={0}, which still yields a UNIQUE maximal risk function; BCDK Prop 2.15 for the scale-invariant case; FMP Prop 24(i) on distributions; CMMM Thm 1). Monotonicity enters only in (2) to restrict the dual variables from the whole dual L^1 (signed kernels Z) to probability densities (Z>=0) -- i.e. it is exactly the positivity of the 'supporting kernels', nothing else. Hence F2 (LR is never pointwise monotone) does NOT kill the Cherny-Madan programme: it replaces coherent risk measures rho_beta = sup_{Q in D_beta} E_Q[.] by 'coherent-minus-monotone' functionals, i.e. sublinear/superlinear lsc cash-additive functionals represented over weak*-closed convex sets of SIGNED measures of total mass 1 -- which is precisely the class of signed Choquet integrals of F7 when law invariance and comonotone additivity are added (Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020). Two further things LR supplies that general non-monotone quasiconvex functions lack: (a) the normalisation of the kernels (Drapeau-Kupper Thm 2.8 / BCDK Remark 2.13 need an order unit kappa with <Z,kappa> > 0 on the polar) is restored by kappa = 1 because, for beta > 1, 'translation to break-even' (F1) makes the level cone {LR <= beta} cash-monotone upward with 1 in its norm-interior (LR(1+eps Y) -> 1 uniformly), forcing E[Z] < 0 on the polar, so one may normalise E[Z] = 1 after the sign flip; (b) for law-invariant LR the Fatou/weak*-lsc hypothesis is free (Drapeau-Kupper-Reda Prop 2.2 / Jouini-Schachermayer-Touzi / Svindland: norm-lsc + quasiconvex + law-invariant => Fatou, no monotonicity). What this strand CANNOT deliver from LR alone is the AFFINE-in-beta structure rho_beta ∝ rho - beta Pi (a single pair (rho,Pi)): the index/family duality only gives a nested family of signed-kernel cones; affinity in beta is an additional axiom (equivalently a weighted-mediant property LR(X+Y) = (Pi(X)LR(X)+Pi(Y)LR(Y))/(Pi(X)+Pi(Y)) for comonotone X,Y, which identifies Pi up to scale and makes rho := LR·Pi comonotone additive), and separate monotonicity of rho and of Pi is invisible in LR (F2) and must be stated on the pair.
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (signed-kernel acceptability representation of the loss ratio). Let (Omega,F,P) be a probability space, L = L^infty, L_+ = {X in L : X >= 0}, losses X in L_+\\{0}, and LR: L_+\\{0} -> [0,+infty]. Fix the 'conic region' beta in (1,+infty) and write A_beta := {X : LR(X) <= beta}. Consider the axioms, ALL stated on LR alone: (A1) scale invariance: LR(lambda X) = LR(X), lambda > 0; (A2) quasiconvexity: A_beta is convex for every beta; (A3) Fatou/lsc: A_beta is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-closed for every beta [automatic if LR is law-invariant and ||.||_infty-lsc on a non-atomic space, by Drapeau-Kupper-Reda Prop 2.2]; (A4) break-even cash-monotonicity: for beta >= 1, X in A_beta and c >= 0 imply X + c in A_beta, and for beta > 1 the constant 1 is a ||.||_infty-interior point of A_beta (equivalently LR(1 + eps Y) -> 1 uniformly on ||Y||_infty <= 1). THEN THE FOLLOWING ARE EQUIVALENT: (i) LR satisfies (A1)-(A4); (ii) there exists a family (D_beta)_{beta > 1} of nonempty, sigma(L^1,L^infty)-closed, convex, L^1-bounded subsets of the hyperplane {Z in L^1 : E[Z] = 1} -- SIGNED kernels, Z >= 0 NOT required -- nonincreasing in beta (D_gamma subset D_beta for 1 < beta <= gamma), such that, with rho_beta(X) := inf_{Z in D_beta} E[Z X] (a superlinear, cash-additive rho_beta(X+c) = rho_beta(X)+c, weak*-usc, NON-monotone functional), LR(X) = inf{ beta > 1 : rho_beta(X) >= 0 } (inf emptyset := +infty) for all X with LR(X) > 1; i.e. LR is in Assa's association with the family {rho_beta}: {LR <= beta} = {rho_beta >= 0}, and LR(X) = beta iff rho_beta(X) = 0 (for the left-continuous version). The maximal family D_beta = {Z : E[Z]=1, E[ZX] >= 0 for all X in A_beta} = -(A_beta)° ∩ {E[Z]=1} is unique. Proof: (i)=>(ii) A_beta is a weak*-closed convex cone (A1-A3) with 1 in its interior and A_beta + R_+ subset A_beta (A4); bipolar theorem (Drapeau-Kupper Thm 2.7 with trivial preorder K={0} / Volle Thm 3.4 / BCDK Prop 2.15) gives A_beta = {X : E[ZX] >= 0 for all Z in -(A_beta)°}, interiority of 1 gives E[Z] > 0 on -(A_beta)°\\{0} and r||Z||_1 <= E[Z] (r = interior radius), so normalising E[Z]=1 yields the bounded sets D_beta, nested because A_beta is nondecreasing in beta; (ii)=>(i) intersections of weak*-closed half-spaces through 0 are weak*-closed convex cones, nondecreasing in beta, and E[Z]=1 gives cash-monotonicity and interiority of 1. [Mirror statement for the actuarial region beta < 1 with rho_beta sublinear, D_beta subset {E[Z]=1}, LR(X) = sup{beta < 1 : rho_beta(X) <= 0}, under the downward cash-monotonicity A_beta - R_+ cap L_+ subset A_beta.] WHAT IS ON THE PAIR, NOT ON LR: LR = rho/Pi for a SINGLE pair holds iff additionally the family is affine in beta, rho_beta ∝ rho - beta Pi, which is equivalent to the intrinsic weighted-mediant axiom (A5): there is a positive comonotone-additive Pi with LR(X+Y) = (Pi(X)LR(X) + Pi(Y)LR(Y))/(Pi(X)+Pi(Y)) for all comonotone X,Y; then rho := LR·Pi is comonotone additive and, under (A1)-(A4)+law invariance, (rho, Pi) is a pair of signed Choquet integrals (Wang-Wei-Willmot 2020, F7), with Pi an ordinary (monotone) Choquet premium iff the weights in (A5) are monotone, and rho monotone iff additionally ... -- pointwise monotonicity of rho and of Pi are NOT detectable from LR (F2) and must be assumed on the pair. In the canonical Choquet case rho = C_h, Pi = C_g: (A2) holds (hence the theorem applies) when h is concave and g convex (conic picture, F4), giving D_beta = { (Q_h - beta Q_g)/(1-beta) : Q_h in core(h), Q_g in anticore(g) } -- explicit signed kernels.
THEOREMS:
  [1] A.S. Cherny, D.B. Madan, 'New measures for performance evaluation', Review of Financial Studies 22(7) (2009) 2571-2606, Theorem 1 (static representation of coherent acceptability indices). Read as reproduced verbatim in Bielecki-Cialenco-Zhang, arXiv:1010.4339, Definition 2.1 and Theorem 2.2 (they take Omega finite, P>0; Cherny-Madan state it on L^infty of a general probability space with the Fatou property). (confidence: medium (statement read online only in the Bielecki-Cialenco-Zhang reproduction with Omega finite; the original RFS/SSRN PDF could not be opened))
      STATEMENT: Setting: L^infty(Omega,F,P). alpha: L^infty -> [0,+infty]. Axioms: (S1) monotonicity: X <= Y => alpha(X) <= alpha(Y); (S2) scale invariance: alpha(lambda X)=alpha(X) for lambda>0; (S3) quasi-concavity: alpha(X)>=x, alpha(Y)>=x => alpha(lambda X+(1-lambda)Y)>=x, lambda in [0,1]; (S4) Fatou: |X_n|<=1, alpha(X_n)>=x, X_n -> X in probability => alpha(X)>=x. THEOREM: alpha (unbounded from above / normalised) is a coherent acceptability index (S1-S4) IFF there exists a family (D_x)_{x in [0,infty)} of (L^1-closed convex) sets of probability measures Q << P, increasing in x (D_x subset D_y for x<=y), such that alpha(X) = sup{ x >= 0 : inf_{Q in D_x} E_Q[X] >= 0 } (sup of empty set = 0, inf of empty set = +infty). Equivalently alpha(X) = sup{ x >= 0 : rho_x(X) <= 0 } for an increasing family of coherent (Fatou) risk measures rho_x(X)= sup_{Q in D_x} E_Q[-X].
      USE: Template for the loss ratio: 1/LR(X) = sup{x : x rho(X) - Pi(X) <= 0} (F5) has exactly this shape with rho_x := x rho - Pi. But (S1) fails for LR (F2) and the natural rho_x is not monotone, so the theorem cannot be applied verbatim; its PROOF, however, only uses that the upper level sets {alpha >= x} are weak*-closed convex cones plus the bipolar theorem; positivity of the kernels Q is exactly what monotonicity buys and is the only thing lost when monotonicity is dropped.
  [2] M. Volle, 'Duality for the level sum of quasiconvex functions and applications', ESAIM: COCV 3 (1998) 329-343, Theorem 3.4; also J.-P. Penot & M. Volle, 'On quasi-convex duality', Math. Oper. Res. 15 (1990) 597-625. Quoted verbatim as Theorem 1 in M. Frittelli & M. Maggis, 'Dual representation of quasiconvex conditional maps', SIAM J. Financial Math. 2 (2011) 357-382, arXiv:1001.3644. (confidence: high (Frittelli-Maggis Theorem 1 read on arXiv; original Volle statement from memory with citation))
      STATEMENT: Let L be a locally convex topological vector space with dual L', f: L -> [-infty,+infty]. If f is quasiconvex (all lower level sets {X : f(X) <= c} convex) and lower semicontinuous, then f(X) = sup_{X' in L'} R(X'(X), X') for all X in L, where R(t,X') := inf_{xi in L} { f(xi) : X'(xi) >= t }. NO monotonicity, NO cash-additivity, NO positivity of X' is assumed. (Converse: any such sup of functions nondecreasing in t composed with continuous linear functionals is quasiconvex and lsc.)
      USE: This is the key 'what survives when monotonicity is dropped' result: a quasiconvex lsc LR (or 1/LR) always has a robust representation LR(X) = sup_{Z in L^1} R(E[ZX], Z) over SIGNED kernels Z (not densities). For scale-invariant LR, R(t,Z) depends only on sign(t) and the sup over the whole dual can be reorganised as inf over beta of indicator-type functions of polar cones; this gives the signed-kernel version of Cherny-Madan.
  [3] M. Frittelli & M. Maggis, 'Dual representation of quasiconvex conditional maps', SIAM J. Financial Math. 2 (2011) 357-382, arXiv:1001.3644, Theorem 8 (and Proposition 13). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let G subset F, L_F a lattice of F-measurable random variables with order-continuous dual L_F^* such that (L_F, sigma(L_F,L_F^*)) is a locally convex Riesz space; P = set of densities dQ/dP, Q << P. If pi: L_F -> L_G is (MON) monotone, (QCO) quasiconvex, (REG) regular (local), and (LSC) sigma(L_F,L_F^*)-lower semicontinuous, then pi(X) = sup_{Q in L_F^* cap P} K(X,Q), with K(X,Q) := inf_{xi in L_F} { pi(xi) : E_Q[xi|G] >=_Q E_Q[X|G] }. Proposition 13: under extra conditions LSC is equivalent to continuity from below (CFB). Monotonicity is what restricts the dual variables from L_F^* to the densities P.
      USE: Shows precisely the role of monotonicity in the dual: it only shrinks the dual set from all order-continuous functionals to probability densities. For LR, dropping monotonicity means keeping signed kernels Z in L^1 with no sign restriction -- matching the signed-Choquet picture (F7). Also provides a conditional/dynamic version for a dynamic loss ratio.
  [4] S. Drapeau & M. Kupper, 'Risk preferences and their robust representation', Mathematics of Operations Research 38(1) (2013) 28-62 (SSRN 1557083); same result as Theorem 2.7, Theorem 2.8, Proposition 2.9, Remark 2.10 of S. Drapeau, PhD thesis HU Berlin 2010 (edoc.hu-berlin.de). Risk orders/risk measures/acceptance families one-to-one: Theorem 1.10 of the thesis (= Theorem 1.7 in the MOR paper as cited by Biagini-Bion-Nadal). (confidence: medium (thesis introduction and Chapter 1 read online; exact wording of Thm 2.7 in Chapter 2 and of the MOR version not accessible))
      STATEMENT: Setting: X a locally convex topological vector space (more generally a convex set), K a convex cone defining a vector preorder (K may be nonregular, e.g. first stochastic order), K° its polar. A risk measure is rho: X -> [-infty,+infty] quasiconvex and monotone w.r.t. K. Theorem 1.10: risk orders, risk measures and risk acceptance families (A_m)_{m in R} (convex, monotone A_m + K subset A_m, decreasing/right-continuous in m) are in one-to-one correspondence, rho(x)=inf{m : x in A_m}, with NO topological assumption. Theorem 2.7 (robust representation): a function rho: X -> [-infty,+infty] is a lower semicontinuous risk measure IFF rho(x) = sup_{x* in K°} R(x*, <x*,-x>) for some risk function R: K° x R -> [-infty,+infty] (nondecreasing and left-continuous in s, jointly quasiconcave, R(lambda x*, s)=R(x*, s/lambda), uniform asymptotic minimum, right-continuous version R^+ usc in x*); the representation holds with a UNIQUE R in the class R^max, namely R(x*,s)=sup{m : alpha_min(x*,m) < s}, alpha_min(x*,m)=sup_{x in A_m} <x*,-x>. Theorem 2.8: under regularity of the preorder the sup can be restricted to normalised x* in K°\{0}. Prop. 1.16: rho positively homogeneous iff lambda A_m = A_{lambda m}; rho scale invariant iff lambda A_m = A_m (level sets are cones).
      USE: CRUCIAL for the non-monotone case: taking the TRIVIAL preorder K={0} (so monotonicity is vacuous) gives K°= X* and the theorem becomes: rho is lsc and quasiconvex IFF rho(x) = sup_{x* in X*} R(x*,<x*,-x>) with a UNIQUE maximal R -- i.e. Volle's representation PLUS uniqueness, with no monotonicity. The normalisation (Thm 2.8) is what fails without monotonicity, but for LR the constant 1 lies in the interior of the level cones {LR<=beta}, beta>1 (translation-to-break-even, F1), which restores a normalisation E[Z]=1 by signed kernels.
  [5] S. Drapeau, M. Kupper, R. Reda, 'A note on robust representations of law-invariant quasiconvex functions', Advances in Mathematical Economics 15 (2011) 27-39, PDF www.samuel-drapeau.info/downloads/DKR_LIRM1010.pdf, Proposition 2.2 and Theorem 3.2 (relying on Jouini-Schachermayer-Touzi 2006 and Svindland 2010). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Proposition 2.2: Let f: L^infty -> [-infty,+infty] be ||.||_infty-lower semicontinuous, quasiconvex and law-invariant (standard probability space; Svindland: non-atomic). Then f is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lower semicontinuous and has the Fatou property (f(X) <= liminf f(X_n) whenever sup_n ||X_n||_infty < infty and X_n -> X in probability). NO monotonicity assumed. Theorem 3.2: let rho: L^infty -> [-infty,+infty] be a law-invariant ||.||_infty-lsc risk measure (monotone and quasiconvex). Then there exists a unique risk function R in R^max such that rho(X) = sup_{psi in Psi_1} R(psi, int_0^1 q_X^-(t) psi(t) dt), Psi_1 = nonincreasing densities on [0,1], R(psi,x)= sup{m : alpha_min(psi,m) < x}, alpha_min(psi,m)= sup_{X in A_m} int q_X^- psi.
      USE: Since LR = rho_h/Pi_g is law-invariant (F1) and bounded-continuous (F1), the Fatou/weak*-lsc hypothesis needed in every representation theorem is AUTOMATIC from quasiconvexity + norm-lsc, without monotonicity (Prop 2.2). Theorem 3.2 gives the quantile ('Kusuoka-type') form of the dual variables; for the non-monotone LR the dual psi are signed functions on [0,1] rather than nonincreasing densities, consistent with signed distortions (h - beta g)/(1-beta) in F7.
  [6] S. Cerreia-Vioglio, F. Maccheroni, M. Marinacci, L. Montrucchio, 'Complete monotone quasiconcave duality', Mathematics of Operations Research 36(2) (2011) 321-339; Carlo Alberto Notebook no. 80 (2008), Theorem 1 (incomplete duality, no monotonicity), Theorem 2 (complete duality, monotone evenly quasiconcave), Theorem 3 (lsc). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Theorem 1 (X normed space, S* unit sphere of X*): g: X -> [-infty,+infty] is evenly quasiconcave IFF g(x) = inf_{xi in S*} G_xi(<xi,x>) with G_xi(t) := sup{ g(x) : <xi,x> <= t }; (i) if X is ordered, g is monotone iff S* can be replaced by Delta (positive unit-norm functionals); (ii) g strictly evenly quasiconcave iff the infima are attained; (iii) g usc iff G_xi can be replaced by its lsc envelope. The duality is INCOMPLETE: many G represent the same g. Theorem 2 (X an M-space with unit e, Delta = positive normalised functionals): the map T: g -> G, G(t,xi) = sup{g(x): <xi,x> <= t} is a bijection between monotone evenly quasiconcave functions M_eqc(X) and the class of ♦-evenly quasiconvex G on R x Delta, with inverse g(x)= inf_{xi in Delta} G(<xi,x>,xi) (COMPLETE duality). Theorem 3: same bijection between monotone lsc quasiconcave g and lsc quasiconvex G increasing in t, with the inf attained.
      USE: Makes explicit what is lost without monotonicity: the representation survives (Thm 1) but UNIQUENESS of the dual function G fails in general (incomplete duality) unless one normalises differently; for LR, the natural unit e=1 is not an order unit for the (trivial) preorder, so complete duality must be re-derived via the cone structure of the level sets of a scale-invariant functional (polar cones are unique by the bipolar theorem -- uniqueness is recovered at the level of acceptance cones, as in Drapeau-Kupper's R^max).
  [7] S. Cerreia-Vioglio, F. Maccheroni, M. Marinacci, L. Montrucchio, 'Risk measures: rationality and diversification', Mathematical Finance 21(4) (2011) 743-774; Carlo Alberto Notebook no. 100 (2008), Theorem 1, Lemma 1, Proposition 2, Theorem 2. (confidence: high for Thm 1 and Thm 2 (read, with garbled symbols); medium for Lemma 1)
      STATEMENT: Setting: bounded random variables (payoff convention), ba-dual, Delta = finitely additive probabilities. Theorem 1: rho is a quasiconvex, monotone, cash-subadditive risk measure IFF rho(X) = max_{Q in Delta} R(E_Q[X], Q) for a (unique) R in R_1 (usc, quasiconcave, increasing and nonexpansive in t), R(t,Q)= inf{rho(X): E_Q[X]=t}. Lemma 1: dropping cash-subadditivity, a quasiconvex (monotone) usc rho has rho(X)= max_Q R(E_Q X,Q) with unique R in R_0. Proposition 2: continuity from below iff the max can be taken over countably additive M_1. Theorem 2: for quasiconvex continuous-from-below rho, preserving second-order stochastic dominance iff R(t,.) Schur-concave on M_1; then rho(X)= max_{Q in M_1} R( int q_X(s) q_{dQ/dP}(s) ds , Q ); on adequate spaces equivalent to law invariance.
      USE: Gives the quasiconvex analogue of the Kusuoka representation: for a law-invariant quasiconvex LR the dual objects are functions of the 'quantile inner product' int q_X q_Z. Nonexpansiveness/cash-subadditivity has an LR-analogue: translation to break-even (F1) is a one-sided cash property of the level sets for beta>1, which is what replaces cash-additivity for the associated family. Monotonicity is in their definition of risk measure, so the theorem does NOT apply directly to LR; it applies to the monotone pieces rho and Pi separately.
  [8] S. Biagini & J. Bion-Nadal, 'Dynamic quasi-concave performance measures', Journal of Mathematical Economics 55 (2014) 143-153, arXiv:1212.3958, Definition 2.1, Proposition 3.12, Remark 3.13. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: L^bb_T random variables bounded from below. A (conditional) performance measure beta_t: L^bb_T -> L^bb_t satisfies (1) quasi-concavity beta_t(cX+(1-c)Y) >= min(beta_t(X),beta_t(Y)); (2) non-random thresholds z_d<z_u; (3a) monotonicity X>=Y => beta_t(X)>=beta_t(Y) and (3b) strict increase under constant positive shifts on the relevant set; (4) continuity from below; (5) locality; (6) upper level sets uniformly bounded from below. Acceptability index (CAI): additionally (7) scale invariance beta_t(cX)=beta_t(X), c>0, and (8) beta_t >= 0. Proposition 3.12: a CPM induces a unique 'standard family' of convex (coherent if CAI) risk measures rho^z_t(X) := ess inf{ xi in L^bb_t : beta_t(X+xi) >= z }, and conversely every standard family (convex, monotone, translation invariant, local, continuous from below, z -> rho^z nondecreasing and continuous) generates a CPM. Remark 3.13 (static, scale invariant): beta: L^bb_T -> [0,+infty] is an acceptability index IFF there is a UNIQUE standard family of coherent risk measures (sigma^z)_{z in (0,z_u)} with beta(X) = sup{ z : sigma^z(X) < 0 } (sup emptyset = 0) [strict inequality, versus Cherny-Madan's non-strict sup{z: sigma^z(X) <= 0} with a maximal, possibly non-unique family].
      USE: Clarifies the strict/non-strict and uniqueness issues in the association LR(X)=beta <=> rho_beta(X)=0 (F6): uniqueness holds for the family defined by the cash-shift formula rho^z(X)=inf{c : beta(X+c) >= z} -- which for LR is exactly Assa's Prop 1 'LR(X-m)=beta solved by m=-rho_beta(X)'. Axiom (3b) 'strictly increasing under constant shifts' is the part of monotonicity that LR does retain (translation to break-even), suggesting that only CASH-monotonicity, not pointwise monotonicity, is needed to build the family from LR.
  [9] T.R. Bielecki, I. Cialenco, S. Drapeau, M. Karliczek, 'Dynamic assessment indices', Stochastics 88(1) (2016) 1-44, arXiv:1306.5198, Definition 2.1, Definition 2.2, Theorem 2.4, Definition 2.7, Theorem 2.12, Proposition 2.14, Proposition 2.15, Theorem 3.4. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: X a locally L^0-convex topological L^0-module (static case: G trivial, X a locally convex space), K a closed convex cone, K° its polar. A conditional assessment index is alpha: X -> bar L^0 that is local, quasiconcave and monotone w.r.t. K (usc not part of the definition). Theorem 2.4: A^m := {alpha >= m} is a risk acceptance family (convex, decreasing in m, A^m + K = A^m, jointly sigma-stable, left-continuous) and alpha_A(X) = ess sup{m : X in A^m}; the two maps are mutually inverse (one-to-one correspondence, no topology needed). Theorem 2.12: (i) if alpha is an UPPER SEMICONTINUOUS assessment index then alpha(X) = ess inf_{X* in K°} R(X*, <X*,X>) for a UNIQUE minimal risk function R in R^min; (ii) conversely for any conditional risk function R the right side is an usc assessment index. Proposition 2.14: alpha is concave / positively homogeneous / scale invariant / kappa-cash additive IFF R is convex / positively homogeneous / scale invariant / kappa-cash additive in its second argument. Proposition 2.15 (scale invariant, usc): the unique minimal risk function is R(X*,s) = -infty on {s=-infty}, R(X*,s) = ess inf{ m : X* in (A^m)° } on {-infty<s<0}, R(X*,s)=+infty on {s>=0}; hence alpha(X) = inf_{X* in K°, <X*,X><0} inf{m : X* in (A^m)°}. Remark 2.13: the inf may be restricted to a normalised set K°_kappa when some kappa in K satisfies <X*,kappa> > 0 for all X* in K°\{0}.
      USE: Proposition 2.15 is the cleanest 'acceptability index without coherent risk measures': a scale-invariant usc quasiconcave index is determined by the polar cones (A^m)° of its level cones. With K={0} (no monotonicity) the formula still makes sense with X* ranging over the whole dual (signed kernels); the normalisation of Remark 2.13 is the only step that needs an order unit -- for LR this is supplied by kappa = 1 and the translation-to-break-even property on {LR>1}. Also gives the dynamic/conditional loss ratio for free.
  [10] M. Frittelli, M. Maggis, I. Peri, 'Risk measures on P(R) and Value at Risk with probability/loss function', Mathematical Finance 24(3) (2014) 442-463, arXiv:1201.2257, Definition 1, Proposition 24, Proposition 26, Definition 28, Proposition 29. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: P = P(R) probability measures on R with topology sigma(P,C_b), first-order stochastic dominance P ≼ Q iff F_Q <= F_P. Risk measure on P(R): Phi: P -> R u {+infty} (Mon) monotone decreasing and (QCo) quasi-convex. Proposition 24 (i): ANY sigma(P,C_b)-lsc and quasi-convex Phi: P -> R u {infty} (NO monotonicity) can be represented as Phi(P) = sup_{f in C_b} R( int f dP, f ), R(t,f) := inf_{Q in P}{ Phi(Q) : int f dQ >= t }; (ii) if in addition Phi is monotone, C_b can be replaced by C_b^- (bounded continuous DEcreasing functions). Proposition 26: Phi(P) = sup_{f in C_b^-} R^-( int f dP, f ) with R^-(t,f)= inf{ m : gamma(m,f) >= t }, gamma(m,f)= sup{ int f dQ : Phi(Q) <= m }. Proposition 29: R^- is unique in the class R^max (nondecreasing left-continuous in t, jointly quasi-concave, R(s,lambda f)=R(s/lambda,f), limit condition as s -> -infty, usc in f).
      USE: A law-invariant LR is a functional on distributions of losses; Prop 24(i) represents a quasiconvex lsc LR WITHOUT monotonicity by test functions f in C_b (not necessarily monotone f) -- exactly the distributional analogue of signed kernels; monotone LR would force decreasing f. Since signed Choquet integrals C_{h-beta g} are of the form int f dP only for two-point losses, this also explains why the level sets of a Choquet-ratio LR are in general not convex (F4 open).
  [11] T.R. Bielecki, I. Cialenco, Z. Zhang, 'Dynamic coherent acceptability indices and their applications to finance', Mathematical Finance 24(3) (2014) 411-441, arXiv:1010.4339, Theorems 4.6, 4.7, 4.8, 5.10. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Omega finite, P>0, dividend processes D on {0,...,T}. A dynamic coherent acceptability index (DCAI) is alpha: T x D x Omega -> [0,+infty] satisfying (D1) adaptiveness, (D2) independence of the past, (D3) monotonicity, (D4) scale invariance alpha_t(lambda D)=alpha_t(D), (D5) quasi-concavity, (D6) translation invariance, (D7) dynamic consistency; normalised (values 0 and +infty attained). Theorem 4.6: for a normalised DCAI, rho^x_t(D) := inf{ c : alpha_t(D + c 1_{t}) >= x } is an increasing, left-continuous family of dynamic coherent risk measures. Theorem 4.7: for an increasing family of DCRMs, alpha_t(D) := sup{ x : rho^x_t(D) < 0 } is a normalised right-continuous DCAI. Theorem 4.8 (duality): (i) every normalised right-continuous DCAI has alpha_t(D) = sup{ x : rho^x_t(D) <= 0 } for a left-continuous increasing family of DCRMs; (ii) every left-continuous increasing family of DCRMs is induced this way, with rho^x_t(D) = inf{ c : alpha_t(D + c 1_{t}) >= x }. Theorem 5.10: rho is a DCRM IFF rho_t(D) = - inf_{Q in Q_t} E_Q[ sum_{s>=t} D_s | F_t ] for a dynamically consistent family of sets of probability measures.
      USE: Shows the construction index -> family via the cash-shift inverse (Thm 4.6) uses only translation invariance and monotonicity in the CASH direction, and the converse (Thm 4.7) only needs the family to be increasing. For LR, the analogous family is Assa's m = -rho_beta(X) solving LR(X-m)=beta (Prop 1); the only step using pointwise monotonicity is coherence (positivity of kernels) of the resulting rho^x, which for LR must be replaced by signed kernels.
  [12] E. Rosazza Gianin & C. Sgarra, 'Acceptability indexes via g-expectations: an application to liquidity risk', Mathematics and Financial Economics 7(4) (2013) 457-475 (preprint cited in Biagini-Bion-Nadal as providing 'a (static) characterization of these generalized acceptability indexes in terms of a family of quasi convex risk measures on L^infty', their Proposition 3). (confidence: low (page not accessible; statement reconstructed from the Biagini-Bion-Nadal citation and memory))
      STATEMENT: (From memory / secondary citation only) Quasi-concave acceptability index on L^infty: monotone, scale invariant, quasi-concave, Fatou, without the coherence (positive-homogeneity of the induced risks) -- characterised by an increasing family of quasi-convex (not necessarily coherent or convex) cash-additive risk measures rho_x with alpha(X) = sup{ x : rho_x(X) <= 0 }; dynamic version via g-expectations.
      USE: Shows that the index <-> family duality does not need coherence of the family, only cash-additivity + monotonicity + nestedness; relevant for a non-positively-homogeneous premium Pi.


### Return / star-shaped / scale-free risk measures scholar (Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin return risk measures; Castagnoli–Cattelan–Maccheroni–Tebaldi–Wang star-shaped risk measures; Laeven–Rosazza Gianin–Zullino; Jia–Xia–Zhao; Herdegen–Khan; Han–Lin–Wang diversification quotients; Cherny–Madan acceptability indices). Search log: WebSearch is disabled for this organization (HTTP 403 on every call); only arxiv.org abstract/PDF pages were reachable through WebFetch. Read in full (primary source): arXiv 2103.15790v3, 2307.03447v2, 2012.06751v1, 2206.13679v6, 2009.05498v3, 2202.07610v3, 1010.4339v2, 2012.05219v3. Could NOT be opened: arXiv author listings (bellini_f_1, laeven_r_1, zullino_m_1: proxy rate-limited), doi.org/10.1007/s11579-017-0188-x and link.springer.com (blocked), SSRN/ScienceDirect/Semantic Scholar/RePEc/Google Scholar (blocked). Consequently the statements from Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin (2018, 2021) are from memory and marked medium/low; no paper by Gómez–Tang–Tong on 'geometric risk measures' could be located and none is reported.
KEY INSIGHT: Every classical iff theorem in this strand (Cherny–Madan acceptability indices, Castagnoli et al./Jia–Xia–Zhao lower envelopes of convex or coherent measures, Han–Lin–Wang diversification quotients, Bellini–Laeven–Rosazza Gianin return risk measures) needs MONOTONICITY of the functional or of the generating family, and the canonical loss-ratio family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1 − β) violates it (F2). The way out is to notice that {ρ_β}_β is not an arbitrary family but a PENCIL (projective line) through two monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, comonotone-additive functionals: ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ := lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = Π. Along cash shifts the loss ratio is a Möbius (fractional-linear) function, LR(X + t) = (t + ρ(X))/(t + Π(X)), whose zero −ρ(X) and pole −Π(X) recover the pair; comonotone break-even additivity ('if X and Y are comonotone and both loaded to the same target β, so is X + Y') forces comonotone additivity of both generators by a two-equation argument; and Schmeidler's 1986 representation (comonotone additivity + monotonicity, no continuity) plus law invariance turns each generator into a distortion Choquet integral. So monotonicity is required only at the two endpoints of the pencil, never on ρ_β or on LR; this is precisely what the star-shaped/return literature (Laeven–Rosazza Gianin–Zullino Prop. 8, Herdegen–Khan Thms 3.18/3.20) permits, since those results survive without monotonicity for positively homogeneous cash-additive functionals. The log-transform of return risk measures shows the same thing from another side: log LR(e^X) = ρ̂(X) − Π̂(X) is a cash-INVARIANT inter-risk difference, i.e. LR − 1 = (ρ − Π)/Π is a generalized coefficient of variation (signed Choquet variability measure over a Choquet premium), the structure of the Gini coefficient; such ratios are never monotone and are naturally characterized via comonotone additivity, not via monotone cones.
CANDIDATE IFF: Setting: (Ω,ℱ,P) atomless, L^∞ bounded random variables, L^∞_+ nonnegative losses; an extended loss ratio is a map L: L^∞ → [−∞, +∞] (on L^∞_+ it is the loss ratio; the extension to signed positions is part of the structure, as in Assa's cash-shift LR(X − m)). THEOREM (candidate). TFAE: (I) there exist distortion functions h ≠ g (nondecreasing, h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1) such that L(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) for all X with C_g(X) ≠ 0 (C_h, C_g survival-form Choquet integrals extended to L^∞ by cash-additivity), with (h,g) uniquely determined by L. (II) L satisfies: (A1) scale invariance: L(λX) = L(X), λ > 0; (A2) Möbius cash structure: L ≢ 1, and for every X there are reals z(X), q(X) with L(X + t) = (t − z(X))/(t − q(X)) for all t ∈ ℝ, t ≠ q(X) [equivalently, in Assa's language: the canonical break-even family ρ_β(X) := −m_β(X), m_β(X) the unique solution of L(X + m) = β, exists for all β ≠ 1 and (1 − β)ρ_β(X) is affine in β]; the parameters (z,q) are determined by L on {L ≠ 1} and are required to be continuous along comonotone perturbations X + εY, ε → 0, which pins them down on the fair set {L = 1}; (A3) comonotone break-even additivity: if X, Y are comonotone and L(X + a) = L(Y + b) = β for some β ≠ 1, then L(X + Y + a + b) = β; (A4) law invariance: X =_d Y ⇒ L(X) = L(Y); (A5) endpoint monotonicity: X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ z(X) ≥ z(Y) and q(X) ≥ q(Y) (the zero and the pole of t ↦ L(X + t) both move left when the loss increases). Under (II), ρ := −z and Π := −q are cash-additive (A2), positively homogeneous (A1 + uniqueness of Möbius parameters), comonotone-additive (A3: from r_X + a = β(p_X + a), r_Y + b = β(p_Y + b) and r_{X+Y} + a + b = β(p_{X+Y} + a + b) for an interval of β one gets r_{X+Y} = r_X + r_Y, p_{X+Y} = p_X + p_Y), monotone (A5) and law-invariant (A4); Schmeidler (1986) gives ρ(X) = ∫ X dv with v(A) = ρ(1_A) = h(P(A)), h nondecreasing with h(0) = 0, h(1) = ρ(1) = 1, so ρ = C_h, and likewise Π = C_g; h ≠ g because L ≢ 1; (I) ⇒ (II) is direct from the Choquet properties, and uniqueness of (h,g) is F3. Honest accounting: (A1), (A3), (A4) are intrinsic axioms on L; (A2) is intrinsic but is the strongest hypothesis (it encodes that L is a ratio of two cash-additive functionals; without it L could be any scale-invariant index with a non-pencil association family); (A5) is stated on the derived objects z, q and is the only place where monotonicity enters, deliberately avoiding pointwise monotonicity of L (impossible by F2) and of ρ_β. Variants: dropping (A5) and adding ‖·‖_∞-continuity of z, q yields, by Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020), the same statement with h, g of bounded variation (signed Choquet pair, L a ratio of signed Choquet integrals); replacing (A1) by scale-deterioration L(λX) ≥ L(X), λ ≥ 1, makes ρ_β star-shaped and, by Laeven–Rosazza Gianin–Zullino Prop. 8 (no monotonicity needed), gives L = inf_Z L_Z with L_Z quasi-convex, a star-shaped analogue of F4.
THEOREMS:
  [1] E. Castagnoli, G. Cattelan, F. Maccheroni, C. Tebaldi, R. Wang (2022), 'Star-shaped risk measures', Operations Research 70(5); arXiv:2103.15790v3, Definition 1, Proposition 1, Proposition 2, Theorem 2 (minimax representation), Proposition 5 (dual representation). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: 𝒳 = a space of bounded random variables containing the constants. A risk measure is a map ρ: 𝒳 → ℝ that is monotone (X ≤ Y ⇒ ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y)), translation invariant (ρ(X − m) = ρ(X) − m, m ∈ ℝ) and normalized (ρ(0) = 0). ρ is star-shaped if ρ(λX) ≥ λρ(X) for all λ > 1 and all X. Prop. 1: for a risk measure ρ TFAE (i) ρ star-shaped; (ii) ρ(αX) ≤ αρ(X) ∀X, α ∈ (0,1); (iii) ∀X, β ↦ ρ(βX)/β is increasing on (0,∞). Prop. 2: ρ star-shaped ⇔ 𝒜_ρ = {ρ ≤ 0} is a star-shaped set ⇔ ρ = ρ_𝒜 for some star-shaped acceptance set 𝒜. Theorem 2: for a risk measure ρ TFAE (i) ρ is star-shaped (resp. positively homogeneous); (ii) ρ(X) = min_{γ∈Γ} γ(X) for a collection Γ of convex (resp. coherent) risk measures; (iii) ρ(X) = min{m : X − m ∈ 𝒜_β for some β} for a family of convex (resp. coherent) acceptance sets; Γ can be taken to be the set of ALL convex (resp. coherent) risk measures dominating ρ. Prop. 5: on 𝒳 = L^∞(Ω,ℱ,P), ρ is star-shaped ⇔ there are penalty functions α_γ: 𝒫 → [0,∞] (𝒫 = probabilities ≪ P) with inf_Q α_γ(Q) = 0 such that ρ(X) = min_γ sup_{Q∈𝒫} { E_Q[X] − α_γ(Q) }.
      USE: Scale invariance of LR ⇔ positive homogeneity of the canonical family ρ_β (Assa, Prop. 1); a weakening, 'scale-deterioration' LR(λX) ≥ LR(X) for λ ≥ 1, corresponds to star-shapedness of ρ_β (which holds whenever ρ is star-shaped and Π is positively sub-homogeneous, e.g. a concave premium). BUT Theorem 2 and Prop. 5 are stated for MONOTONE risk measures (Definition 1), so they do not apply verbatim to ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1 − β), which is not monotone (F2). Their use for LR is therefore (a) at the two endpoints of the pencil, β = 0 (ρ) and β → ∞ (Π), which ARE monotone, and (b) via the non-monotone variant in Laeven–Rosazza Gianin–Zullino (entry 3) for the whole family.
  [2] Castagnoli, Cattelan, Maccheroni, Tebaldi, Wang (2022), 'Star-shaped risk measures', arXiv:2103.15790v3, Theorem 4 (SSD-consistent star-shaped) and Theorem 5 (law-invariant star-shaped); companion: G. Jia, J. Xia, R. Zhao (2020) 'Monetary risk measures', arXiv:2012.06751v1, Theorem 4.3 and Theorems 6.1–6.2. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Castagnoli et al. Thm 5: on an atomless probability space, for ρ: 𝒳 → ℝ (𝒳 bounded r.v.s) TFAE (i) ρ is a star-shaped (monotone, cash-additive, normalized) law-invariant risk measure; (ii) ρ(X) = inf_{g∈𝒢} sup_{α∈(0,1)} { VaR_α(X) − g(α) } for a star-shaped set 𝒢 of increasing functions g:(0,1) → ℝ with g(0+) ≤ 0. Thm 4: same with ES_α in place of VaR_α ⇔ ρ star-shaped and SSD-consistent. Jia–Xia–Zhao Thm 4.3: (Ω,ℱ,P) nonatomic, ρ: L^∞ → ℝ law-invariant monetary (monotone + cash-additive, NO normalization, NO convexity) ⇔ there exist convex α_λ: 𝒢 → (−∞,∞] (𝒢 = densities on (0,1)) with ρ(X) = min_{λ∈Λ} sup_{g∈𝒢} ( ∫_0^1 VaR_t(X) g(t) dt − α_λ(g) ); Remark 4.4: one may take α_Z(g) = ∫_0^1 VaR_t(Z) g(t) dt, Z ∈ 𝒜_ρ. Thm 6.1(b)/6.2(b): nonatomic; ρ law-invariant monetary (resp. positively homogeneous) ⇔ ρ is the lower envelope of law-invariant comonotonic-convex (resp. comonotonic-coherent) risk measures.
      USE: Gives the Kusuoka-type target class for the two monotone endpoints ρ = ρ_0 and Π = lim_{β→∞} ρ_β of the LR pencil when the panel wants to go beyond distortion pairs: 'LR law-invariant ⇔ {ρ_β} law-invariant' (Assa) combined with these theorems yields that any law-invariant monetary ρ, Π are min–sup of VaR-based functionals, so LR = ρ/Π is a ratio of two min–sup VaR functionals. The lower-envelope step in Jia–Xia–Zhao uses monotonicity (upward-closed sets {Y ≥ Z}), so again it must be applied to the endpoints, not to ρ_β.
  [3] R. J. A. Laeven, E. Rosazza Gianin, M. Zullino (2023/24), 'Dynamic return and star-shaped risk measures via BSDEs', arXiv:2307.03447v2, Definition 1 (return risk measure), Definition 6, Lemma 7, Proposition 8, Remark 9, Theorem 13, Proposition 3. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Static setting of Section 3: 𝒳 a locally convex Fréchet lattice, order complete and order separable, containing the constants, such that σ(𝒳,𝒳*) has the C-property (examples: L^p, Orlicz, Morse spaces). Def. 6: f: 𝒳 → ℝ ∪ {+∞} is star-shaped if f(0) < +∞ and f(λX) ≤ λf(X) + (1 − λ)f(0) ∀X, λ ∈ [0,1]. Lemma 7: inf of star-shaped f_γ with common f_γ(0) = c is star-shaped. Proposition 8 (NO monotonicity, NO normalization, NO cash-additivity assumed): if f is star-shaped with f(0) < ∞ then f(X) = min_{Z∈dom f} f_Z(X) = min_{Z∈dom f} sup_{q∈𝒳*} { ⟨q,X⟩ − f_Z*(q) }, where each f_Z is convex and order lower semicontinuous; if in addition f is monotone, the f_Z can be taken to be proper convex order-lsc risk measures and the sup runs over 𝒳*_+. Remark 9: if f is positively homogeneous then f(X) = min_Z f_Z(X) with f_Z(X) = αf(Z) if X = αZ, α ≥ 0, and +∞ otherwise; each f_Z is proper, lsc, sublinear and f(X) = min_Z sup_{q∈C_Z} ⟨q,X⟩. Theorem 13: ρ normalized star-shaped (monotone, cash-additive) risk measure ⇔ ρ = min_γ ρ_γ over normalized convex risk measures ⇔ ρ(X) = inf{m : X ∈ A^m_γ for some γ} with convex acceptance families, inf{m : 0 ∈ A^m_γ} = 0. Def. 1 (return risk measure): ρ_t: L^p_+(ℱ_T) → L^p_+(ℱ_t), p ∈ [1,∞], monotone and positively homogeneous ρ_t(αX) = αρ_t(X) for α ∈ L^p_+(ℱ_t); static case t = 0; normalization ρ(1) = 1 optional. Proposition 3 (static case t = 0): if ρ: L^∞ → ℝ is monotone, convex, continuous from below, positively homogeneous with ρ(1) = 1, then ρ(X) = sup_{(D,Q)} D E_Q[X], D ∈ [0,1], Q ≪ P.
      USE: This is the one result in the strand that applies DIRECTLY to the non-monotone canonical family: ρ_β is positively homogeneous (⇔ LR scale invariant) and star-shaped under weak conditions, and Prop. 8/Remark 9 need neither monotonicity nor cash-additivity. Hence each ρ_β is a minimum of convex lsc functionals, so {LR ≤ β} = {ρ_β ≤ 0} is a union of convex sets and LR = inf_Z LR_Z with LR_Z quasi-convex: a 'minimum of quasi-convex loss ratios' representation generalizing F4 without convexity of ρ. Caveat: for positively homogeneous f the representation of Remark 9 is the trivial ray decomposition and carries no information beyond positive homogeneity; the substantive content comes only from the monotone endpoints. Prop. 3 (t = 0) gives the sublinear-premium representation Π(X) = sup_{Q∈𝒬} E_Q[X] for the premium endpoint.
  [4] X. Han, L. Lin, R. Wang (2024), 'Diversification quotients: Quantifying diversification via risk measures', Management Science; arXiv:2206.13679v6, Definition 1, Theorem 1, Theorem 2, Proposition 1, Theorem 4. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: 𝒳 = L^∞ (bounded r.v.s), n ≥ 2 portfolio components. Def. 1: for a family ρ = (ρ_α)_{α∈I}, I = (0, ᾱ), ᾱ ∈ (0,∞], with α ↦ ρ_α DECREASING, DQ^ρ_α(𝐗) = α*/α where α* = inf{ β ∈ I : ρ_β(Σ_i X_i) ≤ Σ_i ρ_α(X_i) }, inf ∅ = ᾱ. Axioms on an index D: 𝒳^n → ℝ̄, for a fixed MCP (monotone, constant-additive, positively homogeneous) risk measure φ: [+] D ≥ 0; [LI] D(𝐗 + 𝐜) = D(𝐗), 𝐜 ∈ ℝ^n; [SI] D(λ𝐗) = D(𝐗), λ > 0; [R]_φ if φ(X_i) = φ(Y_i) ∀i and ΣX_i ≤ ΣY_i then D(𝐗) ≤ D(𝐘); [N]_φ D(𝟎) = 0, D ≤ 1 on duplicates (X,…,X), D ≥ 1 on vectors worse than duplicate; [C]_φ if 𝐘^k are φ-marginally equivalent to 𝐗 and (ΣX_i − ΣY^k_i)_+ → 0 in L^∞ then (D(𝐗) − D(𝐘^k))_+ → 0. Theorem 1: D satisfies [+],[LI],[SI],[R]_φ,[N]_φ,[C]_φ for some MCP φ ⇔ D = DQ^ρ_α for some α ∈ I and some decreasing family ρ of MCP risk measures; in both directions one can take ρ_α = φ. Theorem 2: n ≥ 4, φ non-linear coherent: add [PC] (for each 𝐗, d, the set {𝐰 ∈ Δ_n : D(𝐰 ⊙ 𝐗) ≤ d} is convex) ⇔ D = DQ^ρ_α with ρ a decreasing family of COHERENT risk measures, ρ_α = φ. Recovery: under mild conditions α* is the unique solution of ρ_{α*}(ΣX_i) = Σρ_α(X_i).
      USE: This is the closest published iff theorem of exactly Assa's 'association' type: a scale-invariant (degree-zero) index is characterized as D = inf{β : ρ_β(·) ≤ threshold}/α for a monotone-in-parameter family of MCP risk measures, with axioms on the index only, and rationality/continuity stated RELATIVE to one reference measure φ. The template for LR: replace the comparison 'ρ_β(ΣX_i) ≤ Σρ_α(X_i)' by 'ρ_β(X) ≤ 0', i.e. LR(X) = inf{β : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} (actuarial picture ρ ≤ Π, where β ↦ ρ_β is decreasing). The difference is that the DQ family is monotone (MCP) while the LR family is a pencil of a monotone and a negative monotone functional; the [R]_φ/[C]_φ axioms (monotonicity 'conditional on fixed marginal risk') show how to state monotonicity relative to a reference functional instead of pointwise, which is what F2 forces for LR.
  [5] A. Cherny, D. Madan (2009), 'New measures for performance evaluation', Review of Financial Studies 22, Theorem 1; stated as Definition 2.1 and Theorem 2.2 in T. R. Bielecki, I. Cialenco, Z. Zhang (2014), 'Dynamic coherent acceptability indices and their applications to finance', Math. Finance; arXiv:1010.4339v2 (also eq. (4.1), (4.3)). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Def. 2.1: α: L^∞ → [0,+∞] is a coherent acceptability index if (S1) monotonicity X ≤ Y ⇒ α(X) ≤ α(Y); (S2) scale invariance α(λX) = α(X), λ > 0; (S3) quasi-concavity α(X) ≥ x, α(Y) ≥ x ⇒ α(λX + (1−λ)Y) ≥ x, λ ∈ [0,1]; (S4) Fatou: |X_n| ≤ 1, α(X_n) ≥ x, X_n → X in probability ⇒ α(X) ≥ x. Theorem 2.2: let α: L^∞ → [0,+∞] be unbounded from above; then α is a coherent acceptability index ⇔ there is a family (𝒟_x)_{x∈[0,+∞]} of sets of probability measures, increasing in x (𝒟_x ⊂ 𝒟_y for x ≤ y), such that α(X) = sup{ x ∈ [0,∞) : inf_{Q∈𝒟_x} E_Q[X] ≥ 0 } (inf ∅ = ∞, sup ∅ = 0); equivalently (eq. 4.1) α(X) = sup{ x ≥ 0 : ρ^x(X) ≤ 0 } for an increasing family (ρ^x) of coherent risk measures, with the family recovered from the index by ρ^x(X) = inf{ c ∈ ℝ : α(X + c) ≥ x } (eq. 4.3). Dynamic version Thm 4.8: normalized right-continuous DCAI ⇔ left-continuous increasing family of DCRMs with α_t = sup{x > 0 : ρ^x_t ≤ 0}.
      USE: The exact structural template of F5/F6: 1/LR(X) = sup{x : xρ(X) − Π(X) ≤ 0} and the Cherny–Madan recovery formula ρ^x(X) = inf{c : α(X + c) ≥ x} is precisely Assa's 'LR(X − m) = β solved by m = −ρ_β(X)'. The theorem FAILS for LR exactly at (S1): LR is not monotone (F2), and the level sets {LR ≤ β} = {ρ_β ≤ 0} are cones that are not upward closed, so the family produced by the recovery formula (ρ_β) is cash-additive and positively homogeneous but not monotone and not coherent. The lesson is that the correct generalization for LR replaces (S1)+(S3) by (i) a Möbius/pencil structure along cash shifts and (ii) comonotone additivity of the break-even cash amounts, with monotonicity imposed only on the two generators of the pencil.
  [6] M. Herdegen, N. Khan (2022), 'Mean-ρ portfolio selection and ρ-arbitrage for coherent risk measures', Math. Finance 32; arXiv:2009.05498v3, Theorems 3.18, 3.20, Proposition 3.15; and Herdegen & Khan (2024) 'ρ-arbitrage and ρ-consistent pricing for star-shaped risk measures', Math. OR; arXiv:2202.07610v3, Definitions 3.2/3.20, Theorem 3.4, Theorem 3.23, Theorem 4.6. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: 2022 paper, Section 3: ρ: L → (−∞,∞], L^∞ ⊂ L ⊂ L^1 a Riesz space, ρ positively homogeneous and cash-invariant (ρ(X + c) = ρ(X) − c); Section 3 does NOT use monotonicity or convexity. One-period market with d risky assets, X_π = excess return of portfolio π, Π_ν = {π : E[X_π] = ν}, ρ_ν = inf{ρ(X_π) : π ∈ Π_ν}; scaling: Π^ρ_ν = νΠ^ρ_1, ρ_ν = νρ_1. Strong ρ-arbitrage: ∀π ∃π′ with E[X_{π′}] > E[X_π] and ρ(X_{π′}) < ρ(X_π); ρ-arbitrage: no ρ-efficient portfolio exists. Theorem 3.18: the market admits strong ρ-arbitrage ⇔ ρ_1 < 0. Theorem 3.20: (a) if Π^ρ_1 ≠ ∅: ρ-arbitrage ⇔ ρ_1 ≤ 0; (b) if Π^ρ_1 = ∅, Π^ρ_0 ≠ ∅: ρ-arbitrage ⇔ ρ_1 < 0; (c) if Π^ρ_1 = Π^ρ_0 = ∅: ρ-arbitrage. Prop. 3.15: if Π^ρ_ν ≠ ∅ ∀ν ≥ 0 then ρ_1 > 0 ⇔ the efficient frontier ℰ_ρ ≠ ∅, and then ℰ_ρ = {(kρ_1, k) : k ≥ 0}, a ray. Section 4 (coherent, expectation-bounded, ρ(X) = sup_{Z∈𝒬} E[−ZX]): no ρ-arbitrage ⇔ 𝒫 ∩ 𝒬̃ ≠ ∅ (𝒫 = equivalent martingale measures, 𝒬̃ = 'interior' of 𝒬 under conditions POS, MIX, INT); for ES_α: no ρ-arbitrage ⇔ ∃ EMM Q with ‖dQ/dP‖_∞ < 1/α. 2024 paper: risk functional = monotone, normalized, star-shaped (ρ(λX) ≥ λρ(X), λ ≥ 1) map ρ: L → (−∞,∞], cash-invariance NOT assumed; weak sensitivity to large losses: ∀X with P[X<0] > 0, E[X] = 0, ∃λ > 0 with λX ∉ 𝒜_ρ; Theorem 3.4: Fatou + weak sensitivity ⇒ ρ-optimal portfolios exist and form a compact set; Theorem 3.23: ρ convex, Fatou, weakly sensitive ⇒ [arbitrage-free market and strong sensitivity] ⇔ no ρ^∞-arbitrage ⇔ no ρ-arbitrage, ρ^∞(X) = lim_{t→∞} ρ(tX)/t.
      USE: Two uses. (1) Theorems 3.18/3.20 hold for positively homogeneous cash-invariant functionals WITHOUT monotonicity, so they apply verbatim to the non-monotone canonical family ρ_β: 'strong ρ_β-arbitrage' ⇔ inf{ρ_β(X_π) : E X_π = 1} < 0 ⇔ some unit-mean portfolio has LR(X_π) < β (for Π > 0), i.e. a 'loss-ratio arbitrage' characterization, and the ρ_β-efficient frontier is a ray because ρ_β is degree-one homogeneous. (2) The scale-free quantity ρ_1 = inf_{E X_π = 1} ρ(X_π) is itself a loss-ratio-type ratio (risk per unit of expected return) whose sign governs existence; the star-shaped paper shows which non-convex, non-cash-additive functionals still admit this ray/sign structure (sensitivity to large losses replaces monotonicity-type conditions).

Rosazza Gianin (2018), 'Robust return risk measures', Mathematics and Financial Economics 12(1), 5–32 (DOI 10.1007/s11579-017-0188-x); and Bellini, Laeven, Rosazza Gianin (2021), 'Dynamic robust Orlicz premia and Haezendonck–Goovaerts risk measures', European J. Oper. Res. 291, 1113–1130. [Primary pages blocked by the proxy; statements reconstructed from memory and cross-checked against Definition 1 and Proposition 3 of arXiv:2307.03447v2, which cite [3] = BLR 2018 as introducing return risk measures.] (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: A (static) return risk measure is a map ρ̃: L^∞_{+}(or L^∞_{++}) → [0,∞) that is monotone (X ≤ Y ⇒ ρ̃(X) ≤ ρ̃(Y)) and positively homogeneous (ρ̃(λX) = λρ̃(X), λ > 0), usually normalized ρ̃(1) = 1; cash-additivity is NOT required. Log-correspondence: ρ̃ is a return risk measure on L^∞_{++} ⇔ ρ(X) := log ρ̃(e^X) is a monetary risk measure (monotone, cash-additive) on L^∞, and ρ̃(Y) = exp ρ(log Y); under this bijection positive homogeneity ↔ cash-additivity, and multiplicative ('geometric') convexity ρ̃(X^λ Y^{1−λ}) ≤ ρ̃(X)^λ ρ̃(Y)^{1−λ} ↔ convexity of ρ. Orlicz premium (Haezendonck–Goovaerts): H_Φ(X) = inf{k > 0 : E[Φ(X/k)] ≤ 1}, Φ a Young function, is a subadditive, monotone, positively homogeneous return risk measure; its cash-additive counterpart is the HG risk measure π_Φ(X) = inf_{x∈ℝ} { x + H_Φ((X − x)_+) }. Representation (BLR 2018, main theorems, recalled): a return risk measure is 'convex' (in the sense ρ̃(λX + (1−λ)Y) ≤ λρ̃(X) + (1−λ)ρ̃(Y), hence sublinear) and satisfies the Fatou property ⇔ ρ̃(X) = sup_{Q∈𝒬} E_Q[X] for a set 𝒬 of probability measures ≪ P (the static t = 0 case of Prop. 3 in arXiv:2307.03447 confirms: monotone, convex, continuous from below, positively homogeneous, ρ(1) = 1 ⇒ ρ(X) = sup_{(D,Q)} D·E_Q[X], D ∈ [0,1]); more generally, 'robust' return risk measures are worst-case Orlicz premia ρ̃(X) = sup_{Q∈𝒬} H_{Φ,Q}(X), equivalently gauges ρ̃(X) = inf{k > 0 : X/k ∈ 𝒜} of a monotone, solid, convex unit-acceptance set 𝒜 = {ρ̃ ≤ 1}, and every normalized return risk measure is the Minkowski gauge of its unit acceptance set.
      USE: Return risk measures are precisely the 'ingredients' of a loss ratio: both ρ and Π (restricted to nonnegative losses) are return risk measures, and LR = ρ̃_1/ρ̃_2 is the ratio of two return risk measures, i.e. a degree-ZERO homogeneous functional. The log-correspondence turns LR into a cash-INVARIANT functional: Λ(X) := log LR(e^X) satisfies Λ(X + c) = Λ(X) and Λ = ρ̂ − Π̂ with ρ̂ = log ρ∘exp, Π̂ = log Π∘exp monetary risk measures; thus log LR∘exp is an 'inter-risk difference' (a signed variability-type measure, cf. entry 9) and can never be monotone unless ρ̂ − Π̂ ≡ const, which gives an alternative one-line proof of F2's spirit. The gauge representation ρ̃(X) = inf{k : X/k ∈ 𝒜} also shows how scale-free indices arise: LR(X) = β ⇔ X ∈ ∂(𝒜_β) for the cone 𝒜_β = {ρ ≤ βΠ}.
  [8] G. Jia, J. Xia, R. Zhao (2020), 'Monetary risk measures', arXiv:2012.06751v1, Definition 2.1, Theorem 3.1, Theorem 3.4, Remark 3.5 (and proof of Theorem 3.1 showing where monotonicity enters). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: (Ω,ℱ,P) complete probability space, ρ: L^∞(P) → ℝ. ρ is a monetary risk measure if monotone (X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ ρ(X) ≥ ρ(Y)) and translation invariant (ρ(X + m) = ρ(X) − m); no normalization, no convexity. Theorem 3.1: TFAE (a) ρ is a monetary risk measure; (b) ρ(X) = min_{λ∈Λ} sup_{Q∈𝓜_1(P)} (E_Q[−X] − α_λ(Q)) with convex α_λ; (c) ρ(X) = min_λ max_{Q∈𝓜_{1,f}(P)} (E_Q[−X] − α_λ(Q)) with weak*-lsc convex α_λ on finitely additive measures; (d) ρ = min_λ ρ_λ for a family of convex risk measures; (e) ρ(X) = inf{h(X) : h convex risk measure, h ≥ ρ}. The outer minimum is attained at Z_0 = X + ρ(X) ∈ 𝒜_ρ, using ρ_{𝒜(Z)}(X) = inf{m : X + m ≥ Z} (the convex risk measure generated by the single point Z), 𝒜(Z) = {Y ≥ Z}. Theorem 3.4: TFAE (a) ρ positively homogeneous monetary; (b) ρ(X) = min_λ max_{Q∈𝒬_λ} E_Q[−X], 𝒬_λ nonempty weak*-compact convex; (c) ρ = min_λ ρ_λ with ρ_λ coherent; (d) ρ = inf{h : h coherent, h ≥ ρ}; Remark 3.5: ρ(X) = min_{Z∈𝒜_ρ} max_{Q : E_Q[Z] ≥ 0} E_Q[−X].
      USE: Shows exactly where monotonicity is used in the 'lower envelope of coherent/convex' machinery: the single-point acceptance sets {Y ≥ Z} must be upward closed. For the LR pencil, ρ_β is positively homogeneous and cash-additive but NOT monotone, so {Y : ρ_β(Y) ≤ 0} is a cone that is not upward closed and the Jia–Xia–Zhao construction gives only the trivial ray envelope (Remark 9 of entry 3). Therefore any 'min of coherent' representation for LR can only be obtained for the generators ρ and Π: with Theorem 3.4, ρ = min_λ ρ_λ and Π = min_μ Π_μ (coherent) and LR = (min_λ ρ_λ)/(min_μ Π_μ); this clarifies that the right axiom system for LR must be comonotone/pencil-based (Schmeidler/signed-Choquet route) rather than cone/envelope-based.
  [9] F. Bellini, T. Fadina, R. Wang, Y. Wei (2022), 'Parametric measures of variability induced by risk measures', Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 106; arXiv:2012.05219v3, Definition 1, Theorem 1, Theorem 3; relying on R. Wang, Y. Wei, G. Willmot (2020), 'Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals', Math. Oper. Res. 45(3), Theorems 1, 3, 5. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: 𝒳 = L^0 on an atomless probability space. A variability measure is ν: 𝒳 → [0,∞] with (A1) law invariance; (A2) standardization ν(m) = 0 ∀m ∈ ℝ; (A3) positive homogeneity ν(λX) = λ^α ν(X), λ > 0, for some homogeneity index α ∈ [0,∞) (α = 0 allowed: scale-INVARIANT variability measures). Theorem 1: the inter-quantile, inter-ES and inter-expectile differences Δ^ES_p = ES_p − ES^−_{1−p} etc. are variability measures, increasing in p, and Δ_p(X) = ρ_p(X) + ρ_p(−X). Theorem 3: for a variability measure ν TFAE (i) ν satisfies (B1) relevance [ν(X) > 0 for non-constant X and ν ≤ β on {|X| ≤ 1}], (B2) continuity ν((X∧M)∨(−M)) → ν(X), (B3) symmetry ν(X) = ν(−X), (B4) comonotonic additivity, (B5) cx-consistency, (B6) convexity, (B7)-(B8) [incl. location invariance]; (ii) (B1)–(B4) and one of (B5)/(B6); (iii) ν(X) = ∫_0^1 Δ^ES_p(X) dμ(p) for a finite Borel measure μ ≠ 0 on (0,1]. The proof uses the signed-Choquet characterization of Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020a): a law-invariant, comonotonic-additive functional on L^∞ (with a mild continuity/boundedness condition equivalent to the distortion h being of bounded variation) is a signed Choquet integral ∫ X d(h∘P) with h of bounded variation, h(0) = 0, and it is a variability measure (location invariant) iff h(1) = 0, convex iff h concave.
      USE: The LR pencil is ρ_β = Π + (ρ − Π)/(1 − β), so LR − 1 = ν/Π with ν := ρ − Π = C_{h−g}: a location-invariant, positively homogeneous (index 1), law-invariant, comonotonic-additive signed Choquet functional, i.e. exactly a (signed) variability measure in the Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei/Wang–Wei–Willmot sense, divided by a monetary premium. Hence LR − 1 is a 'generalized coefficient of variation' (compare σ/μ, and the Gini coefficient = Gini mean difference/(2E[X]), the classical scale-free ratio of a signed Choquet integral to a Choquet integral), and ν ≥ 0 ⇔ LR ≥ 1 ⇔ h ≥ g. Theorem 3 then classifies the loadings ρ − Π that are cx-consistent and symmetric as ES-mixtures; and the signed-Choquet theorem (WWW 2020a, Thm 1) is the tool that converts the comonotone-additive, cash-additive, NON-monotone family ρ_β into C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (F7) without any monotonicity hypothesis, monotonicity being needed only to make h and g nondecreasing at the endpoints.


### Elicitability / CxLS / identification-function scholar (Gneiting; Fissler–Ziegel; Steinwart et al.; Ziegel; Bellini–Bignozzi; Weber; Delbaen–Bellini–Bignozzi–Ziegel; Nolde–Ziegel; Kou–Peng; Embrechts–Mao–Wang–Wang; Wang–Ziegel; Wang–Wei; Wang–Wei–Willmot; Fissler–Hlavinová–Rudloff; Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei). Sources read online: arXiv 0912.0902, 1303.1690, 1411.0426, 1503.08123, 1401.4787, 1608.05498, 1907.01306, 2507.13763 (abstract); author PDFs at sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/ (2015Wang-Ziegel-SPL, 2020Wang-Wei-MF, 2019Wang-Wei-Willmot-MOR, 2021Embrechts-Mao-Wang-Wang-MF, 2025Fissler-Liu-Wang-Wei-MF); proceedings.mlr.press/v35/steinwart14.pdf; Crossref API for bibliographic data. BLOCKED (report): WebSearch disabled for org; arxiv.org/search and export.arxiv.org robots-blocked; onlinelibrary.wiley.com (mafi.12313, Weber 2006 DOI 10.1111/j.1467-9965.2006.00277.x), tandfonline.com (Bellini–Bignozzi DOI 10.1080/14697688.2014.946955), SSRN Delivery.cfm, api.semanticscholar.org all refused by proxy.
KEY INSIGHT: Assa's association LR(X)=β ⟺ ρ_β(X)=0 is a FUNCTIONAL-LEVEL identification function; the literature's identification functions (Fissler–Ziegel Def 2.3, Nolde–Ziegel Def 2, Steinwart Def 3) are POINTWISE, V̄(β,F) = ∫V(β,y)dF(y), i.e. mixture-linear in F. Gneiting's Theorem 3.2 / Fissler–Ziegel Table 1 show that the ratio-of-expectations identification function is V(β,y) = β s(y) − r(y), which is literally the canonical family ρ − βΠ with ρ(F)=∫r dF, Π(F)=∫s dF. Hence LR admits a pointwise identification function with the canonical association iff ρ and Π are mixture-linear (expectations of functions of the loss). For the actuarial Choquet pair this never happens nontrivially: by Steinwart et al. (Cor 9) pointwise identifiability ⟺ CxLS ⟺ elicitability (under TV-continuity and strict local non-constancy), and by Wang–Wei (Thm 3.2, Cor 3.3) a signed Choquet integral such as ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} has CxLS only if it is monotone and of VaR/mean/ess-sup-inf type; since the canonical ρ_β is cash-additive with unit slope ((h−βg)(1)/(1−β) = 1), CxLS of the zero-level set of ρ_β for each β is equivalent to CxLS of ρ_β at all levels (up to the nonnegative-loss domain technicality), and requiring membership in Wang–Wei's class H* for a range of β forces h = g, i.e. LR ≡ 1. So the non-monotonicity of ρ_β (F2) is not an obstacle to be worked around — it is exactly what Wang–Wei–Willmot (Thm 1: law-invariant + comonotonic-additive + uniformly norm-continuous ⟺ signed Choquet, no monotonicity) and Wang–Wei (CxLS ⇒ monotone) are built to handle, and together they close the question: within the distortion class, a loss ratio is identifiable/elicitable/CxLS iff it is constant. The positive escape routes found in this strand are (a) the expectation-type loss ratio LR = E[r(X)]/E[s(X)] (fully characterised scoring functions, Gneiting Thm 3.2(c)), and (b) joint elicitability of a tail loss ratio with its quantile (Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei Prop 5.9: (VaR_p, E[u(X)|X>VaR_p]/E[t(X)|X>VaR_p]) is elicitable), mirroring (VaR,ES). The β-shortfall structure LR(X) = inf{β: ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} (valid when ρ ≤ Π, i.e. actuarial regime) is the exact analogue of the Weber/Delbaen–Bellini–Bignozzi–Ziegel shortfall representation, with the family ρ_β in place of E[ℓ(· − m)], and Fissler–Hlavinová–Rudloff Prop 3.7/Thm 3.1 give the template 'level-set functional identifiable ⟺ generator identifiable'.
CANDIDATE IFF: Theorem (Elicitability/CxLS characterisation of the loss ratio). Let (Ω,𝒜,P) be atomless, 𝒳 = L^∞_+ \\ {0} the nonnegative bounded losses, and let LR: 𝒳 → (0,∞) be law-invariant, with associated canonical family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) for some law-invariant ρ, Π: L^∞ → ℝ with Π > 0 on 𝒳 (so LR = ρ/Π and LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0). (A) [positive part, mixture-linear case] The following are equivalent: (i) LR is identifiable in the Fissler–Ziegel/Nolde–Ziegel sense with a strict identification function that is affine in the forecast, V(β,y) = β s(y) − r(y), s > 0; (ii) every member ρ_β of the canonical family is mixture-linear, ρ_β(F) = ∫ V_β dF; (iii) ρ(F) = ∫ r dF and Π(F) = ∫ s dF for measurable r, s>0, i.e. LR(X) = E[r(X)]/E[s(X)]. In this case, under (S0)–(S2), s continuous and r = y·s (weighted-mean case), the consistent scoring functions for LR on compactly supported laws are exactly Gneiting's (22), S(x,y) = s(y)(φ(y) − φ(x)) − φ'(x)(r(y) − x s(y)) + φ'(y)(r(y) − y s(y)) with φ convex; and if additionally LR is scale invariant with r, s homogeneous of a common degree, then LR is constant. (B) [negative part, distortion case] Suppose ρ = C_h and Π = C_g are Choquet integrals with distortion functions h, g (equivalently, by Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 1 + Lemma 2(i): ρ, Π law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous and monotone), and extend LR to signed bounded losses on which Π ≠ 0 so that three-point laws are available (Wang–Wei's standing domain). Then the following are equivalent: (i) LR has convex level sets (CxLS) on discrete laws; (ii) LR is identifiable (strict pointwise identification function); (iii) LR is elicitable; (iv) every ρ_β, β in the range of LR, has CxLS; (v) every such ρ_β is monotone and belongs to Wang–Wei's class H* (VaR-type, mean, or ess-sup/ess-inf mixture); (vi) h = g, i.e. LR ≡ 1. Proof sketch: (iii)⇒(i), (ii)⇒(i) Osband/Wang–Wei Prop 6.4; (i)⟺(iv) via cash-additivity ρ_β(X+c) = ρ_β(X) + c and the fact that mixtures commute with translation of laws; (iv)⟺(v) Wang–Wei Thm 3.2 + Cor 3.3 (the non-monotone signed Choquet ρ_β of (F7) is in the scope of this theorem); (v)⇒(vi) affine-in-β combinations of two distinct H*-shapes leave H*, so h ∝ g, and h(1)=g(1)=1 gives h=g; (vi)⇒ all. [Honest accounting: hypotheses on the pair are the Choquet/comonotonic-additive structure; the hypotheses on LR alone are law invariance, scale invariance and CxLS/identifiability/elicitability, plus Steinwart-type regularity (continuity in total variation, strict local non-constancy) if one wants (i)⟺(ii)⟺(iii) outside the distortion class. The restriction of CxLS to nonnegative losses only is a remaining technical gap for part (B).] (C) [rescue] If ρ is a p-tail risk measure with ratio generator ρ*(F) = ∫u dF/∫t dF (u, t differentiable) and Π the corresponding tail expectation, then (VaR_p, LR) is jointly elicitable (Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei Prop 5.9), so a tail loss ratio is backtestable comparatively only together with its VaR.
THEOREMS:

      STATEMENT: Thm 3.2. Let I ⊆ ℝ be an interval, r: I → ℝ and s: I → (0,∞) measurable, and T(F) = E_F[r(Y)]/E_F[s(Y)] (21). (a) T is elicitable relative to the class of probability measures on I for which E_F[r(Y)], E_F[s(Y)], E_F[Y s(Y)] exist and are finite. (b) Any S of the form (22) S(x,y) = s(y)(φ(y) − φ(x)) − φ'(x)(r(y) − x s(y)) + φ'(y)(r(y) − y s(y)), φ convex with subgradient φ', is consistent for T relative to the class of F for which E_F[r(Y)], E_F[s(Y)], E_F[r(Y)φ'(Y)], E_F[s(Y)φ(Y)], E_F[Y s(Y)φ'(Y)] exist and are finite; if φ is strictly convex, S is strictly consistent. (c) Suppose S satisfies (S0) S(x,y) ≥ 0 with equality iff x = y, (S1) S continuous in x, (S2) ∂_x S exists and is continuous in x for x ≠ y, on D = I × I. If s is continuous and r(y) = y s(y), then S is consistent for T relative to the class of compactly supported probability measures on I IF AND ONLY IF S is of the form (22) with φ convex. Thm 2.8 (Osband). If T is elicitable relative to F, then its level sets are convex: F_0, F_1 ∈ F, p ∈ (0,1), F_p = (1−p)F_0 + pF_1 ∈ F, t ∈ T(F_0) ∩ T(F_1) ⇒ t ∈ T(F_p). (Note: the iff in (c) is stated only for the weighted-mean case r = y s and compactly supported F; no general ratio iff is claimed.)
      USE: This is THE existing iff theorem about a ratio functional. Its identification function is V(x,y) = x s(y) − r(y), i.e. E_F[V(x,Y)] = x Π(F) − ρ(F) with ρ(F) = E_F[r(Y)], Π(F) = E_F[s(Y)] — exactly Assa's canonical family ρ_β ∝ ρ − βΠ, specialised to mixture-linear (expectation-type) ρ, Π. Hence Assa's 'association' is the functional-level generalisation of Gneiting's ratio identification function, and LR = E[r(X)]/E[s(X)] is the unique sub-case where the association is realised by a pointwise identification function. Scale invariance LR(λX)=LR(X) with r, s homogeneous of the same degree forces r = c s, i.e. LR constant; so a nonconstant, scale-invariant, elicitable-by-(22) loss ratio does not exist — this pins down precisely why the distortion loss ratio is not elicitable.
  [2] Fissler, T. & Ziegel, J.F. (2016), 'Higher order elicitability and Osband's principle', Ann. Statist. 44(4), 1680–1707 (arXiv:1503.08123): Definition 2.3 (identification function), Definition 2.7 (oriented), Theorem 3.2 (Osband's principle), Proposition 2.13 (revelation principle), Proposition 2.14, Table 1 and Corollary 4.3 (ratios).       STATEMENT: Def 2.3. V: A × O → ℝ^k is an F-identification function for T if V is F-integrable and V̄(T(F),F) := ∫V(T(F),y)dF(y) = 0 ∀F ∈ F; it is strict if V̄(x,F) = 0 ⟺ x = T(F) ∀x ∈ A, F ∈ F. Def 2.7: strict V is oriented if for all unit v, F and admissible s: vᵀV̄(T(F)+sv,F) > 0 ⟺ s > 0. Thm 3.2 (Osband's principle). Let F be convex, T: F → A ⊆ ℝ^k surjective, elicitable and identifiable with strict F-identification function V and strictly F-consistent S. Assume (V1): for each x ∈ int(A) there are F_1,…,F_{k+1} ∈ F with 0 ∈ int conv{V̄(x,F_1),…,V̄(x,F_{k+1})}, and (S1): S̄(·,F) continuously differentiable ∀F. Then there is a matrix-valued h: int(A) → ℝ^{k×k} such that ∂_l S̄(x,F) = Σ_m h_{lm}(x) V̄_m(x,F) ∀x ∈ int(A), F ∈ F. If (V2) (V̄(·,F) continuous) holds, h is continuous; if (V3) (V̄(·,F) ∈ C¹) and (S2) (∇S̄(·,F) locally Lipschitz, S̄(·,F) twice C¹ at T(F)) hold, h is locally Lipschitz. Prop 2.13. For a bijection g, T is identifiable (elicitable) iff g∘T is, with V_g(x,y) = V(g⁻¹(x),y), S_g(x,y) = S(g⁻¹(x),y). Prop 2.14 (Osband): elicitable ⇒ CxLS (level sets convex under mixtures). Table 1: T(F) = E_F[p(Y)]/E_F[q(Y)] has identification function V(x,y) = x q(y) − p(y). Cor 4.3: for a vector of ratios with common denominator q > 0, under the assumptions of Thm 3.2, strictly consistent scores have the Bregman form (4.5) with strictly convex φ.
      USE: (i) Osband's principle is the tool to prove the 'only if' direction of any LR-characterisation that starts from an assumed scoring/identification structure: given a strict identification function V(β,y) for LR, all consistent scores are ∫-integrals of V (via h). (ii) The revelation principle shows that LR, 1/LR, log LR, and the acceptability index of (F5) are simultaneously elicitable/identifiable or not — the choice of the ratio orientation is immaterial. (iii) Table 1/Cor 4.3 again exhibit x q(y) − p(y) = the canonical ρ_β-pattern, confirming that Assa's family is the functional-level analogue of a ratio identification function.
  [3] Steinwart, I., Pasin, C., Williamson, R.C. & Zhang, S. (2014), 'Elicitation and identification of properties', JMLR W&CP 35 (COLT 2014), 482–526: Theorem 2, Theorem 5, Theorem 8, Corollary 9 (equivalence theorem). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: (Y,𝒜,μ) separable finite measure space; P(Δ) = probability measures with bounded μ-densities (Δ = Δ_{≥0} or Δ_{>0}); T: P(Δ₁) → ℝ a property (real-valued functional), continuous w.r.t. the L^p(μ)-metric on densities (p=1: total variation), and strictly locally non-constant: ∀t ∈ int(im T), ∀ε>0, ∀P with T(P)=t ∃P_−, P_+ with T(P_−) < t < T(P_+) and ‖P − P_±‖_{L^∞(μ)} ≤ ε. Definition 3: V: A × Y → ℝ is a P-identification function for T if E_{Y∼P}V(t,Y) = 0 ⟺ t = T(P) (t ∈ int im T, P ∈ P); oriented if E_P V(t,Y) > 0 ⟺ t > T(P). Corollary 9. Under these hypotheses the following are EQUIVALENT: (i) all level sets {T = t} are convex; (ii) all sets {T < t} and {T > t} are convex (T is quasi-linear in mixtures); (iii) T is identifiable with a bounded (oriented, Thm 5/8) identification function; (iv) T is elicitable; (v) there is a non-negative, measurable, locally Lipschitz scoring function that is P(Δ)-order-sensitive for T. Theorem 2 (Osband): if P is convex and T elicitable, then each level set is convex. Theorem 8: under the same setting, CxLS + strict local non-constancy ⇒ ∃ measurable oriented bounded V* with ‖V*(t,·)‖_{L^∞(μ)} = 1 for a.a. t (Hahn–Banach, non-constructive).
      USE: This is the only genuine IFF linking identifiability, CxLS and elicitability for real-valued functionals, and it is the bridge between (F6) and (F2): a POINTWISE identification function for LR exists iff LR has mixture-convex level sets (plus continuity/non-constancy). Combined with Wang–Wei (below), it shows the functional-level identification V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) of (F6) cannot be upgraded to a pointwise one unless LR is constant. It also gives the exact regularity one must assume on LR itself (continuity in total variation on bounded-density laws; strict local non-constancy) to turn 'CxLS' into 'elicitable'.

(2020), 'Risk functionals with convex level sets', Mathematical Finance 30(4), 1337–1367 (author PDF: sas.uwaterloo.ca/~wang/papers/2020Wang-Wei-MF.pdf): Definition 1.1, Definition 3.1, Theorem 3.2, Corollary 3.3, Proposition 3.4, Proposition 5.2, Theorem 5.3, Proposition 6.4, Theorem 6.9, Corollary 6.12.       STATEMENT: Def 1.1. ρ has CxLS on M if ρ(λF + (1−λ)G) = ρ(F) whenever ρ(F) = ρ(G), λ ∈ (0,1), λF+(1−λ)G ∈ M. Def 3.1. Signed Choquet integral I_h(F) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(1−F(x)) − h(1))dx + ∫_0^∞ h(1−F(x))dx, h ∈ H := {h:[0,1]→ℝ of bounded variation, h(0)=0}. Theorem 3.2. Let M be convex and contain all three-point distributions. I_h has CxLS on M IF AND ONLY IF h ∈ H*, where H* = H*₁ ∪ H*₂ ∪ H*₃: H*₁: I_h = h(1)(c·ess-sup + (1−c)·ess-inf), c ∈ [0,1]; H*₂: I_h = h(1)·E; H*₃: I_h = h(1)·VaR^c_{1−α} (mixture of left/right α-quantiles), α ∈ (0,1), c ∈ [0,1]. Corollary 3.3. If I_h has CxLS on M_dis (discrete laws), then I_h is monotone. Prop 3.4: CxLS on M_con (laws with densities) ⇒ CxLS on M_dis ⇒ h ∈ H*. Prop 5.2. A functional on M_∞ is a d-dimensional signed Choquet integral iff it is comonotonic-additive and uniformly continuous w.r.t. the Wasserstein-L^∞ metric. Theorem 5.3. For p ∈ (0,1), h ∈ H, (I_h, VaR_p) has CxLS on M_dis iff (i) I_h = a VaR_p + I_{h*}, h* ∈ H*; or (ii) I_h = a VaR_p + b E + c ES_p; or (iii) I_h = a VaR_p + b VaR⁺_p + c ess-sup, bc>0; or (iv) I_h = a VaR_p + b VaR⁺_p + c ess-inf, bc<0. Prop 6.4: elicitable or identifiable functionals have CxLS. Thm 6.9: a spectral risk measure ρ: M_∞ → ℝ is co-elicitable with VaR_p on M*_∞(p) iff ρ = aE + (1−a)ES_p; Cor 6.12: if moreover ρ is lsc under weak convergence, iff ρ = ES_p.
      USE: Decisive for the LR problem because it treats NON-monotone (signed) Choquet integrals, which is exactly what the canonical family ρ_β = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} is (F7). Consequences: (a) Cor 3.3 shows the non-monotonicity of ρ_β (F2) is fatal for CxLS: ρ_β has CxLS only if it is monotone, i.e. only if h − βg is monotone, and then only if (h−βg)/(1−β) ∈ H*. (b) Requiring this for all β in the range of LR forces h and g to be proportional (two distinct H*-shapes cannot combine affinely in β while staying in H*), hence h = g and LR ≡ 1. (c) Since LR(F)=β ⟺ ρ_β(F) = 0, and I_k(X+c) = I_k(X) + c·k(1) with k(1) = 1 ≠ 0 for the canonical family, CxLS of LR (zero level of every ρ_β) is equivalent (up to the nonnegativity-of-losses domain technicality) to CxLS of ρ_β at every level — giving a clean negative iff: a distortion loss ratio has CxLS iff it is constant. (d) Thm 5.3/6.9 suggest the positive escape route: LR is not elicitable alone but pairs such as (VaR_p, ES_p/E)-type tail loss ratios may be jointly elicitable with a quantile (see Fissler–Liu–Wang–Wei Prop 5.9).
  [5] Wang, R., Wei, Y. & Willmot, G.E. (2020), 'Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals', Math. Oper. Res. 45(3), 993–1015 (author PDF 2019Wang-Wei-Willmot-MOR.pdf): Definition 1, Theorem 1, Lemma 1, Lemma 2, Theorem 3. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,𝒜,P), functionals on L^∞. H = {h: [0,1] → ℝ of bounded variation, h(0) = 0}. Def 1: I_h(X) = ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X ≥ x)) − h(1))dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X ≥ x))dx. Theorem 1. A functional I: L^∞ → ℝ is a signed Choquet integral (I = I_h for some h ∈ H) IF AND ONLY IF I is (a) law-invariant, (b) comonotonic-additive [I(X+Y) = I(X)+I(Y) for comonotonic X,Y], and (c) uniformly L^∞-norm-continuous. No monotonicity is assumed. (Remark 3: with mere norm-continuity the representation persists but h need not be of bounded variation.) Lemma 1: if h₁(1) = h₂(1) then h₁ ≤ h₂ on [0,1] iff I_{h₁} ≤ I_{h₂} on L^∞; in particular I_{h₁} = I_{h₂} iff h₁ = h₂. Lemma 2: (i) I_h is increasing iff h is increasing (decreasing iff h decreasing); (ii) I_h(X+c) = I_h(X) + c·h(1); (iii) I_h(λX) = λI_h(X), λ>0; (iv) I_h(−X) = I_{ĥ}(X), ĥ(x) = h(1−x) − h(1). Lemma 4: |I_h(X) − I_h(Y)| ≤ TV_h ‖X−Y‖_∞. Theorem 3. For h ∈ H the following are equivalent: (1) h concave; (2) I_h is convex-order consistent; (3) I_h subadditive; (4) I_h convex; (5) I_h quasi-convex; (6) I_h concave on mixtures.
      USE: Supplies the iff representation for the NON-monotone associated family, replacing the Cherny–Madan/Kusuoka theorems that need monotonicity (F2). Direct consequences for LR: (a) [pair-level iff] the canonical family {ρ_β} consists of signed Choquet integrals for two distinct β ⟺ ρ and Π are both signed Choquet integrals ⟺ ρ, Π are law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly norm-continuous; with ρ, Π monotone this is the distortion pair (h,g) of (F1)–(F3) by Lemma 2(i). (b) [quasi-convexity answer to F4] ρ_β is quasi-convex ⟺ ρ_β convex ⟺ h − βg concave (β<1), which gives the quasi-convexity of the level-set structure of LR exactly in the 'conic' regime h concave, g convex, and shows quasi-convexity of ρ_β is NOT automatically inherited from quasi-convexity of ρ. (c) Lemma 1 is the identifiability statement behind (F3): I_{h} = I_{βg} on a cone determines h − βg. (d) Thm 3 (6) 'concave on mixtures' explains why zero-sets of ρ_β are not mixture-convex when h − βg is strictly concave, i.e. why LR fails CxLS.
  [6] Ziegel, J.F. (2016), 'Coherence and elicitability', Mathematical Finance 26(4), 901–918 (arXiv:1303.1690): Definitions 2.1–2.2, Theorem 2.1, Theorem 3.1 (Kusuoka), Theorem 4.1, Corollary 4.2, Corollary 4.3, Proposition 4.4. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: standard atomless probability space; coherent risk measure ρ: L^∞ → ℝ (monotone, cash-invariant, subadditive, positively homogeneous); P = probability measures on [0,1] with weak topology. Thm 3.1 (Kusuoka): a law-invariant coherent ρ has ρ = sup_{m ∈ M} ν_m with M ⊂ P closed convex, ν_m the spectral (mixture-of-ES) functional. Thm 2.1 (Osband): elicitable ⇒ CxLS as in Gneiting Thm 2.8. Theorem 4.1. Let P* be the class of two-point distributions on ℝ, M ⊆ P a closed set not containing δ_0, and ν: P* → ℝ, ν(P) = inf_{m∈M} ν_m(P) (ν_m at (3)). If ν has convex level sets, then m({0}) = 0 for all m ∈ M and there exists C ∈ (0,1] such that all measures m_p = (1−p)δ_1 + p·δ_{...} of (6) are in M, and (7)–(8) hold for each m ∈ M. Cor 4.2: if moreover M₀ is finite (ν(P) = inf_{m ∈ M₀} ν_m(P) on P*), then M₀ = {δ₁} and ν(P) = E_P(Y). Cor 4.3: spectral risk measures other than minus the expected value are not elicitable relative to any class of distributions containing the two-point distributions. Prop 4.4: the law-invariant coherent risk measure ℓ_C of (9) [generated by the family (6)] equals minus the τ-expectile with τ = C/(C+1) ∈ (0,1/2]. Summary (with Weber 2006 for the converse): 'Expectiles are the only elicitable law-invariant coherent risk measures'.
      USE: Template for the 'only if' proof technique relevant to LR: CxLS is tested only on TWO-POINT (Bernoulli-type) distributions, which is exactly the class on which (F3) shows LR only sees h/g. For a loss ratio of a coherent ρ and a 'bid-price' Π (conic picture) one can run the same two-point computation on the zero-set of ρ − βΠ. Also warns that Choquet/spectral ρ with ρ_β-type families is never elicitable, so any iff theorem for LR obtained from this strand must be either a negative one (CxLS ⟺ trivial) or restricted to the expectile/shortfall-like world where ρ(X) = inf{m: E ℓ(X−m) ≤ 0} — which is precisely the form LR(X) = inf{β: ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} takes under Assa's association (monotone-in-β regime ρ ≤ Π).
  [7] Delbaen, F., Bellini, F., Bignozzi, V. & Ziegel, J.F. (2016), 'Risk measures with the CxLS property', Finance and Stochastics 20(2), 433–453 (arXiv:1411.0426): Section 2 (setting), Proposition 2.4, Proposition 2.5, Lemma 3.1, Theorem 3.8. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless probability space; utility u: L^∞ → ℝ monetary (translation invariant u(ξ+h) = u(ξ)+h, monotone, u(0) = 0), law-determined (hence a function on M_{1,c}(ℝ), compactly supported laws); acceptance set 𝒜 = {ξ: u(ξ) ≥ 0}; u concave iff 𝒜 convex; Fatou: ξ_n → ξ in probability, bounded ⇒ u(ξ) ≥ limsup u(ξ_n); WC: lower level sets of the penalty c compact in σ(L¹,L^∞) ⟺ Lebesgue property. CxLS: u(F) = u(G) = γ ⇒ u(λF + (1−λ)G) = γ ∀λ ∈ (0,1) (= 'betweenness' in decision theory). Prop 2.4 (trichotomy): exactly one of (a) u = ess inf, (b) u has WC, (c) u ≠ ess inf and u lacks WC. Prop 2.5: for concave law-determined monetary u TFAE: (a) WC; (b) u is ψ-weakly continuous for some gauge ψ; (c) for each x<y, λ ↦ u(λδ_x + (1−λ)δ_y) is continuous as λ → 0+. Theorem 3.8. Let u be a monetary, concave, law-determined utility with CxLS. Then there exists a concave φ̄: ℝ → ℝ ∪ {−∞} such that u(ξ) ≥ 0 IF AND ONLY IF E[φ̄(ξ)] ≥ 0 (generalized shortfall representation; the loss may take the value +∞). Conversely such generalized shortfall utilities have CxLS. Corollary (stated in the Introduction): the only elicitable coherent law-determined risk measures are expectiles (extending Ziegel 2014, Bellini–Bignozzi 2014); and for convex law-determined risk measures robustness (ψ-weak continuity) ⟺ the weak mixture continuity lim_{λ→0+} ρ(λδ_x + (1−λ)δ_y) = ρ(δ_y).
      USE: Gives the structural meaning of 'implicitly defined by a zero-set of an expectation': u(ξ) = sup{m: E φ̄(ξ − m) ≥ 0}. Assa's LR(X) = inf{β: ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} (when β ↦ ρ_β(X) is monotone, i.e. ρ ≤ Π or ρ ≥ Π) is the same 'shortfall-in-β' construction with the loss ρ_β(·) in place of E φ̄(· − m). Thm 3.8 then says: LR is a (β-)shortfall of EXPECTATION type (equivalently: has CxLS with concavity/monotonicity in β) iff the acceptance sets {X: LR(X) ≤ β} = {X: ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} are described by an expectation E[φ̄_β(X)] ≤ 0 — i.e. iff each ρ_β is replaceable by a mixture-linear functional on its acceptance set. With the canonical family this forces ρ, Π mixture-linear ⇒ LR = E r(X)/E s(X). Also note the hypotheses NOT satisfied by ρ_β: monotonicity (F2) and cash-additivity-with-unit-slope (ρ_β(X+c) = ρ_β(X) + c holds for the canonical family since (h−βg)(1)/(1−β) = 1 — so cash-additivity is fine, it is only monotonicity that fails).
  [8] Kou, S. & Peng, X. (2016), 'On the measurement of economic tail risk', Operations Research 64(5), 1056–1072 (arXiv:1401.4787): Axioms A1–A5, Definition 2.1, Lemma 2.1, Theorem 2.1, Lemma 3.1. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Space: 𝒳 ⊃ L^∞ on a probability space rich enough to carry a U[0,1] variable. Axioms: A1 (comonotonic independence) for pairwise comonotonic X,Y,Z and α ∈ (0,1), ρ(X) < ρ(Y) ⇒ ρ(αX + (1−α)Z) < ρ(αY + (1−α)Z); A2 (monotonicity) X ≤ Y ⇒ ρ(X) ≤ ρ(Y); A3 (standardization) ρ(x·1_Ω) = s x, s > 0; A4 (law invariance); A5 (continuity) lim_{M→∞} ρ(min(max(X,−M),M)) = ρ(X). Def 2.1: ρ is elicitable w.r.t. 𝒫 if ∃ S: ℝ² → ℝ with ρ(F) = argmin_x ∫S(x,y)dF(y) ∀F ∈ 𝒫. Lemma 2.1 (Schmeidler 1986): ρ satisfies A1–A5 IF AND ONLY IF there is a distortion function h: [0,1] → [0,1], h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1, h increasing (not necessarily left/right continuous) with ρ(X) = s∫X d(h∘P) (Choquet integral), X not required to be bounded. Theorem 2.1. Let ρ: 𝒳 → ℝ satisfy A1–A5 and 𝒫 := {F_X: X ∈ 𝒳}. Then ρ is elicitable w.r.t. 𝒫 IF AND ONLY IF either (i) ρ = VaR_α for some α ∈ (0,1] (which includes median shortfall MS_α = VaR_{(1+α)/2}), or (ii) ρ(F) = ∫x dF(x) (the mean). Lemma 3.1 (multiple scenarios): f satisfies B1 (f(a x̃ + b1) = a f(x̃) + s b), B2 (componentwise monotone), B3 (uncertainty aversion: f(x̃) = f(ỹ) ⇒ f(αx̃ + (1−α)ỹ) ≤ f(x̃)) iff f(x̃) = s·sup_{w ∈ 𝒲} Σ w_i x_i for a set 𝒲 of probability weights. CxLS is stated only as a necessary condition for elicitability.
      USE: Model for the style of theorem the panel wants: an axiomatic representation (A1–A5 ⟺ Choquet) composed with an elicitability selection (⟺ VaR or mean). For LR the analogous two-stage theorem is: [axioms on the pair ⟺ (ρ,Π) Choquet] + [CxLS/elicitability selection ⟺ trivial]. Note the exact hypotheses A1 (comonotonic independence, a Schmeidler axiom) and A2: dropping A2 is what Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 1 does, producing the signed Choquet class to which ρ_β belongs. Kou–Peng's Theorem 2.1 together with Wang–Ziegel/Wang–Wei shows that even the monotone members ρ_β (β below the range of LR, where h ≥ βg) are elicitable only if (h − βg)/(1−β) is a step or linear function, which again cannot hold for a range of β unless LR is constant.
  [9] Wang, R. & Ziegel, J.F. (2015), 'Elicitable distortion risk measures: a concise proof', Statistics & Probability Letters 100, 172–175 (author PDF 2015Wang-Ziegel-SPL.pdf), Theorem 3.1. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: standard atomless probability space; D = distribution functions on ℝ; distortion risk measure ρ(F) = Choquet integral (1.1) with h ∈ H = {h: [0,1] → [0,1] non-decreasing, h(0) = 0, h(1) = 1} (no continuity of h assumed), defined on D_ρ. CxLS (Osband): t ∈ ρ(F) ∩ ρ(G) ⇒ t ∈ ρ(λF + (1−λ)G), λ ∈ [0,1], whenever the mixture is in D_ρ. Theorem 3.1.
