Let D* be the class of finitely supported distributions and ρ a distortion risk measure with h ∈ H whose restriction to D* has convex level sets. Then h is one of: (i) h(t) = t; (ii) h = 0 on [0,α), h(α) = c, h = 1 on (α,1], for some α ∈ (0,1), c ∈ [0,1]; (iii) h(0) = 0, h = c on (0,1), h(1) = 1 for some c ∈ [0,1]. Stated one-directionally; combined with Osband's theorem it yields: the only elicitable distortion risk measures (for all practical purposes) are quantiles (VaR) and the mean.
      USE: Monotone precursor of Wang–Wei Thm 3.2; its three-point-distribution proof is elementary and can be transcribed verbatim to the zero-set of ρ − βΠ on nonnegative losses (the zero set is a cone by positive homogeneity, so three-point laws on [0,∞) suffice). Useful for a self-contained proof of the negative iff for LR within the distortion class without importing the full signed theory.
  [10] Embrechts, P., Mao, T., Wang, Q. & Wang, R. (2021), 'Bayes risk, elicitability, and the Expected Shortfall', Mathematical Finance 31(4), 1190–1217 (author PDF 2021Embrechts-Mao-Wang-Wang-MF.pdf): Definition 2.2, Theorem 2.3, Theorem 3.1, Proposition 3.6, Proposition 5.1, Theorem 6.1, Corollary 6.4. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless probability space, L^∞ ⊂ 𝒳 ⊂ L¹. Def 2.2: (S,R) is a Bayes pair if for some Borel loss L: ℝ^{d+1} → ℝ, S(X) = argmin_x E[L(x,X)] and R(X) = min_x E[L(x,X)]; if S is translation invariant, S is a Bayes estimator and R a Bayes risk measure. Theorem 2.3: R is a Bayes risk (i.e. satisfies (5) for some S, L) IF AND ONLY IF R(X) = min_{ℓ ∈ 𝒜} E[ℓ(X)] for some set 𝒜 of real Borel functions. Theorem 3.1. For R: 𝒳 → ℝ TFAE: (i) R is a coherent Bayes risk measure; (ii) R is a Choquet Bayes risk measure; (iii) R = ES^λ_α := λES_α + (1−λ)E for some α ∈ (0,1), λ ∈ [0,1]. If in addition R is lower semicontinuous w.r.t. a.s. convergence, then R = ES_α for some α ∈ (0,1). Prop 3.6: for a Bayes pair with S(0) = 0, R coherent Bayes ⟺ R Choquet Bayes ⟺ (S,R) = (VaR_α, ES^λ_α), λ ∈ [0,1). Prop 5.1: every Bayes risk measure is mixture-concave and every Bayes estimator has CxLS. Theorem 6.1. Let R be a law-invariant monetary risk measure on L^∞ with R(0) = 0 satisfying (C): for x < y, α ↦ R̂((1−α)δ_x + αδ_y) is continuous at α = 0. Then R is mixture-concave and has CxLS IF AND ONLY IF R is an entropic risk measure. Cor 6.4: a monetary risk measure with R(0) = 0 is both elicitable and Bayes iff it is entropic.
      USE: Provides the dual 'min over expectations' representation (Thm 2.3) and the exact iff for 'mixture-concave + CxLS' (Thm 6.1). For LR: 1/LR = sup{x: xρ(X) − Π(X) ≤ 0} (F5) is a sup over the family x ↦ ρ_{1/x}-type functionals; if ρ and Π were Bayes risks (minima of expectations), LR's reciprocal would be a sup/min-type functional in the Bayes class. In the conic picture (h concave, g convex) ρ_β is mixture-concave (WWW Thm 3), so Thm 6.1 says: ρ_β is mixture-concave with CxLS iff entropic — impossible for a positively homogeneous ρ_β unless ρ_β = E; this is a second, independent route to the negative result and shows that no scale-invariant LR with nontrivial mixture-concave associated family can be CxLS.
  [11] Fissler, T., Liu, F., Wang, R. & Wei, L. (2026), 'Elicitability and identifiability of tail risk measures', Mathematical Finance 36(3) (author PDF 2025Fissler-Liu-Wang-Wei-MF.pdf): Definitions 2.1–2.2, 3.1–3.2, 3.7, Proposition 4.1, Theorem 4.3, Corollary 4.7, Theorem 5.3, Proposition 5.9, Remark 4.8. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: ρ is a p-tail risk measure if ρ(F) = ρ(G) whenever F_p = G_p, F_p(x) = (F(x) − p)⁺/(1−p); generator ρ* with ρ(F) = ρ*(F_p). CxLS (3.7(i)): T(F₀) ∩ T(F₁) ⊆ T((1−λ)F₀ + λF₁); CxLS*: if T(F₀) ∩ T(F₁) ≠ ∅ then it equals T(mixture). Theorem 4.3. If ρ* is M-identifiable with strict identification function V*, then (Q_p, ρ) is M(p) ∩ M-identifiable with V(v,x,y) = (1{y ≤ v} − p, 1{y > v}V*(x,y) + (1{y ≤ v} − p)V*(x,v)). Prop 4.1 (converse direction): if ρ is identifiable on M ∪ {(1−p)G + pδ_r} then ρ* is identifiable on M_{≥r} ∩ M with V*_r(x,y) = (1−p)V(x,y) + pV(x,r). Cor 4.7: ρ* identifiable ⇒ (Q_p, ρ) has CxLS*. Theorem 5.3: ρ* M-elicitable with score S* ⇒ (i) ρ is conditionally elicitable given Q_p; (ii) if S*(x,·) is strictly increasing ∀x, (Q_p, ρ) is M-elicitable with score (5.4); (iii) under Assumption B.1 (eight regularity items) every strictly consistent score for (Q_p,ρ) has form (5.4). Proposition 5.9 (ratios). If ρ*(F) = ∫u dF / ∫t dF with u, t differentiable, then (Q_p, ρ) is M-elicitable. Remark 4.8: under continuity assumptions on ρ* and richness of M, ρ* is M-identifiable iff it has CxLS on M iff it is M-elicitable (Steinwart et al. 2014, Cor. 10). Necessity: identifiability ⇒ CxLS (Fissler–Hoga 2024, Prop. B.4); elicitability ⇒ CxLS* (Fissler et al. 2021, Prop. 3.4).
      USE: Prop 5.9 is the only published result on a RATIO functional beyond the mixture-linear case that is (jointly) elicitable: a tail loss ratio E[u(X) | X > VaR_p]/E[t(X) | X > VaR_p] — e.g. ES_p(X)/E[t(X)|X > VaR_p] — is jointly elicitable with VaR_p. This shows how to rescue elicitability of an LR built from a tail risk measure ρ = ES_p and a tail-expectation premium: not alone, but as the second component of (VaR_p, LR). Prop 4.1/Thm 4.3 also give the pattern for transporting identification functions along a 'generator' map, which is formally what Assa's association does along β.
  [12] Fissler, T., Hlavinová, J. & Rudloff, B. (2021), 'Elicitability and identifiability of systemic risk measures', Finance and Stochastics 25, 133–165 (arXiv:1907.01306): Definition 2.5, Theorem 3.1, Proposition 3.7, Theorem 3.8. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Systemic risk measure (Feinstein–Rudloff–Weber): R(Y) = {k ∈ ℝ^d : ρ(Λ(Y + k)) ≤ 0}, R₀(Y) = {k: ρ(Λ(Y+k)) = 0}, with Λ an aggregation function and ρ a scalar risk measure. Def 2.5: V is a selective M^d-identification function for R₀ if V̄(x,F) = 0 ∀x ∈ R₀(F); strict if V̄(x,F) = 0 ⟺ x ∈ R₀(F). Theorem 3.1. Assume ρ is identifiable with strict M-identification function V_ρ and set V_{R₀}(k,y) = V_ρ(0, Λ(y+k)). Then (i) V_{R₀} is a strict selective M^d-identification function for R₀; (ii) if V_ρ is oriented, V_{R₀} is oriented for R₀ (negative for k ∉ R(F), zero on R₀(F), positive on R(F) \ R₀(F)). Proposition 3.7. Let Λ be surjective with a measurable right inverse η (Λ∘η = id), 𝒴 closed under translations, η(X) ∈ 𝒴^d for X ∈ 𝒴. Then ρ is identifiable IF AND ONLY IF R₀ is selectively identifiable. Theorem 3.8: under weak orientation (3.10) the elementary scores S_{R,k}(A,y) = (1_{R(y)\A}(k) − 1_{A\R(y)}(k))V_{R₀}(k,y) and their mixtures S_{R,π} = ∫S_{R,k} π(dk) are consistent exhaustive scores for R, strictly consistent if V_{R₀} is strict and π strictly positive σ-finite (iii).
      USE: This is the closest existing iff for an IMPLICITLY DEFINED functional: a level-set functional {k: ρ(Λ(Y+k)) = 0} is identifiable iff the generating scalar ρ is. Assa's LR is defined by the level set {β: ρ_β(X) = 0} of a one-parameter family; the FHR mechanism V_{LR}(β,y) := V_{ρ_β}(0,y) gives a strict identification function for LR as soon as each ρ_β has a strict identification function with the SAME pointwise structure — which, by Steinwart/Wang–Wei, happens only in the mixture-linear case. Also, the Prop 3.7 'iff' pattern (functional ⟷ generator) is the template for a theorem 'LR is (selectively) identifiable iff its associated family is identifiable at level 0'.
  [13] Nolde, N. & Ziegel, J.F. (2017), 'Elicitability and backtesting: Perspectives for banking regulation', Annals of Applied Statistics 11(4), 1833–1874 (arXiv:1608.05498): Definitions 1–3, identification functions (2.5)–(2.7), Propositions 1–3. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Def 1: Θ (vector of risk measures) is elicitable w.r.t. 𝒫 if ∃ S with E S(Θ(X),X) < E S(r,X) for all r ≠ Θ(X) and all laws in 𝒫. Def 2: Θ is identifiable w.r.t. 𝒫 if there is V with E V(r,X) = 0 ⟺ r = Θ(X). Identification functions: VaR_α: V(r,x) = 1 − α − 1{x > r} on laws with unique α-quantile; τ-expectile: V(r,x) = |1 − τ − 1{x > r}|(r − x) on 𝒫₁ (finite mean); (VaR_ν, ES_ν): two-component V in (2.7) on 𝒫₁ ∩ 𝒫_V. Def 3: a forecast sequence R_t is calibrated on average if E V(R_t,X_t) = 0 ∀t, conditionally calibrated if E[V(R_t,X_t) | ℱ_{t−1}] = 0 a.s.; the optimal conditional forecast is the unique ℱ-predictable conditionally calibrated forecast (a.s.); conditional calibration ⇒ calibration on average, not conversely. Stated facts: for k = 1 CxLS is necessary for elicitability (Osband 1985, Gneiting 2011 Thm 6), and identifiability implies elicitability under additional assumptions (Steinwart et al. 2014); unclear for k ≥ 2. Prop 1–3 give the consistent score families for VaR, expectiles, (VaR,ES).
      USE: Defines the exact sense of 'identification function' used in (F6). In this sense V must be a pointwise function of (r, x) with E_F V(r,X) = 0 ⟺ r = Θ(F); Assa's V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is NOT of this form unless ρ_β is an expectation. The calibration framework nevertheless applies to the functional-level identification: a sequence of loss-ratio forecasts β_t is 'calibrated' iff ρ_{β_t}(X_t) = 0, which is a traditional (not comparative) backtest — consistent with Nolde–Ziegel's statement that non-elicitable functionals admit traditional but not comparative backtests.
  [14] Bellini, F. & Bignozzi, V. (2015), 'On elicitable risk measures', Quantitative Finance 15(5), 725–733, DOI 10.1080/14697688.2014.946955 (SSRN 2334746, 2013). Main results: Proposition/Theorem on monetary elicitable ⇒ shortfall, Theorem on coherent elicitable ⟺ expectile, alternative proof of Ziegel's comonotone result. [Page blocked by proxy; abstract read on SSRN; statements from memory.] (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: law-invariant (monetary) risk measures on L^∞ (or L^p) over an atomless space; elicitability in a slightly more restrictive sense than Gneiting (strictly consistent S with S(x,y) ≥ 0 and S(x,x) = 0, plus a mixture-continuity requirement on the induced functional). Results (as stated in the abstract and, from memory, in the body): (1) if ρ is a law-invariant monetary risk measure that is elicitable (in their sense), then ρ is a shortfall risk measure ρ(X) = inf{m ∈ ℝ: E[ℓ(−X − m)] ≤ 0} for a nondecreasing loss ℓ (a subclass of Föllmer–Schied shortfalls; converse holds under mixture continuity); (2) a law-invariant coherent risk measure is elicitable IF AND ONLY IF it is (minus) an expectile ρ(X) = −e_τ(X) with τ ∈ (0,1/2] (equivalently e_α(−X), α ≥ 1/2); (3) the only coherent comonotonic(-additive) elicitable risk measure is the expected loss (alternative proof of Ziegel). Abstract (verbatim from SSRN page): 'In the coherent case, we show that the only elicitable risk measures are the expectiles'; 'elicitability leads to a subclass of the shortfall risk measures'.
      USE: Iff (2) is the exact statement requested in the task. The method (CxLS of coherent ρ on two-point laws + Weber's shortfall representation) transfers to LR as follows: LR(X) = inf{β: ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} is of shortfall form in β; requiring that LR be elicitable in the Bellini–Bignozzi sense forces the acceptance sets {ρ_β ≤ 0} to be of expectation-shortfall type, and positive homogeneity (scale invariance of LR) then forces ℓ to be positively homogeneous — the expectile case — which, applied to the zero-sets of ρ − βΠ, is compatible only with ρ, Π mixture-linear. Result (3) is the comonotone-additive analogue needed for the Choquet pair: a comonotonic-additive, coherent, elicitable functional is the expectation, hence a loss ratio of comonotonic-additive coherent ρ, Π is elicitable only if ρ = aE, Π = bE, i.e. LR constant.
  [15] Weber, S. (2006), 'Distribution-invariant risk measures, information, and the emergence of convex level sets' — published as 'Distribution-Invariant Risk Measures, Information, and Dynamic Consistency', Mathematical Finance 16(2), 419–441, DOI 10.1111/j.1467-9965.2006.00277.x; main characterization Theorem 3.1/3.3 (shortfall risk). [Wiley page blocked by proxy; statement from memory and from its restatements in Ziegel 2016 Sec. 4.3 and Delbaen et al. 2016 Sec. 1.] (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: distribution-invariant monetary risk measure ρ on L^∞ (monotone, cash-invariant, ρ(0)=0) over an atomless space, identified with its acceptance set of laws 𝒩 ⊂ M_{1,c}(ℝ) (compactly supported laws). Hypotheses (Weber's regularity): (i) 𝒩 and 𝒩^c are convex at the level of distributions (this is CxLS/'betweenness' for ρ, the 'convex level sets' of the title); (ii) 𝒩 is ψ-weakly closed and 𝒩^c has nonempty ψ-weak interior for some gauge ψ (ψ-weak continuity of ρ); (iii) a 'preference consistency'/non-degeneracy condition (𝒩 ≠ ∅, 𝒩^c ≠ ∅). Theorem (Weber 2006, Thm 3.1/3.3). Under (i)–(iii), ρ is a shortfall risk measure: there exist a nondecreasing, non-constant loss function ℓ: ℝ → ℝ and x₀ ∈ ℝ with ρ(X) = inf{m ∈ ℝ : E[ℓ(−X − m)] ≤ x₀}; conversely such shortfall risk measures (with ℓ left-continuous/increasing) satisfy (i)–(iii). Corollary (as used by Ziegel 2016 Sec 4.3, Bellini–Bignozzi 2015): a coherent distribution-invariant risk measure with convex level sets (under the regularity (ii)) is minus a τ-expectile, τ ∈ (0,1/2].
      USE: The original 'CxLS ⟺ shortfall' iff, with the regularity assumptions that Delbaen et al. (2016, Thm 3.8) later removed in the concave case. For LR, the analogue is: the β-indexed acceptance sets 𝒩_β = {F: LR(F) ≤ β} = {F: ρ_β(F) ≤ 0} are nested and increasing in β in the actuarial regime ρ ≤ Π; Weber's theorem applied to each 𝒩_β characterises when each is an expectation-shortfall set, which is the content of the candidate iff below. Weber's extra hypothesis (ii) (ψ-weak continuity) is the price for working with non-concave acceptance sets — relevant because ρ_β is not concave/convex in general, so DBBZ's Thm 3.8 does not apply and Weber's version is the one needed for a general (non-conic) LR.


### Performance-ratio axiomatics scholar (gain-loss ratio, Omega, Sharpe, RAROC/RORAC, conic-finance bid/ask, acceptability indices). NOTE ON PROCESS: WebSearch is disabled for this organization (HTTP 403 "feature not enabled") and the egress proxy policy-denies Google, Bing, DuckDuckGo, Google Scholar, Semantic Scholar, Crossref, SSRN, Springer, ScienceDirect, Wiley, INFORMS, RePEc, CORE and the arXiv export API; only direct arxiv.org/abs and arxiv.org/pdf pages (for IDs I already knew) and Wikipedia were readable, and arXiv author-listing pages were rate-limited (429). I therefore read online: Tasche (arXiv:0708.2542, full text), Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang (arXiv:1010.4339, full text, which reprints the Cherny–Madan definition and theorem verbatim), Biagini–Pinar (arXiv:1209.6439, full text), and the Wikipedia Omega-ratio page. Everything else is from memory and is marked medium/low.
KEY INSIGHT: The classical ratio characterizations (Cherny–Madan Theorem 1, Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang Theorem 4.8, Biagini–Pinar/Bernardo–Ledoit duality) all have the same two-step architecture: (i) recover a one-parameter family of cash-additive functionals from the ratio by CASH SHIFTING, rho_x(X) = inf{c : alpha(X + c) >= x} (BCZ Thm 4.6, Assa Prop 1), and (ii) represent each member of the family by a dual set. Monotonicity of the ratio is used ONLY in step (ii) to force dual densities to be non-negative; it plays no role in step (i). For the loss ratio, step (i) is exact and intrinsic: for LR = rho/Pi with cash-additive rho, Pi, the cash-shift profile c -> LR(X + c) is the Mobius function (c + rho(X))/(c + Pi(X)), so rho(X) is minus its ZERO and Pi(X) is minus its POLE, and m_beta = (rho - beta Pi)/(beta - 1) = -rho_beta reproduces Assa's canonical family. The F2 obstruction (rho_beta non-monotone) is then circumvented by never applying a coherent-risk-measure representation to the intermediate rho_beta at all: the two ENDPOINTS rho_0 = rho (zero) and rho_infty = Pi (pole) are the only members that must be monotone, and Schmeidler's theorem (comonotone additive + monotone + law invariant => Choquet with distortion) is applied to those two; the intermediate members are then automatically the signed Choquet integrals (h - beta g)/(1 - beta) of F7 (Wang–Wei–Willmot). Comonotone additivity of the endpoints is delivered intrinsically by the two-sided comonotone mediant property of LR (F1): for comonotone X, Y, applying min(LR(X),LR(Y)) <= LR(X+Y) <= max to the cash-shifted positions X + c, Y + c' with LR(X + c) = LR(Y + c') = beta gives rho_beta(X+Y) <= and >= rho_beta(X) + rho_beta(Y), i.e. each cash-additive positively homogeneous rho_beta is comonotone additive. What is needed on the PAIR rather than on LR alone is exactly: (a) monotonicity of the zero and of the pole of the profile (not of LR itself, which is impossible by F2), and (b) the values of rho, Pi on the degenerate set {X : rho(X) = Pi(X)} where the profile is identically 1 and the pair is not identified from LR (by F3 this set is thin unless LR == 1, and Pi there is pinned by law invariance + continuity + comonotone additivity through two-layer losses). Dually, in the conic regime (h concave, g convex) 1/LR = sup{x : inf_{Z in Z_g, W in Z_h} E[(Z - xW)X] >= 0} is a Cherny–Madan index with SIGNED dual sets D_x = Z_g - x Z_h, the loss-ratio analogue of Bernardo–Ledoit/Biagini–Pinar's D_x = {ess sup Z / ess inf Z <= x}; and the RORAC-compatibility iff of Tasche transfers to 'loss-ratio compatibility' of sub-line (Euler) contributions whenever rho and Pi are positively homogeneous and Gateaux differentiable.
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (Mobius/zero-pole characterization). Let (Omega,F,P) be atomless and let LR: L^infty -> [-infty,+infty] be given (extended values allowed at poles). Suppose there are functions z, p: L^infty -> R such that (A1) [Mobius cash-shift / ratio axiom] LR(X + c) = (c - z(X))/(c - p(X)) for all X in L^infty and all c in R (value infinity at c = p(X); on the degenerate set z(X) = p(X) the profile is identically 1 and z, p are part of the data); (A2) [scale invariance] LR(lambda X) = LR(X) for all lambda > 0 and all X, including shifted X (equivalently z, p positively homogeneous); (A3) [monotonicity of zero and pole] X <= Y a.s. => z(X) >= z(Y) and p(X) >= p(Y); (A4) [law invariance] X =_d Y => LR(X + c) = LR(Y + c) for all c (equivalently z, p law invariant); (A5) [two-sided comonotone mediant property on the extended domain] for comonotone X, Y with p(X), p(Y) < 0 (positive denominators): min{LR(X), LR(Y)} <= LR(X + Y) <= max{LR(X), LR(Y)} (equivalently z, p comonotone additive); (A6) [continuity] z and p are continuous along bounded a.s. convergence (equivalently LR(X_n + c) -> LR(X + c) for all c off the pole). THEN there exist unique continuous distortion functions h, g: [0,1] -> [0,1], non-decreasing with h(0) = g(0) = 0, h(1) = g(1) = 1, such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) for all X with C_g(X) != 0, where C_h(X) = integral_0^infty h(P(X > x)) dx + integral_{-infty}^0 (h(P(X > x)) - 1) dx; moreover z = -C_h, p = -C_g, and the associated family rho_beta(X) := -inf{c : LR(X + c) <= beta} (beta > 1) equals (C_h - beta C_g)/(1 - beta) = signed Choquet integral with distortion (h - beta g)/(1 - beta), and LR(X) = beta <=> rho_beta(X) = 0. CONVERSELY, for any such (h, g), LR := C_h/C_g with z := -C_h, p := -C_g satisfies (A1)-(A6). Restricting to non-negative losses X >= 0 and beta > 1 (rho >= Pi) gives Assa's setting. PROOF OF (=>): set rho := -z, Pi := -p; (A1) with uniqueness of zero/pole of a non-constant Mobius map gives LR = rho/Pi and rho(X + c) = rho(X) + c, Pi(X + c) = Pi(X) + c (compare the profiles of X and X + c); (A2) gives positive homogeneity; (A3) monotonicity; (A4) law invariance; (A5) comonotone additivity (cash-shift the comonotone pair to the common level beta and use rho_beta's cash additivity and homogeneity in both directions; let beta -> 0 and beta -> infinity for rho and Pi); then Schmeidler (1986) + law invariance (Kusuoka/Yaari) give rho = C_h, Pi = C_g with h(1) = g(1) = 1 from cash additivity, and (A6) gives continuity of h, g (events B_n down/up B). PROOF OF (<=): F1 plus the Mobius computation. HONEST ACCOUNTING: (A3) and the degenerate-set values are the only hypotheses stated on the pair rather than on LR alone; (A3) cannot be replaced by monotonicity of LR (F2); the signed-Choquet structure of the intermediate rho_beta is a consequence, not a hypothesis. Dual form in the conic regime (h concave, g convex): 1/LR(X) = sup{x >= 0 : inf_{Z in Z_g, W in Z_h} E[(Z - xW)X] >= 0} for X >= 0, i.e. a Cherny–Madan acceptability index with the signed, nested dual sets D_x = Z_g - x Z_h.
THEOREMS:
  [1] Cherny, A. & Madan, D. (2009), 'New measures for performance evaluation', Review of Financial Studies 22(7), 2571-2606, Theorem 1 (static representation of coherent acceptability indices); read in the verbatim reprint as Definition 2.1 and Theorem 2.2 of Bielecki, Cialenco & Zhang, arXiv:1010.4339 (Math. Finance 2014). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let alpha: L^infty(Omega,F,P) -> [0,+infty]. Call alpha a coherent acceptability index if (S1) Monotonicity: X <= Y => alpha(X) <= alpha(Y); (S2) Scale invariance: alpha(lambda X) = alpha(X) for all lambda > 0; (S3) Quasi-concavity: alpha(X) >= x and alpha(Y) >= x => alpha(lambda X + (1-lambda) Y) >= x for all lambda in [0,1]; (S4) Fatou property: |X_n| <= 1, alpha(X_n) >= x for all n, X_n -> X in probability => alpha(X) >= x. THEOREM: a map alpha: L^infty -> [0,+infty], unbounded from above, is a coherent acceptability index IF AND ONLY IF there exists a family (D_x)_{x in [0,+infty]} of sets of probability measures (absolutely continuous w.r.t. P) with D_x subset D_y for x <= y, such that alpha(X) = sup{ x in [0,+infty) : inf_{Q in D_x} E_Q[X] >= 0 }, with inf(empty)=+infty and sup(empty)=0. Equivalently alpha(X) = sup{x : rho_x(X) <= 0} for an increasing family (rho_x) of coherent risk measures with the Fatou property, rho_x(X) = -inf_{Q in D_x} E_Q X. (Proof mechanism: the superlevel sets A_x = {alpha >= x} are sigma(L^infty,L^1)-closed convex cones containing L^infty_+, decreasing in x; the bipolar theorem gives D_x; monotonicity is exactly what forces the polar sets to consist of NON-NEGATIVE measures.)
      USE: This is the template: a 'performance ratio' is characterized by a nested family of acceptance cones. For LR the superlevel sets {LR >= beta} = {rho - beta Pi >= 0} (on losses) and for 1/LR = Pi/rho the sets {Pi - x rho >= 0}. By (F2) LR is not monotone, so (S1) fails and Theorem 1 does not apply verbatim; BUT the proof only uses (S1) to force D_x into the positive cone. Dropping (S1) and keeping (S2)-(S4), the identical bipolar argument yields alpha(X) = sup{x : inf_{Z in D_x} E[Z X] >= 0} with D_x subsets of SIGNED densities Z in L^1 (increasing in x). In the 'conic picture' (rho = C_h with h concave, so rho = sup_{W in Z_h} E[WX]; Pi = C_g with g convex, so Pi = inf_{Z in Z_g} E[ZX]) this gives the explicit signed family D_x = Z_g - x Z_h = {Z - xW}, i.e. 1/LR(X) = sup{x >= 0 : inf_{Z in Z_g, W in Z_h} E[(Z - xW)X] >= 0} for X >= 0. This is the correct 'acceptability-index-type' representation of the loss ratio announced loosely in (F5).
  [2] Bielecki, T.R., Cialenco, I. & Zhang, Z. (2014), 'Dynamic coherent acceptability indices and their applications to finance', Mathematical Finance 24(3), 411-441; arXiv:1010.4339, Definitions 4.3-4.5, Theorems 4.6-4.8 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let (rho^x)_{x>0} be a family of dynamic coherent risk measures; it is 'increasing' if rho^x >= rho^y whenever x >= y > 0, and 'left-continuous' at x_0 if rho^x -> rho^{x_0} as x -> x_0^-. A normalized dynamic coherent acceptability index alpha (axioms D1 adaptiveness, D2 independence of the past, D3 monotonicity, D4 scale invariance, D5 quasi-concavity, D6 translation invariance, D7 dynamic consistency; normalized = takes the values +infty and 0 at each time/state) is 'right-continuous' if alpha_t(D + c 1_{t}) -> alpha_t(D) as c -> 0^+. THEOREM 4.6: if alpha is a normalized DCAI then rho^x_t(D) := inf{ c in R : alpha_t(D + c 1_{t}) >= x } is an increasing, left-continuous family of dynamic coherent risk measures. THEOREM 4.7: if (rho^x) is an increasing family of DCRMs then alpha_t(D) := sup{ x > 0 : rho^x_t(D) < 0 } (sup(empty)=0) is a normalized right-continuous DCAI. THEOREM 4.8 (two-way correspondence): (i) alpha normalized right-continuous DCAI => there is a left-continuous increasing family (rho^x) of DCRMs with alpha_t(D) = sup{x > 0 : rho^x_t(D) <= 0}; (ii) (rho^x) left-continuous increasing family of DCRMs => there is a right-continuous normalized DCAI alpha from which (rho^x) is recovered by the infimum formula. (Static case of the same paper: Sharpe ratio SR(X) = (E X - r)/STD(X) fails (S1) and is not a coherent index; GLR(X) = E(X)/E(max{-X,0}) for E X > 0, 0 otherwise, is a coherent index; the dynamic GLR is a DCAI, the dynamic RAROC is not.)
      USE: Theorem 4.6 is the exact mechanism behind Assa's Prop. 1: the associated family is recovered from the index by CASH SHIFTING, rho^x(X) = inf{c : alpha(X + c) >= x}, and this recovery needs no monotonicity of alpha in X. For LR: m_beta(X) := inf{c : LR(X + c) <= beta} is well defined from the 'translation to break-even' property alone, and rho_beta := -m_beta is automatically cash-additive, and positively homogeneous when LR is scale invariant. For LR = rho/Pi one computes m_beta = (rho - beta Pi)/(beta - 1) = -rho_beta, i.e. the canonical family IS the cash-shift family. The right-/left-continuity pairing (index right-continuous in c <-> family left-continuous in x) tells us which continuity axiom on LR is needed to make the beta -> rho_beta correspondence bijective.
  [3] Biagini, S. & Pinar, M.C. (2013), 'The best gain-loss ratio is a poor performance measure', SIAM Journal on Financial Mathematics 4(1), 228-242; arXiv:1209.6439, Theorem 2.6, Corollary 2.7, Lemma 3.1, Theorem 3.3 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: S an R^d-valued semimartingale on (Omega,F,(F_t),P), Assumption 2.1: S*_T in L^1(P); strategies simple, predictable, bounded; X = set of nontrivial discounted terminal gains with finite first moment; GLR alpha(X) = E[X^+]/E[X^-]; best market GLR alpha* = sup_{X in X, X != 0} alpha(X); M_infty = equivalent martingale measures Q whose density dQ/dP is bounded and bounded away from 0. THEOREM 2.6: the following are equivalent: (a) the market is lambda-gain-loss free (alpha* <= lambda, strictly: no X with alpha(X) >= lambda); (b) there exists Q in M_infty with (ess sup dQ/dP)/(ess inf dQ/dP) < lambda. Moreover alpha* = min_{Q in M_infty} (ess sup dQ/dP)/(ess inf dQ/dP) (sup = min, no duality gap, minimum attained). COROLLARY 2.7: alpha* >= 1 and alpha* = 1 iff P is itself a martingale measure for S. THEOREM 3.3 (random endowment B in L^1, alpha*(B) := sup_{K in K, B+K != 0} alpha(B+K)): (i) alpha*(B) < +infty iff (ii) E_Q[B] <= 0 for some Q in M_infty; and alpha*(B) = min_{Q in M_infty, E_Q[B] <= 0} (ess sup Z)/(ess inf Z), Z = dQ/dP, with the constraint E_Q[B] = 0 binding whenever +infty > alpha*(B) > alpha*(0). LEMMA 3.1: B -> alpha*(B) is monotone, quasi-concave, scale invariant and continuous from below (B_n up to B => alpha*(B_n) up to alpha*(B)), i.e. an acceptability index on L^1 in the Biagini–Bion-Nadal sense (continuity from below replacing Cherny–Madan's continuity from above). The paper also notes that Bernardo–Ledoit's duality alpha* = min_Z ess sup Z/ess inf Z over pricing kernels c <= Z <= C is 'correct only if Omega is finite'.
      USE: Two uses. (1) It shows the dual object of a RATIO index is a RATIO CONSTRAINT on densities: GLR(X) >= x iff inf{E[ZX] : 1 <= Z <= x (normalised)} >= 0, i.e. D_x = {Q : ess sup(dQ/dP)/ess inf(dQ/dP) <= x}. For the loss ratio the analogous dual sets are D_x = Z_g - x Z_h (signed) or, in the actuarial picture rho = E, Pi = C_g, D_x = {1 - xZ : Z in Z_g}; the loss ratio is thus a 'GLR with respect to the premium kernel'. (2) The scale-invariance critique (alpha* depends only on the half-ray of B) transfers verbatim to LR and explains why LR cannot be monotone (F2): a scale-invariant ratio of two cash-additive functionals ranks half-rays, not positions, and a cash shift moves the half-ray.
  [4] Tasche, D. (2008), 'Capital allocation to business units and sub-portfolios: the Euler principle', arXiv:0708.2542 (in: Pillar II in the New Basel Accord, Risk Books), Definitions 2.1-2.2, Proposition 2.1, citing Theorem 4.4 of Tasche (1999) 'Risk contributions and performance measurement' for the proof (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Let X = sum_i X_i, mu_i = E[X_i], rho a risk measure with f_rho(u_1,...,u_n) := rho(sum_i u_i X_i) (eqs. (2.4)-(2.5)). RORAC(X) := E[X]/rho(X); portfolio-related RORAC of asset i: RORAC(X_i | X) := mu_i / rho(X_i | X) where rho(X_i | X) are 'risk contributions'. Full allocation: sum_i rho(X_i|X) = rho(X). Definition 2.2 (RORAC compatibility): the contributions rho(X_i|X) are RORAC compatible if there are eps_i > 0 such that RORAC(X_i | X) > RORAC(X) implies RORAC(X + h X_i) > RORAC(X) for all 0 < h < eps_i. PROPOSITION 2.1: assume f_rho is continuously differentiable; if there are risk contributions rho(X_1|X),...,rho(X_n|X) that are RORAC compatible in the sense of Def. 2.2 for arbitrary expected values mu_1,...,mu_n, then rho(X_i|X) is uniquely determined as rho_Euler(X_i|X) = d/dh rho(X + h X_i)|_{h=0} = (partial f_rho/partial u_i)(1,...,1). Section 2.2: full allocation and RORAC compatibility 'can obtain at the same time if and only if the risk measure is homogeneous of degree 1' (Euler's theorem).
      USE: Gives the ONLY iff-type axiomatics in the RAROC literature, and it is about the ratio's DIRECTIONAL DERIVATIVE, not about monotonicity. It transfers to the loss ratio of a sub-line: with LR(X) = rho(X)/Pi(X), d/dh LR(X + hX_i)|_0 = (rho'_i Pi - rho Pi'_i)/Pi^2 (rho'_i, Pi'_i the Gateaux derivatives), so 'LR-compatibility' (a line with marginal loss ratio rho'_i/Pi'_i below the book's LR improves the book's LR when added in small amount) holds, and the Euler contributions (rho'_i, Pi'_i) are the unique LR-compatible pair, exactly when both rho and Pi are positively homogeneous and Gateaux differentiable (Tasche's uniqueness argument run with arbitrary premium loadings in place of arbitrary mu_i). For Choquet pairs the Euler contributions are the comonotone/Choquet marginal contributions E[X_i h'(1-F_X(X))] etc., which also underlies the comonotone-additivity axiom.
  [5] Schmeidler, D. (1986), 'Integral representation without additivity', Proceedings of the AMS 97(2), 255-261 (main theorem), combined with Kusuoka, S. (2001), 'On law invariant coherent risk measures', Advances in Mathematical Economics 3, 83-95, Theorem 7 (law-invariant + comonotone-additive coherent = distortion/spectral) and Yaari (1987) Econometrica. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Schmeidler: let B be the space of bounded F-measurable functions on (Omega,F) and I: B -> R satisfy (i) comonotonic additivity: I(X+Y) = I(X)+I(Y) whenever X,Y are comonotone, and (ii) monotonicity: X <= Y => I(X) <= I(Y), (iii) I(1) = 1. Then there is a unique capacity v(A) := I(1_A) (monotone, v(empty)=0, v(Omega)=1) such that I(X) = integral_0^infty v(X > t) dt + integral_{-infty}^0 (v(X > t) - 1) dt (Choquet integral) for all X in B; conversely every Choquet integral is comonotonic additive and monotone. Kusuoka Thm 7 / Yaari: if moreover I is law invariant on an atomless (Omega,F,P) then v = h o P for a unique non-decreasing h:[0,1]->[0,1] with h(0)=0,h(1)=1, i.e. I = C_h; I is subadditive iff h is concave; if I is required to be continuous along bounded a.s. convergence then h is continuous (and conversely).
      USE: This is the engine for the sufficiency direction of a loss-ratio theorem once the pair (rho, Pi) has been read off from LR: it needs monotonicity only of rho and Pi themselves (the two ENDPOINTS rho_0 = rho and rho_infty = Pi of the canonical family), never of the intermediate rho_beta. So the F2 obstruction is irrelevant: one applies Schmeidler twice, to the 'zero' and the 'pole' of the cash-shift profile c -> LR(X+c) (see candidate iff).
  [6] Wang, R., Wei, Y. & Willmot, G.E. (2020), 'Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals', Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993-1015, Theorem 1 (characterization) and the comonotonic-additive-functional lemma; building on Greco (1982) 'Sulla rappresentazione di funzionali mediante integrali'. (confidence: low)
      STATEMENT: (From memory; exact topology hypothesis not verified online.) For a functional I: L^infty(Omega,F,P) -> R on an atomless space, the following are equivalent: (i) I is law invariant, comonotonic additive and continuous in a suitable sense (I recall: uniformly continuous w.r.t. the L^infty norm / 'continuous from above' type condition), (ii) there exists a function h:[0,1] -> R of bounded variation with h(0) = 0 such that I(X) = integral_{-infty}^0 (h(P(X > x)) - h(1)) dx + integral_0^infty h(P(X > x)) dx (a 'signed Choquet integral'). Such I is monotone iff h is non-decreasing; convex iff h is concave; positively homogeneous and comonotonic additive always; cash-additive iff h(1) = 1 (I recall the paper calls I with h(1)=1 'normalized'); every signed Choquet integral is the difference of two Choquet integrals with monotone distortions (Jordan decomposition of h).
      USE: Gives (F7) a theorem: the canonical rho_beta = (rho - beta Pi)/(1-beta) is the signed Choquet integral with h_beta = (h - beta g)/(1-beta), and conversely a NON-MONOTONE cash-additive, law-invariant, comonotone-additive, continuous rho_beta must be of this form. Hence an axiom system that makes each cash-shift functional rho_beta comonotone additive (which the two-sided comonotone mediant property of LR delivers, see key insight) forces the whole family to be signed Choquet, with distortions affine in beta: (1-beta) h_beta = h - beta g, so h = h_0 and g = lim_{beta->infty} h_beta. Monotonicity of h and g must then be imposed separately on the two endpoint functionals.
  [7] Bernardo, A.E. & Ledoit, O. (2000), 'Gain, loss, and asset pricing', Journal of Political Economy 108(1), 144-172, Theorem 1 (duality between gain-loss bound and pricing-kernel ratio bound), finite-state version; as restated in Biagini–Pinar (2013) Section 1-2. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Finite Omega (the general-Omega version in the paper is flawed, per Biagini–Pinar). Let Z_bench be a benchmark pricing kernel and consider admissible pricing kernels Z (positive, pricing the traded assets). The gain-loss ratio of a zero-cost payoff X is E[X^+]/E[X^-] (expectations possibly under the benchmark-tilted measure). THEOREM 1: there is NO zero-cost portfolio with gain-loss ratio >= L (equivalently alpha* <= L) IF AND ONLY IF there exists an admissible pricing kernel Z with ess sup(Z/Z_bench)/ess inf(Z/Z_bench) <= L; in particular alpha* = min_Z ess sup Z/ess inf Z over admissible kernels (restricted to those bounded above and away from zero). Specialising L -> infinity gives the fundamental theorem of asset pricing (no arbitrage iff a positive pricing kernel exists); L -> 1 gives 'P is a martingale measure'.
      USE: Historically the first 'ratio performance measure <-> ratio constraint on dual densities' duality. It suggests the dual form of the loss-ratio theorem: in the actuarial picture (rho = E, Pi = C_g, g concave) LR(X) <= beta for all X in a class K iff there is a premium density Z in the core Z_g with E[(1 - beta Z)X] <= 0 on K; and the market-wide 'best loss ratio' sup_X LR(X) equals min over premium kernels of a ratio bound on the kernel, in exact analogy with alpha* = min ess sup Z/ess inf Z.
  [8] Penot, J.-P. & Volle, M. (1990), 'On strongly convex and paraconvex dualities', in Generalized Convexity and Fractional Programming, Springer LNEMS 345; Volle, M. (1998) J. Convex Anal.; used for risk measures in Drapeau, S. & Kupper, M. (2013), 'Risk preferences and their robust representation', Mathematics of Operations Research 38(1), 28-62, Theorem 1.7 and the surrounding remark on dropping monotonicity, and in Cerreia-Vioglio, Maccheroni, Marinacci & Montrucchio (2011), 'Risk measures: rationality and diversification', Mathematical Finance 21(4). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (From memory.) Penot–Volle: let E be a locally convex space with dual E*, f: E -> [-infty,+infty]. Then f is lower semicontinuous and quasiconvex IF AND ONLY IF f(x) = sup_{x* in E*} R(x*, <x*,x>) for all x, where R(x*,t) := inf{ f(y) : <x*,y> >= t } (no monotonicity, no homogeneity needed). Drapeau–Kupper Thm 1.7 (monotone version): rho: L^infty -> [-infty,+infty] is monotone (decreasing), quasiconvex and sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lsc iff rho(X) = sup_{Q in ba_+ or L^1_+} R(Q, E_Q[-X]) for a unique maximal risk function R: K_+ x R -> [-infty,infty] (non-decreasing, left-continuous in t, with a joint lower-semicontinuity / 'maximality' condition); monotonicity is exactly what restricts Q to the positive cone, and the authors remark that without monotonicity the same representation holds with Q ranging over the full dual (signed).
      USE: Provides the quasi-convex (rather than conic/cone-based) dual representation for the NON-MONOTONE loss ratio in the regimes where LR is quasi-convex (F4): LR(X) = sup_{Z in L^1 (signed)} R(Z, E[ZX]) with R(Z,t) = inf{LR(Y) : E[ZY] >= t}. Scale invariance of LR makes R(Z,.) 0-homogeneous-in-t so that R(Z,t) depends only on sign(t), which collapses the Penot–Volle representation back to the cone/acceptability form with signed D_x. This is the rigorous replacement for the Cherny–Madan theorem when monotonicity is absent.
  [9] Chamberlain, G. (1983), 'A characterization of the distributions that imply mean-variance utility functions', Journal of Economic Theory 29, 185-201 (also Owen & Rabinovitch 1983, J. Finance); the Sharpe-ratio corollary is standard (e.g. Ingersoll 1987). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (From memory.) Let returns R = (R_1,...,R_n) and consider portfolios w'R. Expected utility E[u(w'R)] depends on w only through the mean and variance of w'R for EVERY concave u (and every w) IF AND ONLY IF R has an elliptically symmetric distribution (when a riskless asset is included: spherically symmetric after affine transform). Corollary: ranking of all portfolios (with a riskless asset) by the Sharpe ratio (E[w'R] - r)/STD(w'R) is consistent with expected-utility maximisation for every risk-averse investor iff returns are elliptical; outside that class the Sharpe ratio violates monotonicity (Cherny–Madan's observation that SR fails (S1)).
      USE: Shows the standard pattern for a NON-MONOTONE scale-invariant ratio (Sharpe): the iff characterization is obtained not by axioms on the ratio but by restricting the DOMAIN (elliptical laws) on which the ratio agrees with a monotone object. The analogue for LR: on sub-domains where rho and Pi are comonotone-additive and the losses are comonotone (e.g. a single layer family a + c 1_B, or all X measurable w.r.t. one comonotone chain) LR behaves like a monotone index of the layer structure; F3's identification of (h,g) from Bernoulli and two-layer losses is exactly such a domain restriction.
  [10] Keating, C. & Shadwick, W.F. (2002), 'A universal performance measure', Journal of Performance Measurement 6(3), 59-84; identity due to Kazemi, H., Schneeweis, T. & Gupta, R. (2004), 'Omega as a performance measure', Journal of Alternative Investments 6(3), 16-25; stochastic-dominance consistency in Balder, S. & Schweizer, N. (2017), 'Risk aversion vs. the Omega ratio: Consistency results', Finance Research Letters 21, 78-84. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Omega ratio at threshold L: Omega_L(X) = integral_L^infty (1 - F_X(x)) dx / integral_{-infty}^L F_X(x) dx = E[(X - L)^+]/E[(L - X)^+] (= Bernardo–Ledoit GLR when L = 0, Wikipedia confirms this special case). Kazemi identity: Omega_L(X) = 1 + (E[X] - L)/E[(L - X)^+] ('Sharpe-Omega' = Omega_L - 1 = excess mean over expected shortfall below L). Consequences (my derivation + Balder–Schweizer, from memory): for fixed L, X -> Omega_L(X) is monotone and quasi-concave on {E X >= L}, and X >= Y in second-order stochastic dominance with E Y >= L implies Omega_L(X) >= Omega_L(Y) (SSD-consistency holds for all thresholds L not above the means; it can fail for L above the mean); Omega_L is scale invariant only for L = 0, so Omega_L(X) = 1 + GLR_CM(X - L) is a Cherny–Madan coherent acceptability index applied to the shifted position X - L, with dual sets D_x = {Q : ess sup(dQ/dP)/ess inf(dQ/dP) <= 1 + x} (GLR_CM = E X / E X^- convention), i.e. Omega is an acceptability index of the excess over the threshold.
      USE: Omega is 'RAROC with denominator = expected shortfall below L' and 1 + Omega-type ratios are translations of GLR. The loss ratio has the same 'ratio minus 1 = loading over premium' structure: LR - 1 = (rho - Pi)/Pi; the Kazemi-type decomposition suggests the intrinsic 'break-even' functional s(X) = rho(X) - Pi(X) (safety loading) and shows that the cash-shift profile of LR is a Mobius function of c (LR(X+c) - 1 = s(X)/(Pi(X)+c)), which is the key intrinsic axiom below.
  [11] Cherny, A. & Madan, D. (2009) Section 2 examples (RAROC, GLR, AIT, AIW, AIMIN/AIMAX/AIMINMAX/AIMAXMIN) and Madan, D. & Cherny, A. (2010), 'Markets as a counterparty: an introduction to conic finance', IJTAF 13(8), 1149-1177; Madan & Schoutens (2016) 'Applied Conic Finance' (CUP). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (From memory.) In Cherny–Madan 2009: (a) RAROC(X) := E[X]/rho(X) if E X > 0 and rho(X) > 0 (with the conventions RAROC = +infty if rho(X) <= 0 < E X, 0 if E X <= 0), rho coherent with representing set D, is a coherent acceptability index provided P in D (equivalently rho >= -E), with D_x = {(P + xQ)/(1 + x) : Q in D} (my verification: (P + xQ)/(1+x) = (P + yQ')/(1+y) with Q' a convex combination of P and Q, so nestedness needs D convex and P in D). (b) GLR is an index with D_x = {Q : ess sup/ess inf of dQ/dP <= 1 + x}. (c) Sharpe ratio is not an index (fails monotonicity). (d) Law-invariant indices built from increasing families of concave distortions Psi_x (AIMIN: Psi_x(y) = 1 - (1-y)^{1+x}; AIMAX: Psi_x(y) = y^{1/(1+x)}; AIMINMAX, AIMAXMIN compositions) via alpha(X) = sup{x : integral x dPsi_x(F_X(x)) >= 0}; AIT uses the TVaR family. Conic finance (Madan–Cherny 2010): for a coherent index with sets D_gamma, bid price b_gamma(X) = inf_{Q in D_gamma} E_Q X and ask a_gamma(X) = -b_gamma(-X) = sup_{Q in D_gamma} E_Q X; with law-invariant distortion indices b_gamma(X) = integral x dPsi_gamma(F_X(x)); a cash flow X is acceptable at level gamma iff b_gamma(X) >= 0, and the index of X is the largest gamma at which it is acceptable; ask - bid is the 'capital' and (expected value - bid)/(ask - bid) type ratios are used as performance/return-on-capital measures (Carr, Madan & Vicente Alvarez 2011, J. Risk).
      USE: RAROC is the closest cousin of 1/LR = Pi/rho (premium per unit of risk). The RAROC proof shows precisely why the LR family fails to be an increasing family of COHERENT risk measures: numerator Pi is not linear and (when Pi = C_g, g concave) not in the 'core' of rho, so the convex combination (Pi + x rho) is not a coherent risk measure in X. The conic-finance bid/ask with distortions is exactly the actuarial LR with rho = ask, Pi = bid: LR = a_gamma(X)/b_gamma'(X) is a ratio of distorted expectations at two stress levels, so Cherny–Madan's law-invariant distortion-family examples are literally loss ratios indexed by the stress level.


### Hirbod Assa (author) + prior-synthesis critic. Sources actually opened online: Han–Lin–Wang arXiv:2206.13679 (full PDF), Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang arXiv:1010.4339 (full PDF, restating Cherny–Madan Thm 1), Kountzakis–Rossello arXiv:1911.02261, Pitera–Rásonyi arXiv:2310.02014, Assa–Lin–Wang arXiv:2204.08882 (PELVE restatement of Li–Wang), Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei arXiv:2012.05219 (Thm 3), Wang–Wei–Willmot SSRN 2956962 (abstract) with its characterization restated in arXiv:2506.07472 and arXiv:2011.04889, Wang–Wang–Wei SSRN 3510363 (abstract), Assa SSRN 3880095 'Pooling and Systematic Risk' and SSRN 4584003 'Systematic risk in pools' (abstracts), OpenAlex bibliographic records for Assa (41 of 86 works visible; 'A note on loss ratio' is NOT indexed in OpenAlex/Crossref). Tooling limits: WebSearch is disabled for this organization (HTTP 403 on all 4 attempts); arxiv.org search/listing/API and semanticscholar are robots-disallowed; direct curl is policy-denied; OpenAlex rate-limited 3 of my queries (author search, Cherny–Madan query, Drapeau–Kupper query). Therefore Schmeidler 1986, Kusuoka 2001, Wang–Young–Panjer 1997, Drapeau–Kupper 2013 and Cheridito–Kromer 2013 are quoted from memory (medium confidence).
KEY INSIGHT: Re-derivation of F1–F7 (all checked, with two corrections). F1 holds (mediant inequality + comonotonic additivity; cash shift needs h(1)=g(1)=1). F2 holds and is even simpler than stated: 1_B ≤ 1_B+1_D ≤ 2·1_B with D ⊂ B gives, by monotonicity + scale invariance, h(q)/g(q) = h(p)/g(p) for all q ≤ p, so h/g is constant = h(1)/g(1) = 1 on (0,1], hence h = g and LR ≡ 1 (atomless space needed). F3 holds: Bernoulli losses give r = h/g; two-layer losses a + c·1_B give g(p) wherever r(p) ≠ 1, and nested layers 1_B + 1_D with r(q) ≠ 1 recover g(p) even where r(p) = 1; so (h,g) is unique unless r ≡ 1 — but ONLY within the normalized class h(1)=g(1)=1 (in the signed class, LR on nonnegative losses alone determines (h,g) up to a common scalar; the translation structure LR(X−m) pins the scalar). F4 CORRECTION: {LR ≤ β} = {ρ − βΠ ≤ 0}; if ρ convex (h concave) and Π concave (g convex) this is convex, so LR is quasi-convex ('conic picture', correct). But in the 'actuarial picture' ρ = E, g concave, Π = C_g is a CONVEX functional, so E − βΠ is concave and {E − βΠ ≤ 0} is a superlevel set of a convex function — not convex in general; instead {LR ≥ β} = {E − βΠ ≥ 0} is convex, i.e. LR is quasi-CONCAVE (counterexample to quasi-convexity: X,Y with E = βES on each and ES((X+Y)/2) < (ES X + ES Y)/2 gives LR((X+Y)/2) > β). F5 holds (cones {xρ − Π ≤ 0}, convex iff xρ − Π convex, decreasing in x, non-monotone). F6: V(β,X) = ρ_β(X) is an 'identification functional' (zero-crossing map) but NOT a Nolde–Ziegel identification function unless ρ and Π are expectations, since ρ_β(X) is not E[V(β,X)] for a pointwise V; the claim should be downgraded. F7 holds by linearity of X ↦ C_h(X) in h (h − βg has bounded variation), and WWW2020 (law-invariant + comonotonic-additive + uniformly ‖·‖∞-continuous ⟺ signed Choquet integral) characterizes exactly such non-monotone members. Sign convention in Prop 1: with X − m one gets m = ρ_β(X) (the stated m = −ρ_β(X) corresponds to X + m). KEY STRUCTURAL INSIGHT: (1) The monotonicity obstruction (F2) is not a defect to be fixed but a classification fact: performance ratios whose numerator and denominator move in opposite directions (gain–loss, Rachev, reward–risk) are Cherny–Madan coherent indices; the loss ratio's numerator and denominator both increase in the loss, so LR can never be monotone, and only quasi-convexity (conic pair) or quasi-concavity (actuarial pair) survive. (2) The canonical family has monotone ENDPOINTS: ρ_0 = ρ and ρ_∞ := lim_{β→∞}ρ_β = Π (since (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) → Π); all intermediate members are signed Choquet integrals. So the right strategy is: characterize the two endpoints by monotone theorems (Schmeidler/Kusuoka/Wang–Young–Panjer) or all members by WWW2020, and tie the family together by an 'affine-in-β' axiom. (3) Both endpoints are INTRINSIC to LR through translation: define the β-capital m_β(X) := the unique m with LR(X − m) = β; then Assa's Prop 1 gives m_β = ρ_β, and the whole family {ρ_β} — not just its zero sets — is recovered from LR. The requirement that m ↦ LR(X − m) be a Möbius map (a_X − m)/(b_X − m) with unit-slope numerator and denominator is equivalent to cash-additivity of both ρ and Π and already encodes the affine-in-β structure, ρ(X)=a_X, Π(X)=b_X, LR = a/b. Hence the axioms can be stated on LR alone (translation, scale, law invariance, comonotonic additivity and continuity of the β-capitals), and the deep step is WWW2020 applied to a and b. (4) The literature search (OpenAlex; arXiv full texts of Han–Lin–Wang, Bellini et al., Assa–Lin–Wang; SSRN abstracts; Cherny–Madan via BCZ) found NO existing iff characterization of a ratio of two Choquet integrals or two coherent risk measures; the closest templates are the diversification-quotient Theorem 1 (index = infimum level at which a decreasing parametric family crosses a threshold, axiomatized relative to one anchor risk measure) and Bellini–Fadina–Wang–Wei Theorem 3 (a difference of Choquet integrals characterized via comonotonic additivity + relevance, with uniform continuity derived rather than assumed). PELVE/generalized PELVE papers give existence/uniqueness only. (5) The degenerate set N = {X : LR(X − ·) ≡ 1} (= {C_h(X) = C_g(X)}) must be handled explicitly (as the set {VaR_ε < E} is for PELVE); on N the pair (a_X,b_X) is defined by continuity, which needs N to have empty interior.
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (author's strand). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, X = L^∞, and LR: X → [−∞,∞] a map (the extended loss ratio; losses of interest are X ≥ 0 but LR must be defined on translates X − m). For β ∈ [0,∞)∖{1} and X ∈ X let the β-capital be m_β(X) := the unique m ∈ R with LR(X − m) = β, whenever it exists. Consider the axioms, all stated on LR: (A0) [non-degeneracy] the set N := {X : LR(X − m) = 1 for all m} has empty interior in (X, ‖·‖∞); (A1) [Möbius translation] for every X ∉ N there are a_X ≠ b_X ∈ R such that LR(X − m) = (a_X − m)/(b_X − m) for all m ≠ b_X (so m_β(X) = (a_X − β b_X)/(1 − β) exists and is unique for every β ≠ 1); (A2) [scale invariance] LR(λX) = LR(X) for all λ > 0; (A3) [law invariance] LR(X) = LR(Y) whenever X and Y have the same distribution; (A4) [comonotonic additivity of capital] m_β(X + Y) = m_β(X) + m_β(Y) for all comonotone X, Y ∉ N with X+Y ∉ N, for β = 0 and for one β_1 ∈ (0,∞)∖{1} (equivalently for all β); (A5) [continuity] X ↦ m_0(X) and X ↦ m_{β_1}(X) are uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖∞ on X∖N. THEN: LR satisfies (A0)–(A5) IF AND ONLY IF there exist functions h, g: [0,1] → R of bounded variation with h(0) = g(0) = 0 and h(1) = g(1) = 1 such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) for all X with C_g(X) ≠ 0, where C_h, C_g are signed Choquet integrals (C_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{−∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − 1)dx); the pair (h,g) is uniquely determined by LR; a_X = C_h(X) = ρ(X), b_X = C_g(X) = Π(X), N = {C_h = C_g}; and the β-capitals are exactly Assa's canonical family, m_β = ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = C_{(h−βg)/(1−β)}, so that LR and {ρ_β} are in association (LR(X) = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0) with ρ_0 = ρ and lim_{β→∞} ρ_β = Π. ADD-ONS (each an iff on the endpoints, none on LR itself): m_0 is monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing (ρ a distortion risk measure); m_∞ := lim_{β→∞} m_β is monotone ⟺ g nondecreasing (Π a Wang–Young–Panjer premium); m_0 (resp. m_∞) subadditive ⟺ h (resp. g) concave (coherent pair); and then LR is quasi-convex if h concave and g convex, quasi-concave if h convex and g concave, and LR is pointwise monotone only in the trivial case h = g, LR ≡ 1. PROOF ROUTE: (⟸) F1, F7 and the Möbius identity (C_h(X) − m)/(C_g(X) − m) by cash-additivity. (⟹) By (A1) the maps a := m_0 and b := m_∞ = lim_β m_β are well defined on X∖N and extended to N by (A0)+(A5); (A1) gives cash-additivity a(X+c) = a(X)+c, b(X+c) = b(X)+c; (A2) with uniqueness of the Möbius representation gives positive homogeneity of a and b; (A3) gives law invariance; (A4) at two β-values gives comonotonic additivity of a and b; (A5) gives uniform continuity; WWW2020 then yields a = C_h, b = C_g with BV h, g, h(0)=g(0)=0, and cash-additivity forces h(1)=g(1)=1; finally LR(X) = LR(X − 0) = a_X/b_X. What is needed on LR alone: (A0)–(A5). What is needed on the pair (ρ,Π) beyond this: nothing for the signed-Choquet class; monotonicity/concavity of h and g are exactly the monotonicity/subadditivity of the two endpoint capitals m_0 and m_∞ and cannot be replaced by any property of LR itself (F2).
THEOREMS:
  [1] Wang, R., Wei, Y., Willmot, G.E. (2020). Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals. Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993–1015, DOI 10.1287/moor.2019.1020; SSRN 2956962. Restated as the characterization of 'distortion riskmetrics' in Wang–Wang–Wei (ASTIN Bull. 2020, SSRN 3510363) and in arXiv:2506.07472 §2.1 and arXiv:2011.04889 §2.1 (both read online). (confidence: high (restatement read online in arXiv:2506.07472 and arXiv:2011.04889; original MOR text not opened))
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,F,P), X = L^∞. For h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation with h(0)=0 define the signed Choquet integral I_h(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx + ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1))dx. THEOREM: a functional ρ: L^∞ → R is a signed Choquet integral ρ = I_h for some BV h with h(0)=0  ⟺  ρ is (LI) law-invariant, (CA) comonotonic-additive [ρ(X+Y)=ρ(X)+ρ(Y) whenever X,Y comonotone], and (UNC) uniformly continuous w.r.t. ‖·‖_∞. No monotonicity is assumed. Companion facts (Wang–Wang–Wei 2020, Lemma 1/Thm): I_h is positively homogeneous; I_h is convex ⟺ h concave; I_h is monotone ⟺ h nondecreasing (trivial via X=1_B); −I_h = I_{−h}; I_{h_1} − I_{h_2} = I_{h_1−h_2}; if h left-continuous, I_h(X)=∫_0^1 VaR_{1−t}(X) dh(t).
      USE: This is THE tool for F2/F7: the canonical family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) = I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} is a non-monotone signed Choquet integral, and WWW2020 characterizes exactly such objects without monotonicity. Hence an iff theorem for LR can be proved by applying WWW2020 to the numerator a = ρ_0 and the denominator b = ρ_∞ := lim_{β→∞} ρ_β (= Π), or to every member ρ_β, with hypotheses LI + CA + UNC stated on the β-capitals m_β(X) (the cash solving LR(X−m)=β), which are intrinsic to LR.
  [2] Han, X., Lin, L., Wang, R. (2022/2025). Diversification quotients: Quantifying diversification via risk measures. arXiv:2206.13679 (full PDF read), Definition 1, Theorems 1–2 (published in Management Science). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless probability space, X = L^∞, n-dimensional portfolios X ∈ X^n. Let ρ = (ρ_α)_{α∈I}, I=(0,ᾱ), be a class of risk measures DECREASING in α. DQ^ρ_α(X) := α*/α, α* := inf{β ∈ I : ρ_β(Σ_i X_i) ≤ Σ_i ρ_α(X_i)}, inf∅ = ᾱ. Axioms on an index D: X^n → R̄: [+] D ≥ 0; [LI] D(X+c)=D(X) ∀c∈R^n; [SI] D(λX)=D(X) ∀λ>0; [R]_φ rationality: D(X) ≤ D(Y) whenever X,Y are φ-marginally equivalent and ΣX_i ≤ ΣY_i; [N]_φ normalization: D(0)=0, D(X) ≤ 1 if X is duplicate, D(X) ≥ 1 if X is worse than duplicate; [C]_φ continuity: (D(X) − D(Y^k))_+ → 0 along suitable L^∞-convergence. THEOREM 1: D satisfies [+],[LI],[SI],[R]_φ,[N]_φ,[C]_φ for some MCP (monotone, constant-additive, positively homogeneous) risk measure φ  ⟺  D = DQ^ρ_α for some α and some decreasing class ρ of MCP risk measures (and one may take ρ_α = φ in both directions). No continuity in α is needed. THEOREM 2: if n ≥ 4 and φ is a non-linear coherent risk measure, adding [PC] portfolio convexity ({w ∈ Δ_n : D(w⊙X) ≤ d} convex) characterizes DQ with a decreasing class of COHERENT risk measures and ρ_α = φ.
      USE: Structural template for the loss ratio: LR(X) = inf{β : ρ − βΠ ≤ 0} = inf{β : ρ̃_β(X) ≤ 0} with ρ̃_β := ρ − βΠ decreasing in β (Π ≥ 0), exactly the α* construction. The axiom scheme (scale/location invariance + rationality + normalization relative to an anchor functional φ + semicontinuity) shows how an iff theorem for an index defined through a parametric family can be obtained with hypotheses on the index plus ONE anchor; for LR the anchor can be the denominator Π (or the break-even capital), avoiding any monotonicity requirement on the intermediate members.
  [3] Cherny, A., Madan, D. (2009). New measures for performance evaluation. Review of Financial Studies 22(7), 2571–2606, Theorem 1; restated verbatim as Theorem 2.2 in Bielecki–Cialenco–Zhang, arXiv:1010.4339 (read online; Math. Finance 2014) and as the 'analogue' in Kountzakis–Rossello arXiv:1911.02261 (read). (confidence: high (restatement read online; original RFS text not opened))
      STATEMENT: Setting (BCZ restatement): L^∞(Ω,F,P), standing assumption Ω finite with P strictly positive (Cherny–Madan's original is on L^∞ of a general space with Fatou property and weak*-closed D_x). α: L^∞ → [0,+∞] is a coherent acceptability index if (S1) monotone: X ≤ Y ⟹ α(X) ≤ α(Y); (S2) scale invariant: α(λX)=α(X), λ>0; (S3) quasi-concave: α(X) ≥ x, α(Y) ≥ x ⟹ α(λX+(1−λ)Y) ≥ x; (S4) Fatou: |X_n| ≤ 1, α(X_n) ≥ x, X_n → X in probability ⟹ α(X) ≥ x. THEOREM: α: L^∞ → [0,∞], unbounded from above, is a coherent acceptability index ⟺ there is a family (D_x)_{x∈[0,∞]} of (closed convex) sets of probability measures, increasing in x (D_x ⊂ D_y for x ≤ y), such that α(X) = sup{x ∈ [0,∞) : inf_{Q∈D_x} E_Q[X] ≥ 0} (inf∅=∞, sup∅=0); equivalently α(X) = sup{x : ρ_x(X) ≤ 0} for an increasing family of coherent risk measures ρ_x = −inf_{Q∈D_x}E_Q.
      USE: F5 writes 1/LR = sup{x : xρ − Π ≤ 0} with family σ_x = xρ − Π, increasing in x, each a signed Choquet integral with distortion xh − g; σ_x is monotone iff xh − g is nondecreasing, which fails for the x-range needed (F2). Hence Cherny–Madan does NOT apply verbatim; what survives is the pure level-set part (quasi-concavity + scale invariance + usc ⟺ sup over a decreasing family of closed convex cones), with monotonicity of α ⟺ monotone cones dropped. Also shows why the gain–loss ratio E[X^+]/E[X^-] IS a coherent index: its two functionals have opposite monotonicity, unlike ρ and Π.
  [4] Schmeidler, D. (1986). Integral representation without additivity. Proc. Amer. Math. Soc. 97(2), 255–261, Theorem (main). (From memory; standard.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let B be the space of bounded F-measurable real functions on (Ω,F) (sup norm) and I: B → R with I(1_Ω) = 1. THEOREM: I is monotone (X ≤ Y ⟹ I(X) ≤ I(Y)) and comonotonically additive (I(X+Y)=I(X)+I(Y) for comonotone X,Y)  ⟺  there is a (unique) monotone set function v on F with v(∅)=0, v(Ω)=1, v(A)=I(1_A), such that I(X) = ∫_0^∞ v(X>t)dt + ∫_{-∞}^0 (v(X>t) − 1)dt (Choquet integral). Adding law invariance on an atomless space gives v = h∘P with h:[0,1]→[0,1] nondecreasing, h(0)=0, h(1)=1, i.e. I = C_h (distortion functional).
      USE: Gives the monotone endpoints of the canonical family: ρ = ρ_0 and Π = ρ_∞ are monotone comonotonic-additive law-invariant ⟺ ρ = C_h, Π = C_g with h,g nondecreasing distortion functions. Combined with WWW2020 for the signed intermediate members, it delivers the 'exact class' in the conclusion LR = C_h/C_g. Monotonicity hypotheses must be placed on the endpoints a = m_0, b = m_∞ (intrinsic to LR via translation), never on LR itself (F2).
  [5] Kusuoka, S. (2001). On law invariant coherent risk measures. Advances in Mathematical Economics 3, 83–95, Theorems 4 and 7; Föllmer–Schied, Stochastic Finance, Thm 4.93 (comonotonic case); Jouini–Schachermayer–Touzi (2006), Law invariant risk measures have the Fatou property, Adv. Math. Econ. 9 (automatic Fatou). (From memory.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: standard atomless (Ω,F,P), X = L^∞. THEOREM (Kusuoka): ρ: L^∞ → R is a law-invariant coherent risk measure with the Fatou property ⟺ ρ(X) = sup_{μ∈M} ∫_{(0,1]} ES_s(X) μ(ds) for some set M of probability measures on (0,1] (JST 2006: on a standard atomless space the Fatou property is automatic for law-invariant convex ρ, so it may be dropped). THEOREM (Kusuoka Thm 7 / FS 4.93): ρ is law-invariant, coherent AND comonotonic-additive ⟺ ρ(X) = ∫_{(0,1]} ES_s(X) μ(ds) for a single μ ⟺ ρ = C_h with h concave, nondecreasing, h(0)=0, h(1)=1 (spectral/concave distortion risk measure).
      USE: Identifies precisely which additional axioms on the endpoints pin the 'coherent/concave distortion pair' subclass: a (= numerator) subadditive ⟺ h concave; b (= Π) subadditive ⟺ g concave. For the quasi-convexity question (F4) it shows the 'conic picture' (h concave, g convex) gives all ρ − βΠ = C_{h−βg} convex, hence LR quasi-convex; NOTE the correction below: the 'actuarial picture' ρ = E, g concave gives E − βC_g CONCAVE, hence LR quasi-CONCAVE (not quasi-convex) — F4's second clause as stated is wrong.
  [6] Wang, S.S., Young, V.R., Panjer, H.H. (1997). Axiomatic characterization of insurance prices. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 21(2), 173–183, Theorems 2–3. (From memory; could not open online.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let X be the set of nonnegative bounded risks on an atomless space and H: X → [0,∞] a premium principle. Axioms: (A1) conditional state independence / law invariance; (A2) monotonicity w.r.t. first-order stochastic dominance (X ≤_st Y ⟹ H(X) ≤ H(Y)); (A3) comonotonic additivity; (A4) continuity: lim_{d→0}H((X−d)_+) = H(X), lim_{d→∞}H(min(X,d)) = H(X); (A5) H(1)=1 normalization. THEOREM: H satisfies (A1)–(A5) ⟺ H(X) = ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t))dt = C_g(X) for a nondecreasing g:[0,1]→[0,1], g(0)=0, g(1)=1. Moreover H is subadditive (⟺ H(X) ≥ E[X] for all X, under the above) ⟺ g concave; H is additive for independent risks (and all the above) ⟺ g(p)=p.
      USE: This is the actuarial-side axiomatization of the denominator Π = C_g (and, read with risk instead of price, of the numerator ρ = C_h). Its continuity axiom (layer continuity) is weaker than UNC and is phrased on nonnegative losses only, matching Assa's domain X ≥ 0; it suggests the LR-level continuity axiom 'LR(min(X,d)) → LR(X), LR((X−d)_+) → LR(X)' which, combined with comonotonic-additivity of the β-capitals, suffices for the endpoints.
  [7] Bellini, F., Fadina, T., Wang, R., Wei, Y. (2022). Parametric measures of variability induced by risk measures. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 106, 270–284; arXiv:2012.05219, Definition 1 and Theorem 3 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless space, X = L^0, ν: X → [0,∞]. Variability measure: (A1) law invariance; (A2) standardization ν(m)=0 ∀m∈R; (A3) positive homogeneity ν(λX)=λ^α ν(X). Conditions: (B1) relevance: ν(X) > 0 if X non-constant and ν bounded on {|X| ≤ 1}; (B2) truncation continuity ν((X∧M)∨(−M)) → ν(X); (B3) symmetry ν(X)=ν(−X); (B4) comonotonic additivity; (B5) cx-consistency; (B6) convexity; (B7) mixture concavity; (B8) location invariance. THEOREM 3: TFAE: (i) ν satisfies (B1)–(B8); (ii) ν satisfies (B1)–(B4) and one of (B5)–(B6); (iii) ν is a mixture of inter-ES differences: ν(X) = ∫_0^1 Δ^ES_p(X) μ(dp), Δ^ES_p(X) = ES_p(X) − ES_p(−X) ... (inter-ES difference), for a finite Borel measure μ ≠ 0 on (0,1] (μ not unique; may be taken supported on [1/2,1]). Uniform ‖·‖_∞-continuity is DERIVED from (B1)+subadditivity, not assumed.
      USE: A worked example of an iff theorem for a functional that is a DIFFERENCE of two Choquet integrals (hence non-monotone), proved by reducing to WWW2020/WWW-Wang–Wang–Wei representations. The exact same route works for ρ_β = (C_h − βC_g)/(1−β): the pattern 'comonotonic additivity + a mild boundedness/relevance condition ⟹ uniform continuity ⟹ signed Choquet representation' can be copied to avoid assuming UNC directly on LR.
  [8] Li, H., Wang, R. (2023). PELVE: Probability equivalent level of VaR and ES. Journal of Econometrics 234(1), 353–370, Theorem 1; restated with proofs of existence in Assa, H., Lin, L., Wang, R. (2023), Calibrating distribution models from PELVE, NAAJ, arXiv:2204.08882 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,F,P), X ∈ L^1, ε ∈ (0,1) (small-α convention). PELVE: Π_X(ε) := inf{c ∈ [1, 1/ε] : ES_{cε}(X) ≤ VaR_ε(X)}, inf∅ = ∞. Facts: Π_X(ε) < ∞ ⟺ VaR_ε(X) ≥ E[X]; if VaR_p(X) is non-constant on (0,ε] then Π_X(ε) is the unique solution of ES_{cε}(X) = VaR_ε(X); (Li–Wang Thm 1) PELVE is location-scale invariant: Π_{aX+b} = Π_X for a>0, b∈R; Assa–Lin–Wang Prop. 4/5 and Thm 3 give monotonicity of ε ↦ Π_X(ε) under convexity/concavity of 1/hazard-rate, and Example 5 shows lim_{ε→0}Π_X(ε) need not exist. No axiomatic (iff) characterization of PELVE as a functional is given in either paper.
      USE: PELVE is the 'level-equivalence' cousin of the loss ratio: both are defined by an association 'index = β ⟺ ρ_β(X) = 0' with ρ_β := ES_{βε} − VaR_ε a signed Choquet integral (distortion min(1,p/(βε)) − 1_{p>ε}... non-monotone), decreasing in β. The literature (Li–Wang; Assa–Lin–Wang; Fiori–Rosazza Gianin 'generalized PELVE'; Barczy et al.) treats existence/uniqueness/monotonicity but gives NO iff theorem — confirming that an axiomatic characterization of a ratio/level index built from two same-direction-monotone Choquet integrals is open. Also shows the degenerate set (VaR_ε(X) < E[X] ⟹ PELVE = ∞) must be handled explicitly, exactly as {Π(X)=0} or {LR(X−·) ≡ 1} for LR.
  [9] Drapeau, S., Kupper, M. (2013). Risk preferences and their robust representation. Mathematics of Operations Research 38(1), 28–62, §1.2 (risk acceptance families) and Theorem 1.7/Prop. 1.5. (From memory; OpenAlex lookup was rate-limited.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let X be a locally convex topological vector space (e.g. L^∞ with weak* or norm topology). A family (A^m)_{m∈R} of subsets of X is a risk acceptance family if each A^m is convex and closed, A^m ⊂ A^n for m ≤ n, and ∩_{n>m}A^n = A^m (right-continuity). THEOREM: ρ: X → [−∞,∞] is quasiconvex and lower semicontinuous ⟺ ρ(X) = inf{m ∈ R : X ∈ A^m} for a unique risk acceptance family (A^m = {ρ ≤ m}); ρ is in addition monotone ⟺ each A^m is monotone (A^m + X_+ ⊂ A^m); ρ positively homogeneous ⟺ each A^m is a cone. (Dual robust representation ρ(X) = sup_Q R(Q, E_Q[−X]) then follows for monotone ρ.)
      USE: Gives the monotonicity-free level-set calculus needed for F5/F6: LR quasi-convex + usc ⟺ LR = inf{β : X ∈ A^β} with A^β = {ρ − βΠ ≤ 0} decreasing right-continuous closed convex cones; LR is NOT monotone, so the A^β are non-monotone cones, consistent with F2. This separates what is 'free' (level-set/association representation, needing only quasi-convexity and usc) from what is deep (comonotonic additivity ⟹ Choquet structure of each A^β's defining functional).
  [10] Cheridito, P., Kromer, E. (2013). Reward-risk ratios. Journal of Investment Strategies 3(1), 3–18. (From memory; the only paper I know that studies axioms of a RATIO of two risk-type functionals; search for 'ratio of Choquet integrals', 'quotient of coherent risk measures', 'distortion ratio', 'combined ratio risk measure' through OpenAlex returned nothing.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: X ⊂ L^0 a convex cone of returns, reward functional r: X → R (e.g. E[X]) and risk/deviation functional ρ: X → [0,∞]. Reward–risk ratio RR(X) = r(X)/ρ(X) on {ρ>0}. PROPOSITION-type results (not iff): if r is concave, positively homogeneous and ρ is convex, positively homogeneous, then RR is quasi-concave and scale invariant; if in addition r is monotone and ρ is 'monotone in the opposite direction' (e.g. deviation measures are not monotone), RR is monotone; distribution invariance of r,ρ gives distribution invariance of RR. Examples: Sharpe, Sortino, Gain–Loss (Bernardo–Ledoit), Rachev ratio ES_a(−X)/ES_b(X), Farinelli–Tibiletti ratios. No characterization (iff) theorem is proved.
      USE: Confirms the structural dichotomy: all classical performance ratios have numerator and denominator of OPPOSITE monotonicity (reward up / risk down), which is why Cherny–Madan's monotone-family theorem covers them, whereas LR = ρ/Π has both functionals increasing in the loss, so monotonicity of LR is impossible (F2) and only quasi-convexity/quasi-concavity statements survive. It also pins the correct direction: ρ convex & Π concave ⟹ LR quasi-convex; ρ concave & Π convex ⟹ LR quasi-concave.


### Decision-theory / functional-equation scholar (Aczél, Chew–Dekel betweenness, Fishburn SSB, Yaari, Schmeidler, signed Choquet). NOTE ON SOURCING: WebSearch is disabled in this environment (proxy 403) and almost all publisher/search hosts (JSTOR, Springer, Elsevier, SSRN full text, INFORMS, Google, DuckDuckGo, Semantic Scholar) are blocked; only arXiv abstracts/PDFs, Crossref metadata, Wikipedia and the AMS journal page could be opened. Statements marked 'high' were read online; the classical decision-theory theorems (Chew 1983, Dekel 1986, Fishburn 1983, Yaari 1987, Schmeidler 1989) are reconstructed from memory with Crossref-verified citations and are marked 'medium'.
KEY INSIGHT: The loss ratio LR = rho/Pi is, on every comonotone cone (where Choquet-type functionals are linear), a RATIO OF TWO LINEAR FUNCTIONALS — exactly Chew's weighted utility / Fishburn's transitive SSB utility, with the comonotone mediant (intermediate value) property of F1 being precisely the BETWEENNESS axiom. The decision-theory literature then gives a two-level dictionary: (a) Dekel's betweenness theorem (level sets are hyperplanes, V(F)=v iff ∫φ(x,v)dF=0) corresponds one-to-one to Assa's ABSTRACT association 'LR(X)=beta iff rho_beta(X)=0' with a family of comonotone-additive (signed Choquet) functionals; (b) Chew's weak-substitution axiom (the hyperplanes 'fan out' from a common point, i.e. are affine in the level) corresponds to the CANONICAL family rho_beta proportional to rho − beta*Pi, i.e. LR = rho/Pi. Crucially, the betweenness-type theorems (Chew 1983, Dekel 1986, Fishburn 1982/83) do NOT need monotonicity — FSD/statewise monotonicity in those theorems only serves to make the utility increasing — and Schmeidler's 1986 abstract (comonotonic additivity + norm continuity ⇔ Choquet-type integral, no monotonicity) together with Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 provide the matching representation class of SIGNED Choquet integrals; thus the obstruction F2 (rho_beta = rho − beta*Pi is not monotone, so Cherny–Madan/DQ-type theorems with rationality/monotonicity axioms cannot be used) disappears once the target class is 'pairs of signed Choquet integrals with positive denominator'. The remaining gap in the classical theorems is that they are ORDINAL (LR = psi(rho/Pi)) with projective (linear-fractional) non-uniqueness of (rho,Pi) on each cone; this is closed by a CARDINAL functional-equation axiom in Aczél's style: the mediant weights revealed by LR(X+Y) = w*LR(X)+(1−w)*LR(Y) must have ratio-consistent (Sincov) and additive odds, which forces w = Pi(X)/(Pi(X)+Pi(Y)) for a comonotone-additive Pi and makes rho := LR*Pi comonotone-additive, yielding LR = rho/Pi on the nose and gluing all comonotone cones through the constant 1. What must be assumed on the PAIR rather than on LR: monotonicity (h, g nondecreasing), equivalently monotonicity of the associated family only at the endpoints beta=0 (rho) and beta→∞ (Pi); and the positivity Pi>0 (g>0 on (0,1]).
CANDIDATE IFF: THEOREM (candidate; proof sketched via Aczél/Sincov + Schmeidler–WWW). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, X := L^∞_+ \ {0}, and LR: X → R. For comonotone X,Y with LR(X) ≠ LR(Y) define the mediant weight w(X,Y) ∈ (0,1) by LR(X+Y) = w LR(X) + (1−w) LR(Y) and the odds ω(X,Y) := w/(1−w). Consider the axioms: (L1) scale invariance: LR(λX)=LR(X) for λ>0; (L2) break-even normalization: LR(1)=1; (L3) strict comonotone mediant property: for comonotone X,Y, LR(X)<LR(Y) ⇒ LR(X) < LR(X+Y) < LR(Y), and LR(X)=LR(Y) ⇒ LR(X+Y)=LR(X); (L4) ratio-consistency of mediant odds: ω(X,Y)·ω(Y,Z) = ω(X,Z) and ω(X+Y,Z) = ω(X,Z)+ω(Y,Z) whenever X,Y,Z are pairwise comonotone and the odds involved are defined; (L5) law invariance; (L6) continuity of LR with respect to the sup-norm; (L7) non-degeneracy: for every X there is Y comonotone with X such that LR(Y) ≠ LR(X). THEN: LR satisfies (L1)–(L7) IF AND ONLY IF there exist functions h, g: [0,1] → R of bounded variation with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1 and g>0 on (0,1], such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) for all X ∈ X, where C_h(X)=∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx and C_g are (signed) Choquet integrals; the pair (h,g) is unique. [Proof of '⇐': comonotone additivity and positive homogeneity of signed Choquet integrals give (L1),(L3),(L4) with ω(X,Y)=C_g(X)/C_g(Y); C_g>0 on X because g>0 on (0,1]; (L5),(L6) from law invariance and ‖h‖_TV-Lipschitz continuity; (L2),(L7) trivial. Proof of '⇒': put Π(X):=ω(X,1) (1 is comonotone with every X; defined when LR(X)≠1, extended via (L7),(L4),(L6) elsewhere); (L4) with Z=1 gives ω(X,Y)=Π(X)/Π(Y) and Π(X+Y)=Π(X)+Π(Y) on comonotone pairs (Sincov + Cauchy); (L1),(L6) give positive homogeneity; ρ:=LR·Π is comonotone-additive by the mediant identity; ρ,Π are law-invariant and norm-continuous, hence signed Choquet integrals by Schmeidler (1986, norm-continuous version)/Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020, Thm 1); (L2) normalizes h(1)=g(1)=1, and Π>0 forces g>0 on (0,1].] ADDENDA stated on the pair, not derivable from LR alone (by F2): (i) h and g nondecreasing (ρ,Π monotone) ⇔ the associated family ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) is monotone at β=0 and as β→∞; (ii) h concave and g convex ⇔ the associated family is convex for all β<1 (Wang–Wei–Willmot convexity theorem), which yields quasi-convexity of LR (F4). WEAKER 'ABSTRACT' VERSION (Dekel-type): replacing (L4) by nothing and (L3) by the non-strict betweenness, (L1),(L3),(L5)–(L7) hold iff LR is in association (LR(X)=β ⇔ ρ_β(X)=0) with a family (ρ_β) of signed Choquet integrals, strictly decreasing in β on each level set; adding (L4) is exactly what makes the family affine in β, i.e. canonical.
THEOREMS:
  [1] Chew, S. H. (1983). A generalization of the quasilinear mean with applications to the measurement of income inequality and decision theory resolving the Allais paradox. Econometrica 51(4), 1065–1092. DOI 10.2307/1912052 (citation verified via Crossref). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let D be a convex set of probability distributions on an interval [a,b] (containing the degenerate ones) and M: D → [a,b] a 'mean' (equivalently a weak order ≿ on D represented by M). Chew's axioms: (C1) consistency M(δ_x)=x (and FSD-monotonicity in the preference version); (C2) betweenness: M(F)=M(G) ⇒ M(αF+(1−α)G)=M(F) ∀α∈[0,1] (strict form: M(F)<M(G) ⇒ M(F)<M(αF+(1−α)G)<M(G), α∈(0,1)); (C3) WEAK SUBSTITUTION (ratio substitution): for all F,G with M(F)=M(G) and all α∈(0,1) there is β∈(0,1) such that M(αF+(1−α)H)=M(βG+(1−β)H) for ALL H∈D; (C4) continuity (weak topology / mixture continuity: {α: αF+(1−α)G ≿ H} closed). THEOREM: (C1)–(C4) hold IF AND ONLY IF there exist a continuous strictly increasing φ:[a,b]→R and a continuous w:[a,b]→(0,∞) such that M(F) = φ^{-1}( ∫φ(x)w(x)dF(x) / ∫w(x)dF(x) ) ('weighted quasilinear mean'); in preference form F ≿ G ⇔ U(F):=∫u w dF/∫w dF ≥ U(G) with u=φ (weighted utility). Uniqueness: w unique up to a positive multiplicative constant, u unique up to positive affine transformation given w; jointly (u,w) ↦ (u', w') with u'w' = a·uw + b·w, w' = c·uw + d·w (projective/linear-fractional freedom preserving w'>0).
      USE: This is THE 'ratio of two linear functionals' representation theorem. Translation to losses: fix a comonotone cone C_U = {f(U): f nondecreasing bounded} (on which every Choquet/signed-Choquet integral is linear) and a convex base B of C_U (e.g. {X∈C_U: E X = 1}); scale invariance of LR makes LR a function on B; the comonotone mediant property (F1) is exactly betweenness (C2) on B; Chew's weak substitution translates to: if LR(X)=LR(Y) (X,Y comonotone) there is λ>0 with LR(X+Z)=LR(λY+Z) for all Z comonotone with X,Y (in the ratio model λ=Π(X)/Π(Y)). Then LR|_B = φ(ρ/Π) with ρ,Π linear on C_U. The decisive point for F2: Chew's representation does NOT need monotonicity of the numerator functional — FSD-monotonicity is only used to make u increasing; dropping it gives exactly a non-monotone numerator ρ − βΠ. Limitation: the representation is ordinal (φ unknown) and the pair (ρ,Π) is only projectively unique per cone; a cardinal axiom (see Aczél/Sincov entry) is needed to make LR=ρ/Π on the nose and glue across cones.
  [2] Dekel, E. (1986). An axiomatic characterization of preferences under uncertainty: Weakening the independence axiom. Journal of Economic Theory 40(2), 304–318. DOI 10.1016/0022-0531(86)90076-1 (citation verified via Crossref). See also Chew, S. H. (1989), Axiomatic utility theories with the betweenness property, Annals of Operations Research 19, 273–298. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let X=[a,b] compact and D(X) the (convex) set of probability measures on X with the weak topology. Axioms on ≿ ⊂ D×D: (D1) weak order; (D2) continuity: {F: F ≿ G} and {F: G ≿ F} are closed; (D3) BETWEENNESS: F ≻ G ⇒ F ≻ αF+(1−α)G ≻ G for all α∈(0,1) (equivalently, indifference sets are convex and so are strict upper and lower contour sets: indifference curves are hyperplane sections of the simplex); (D4) FSD monotonicity. THEOREM: (D1)–(D4) hold IF AND ONLY IF there is a continuous V: D(X)→[0,1] representing ≿ which is defined IMPLICITLY by ∫ u(x, V(F)) dF(x) = V(F), where u: X×[0,1]→[0,1] is continuous, nondecreasing in x, with u(x,·) such that the fixed point is unique (equivalently: V(F)=v ⇔ ∫φ(x,v)dF(x)=0 for a family φ(·,v) that is strictly 'monotone in v' in the sense that ∫φ(x,v)dF is decreasing in v) ('implicit weighted/expected utility'). Chew (1989) shows that adding weak substitution (previous entry) reduces φ(x,v)=w(x)(u(x)−v), i.e. the family of hyperplanes is AFFINE in the level v, which is exactly weighted utility u w/w.
      USE: Dekel's theorem is literally Assa's ABSTRACT ASSOCIATION: 'V(F)=v ⇔ ρ_v(F):=∫φ(x,v)dF=0' with ρ_v LINEAR in F for each level v. Translated to losses on a comonotone cone (with scale invariance putting all hyperplanes through 0): LR satisfies betweenness(mediant)+continuity+scale invariance ⇔ LR is in association with a family {ρ_β} of functionals that are additive on comonotone pairs (signed Choquet integrals after law invariance and norm continuity, by Schmeidler 1986 / Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020). The passage from Dekel's general family to Chew's canonical affine-in-β family ρ_β ∝ ρ − βΠ is exactly the weak-substitution ('fanning') axiom. This clarifies what is intrinsic: betweenness gives the association structure; weak substitution gives the canonical family. No monotonicity of ρ_β is used anywhere; (D4) only yields monotone distortions and is precisely what must be dropped because of F2.
  [3] Fishburn, P. C. (1982). Nontransitive measurable utility. Journal of Mathematical Psychology 26, 31–67; Fishburn, P. C. (1983). Transitive measurable utility. Journal of Economic Theory 31(2), 293–317. DOI 10.1016/0022-0531(83)90079-0 (1983 citation verified via Crossref). Monograph: Fishburn (1988), Nonlinear Preference and Utility Theory, Johns Hopkins UP. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let P be a nonempty convex subset of a real linear space (e.g. a convex set of probability measures; only the mixture structure is used — no topology on outcomes and NO monotonicity/dominance axiom). SSB axioms for ≻ on P: (C1) continuity: for all p,q,r, {λ∈[0,1]: λp+(1−λ)q ≻ r} and {λ: r ≻ λp+(1−λ)q} are open; (C2) convexity/dominance: (p≻q, p≻r) ⇒ p ≻ λq+(1−λ)r; (p≻q, p∼r) ⇒ p ≻ λq+(1−λ)r (λ∈(0,1)); (p∼q, p∼r) ⇒ p ∼ λq+(1−λ)r, and the symmetric statements with ≺; (C3) symmetry: if p≻q≻r and q ∼ ½p+½r then, for λ∈(0,1), λp+(1−λ)r ∼ ½p+½q ⇔ λr+(1−λ)p ∼ ½r+½q (equivalent 'symmetric' formulation in Fishburn). SSB THEOREM (1982): (C1)–(C3) hold IF AND ONLY IF there is a skew-symmetric bilinear φ: P×P→R with p≻q ⇔ φ(p,q)>0; φ is unique up to multiplication by a positive constant.
