TRANSITIVE CASE (1983, Theorem 1): if in addition ≻ is transitive (weak order) and the relation is nontrivial, then (C1)–(C3)+transitivity hold IF AND ONLY IF there exist linear functionals u, w on P with w>0 (on the relevant part of P) such that φ(p,q)=u(p)w(q)−u(q)w(p), i.e. p ≻ q ⇔ u(p)/w(p) > u(q)/w(q) (weighted linear utility); (u,w) unique up to the 4-parameter linear-fractional group.
      USE: Fishburn's route is the cleanest template for LR because it contains NO monotonicity axiom at all: the whole content is bilinearity + skew-symmetry. For LR=ρ/Π with Π>0, define Φ(X,Y):=ρ(X)Π(Y)−ρ(Y)Π(X); then LR(X)>LR(Y) ⇔ Φ(X,Y)>0, Φ is skew-symmetric and 'comonotone-biadditive'. Conversely, on each comonotone cone, (C1)–(C3) for the order induced by LR (with transitivity automatic since LR is real-valued) give LR=ψ(u/w) with u,w additive on the cone and w>0; non-monotonicity of ρ_β=ρ−βΠ (F2) is irrelevant. The cardinal gap (ψ unknown) and the cross-cone gluing remain and are handled by the Sincov/mediant-weight axiom below.
  [4] Yaari, M. E. (1987). The dual theory of choice under risk. Econometrica 55(1), 95–115. DOI 10.2307/1911158 (citation verified via Crossref). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: random variables v on a probability space with values in [0,1] (equivalently their distribution functions F_v; Γ = set of such distributions). For u,v ∈ Γ and α∈[0,1], the 'dual mixture' αu ⊕ (1−α)v is the random variable whose quantile function is the mixture of the quantile functions, i.e. the law of αu'+(1−α)v' with u',v' comonotone copies. Axioms on ≿: (A1) neutrality (law invariance: u =_d v ⇒ u ∼ v); (A2) complete weak order; (A3) continuity with respect to L¹ (or weak) convergence of distribution functions; (A4) monotonicity with respect to first-order stochastic dominance; (A5) DUAL INDEPENDENCE: for u,v,w∈Γ and α∈[0,1], u ≿ v ⇒ αu⊕(1−α)w ≿ αv⊕(1−α)w. THEOREM 1 (Yaari): ≿ satisfies (A1)–(A5) IF AND ONLY IF there exists a continuous nondecreasing f:[0,1]→[0,1] with f(0)=0, f(1)=1 such that u ≿ v ⇔ ∫_0^1 f(1−F_u(t))dt ≥ ∫_0^1 f(1−F_v(t))dt; f is unique. (Thus the certainty equivalent is the distortion/Choquet functional C_f, which is comonotone-additive and positively homogeneous.)
      USE: Yaari's theorem is the law-invariant, comonotone-additive 'linear-on-comonotone-cones' representation: dual independence is exactly linearity along comonotone mixtures. It supplies the second step of an LR characterization: once each of ρ and Π is known to be additive on comonotone pairs, law invariant, continuous and positively homogeneous, Yaari/Schmeidler give the distortion form C_h, C_g. (A4) is what forces f monotone; by F2 it must be dropped for ρ_β, giving signed distortions (Wang–Wei–Willmot). Also note the analogy: Assa's canonical family ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) is a 'dual mixture' of ρ and Π with weights 1/(1−β), −β/(1−β) (signed mixture), i.e. betweenness along the segment from ρ to Π in the space of comonotone-additive functionals.
  [5] Schmeidler, D. (1986). Integral representation without additivity. Proceedings of the AMS 97(2), 255–261. DOI 10.1090/S0002-9939-1986-0835875-8 (AMS journal page read online; citation verified via Crossref). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: As read on the AMS page (abstract): 'Let I be a norm-continuous functional on the space B of bounded Σ-measurable real valued functions on a set S', Σ an algebra of subsets of S; define the set function v on Σ by v(E)=I(1_E). Let J(a) = ∫_{-∞}^0 (v({a ≥ α}) − v(S)) dα + ∫_0^∞ v({a ≥ α}) dα (Choquet-type integral). Then I = J on B IF AND ONLY IF I(b+c)=I(b)+I(c) whenever (b(s)−b(t))(c(s)−c(t)) ≥ 0 for all s,t ∈ S (comonotonic additivity). [In the body of the paper the main theorem is stated with monotonicity of I in place of norm continuity, yielding a monotone v; the abstract version with norm continuity in place of monotonicity is the non-monotone/'signed' version. Note the AMS page as fetched does not list monotonicity of v among the hypotheses.]
      USE: This is the key tool for dropping monotonicity: comonotonic additivity + NORM CONTINUITY (no monotonicity) already yields a Choquet representation with a (possibly non-monotone) set function v. Applied to ρ and Π (or to each member ρ_β of the associated family) obtained from LR via the betweenness/mediant axioms, it gives signed Choquet integrals; with law invariance on an atomless space, v = h∘P with h of bounded variation (Wang–Wei–Willmot 2020 make this precise). Hence the natural target class for the LR iff theorem is 'pairs of signed Choquet integrals with positive denominator', not 'coherent/monotone pairs', which resolves the tension in F2/F7.
  [6] Wang, R., Wei, Y., Willmot, G. E. (2020). Characterization, robustness, and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals. Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993–1015. DOI 10.1287/moor.2019.1020; SSRN 2956962 (abstract read online on SSRN: 'non-monotone, law-invariant risk functionals called signed Choquet integrals ... functional characterization based on comonotonic additivity ... six equivalent conditions for a signed Choquet integral to be convex ... many results extend without monotonicity'). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless probability space, X = L^∞. A signed Choquet integral is I_h(X) = ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X>x)) − h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x)) dx with h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0 (h need not be monotone; I_h is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, positively homogeneous, and Lipschitz in the sup-norm with constant ‖h‖_TV). THEOREM 1 (characterization; reconstructed from memory): for I: L^∞→R the following are equivalent: (i) I is a signed Choquet integral; (ii) I is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly continuous with respect to the L^∞-norm (continuity assumption may be weakened to continuity at 0 / boundedness on the unit ball). h is uniquely determined by I via h(t)=I(1_A), P(A)=t. THEOREM (convexity; abstract confirms 'six equivalent conditions'): for a signed Choquet integral I_h, the following are equivalent: I_h is convex; I_h is subadditive; h is concave; I_h is monotone w.r.t. convex order ... (further equivalences involving quasi-convexity and SSD). Further: I_h is monotone iff h is nondecreasing.
      USE: Gives the precise representation class for the LR theorem and for Assa's canonical family: if ρ=C_h, Π=C_g then ρ_β is the signed Choquet integral with distortion (h−βg)/(1−β) (F7). The characterization theorem is the 'representation half' of any LR iff theorem built on comonotone-additive ρ,Π obtained from betweenness/mediant axioms, with NO monotonicity. The convexity theorem gives intrinsic conditions for the associated family to be convex (h−βg concave) and hence for level sets of LR to be convex cones — a route to F4 (quasi-convexity).
  [7] Han, X., Lin, L., Wang, R. (2022/2023). Diversification quotients: Quantifying diversification via risk measures. arXiv:2206.13679 (Operations Research, forthcoming). Theorem 1 and Theorem 2 read online in the arXiv PDF. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: X^n = (L^∞)^n portfolios; φ a reference MCP risk measure (monetary = monotone + cash-additive, and positively homogeneous). Definition 1: for a class ρ=(ρ_α)_{α∈I}, I=(0, ᾱ), ᾱ∈(0,∞], with ρ_α DECREASING in α, DQ^ρ_α(X) = α*/α, α* = inf{β∈I : ρ_β(Σ X_i) ≤ Σ ρ_α(X_i)}, inf ∅ = ᾱ. Axioms on an index D: X^n→[−∞,∞]: [+] D ≥ 0; [LI] D(X+c)=D(X) for constant vectors c; [SI] D(λX)=D(X), λ>0; [R]_φ rationality: if X,Y are φ-marginally equivalent (φ(X_i)=φ(Y_i) ∀i) and Σ X_i ≤ Σ Y_i then D(X) ≤ D(Y); [N]_φ normalization: D(0)=0, D ≤ 1 on duplicate portfolios (X,…,X), D ≥ 1 on portfolios worse than duplicate; [C]_φ continuity: for φ-marginally equivalent Y^k of X with (ΣX_i − ΣY^k_i)_+ → 0 in L^∞, (D(X) − D(Y^k))_+ → 0. THEOREM 1 (verbatim): D satisfies [+],[LI],[SI],[R]_φ,[N]_φ,[C]_φ for some MCP risk measure φ IF AND ONLY IF D is DQ^ρ_α for some α and decreasing class ρ of MCP risk measures (in both directions one can require ρ_α=φ). THEOREM 2: for n ≥ 4 and φ non-linear coherent, adding [PC] (w ↦ D(w⊙X) quasi-convex on the simplex) is equivalent to ρ being a decreasing class of COHERENT risk measures with ρ_α=φ. Proof ('only if') goes through the auxiliary mapping R(X)=inf{D(X): X ≤ Σ X_i, X φ-marginally equivalent to 0}.
      USE: This is the most recent published iff theorem of exactly Assa's 'association with a parametric family' type: an index defined as the level β at which a decreasing family ρ_β crosses zero (compare F5: 1/LR = sup{x: xρ−Π ≤ 0}, and LR(X)=inf{β: ρ(X)−βΠ(X) ≤ 0} with the DECREASING family σ_β=ρ−βΠ, which is cash-additive up to the factor (1−β) — Assa's normalization by 1/(1−β) restores cash-additivity but destroys monotonicity in β). The axiom pattern [+],[LI]/[SI],[R],[N],[C] is the template to imitate; however [R] (rationality) is a MONOTONICITY axiom and the theorem produces MONOTONE (MCP) families — precisely what F2 forbids for LR. Lesson: an LR iff theorem must replace rationality by a comonotone-additivity-type axiom (betweenness/mediant) and accept signed (non-monotone) families; the DQ proof technique (define the family from level sets of the index) still applies.
  [8] Aczél, J. (1966). Lectures on Functional Equations and Their Applications. Academic Press, §8.1 (Sincov's equation f(x,y)+f(y,z)=f(x,z)) and Ch. 2 (Cauchy's equation on cones/with regularity); Aczél, J., Dhombres, J. (1989). Functional Equations in Several Variables. Cambridge UP, Ch. 2–4, 15 (quasi-arithmetic and weighted means; bisymmetry; linear-fractional functions). Classical; section numbers from memory. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: (i) SINCOV'S EQUATION: Let S be a nonempty set and ω: S×S→(0,∞). Then ω(x,y)·ω(y,z)=ω(x,z) for all x,y,z∈S IF AND ONLY IF there is Π:S→(0,∞) with ω(x,y)=Π(x)/Π(y); Π is unique up to a positive multiplicative constant (proof: Π(x):=ω(x,x_0)). Additive form f(x,y)+f(y,z)=f(x,z) ⇔ f(x,y)=g(x)−g(y). (ii) CAUCHY ON A CONE: if C is a convex cone in a normed space and Π: C→R satisfies Π(x+y)=Π(x)+Π(y) and is continuous (or bounded on a ball, or monotone), then Π(λx)=λΠ(x) for all λ>0 (positive homogeneity), i.e. Π is the restriction of a linear functional on C−C. (iii) QUASI-ARITHMETIC/WEIGHTED MEANS (de Finetti–Kolmogorov–Nagumo; Aczél's bisymmetry theorem): a continuous, strictly monotone, reflexive, symmetric and associative/bisymmetric mean on an interval is φ^{-1}(Σφ(x_i)/n); with weights it is the 'weighted quasilinear mean' φ^{-1}(Σ w_i φ(x_i)/Σ w_i) — Chew's theorem is its distributional generalization. (iv) LINEAR-FRACTIONAL functions t ↦ (at+b)/(ct+d) are exactly the continuous bijections of intervals preserving cross-ratios / mapping the ratio u/w of two linear forms to another such ratio; hence the uniqueness group of weighted-utility pairs (u,w).
      USE: Provides the CARDINAL (as opposed to ordinal) step missing from Chew/Dekel/Fishburn. For comonotone X,Y with LR(X)≠LR(Y) the strict mediant property defines the weight w(X,Y)∈(0,1) by LR(X+Y)=w LR(X)+(1−w)LR(Y) and the odds ω(X,Y)=w/(1−w). In the ratio model ω(X,Y)=Π(X)/Π(Y), which satisfies Sincov's equation and ω(X+Y,Z)=ω(X,Z)+ω(Y,Z). Conversely, imposing these two functional equations as axioms on LR yields Π(X):=ω(X,1) (the constant 1 is comonotone with everything), additive on comonotone pairs, positively homogeneous by (ii), and ρ:=LR·Π additive on comonotone pairs by the mediant identity. This gives LR=ρ/Π ON THE NOSE (no unknown monotone transform) and glues all comonotone cones at once, bypassing the projective non-uniqueness of Chew/Fishburn; no monotonicity is used, so F2 is harmless.
  [9] Schmeidler, D. (1989). Subjective probability and expected utility without additivity. Econometrica 57(3), 571–587. DOI 10.2307/1911053 (citation verified via Crossref). Purely-monetary version: Chateauneuf, A. (1991). On the use of capacities in modeling uncertainty aversion and risk aversion. Journal of Mathematical Economics 20, 343–369; Chateauneuf, A. (1994). Modeling attitudes towards uncertainty and risk through the use of Choquet integral. Annals of Operations Research 52, 3–20. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Anscombe–Aumann setting: S a set of states with algebra Σ, Y a convex set of lotteries, acts f: S→Y Σ-measurable with finite range (L_0), extended to L. Two acts f,g are comonotonic if there are no s,t with f(s)≻f(t) and g(t)≻g(s). Axioms: (i) ≿ is a weak order; (ii) COMONOTONIC INDEPENDENCE: for pairwise comonotonic f,g,h and α∈(0,1), f ≻ g ⇒ αf+(1−α)h ≻ αg+(1−α)h; (iii) continuity: f≻g≻h ⇒ ∃α,β∈(0,1) with αf+(1−α)h ≻ g ≻ βf+(1−β)h; (iv) MONOTONICITY: f(s) ≿ g(s) ∀s ⇒ f ≿ g; (v) nondegeneracy. THEOREM (Schmeidler 1989): (i)–(v) hold IF AND ONLY IF there exist a unique (monotone, normalized) non-additive probability v on Σ and an affine u: Y→R, unique up to positive affine transformations, such that f ≿ g ⇔ ∫ u∘f dv ≥ ∫ u∘g dv (Choquet integral). Chateauneuf (1991/1994) obtains the same Choquet representation with LINEAR utility (∫ f dv for monetary acts) from a comonotonic sure-thing/independence axiom plus monotonicity and continuity in a Savage-type (no lotteries) framework.
      USE: Comonotonic independence is the preference-level form of comonotone additivity, and these theorems show that the comonotone-mixture structure alone (plus continuity) delivers Choquet integrals; the monotonicity axiom (iv) is what makes v monotone. For LR, the right analogue is 'comonotone betweenness' (not full comonotonic independence, which would force LR itself to be linear along comonotone mixtures, impossible for a ratio) — i.e. the Chew/Dekel weakening applied inside each comonotone class. The theorems also warn that the state-wise/FSD monotonicity axiom must be dropped: by F2 no non-trivial LR is pointwise monotone, so any axiom of Schmeidler's type (iv) on LR is inconsistent with the goal, and monotonicity of ρ and Π must be stated on the pair or as 'the associated family is monotone at the endpoints β=0 (ρ_0=ρ) and β→∞ (ρ_β→Π)'.
  [10] Cherny, A., Madan, D. (2009). New measures for performance evaluation. Review of Financial Studies 22(7), 2571–2606 (Theorem 1); and its 'index of acceptability' generalization used in F5. (Not re-read online; included as the risk-management counterpart of the DQ theorem.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let α: L^∞→[0,∞]. α is called an acceptability index if (quasi-concavity) α(X)≥x, α(Y)≥x ⇒ α(λX+(1−λ)Y)≥x; (monotonicity) X ≤ Y ⇒ α(X) ≤ α(Y); (scale invariance) α(λX)=α(X), λ>0; (Fatou property) X_n→X in probability, |X_n|≤1 ⇒ α(X) ≥ limsup α(X_n). THEOREM 1: α is an acceptability index IF AND ONLY IF there is a family (ρ_x)_{x≥0} of coherent risk measures with the Fatou property, increasing in x, such that α(X)=sup{x≥0: ρ_x(X) ≤ 0}.
      USE: Same 'level-crossing of a monotone family' architecture as Assa's association and the DQ theorem; the background already notes (F2) that it does not apply because ρ_β is a difference of monotone functionals. The functional-equation/decision-theory strand explains WHY and what replaces it: drop monotonicity and quasi-concavity (hence coherence) of the family and keep only comonotone linearity; the Chew/Fishburn betweenness theorems are the monotonicity-free substitutes for Cherny–Madan's Theorem 1.


### Insurance-pricing axiomatics (Wang–Young–Panjer / Wang layer-premium density / Goovaerts–De Vylder–Haezendonck / Deprez–Gerber / Furman–Zitikis / Haezendonck–Goovaerts–Orlicz premium and Bellini–Rosazza Gianin). NOTE ON METHOD: WebSearch was disabled for this organisation (HTTP 403 on every call), so the search was done through the OpenAlex and Crossref APIs plus WebFetch of primary PDFs (arXiv, Cambridge/ASTIN, KU Leuven Lirias, R. Wang's homepage). Primary sources actually read online: Wang (1996, ASTIN); Wang–Wei–Willmot (2020, MOR); Wang–Wang–Wei (2020, ASTIN); Wang–Wei (2020, Math. Finance); Wang–Ziegel (2015); Nendel–Riedel–Schmeck (2021, arXiv 2006.14272); Gao–Munari–Xanthos (2020, arXiv 1909.10735); Goulard–Grosse-Erdmann (2025, arXiv 2512.03267); Aygün–Bellini–Laeven (2023, arXiv 2302.13070); Escobar–Pflug (2018, arXiv 1809.06592); Hürlimann (1998, ASTIN); Cardin–Pacelli (2005 preprint); Santos–Moresco–Righi–Horta (2023, arXiv 2307.04647); Sendov–Wang–Zitikis (arXiv 1008.3427). NOT obtainable online (paywalled/robots-blocked): Wang–Young–Panjer 1997 itself, Deprez–Gerber 1985, Furman–Zitikis 2008, Bellini–Rosazza Gianin 2008/2012, Goovaerts–De Vylder–Haezendonck 1984, Young's encyclopedia entry, the Dhaene et al. 2006 review; those are reported from memory with located citations and marked medium/low.
KEY INSIGHT: Three things this strand establishes. (1) LAYER PICTURE: for distortion pairs LR(X) = ∫_0^∞ φ(S_X(t)) g(S_X(t))dt / ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t))dt with φ=h/g, i.e. LR is the layer-premium-density-weighted mean of the LAYER loss ratios φ(S_X(t)) (Wang 1996). This single formula explains F1 (comonotone mediant = weighted mean with comonotone-additive weights), F2 (monotonicity would force φ constant), F3 (layers identify g, Bernoulli identify φ) and shows WHY LR alone cannot be characterised without extra data: a degree-0-homogeneous functional cannot see the weights g, so some hypothesis must be placed on the identification family/pair (the weight Π), not on LR. (2) THE MONOTONICITY OBSTACLE IS SOLVED BY WANG–WEI–WILLMOT Thm 1: a functional on L^∞ (atomless) is a signed Choquet integral ⟺ law-invariant + comonotone-additive + uniformly sup-continuous — NO monotonicity, NO cash-additivity. Apply it to each identification functional V(β,·) (=(1−β)ρ_β); monotonicity (Lemma 2(i)) is then demanded only of the two endpoints V(0,·)=ρ and −∂_βV=Π. WWW Thm 3 further gives a one-line quasi-convexity criterion ({LR ≤ β}={I_{h−βg} ≤ 0} convex if h−βg concave) and reveals that in the 'actuarial picture' (ρ=E, g concave) E−βC_g is concave so LR is quasi-CONCAVE, not quasi-convex — F4 needs re-checking. (3) THE IMPLICIT/ASSOCIATION DEFINITION IS CLASSICAL: Orlicz premium E[φ(X/π)]=1−α (Haezendonck–Goovaerts 1982; unique solution for X∈L^∞_+∖{0}), mean-value E[v(X)]=v(π), zero-utility, and Aygün–Bellini–Laeven Thm 36 ('identifiable + positively homogeneous + normalised ⇒ identification function E[Φ(X/x)]−1 ⇒ Orlicz premium') is the exact prototype of the sought theorem. Its hidden assumption is that identification functions are expectations (mixture-linear); LR with Choquet pairs has comonotone-additive identification functions instead. Hence two canonical ratio classes: expectation-type identification ⇒ LR = E[X w_1(X)]/E[X w_2(X)] (ratio of Furman–Zitikis weighted premiums); comonotone-additive identification ⇒ LR = C_h/C_g. The remaining genuinely open lemma is the non-monotone analogue of Santos et al. Thm 2: does comonotonic convexity of all level cones of LR (F1) plus cash-additivity of ρ_β (Assa Prop 1) force comonotone additivity of ρ_β without monotonicity?
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (Choquet-ratio characterisation via a comonotone-additive identification family). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, 𝒳 = L^∞_+∖{0}, and LR: 𝒳 → (0,∞) with LR(1)=1. The following are equivalent. (B) There exist non-decreasing h,g:[0,1]→[0,1] with h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, g(p)>0 for p>0, such that LR(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>t))dt / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>t))dt for all X∈𝒳. (A) (A1) LR is law-invariant and (A2) scale-invariant, LR(λX)=LR(X) for λ>0; and (A3) LR is identifiable by a family V: R×L^∞→R, i.e. LR(X)=β ⟺ V(β,X)=0 for all X∈𝒳, β∈R, such that (a) for every β, V(β,·) is law-invariant, comonotone-additive and uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous on L^∞; (b) β↦V(β,X) is affine for every X, V(β,X)=V(0,X)−βW(X); (c) ρ:=V(0,·) and W are monotone with ρ(1)=W(1)=1 and W>0 on 𝒳. Under (A)/(B) the pair (h,g) is unique, the canonical associated family is ρ_β=V(β,·)/(1−β)=I_{(h−βg)/(1−β)} (a signed Choquet integral, cash-additive and positively homogeneous but monotone only if h ≥ βg pointwise-increasingly), LR ≥ 1 ⟺ h ≥ g, LR ≤ 1 ⟺ h ≤ g, and LR is quasi-convex on {LR ≤ β_0} whenever h−βg is concave for all β ≤ β_0. PROOF SKETCH: (B)⇒(A): V(β,X):=C_h(X)−βC_g(X); properties from Wang–Wang–Wei Prop 2 and WWW Thm 1 ('if' part). (A)⇒(B): by (b) V(0,·)=ρ and W are themselves law-invariant, comonotone-additive, uniformly sup-continuous, so by WWW Thm 1 ρ=I_h, W=I_g with h,g of bounded variation; (c) and WWW Lemma 2(i) give h,g non-decreasing, normalisation gives h(1)=g(1)=1, W(1_A)=g(P(A))>0 gives g>0 on (0,1]; then LR(X)=β ⟺ I_h(X)=βI_g(X) ⟺ β=I_h(X)/I_g(X). HONEST ACCOUNTING: hypotheses (A1)–(A2) and the comonotone-mediant/continuity properties of F1 are statements on LR alone and are NECESSARY; (A3)(a)–(c) are statements on the identification family (equivalently on the pair (ρ,Π) up to the one scale LR cannot see) and are the price of the theorem. Reducing (A3) to LR alone requires proving the non-monotone analogue of Santos–Moresco–Righi–Horta Thm 2 (comonotonic convexity of all level cones of LR + cash-additivity of the LR-quantiles ρ_β ⇒ comonotone additivity of ρ_β), which this strand identifies as THE open lemma; replacing (a) 'comonotone-additive' by 'mixture-linear (expectation-type)' and following Aygün–Bellini–Laeven Thm 36 yields instead the weighted-premium ratio class LR = E[X w_1(X)]/E[X w_2(X)].
THEOREMS:
  [1] Wang, S.S., Young, V.R., Panjer, H.H. (1997). Axiomatic characterization of insurance prices. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 21(2), 173–183. DOI 10.1016/S0167-6687(97)00031-0. (Hürlimann, ASTIN Bull. 28(1) 1998, Sec. 3.3 refers to 'Axiom 4, Theorems 1 to 3 in Wang et al. (1997)'; Escobar–Pflug 2018, Remark 6, refer to 'Theorem 2 in WYP' for identification of g from Bernoulli risks.) (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting (as I recall it): probability space (Ω,F,P); χ = a set of non-negative (insurance) risks containing all Bernoulli risks and closed under the operations used below; H: χ → [0,∞]. Axiom 1 (conditional state independence = law invariance): X =_d Y ⇒ H[X]=H[Y]. Axiom 2 (monotonicity): X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ H[X] ≤ H[Y]. Axiom 3 (comonotonic additivity): X,Y comonotonic ⇒ H[X+Y]=H[X]+H[Y]. Axiom 4 (continuity): lim_{d→0+} H[(X−d)_+] = H[X], lim_{d→∞} H[min(X,d)] = H[X] (truncation/deductible continuity). Axiom 5 (as I recall: a normalisation/no-unjustified-loading axiom, H[1]=1 resp. H[X] ≥ E[X]; the exact content of Axiom 5 is the one point I could not verify online). Theorem 1: H satisfies Axioms 1–4 (with H[1]=1) ⟺ there is a non-decreasing g:[0,1]→[0,1], g(0)=0, g(1)=1, with H[X] = ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t)) dt = Choquet integral of X w.r.t. g∘P. Theorem 2: g is unique (determined by H on Bernoulli risks: H[1_A] = g(P(A))). Theorem 3: H is subadditive (equivalently preserves stop-loss order / concave on mixtures) ⟺ g is concave; and under 'no unjustified safety loading' g(p) ≥ p ⟺ H ≥ E.
      USE: This is the template for the target theorem, but it cannot be applied to the canonical family ρ_β=(ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) because Axiom 2 (monotonicity) fails (F2). It can be applied separately to the two 'endpoints' ρ=ρ_0 and Π=lim(ρ_0−(1−β)ρ_β)/β, and Theorem 2 (identification on Bernoulli + two-layer risks) is exactly the mechanism behind F3. Theorem 3 gives: LR ≥ 1 everywhere ⟺ h ≥ g pointwise (see also Wang–Wang–Wei Prop 2(i) below).
  [2] Wang, S. (1996). Premium calculation by transforming the layer premium density. ASTIN Bulletin 26(1), 71–92. DOI 10.2143/ast.26.1.563234. Definition 6, Section 4.1 items 2–4, Theorems 1–4 (read online, Cambridge Core PDF). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Definition 6: for g increasing concave with g(0)=0, g(1)=1 and X ≥ 0, H(X)=∫_0^∞ g[S_X(t)] dt, S_X(t)=P(X>t); 'the premium for the infinitesimal layer (t,t+dt] is g(S_X(t))dt' (layer premium density). Properties: E(X) ≤ H(X) ≤ max X; H(aX+b)=aH(X)+b (a>0,b≥0); layer additivity H(X)=Σ_k H(X_k) for the layer decomposition X=Σ min((X−a_k)_+, a_{k+1}−a_k); FSD preservation. Section 4.1: the relative loading g(S_X(t))/S_X(t) of the layer at t is increasing in t ⟺ g is concave. Theorem 2: H(U+V) ≤ H(U)+H(V) for all non-negative U,V (g increasing concave). Theorem 3: H is additive for comonotonic risks. Theorem 4 (after Denneberg 1994, Schmeidler 1986): let BV be a set of bounded random variables and H: BV → [0,∞); if H is (i) comonotonic additive, (ii) FSD-preserving (X_1 ≤_st X_2 ⇒ H(X_1) ≤ H(X_2)), (iii) H(1)=1, then there exists a distortion g with H(X)=∫ g[S_X(t)]dt; moreover H(X+Y) ≤ H(X)+H(Y) for all X,Y ⟺ g is concave.
      USE: KEY STRUCTURAL FACT for LR: with ρ=C_h, Π=C_g, LR(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(S_X(t))dt / ∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t))dt = ∫_0^∞ φ(S_X(t)) · g(S_X(t))dt / ∫ g(S_X(t))dt with φ=h/g, i.e. LR is the Π-layer-premium-density-weighted average of the LAYER loss ratios φ(S_X(t)) = h(S_X(t))/g(S_X(t)). This explains F1 (mediant property under comonotone sums = weighted mean of weighted means with comonotone-additive weights), F2 (adding a top layer can only move LR toward φ(0+) ≥ … so monotonicity forces φ constant), F3 (layers identify g, Bernoulli identify φ) and suggests the intrinsic axiom 'layer mixing': LR(X) lies between LR(min(X,a)) and LR((X−a)_+) for every a, with weights that are additive across layers.
  [3] Wang, R., Wei, Y., Willmot, G.E. (2020). Characterization, robustness and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals. Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993–1015. DOI 10.1287/moor.2019.1020. Definition 1, Theorem 1, Lemma 2(i), Theorem 3 (read online, author PDF). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: (Ω,A,P) atomless, functionals on L^∞. H := {h:[0,1]→R of bounded variation, h(0)=0}. Definition 1: the signed Choquet integral I_h(X) = ∫_{-∞}^0 (h(P(X≥x))−h(1)) dx + ∫_0^∞ h(P(X≥x)) dx, h∈H ('distortion function', not required monotone). Theorem 1: a functional I: L^∞→R is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly ‖·‖_∞-continuous IF AND ONLY IF I is a signed Choquet integral I_h for some h∈H ('only if' via Schmeidler 1986 Prop. 2; bounded variation of h comes from uniform norm-continuity). Lemma 2(i): I_h is increasing (resp. decreasing) ⟺ h is increasing (resp. decreasing). Theorem 3: for h∈H the following are equivalent: (i) h concave; (ii) I_h convex-order consistent; (iii) I_h subadditive; (iv) I_h convex; (v) I_h quasi-convex; (vi) I_h concave on mixtures. Theorem 5: the smallest law-invariant convex functional on L^∞ dominating I_h is I_{h*}, h* the concave envelope.
      USE: THIS IS THE THEOREM THAT REMOVES THE MONOTONICITY OBSTACLE (F2). The canonical ρ_β = (ρ−βΠ)/(1−β) is non-monotone but, for distortion pairs, it is the signed Choquet integral with distortion (h−βg)/(1−β) (F7). Conversely, if the associated/identification family V(β,·) is law-invariant, comonotone additive and uniformly sup-continuous for each β, Theorem 1 forces V(β,·)=I_{k_β}; monotonicity is then imposed only on the endpoints k_0=h and g, via Lemma 2(i). Theorem 3(v) gives a one-line quasi-convexity criterion for LR: {LR ≤ β} = {I_{h−βg} ≤ 0}, so LR is quasi-convex whenever h−βg is concave for all β in the range of LR (conic picture: h concave, g convex). CAUTION for F4: in the 'actuarial picture' ρ=E, g concave, the functional E−βC_g is CONCAVE, so its superlevel sets {LR ≥ β} are convex: LR is quasi-CONCAVE there, not quasi-convex; F4 should be re-checked.
  [4] Wang, Q., Wang, R., Wei, Y. (2020). Distortion riskmetrics on general spaces. ASTIN Bulletin 50(3), 827–851. Definition 1, Theorem 1, Proposition 2, Theorem 3, Corollary 1, Theorem 5 (read online, author PDF). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: atomless (Ω,A,P); 𝒳 ⊇ L^∞ a law-invariant convex cone; H = {h:[0,1]→R, h(0)=0, bounded variation}. Definition 1: ρ_h(X)=∫X d(h∘P) (signed Choquet) is a distortion riskmetric on 𝒳 when finite; h is unique. Theorem 1: ρ:𝒳→R is a distortion riskmetric ⟺ ρ is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive, continuous at infinity (truncation continuity) and uniformly sup-continuous. Proposition 2: (i) if h_1(1)=h_2(1) then h_1 ≤ h_2 on [0,1] ⟺ ρ_{h_1} ≤ ρ_{h_2}; in particular ρ_{h_1}=ρ_{h_2} ⟺ h_1=h_2; (ii) ρ_h increasing ⟺ h increasing; (iii) ρ_h(X+c)=ρ_h(X)+c h(1); (iv) ρ_h(λX)=λρ_h(X), λ>0. Theorem 3 (𝒳⊆L^1): h concave ⟺ ρ_h convex-order consistent ⟺ subadditive ⟺ convex ⟺ quasi-convex ⟺ concave on mixtures. Corollary 1 (𝒳⊆L^1): ρ is law-invariant, increasing, cash-invariant, continuous at infinity and comonotonic-additive ⟺ ρ=ρ_h for some increasing h with h(1)=1; it is moreover coherent ⟺ h concave. Theorem 5: ρ is a convex distortion riskmetric ⟺ ρ(X)=∫_0^1 ES_α(X)dμ(α)+∫_0^1 ES_α(−X)dν(α) for finite Borel measures μ,ν (ν=0 if ρ increasing).
      USE: Corollary 1 is the modern, fully verified form of the Wang–Young–Panjer theorem (on L^1, with cash-invariance in place of WYP's Axiom 5) and should be the one quoted in the panel's final theorem for the endpoints ρ and Π. Proposition 2(i) gives immediately: LR ≥ 1 for all X ⟺ h ≥ g (with h(1)=g(1)=1), LR ≤ 1 ⟺ h ≤ g, and uniqueness of (h,g) given (ρ,Π). Theorem 5 yields a Kusuoka/ES-mixture form of ρ_β: (1−β)ρ_β = ∫ES_α dμ_h − β∫ES_α dμ_g, a signed ES-mixture, i.e. the association family is a 'two-measure' Kusuoka object.
  [5] Haezendonck, J., Goovaerts, M.J. (1982). A new premium calculation principle based on Orlicz norms. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 1, 41–53 (Theorem 2); restated with hypotheses in Goulard & Grosse-Erdmann (2025), arXiv:2512.03267, Def. 4.1–4.2, Remark 4.4, Prop. 4.5, Thm 4.6, Def. 4.11, Thm 4.16 (read online), and in Bellini & Rosazza Gianin (2008), J. Banking & Finance 32, 986–994, Prop. 2. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Young function: φ:[0,∞)→[0,∞) convex, φ(0)=0, φ(t)→∞, normalised φ(1)=1. Orlicz space L^φ = {X: E φ(|X|/a)<∞ for some a>0}, L^∞ ⊂ L^φ ⊂ L^1. Orlicz premium (Def. 4.2): for α<1 and X∈L^φ_+, π_{φ,α}(X)=inf{a>0: E φ(X/a) ≤ 1−α}. Existence/uniqueness (Remark 4.4, citing HG82 Thm 2 and BRG08 p.108): if X≠0 and X∈L^∞_+, or X∈L^φ_+ and φ satisfies Δ_2, there is a UNIQUE a>0 with E[φ(X/a)] = 1−α, and a = π_{φ,α}(X) — i.e. the premium is DEFINED IMPLICITLY as the unique level-crossing solution of E[φ(X/π)] = 1−α. Prop 4.5: E(X)/φ^{-1}(1−α) ≤ π_{φ,α}(X) ≤ ess sup X/φ^{-1}(1−α), π_{φ,α}(b)=b/φ^{-1}(1−α). Thm 4.6: π_{φ,α} is monotone, positively homogeneous and subadditive on L^φ_+ (it is NOT cash-additive). Def 4.11 (HG risk measure, Goovaerts–Kaas–Dhaene–Tang 2004 / BRG 2008): ρ_{φ,α}(X)=inf_{x∈R}{x+π_{φ,α}((X−x)^+)}, X∈L^φ; the map x↦x+π((X−x)^+) is convex and for 0<α<1 the infimum is attained. Thm 4.16: ρ_{φ,α} is coherent on L^φ. Cor 4.21: if α=0, φ differentiable at 1 and Δ_2, then ρ_{φ,0}=E.
      USE: DIRECT ANCESTOR of Assa's association: the Orlicz premium is the functional T with T(X)=k ⟺ V(k,X):=E[φ(X/k)]−(1−α)=0, a one-parameter family V(k,·) that is monotone in X and decreasing in k, whose zero-level identifies the premium. Assa's LR has the same shape with V(β,X)=ρ_β(X)·(1−β) = ρ(X)−βΠ(X) (affine in β instead of 'E[φ(X/k)]'). The HG construction inf_x{x+π((X−x)^+)} shows how to turn a positively-homogeneous (scale-type) functional into a cash-additive monetary one by optimising over a cash shift; Assa's Prop 1 (ρ_β cash-additive ⟺ m=−ρ_β(X) solves LR(X−m)=β) is the same 'translation-to-level' mechanism applied to the degree-0 functional LR: ρ_β(X) = the 'LR-quantile' {m: LR(X−m)=β}.
  [6] Bellini, F., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2008). On Haezendonck risk measures. J. Banking & Finance 32(6), 986–994 (Prop. 2, Thm 3–4); Bellini, F., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2012). Haezendonck–Goovaerts risk measures and Orlicz quantiles. Insurance: Math. Econ. 51(1), 107–114; modern verified versions: Gao, N., Munari, C., Xanthos, F. (2020). Stability properties of Haezendonck–Goovaerts premium principles. IME 94, 94–99, arXiv:1909.10735, Prop. 2.2, Thm 3.4, Thm 4.2 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting (GMX): nonatomic (Ω,F,P); Φ a finite Orlicz function (convex, nondecreasing, left-continuous, Φ(0)=0, nonconstant) normalised Φ(1)=1; α∈(0,1); Φ_α:=Φ/(1−α); N_α(X):=‖X‖_{Φ_α} (Luxemburg norm); π_α(X):=inf_{m∈R}{m+N_α((X−m)^+)} on L^Φ (the HG premium principle 'or equivalently HG risk measure'). Prop 2.2 (from BRG08): π_α(X,·) is convex and →∞ as m→±∞; π_α is finite, a minimiser m_X exists; π_α is sublinear, monotone increasing, translation invariant and law invariant. Thm 3.4: the following are equivalent: (i) π_α continuous from above; (ii) Lebesgue property; (iii) Φ satisfies Δ_2. Thm 4.2 (dual representation, no Δ_2 needed): π_α(X)=sup{E_Q[X]: Q≪P, dQ/dP∈L^∞, ‖dQ/dP‖_{L^{Ψ_α}} ≤ 1}, Ψ the conjugate Young function. Remark 4.3: with dQ/dP∈H^Ψ the sup is attained for every X ⟺ Φ∈Δ_2. (BRG 2012, from memory: the minimiser x* ('Orlicz quantile') is characterised by the first-order level-crossing condition and generalises the α-quantile; π_α(X)=x*+H_α((X−x*)^+); uniqueness under strict convexity of Φ.)
      USE: Shows (a) a coherent, law-invariant, cash-additive risk measure is obtained from an implicitly defined scale-type premium by a cash-shift optimisation; (b) the dual (sup over a norm-ball of densities) representation is the natural 'coherent pair' analog: if ρ=sup_{Q∈𝒬_1}E_Q, Π=sup_{Q∈𝒬_2}E_Q then ρ_β is a DIFFERENCE of two support functions (a 'DC' functional), which is what makes Cherny–Madan inapplicable but Nendel–Riedel–Schmeck applicable (next item). The Δ_2-type continuity equivalences are the model for the continuity axiom one must put on LR's family.
  [7] Bellini, F., Laeven, R.J.A., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2018). Robust return risk measures. Mathematics and Financial Economics 12, 5–32 (characterisation of Orlicz premia via multiplicative acceptance sets); and Aygün, M., Bellini, F., Laeven, R.J.A. (2023). Elicitability of return risk measures. arXiv:2302.13070, Def. 2, Def. 23, Prop. 24(b), Thm 26, Thm 29 (quoting BLRG18), Thm 33, Thm 36 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Def 2: a return risk measure is ρ̃: L^∞_+→[0,∞) positively homogeneous, monotone, ρ̃(1)=1; it corresponds to a monetary risk measure via ρ̃(X)=exp(ρ(log X)). Def 23: for Φ:[0,∞)→R nondecreasing, left-continuous, Φ(1)=1, Φ(∞)=∞ (convexity NOT required), H_Φ(X)=inf{k>0: E[Φ(X/k)] ≤ 1}; Prop 24(b): for X∈L^∞_{++}, E[Φ(X/H_Φ(X))]=1. Thm 29 (from BLRG18): if the multiplicative acceptance set B={ρ̃≤1} and its complement are convex under mixtures of distributions and ψ̃-weakly closed (plus a mixture condition), then ρ̃=H_Φ for a nondecreasing Φ with Φ(0)<1<Φ(∞). Thm 33: a law-invariant CONVEX return risk measure with convex level sets (CxLS) equals H_Φ for some nondecreasing convex Φ. Thm 26: if Φ is increasing and H_Φ is convex and translation invariant then Φ(x)=1+a(x−1)^+−b(x−1)^−, a>b, b<1 (expectiles). Thm 36: an identifiable, positively homogeneous ρ̃ with ρ̃(1)=1 is represented by an identification function of the form V(x,X)=E[Φ(X/x)]−1 for some Φ with Φ(1)=1; if ρ̃ is monotone, Φ is nondecreasing; if also convex, Φ convex.
      USE: Thm 36 is the exact prototype of the theorem the panel wants, for a scale-type functional: 'identifiable (association with a family) + homogeneity + normalisation ⇒ the identification family has a canonical parametric form ⇒ the functional is an Orlicz premium'. The hidden hypothesis there is that identification functions are EXPECTATIONS of point functions (mixture-linear in the law). For LR = C_h/C_g the identification function V(β,X)=C_h(X)−βC_g(X) is NOT mixture-linear but COMONOTONE-ADDITIVE; replacing 'E[v(β,X)]' by 'law-invariant, comonotone-additive, uniformly continuous in X' and using Wang–Wei–Willmot Thm 1 instead of the expectation structure gives the Choquet-ratio class. Thm 29's acceptance-set route is the analogue of 'comonotone convexity of the level cones of LR' (= F1's mediant property).
  [8] Nendel, M., Riedel, F., Schmeck, M.D. (2021). A decomposition of general premium principles into risk and deviation. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 100, 193–209, arXiv:2006.14272, Def. 2.1, Thm 2.2, Thm 2.3, Thm 3.1, Cor. 3.3, Prop. 3.7, Example 2.11 (read online). Prop. 3.7 is stated to parallel Deprez & Gerber (1985), Theorem 3. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: measurable space (Ω,F), B_b bounded claims, C ⊂ B_b with 0∈C and X+m∈C for all X∈C, m∈R (no probability assumed). Def 2.1: H:C→R is a premium principle if (P1) H(X+m)=H(X)+m and (P2) H(0)=0 and H(X) ≥ 0 for X ≥ 0. Monotonicity is NOT assumed ('we do not assume premium principles to be monotone ex ante'). Thm 2.2: H is a premium principle ⟺ H=R+D on C with R:B_b→R a (monotone, cash-additive) risk measure and D:C→R a deviation measure. Thm 2.3: R_Max(X):=inf{H(X_0): X_0∈C, X_0 ≥ X} is a risk measure, R_Max ≤ H on C, D_Min:=H−R_Max is a deviation measure, and any other decomposition has R ≤ R_Max, D ≥ D_Min. Thm 3.1: if H is convex then R_Max(X)=max_{P∈ba^1_+}(E_P X − H*(P)) with H*(P)=sup_{X∈C}(E_P X−H(X)). Cor 3.3: H sublinear ⇒ R_Max coherent, R_Max=max_{P∈𝒫}E_P. Prop 3.7 (cf. Deprez–Gerber Thm 3): for convex H satisfying (P1): H is a monotone premium principle ⟺ H(0)=0 and H(X) ≤ 0 for all X ≤ 0. Example 2.11: under law invariance plus the Wang et al. axioms, H is a Choquet integral w.r.t. a distorted probability and H=R_Max.
      USE: Gives an iff for NON-monotone cash-additive functionals, exactly the class of the canonical family: if ρ,Π are cash-additive with ρ(0)=Π(0)=0 then ρ_β is cash-additive with ρ_β(0)=0, and ρ_β(X) ≥ 0 for X ≥ 0 holds iff β ≤ inf LR (β<1). For such β, Thm 2.2 decomposes ρ_β = R_β + D_β with R_β monotone cash-additive and D_β a deviation; the Cherny–Madan machinery can then be applied to R_β (monotone) with D_β as a correction. Also shows how 'convex-pair' hypotheses (ρ,Π convex) translate into a dual 'two-penalty' representation of ρ_β.
  [9] Deprez, O., Gerber, H.U. (1985). On convex principles of premium calculation. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 4(3), 179–189 (Theorems 1–3, Theorem 9 eq. (55) as cited by Cao–Li–Young–Zou 2024, Remark 2.1). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Setting (from memory): risks X in a linear space of random variables with E e^{aX}<∞ (or bounded) on (Ω,F,P); a premium principle H is 'convex' if H(λX+(1−λ)Y) ≤ λH(X)+(1−λ)H(Y), translation-invariant H(X+c)=H(X)+c, H(0)=0. Theorem (Gâteaux representation): if H is convex, translation invariant and Gâteaux differentiable then H'(X;Y)=E[Y ξ_X] with E[ξ_X]=1, and H(X)=∫_0^1 E[X ξ_{tX}] dt; H is monotone ⟺ ξ_X ≥ 0 for all X (cf. Nendel et al. Prop 3.7 for the 'monotone ⟺ H(X) ≤ 0 for X ≤ 0' form). Examples: ξ_X = e^{aX}/E e^{aX} (Esscher/exponential), ξ_X=1+a(X−EX) (variance principle). Theorem 9/eq.(55): optimal-insurance first-order condition under a Gâteaux-differentiable convex principle.
      USE: Gives the 'convex pair' calculus: if ρ,Π are convex premium principles with densities ξ^ρ_X, ξ^Π_X, then ρ_β is Gâteaux differentiable with derivative E[Y(ξ^ρ_X−βξ^Π_X)]/(1−β) — a SIGNED density, the convex-functional counterpart of the signed distortion (h−βg)/(1−β) of F7. The first-order condition for the LR-quantile m=ρ_β(X) is E[ξ^ρ_{X−m}]−βE[ξ^Π_{X−m}]=0, i.e. LR is identified by a signed-density zero-crossing exactly as the Orlicz premium is.
  [10] Furman, E., Zitikis, R. (2008). Weighted premium calculation principles. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 42(1), 459–465; definitions and loading result restated in Sendov, Wang, Zitikis (2010/2011), arXiv:1008.3427, Section 1, eq. (2.2)–(2.4), Thm 2.1 (read online). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Weighted premium: for X ≥ 0 (X∈𝒳_+) and a non-negative Borel weight w with 0<E[w(X)]<∞, E[Xw(X)]<∞: π_w(X)=E[X w(X)]/E[w(X)] = E[X_w] where X_w has the weighted cdf F_w(x)=E[1{X≤x}w(X)]/E[w(X)]. Properties (FZ08, as restated): (i) non-negative loading π_w(X) ≥ E[X] whenever w is non-decreasing (Chebyshev/Lehmann covariance inequality; SWZ state this as a sufficient condition); (ii) F_{uw}=(F_u)_w=(F_w)_u and F_{hw} ≤ F_w ≤ F for nondecreasing u,w,h; (iii) Thm 2.1 (SWZ): if (λ,x)↦w(λ,x) is log-supermodular then λ↦π_{w(λ,·)}(X) is non-decreasing (loading monotonicity). Examples: w=e^{λx} (Esscher), w=1{x>λ} (CTE E[X|X>λ]), w=1−e^{−x/λ} (Kamps); FZ08 also note that distortion premiums with differentiable g are weighted premiums with the law-dependent weight w(x)=g'(S_X(x)).
      USE: Second natural ratio class: LR = E[X w_1(X)]/E[X w_2(X)] (ratio of two weighted premiums), identifiable with the MIXTURE-LINEAR identification function V(β,X)=E[X(w_1(X)−βw_2(X))]. This is the class the ABL Thm 36 route would produce for LR (expectation-type identification functions), whereas comonotone-additive identification functions produce C_h/C_g; the two classes meet only at ρ,Π ∈ {E} (and at distortion pairs via the law-dependent weights g'(S_X)). The monotone-loading result (Thm 2.1) is the model for 'β ↦ ρ_β monotone' statements.
  [11] Santos, S.S., Moresco, M.R., Righi, M.B., Horta, E. (2023). A note on the induction of comonotonic additive risk measures from acceptance sets. arXiv:2307.04647 (Statistics & Probability Letters 2024), Theorem 2 (read online). (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Setting: L^∞ ⊆ 𝒳 ⊆ L^0 Hausdorff; comonotonicity in the P⊗P-a.s. sense. A set A is comonotonic convex if X,Y∈A comonotone ⇒ λX+(1−λ)Y∈A. Theorem 2: (1) if A is a monetary acceptance set (nonempty, monotone, normalised) such that A and A^c are comonotonic convex, then ρ_A(X)=inf{m: X−m∈A} is comonotonic additive; the converse holds if A is closed. (2) a monetary risk measure ρ (monotone, cash-invariant, normalised) is comonotonic additive ⟺ A_ρ={ρ≤0} and A_ρ^c are both comonotonic convex.
      USE: F1's comonotone mediant property says precisely that every sub-level cone {LR ≤ β} and super-level cone {LR ≥ β} is comonotonic convex (closed under comonotone sums, hence under comonotone mixtures by scale invariance). Theorem 2 is the 'acceptance-set ⇒ comonotone additivity' bridge for MONOTONE cash-additive functionals; the open step for LR is the analogous bridge for the non-monotone ρ_β, i.e. whether comonotonic convexity of {ρ_β ≤ 0} and {ρ_β ≥ 0} for all β (an intrinsic property of LR) plus cash-additivity (Assa Prop 1) forces comonotone additivity of ρ_β. The Santos et al. proof uses monotonicity; this is the exact lemma the panel must either prove without monotonicity or replace by a hypothesis on the family.
  [12] Goovaerts, M.J., De Vylder, F., Haezendonck, J. (1984). Insurance Premiums: Theory and Applications. North-Holland (Ch. 2–3, characterisations of the mean-value, zero-utility, Esscher and Orlicz principles); Gerber, H.U. (1974). On additive premium calculation principles. ASTIN Bulletin 7, 215–222; Kaas, Goovaerts, Dhaene, Denuit (2008). Modern Actuarial Risk Theory, 2nd ed., Springer, Sect. 5.4 'Characterizations of premium principles'. (confidence: low)
      STATEMENT: (From memory.) Mean-value principle: π_v(X)=v^{-1}(E v(X)), v increasing convex — defined implicitly by E[v(X)]=v(π); Theorem (GDH84, after de Finetti/Kolmogorov–Nagumo): a law-invariant premium principle with π(c)=c that is 'iterative' π(X)=π(π(X|Y)) (or 'consistent': π(X+c)=π(X)+c only for exponential) is a mean-value principle. Zero-utility principle: π_u(X) solves u(w)=E[u(w+π−X)]. Theorem (Gerber 1974 / KGDD Thm 5.4.x): a zero-utility principle is additive for independent risks (π(X+Y)=π(X)+π(Y), X⊥Y) ⟺ u is linear (net premium) or exponential (π=(1/a)log E e^{aX}); the exponential principle is the only zero-utility principle that is also iterative/translation-consistent. Orlicz principle: π solves E[φ(X/π)]=1 (GDH84 Ch. 3 = Haezendonck–Goovaerts 1982).
      USE: All three classical principles are IMPLICITLY defined by a single level-crossing equation E[v(π,X)]=const — the common ancestor of Assa's association; the characterisation theorems in GDH84 all work by (a) showing the identification map (π,X)↦E[v(π,X)] has a specific parametric form and (b) using an additivity/iterativity axiom to pin v. For LR the analogous 'parametric form' is affine in β with comonotone-additive coefficients; the analogous 'additivity axiom' is comonotone (layer) additivity of the weights.
  [13] Cardin, M., Pacelli, G. (2005). On characterization of a class of convex operators for pricing insurance risks. EconWPA working paper wpa:wuwpga:0511011 (PDF read online), Theorems 1–2; restating Wang–Young–Panjer (1997) property (P10) and eq. (4.3). Caveat: unpublished preprint with an unfilled citation in the proof of Thm 2. (confidence: high)
      STATEMENT: Properties for H:L→R on a lattice L of non-negative bounded risks: (P1) H ≥ 0; (P2) H(c)=c for c ≥ 0; (P3) H(X) ≤ sup X; (P4) H(aX+b)=aH(X)+b, a,b ≥ 0; (P5) H(X)=H(X−(X−a)_+)+H((X−a)_+) for all a ≥ 0 (layer/comonotonic splitting); (P6) monotonicity; (P7) stop-loss order preservation; (P8) subadditivity; (P9) convexity; (P10) H depends only on F_X; (P11) lim_n H(X−(X−n)_+)=H(X). Eq. (4.3): H(X)=∫_0^∞ g(S_X(t))dt=∫_Ω X dυ with υ=g∘P. Theorem 1 (modified Greco theorem): if H satisfies (P1),(P2),(P5),(P6),(P8),(P11) then there is a convex (submodular) capacity υ on 2^Ω with H(X)=∫_Ω X dυ=∫_0^∞ υ{X>x}dx. Theorem 2: if H satisfies (P1),(P2),(P5),(P7),(P10),(P11) and 1_A∈L for all A, then there is a probability measure m on [0,1] with H(X)=∫_0^1 TVaR_α(X) dm(α).
      USE: Shows that the LAYER-splitting axiom (P5) (additivity across a two-layer comonotone decomposition at every retention a) is enough, with monotonicity and truncation continuity, to replace full comonotonic additivity in the Greco/Schmeidler representation. For LR this suggests the weakest intrinsic axiom: a two-layer 'mediant with additive weights' at every retention a, which is exactly what the layer-premium-density picture of Wang (1996) delivers.


### Law-invariant coherent/convex scholar (Kusuoka / Frittelli-Rosazza Gianin / Jouini-Schachermayer-Touzi / Föllmer-Schied / Delbaen / Acerbi strand). CAVEAT ON METHOD: no web access was possible in this session (WebSearch 403 'not enabled for your organization'; WebFetch and curl CONNECT to arxiv.org, export.arxiv.org, papers.ssrn.com, sas.uwaterloo.ca, link.springer.com, projecteuclid.org, sciencedirect.com, api.crossref.org, api.semanticscholar.org all policy-denied by the egress proxy). Every statement below is reconstructed from memory; theorem numbers are given with the edition they refer to and should be verified before quotation. No item is marked 'high'. Conventions used: Assa's loss convention (X >= 0 a loss; rho(X) = ES-type), translated from the gain convention of Kusuoka/Föllmer-Schied where AVaR_lambda(X_gain) = -(1/lambda) int_0^lambda q_X(s) ds; the dictionary is C_h(X) = int_0^1 ES_u(X) m(du) with h(p) = int_(0,1] min(p/u,1) m(du) for concave h.
KEY INSIGHT: The classical law-invariant theorems split into two groups with respect to the obstruction (F2). Group A (need monotonicity): Schmeidler 1986, Kusuoka Thm 7, Föllmer-Schied Thm 4.93/4.94, Acerbi's positivity of the spectrum, Wang-Young-Panjer, Cherny-Madan Thm 1. Group B (do NOT need monotonicity): Jouini-Schachermayer-Touzi/Svindland automatic Fatou property, the Kusuoka/Frittelli-Rosazza Gianin dual representation with law-invariant penalty, Dana's Schur-convexity (rho(E[X|G]) <= rho(X)), Wang-Wei-Willmot's signed Choquet characterization and its convexity<=>concave-distortion theorem, and Cerreia-Vioglio-Maccheroni-Marinacci-Montrucchio quasiconcave duality. The resolution of (F2) is structural: the canonical family rho_beta = Pi + (rho - Pi)/(1-beta) is a hyperbola in beta whose two ENDPOINTS beta = 0 (rho_0 = rho) and beta -> -infty (rho_{-infty} = Pi) are monotone, while all intermediate members are only law-invariant, comonotone-additive, cash-additive, sup-norm-Lipschitz, and (in the right class) convex. So Group A theorems are applied ONLY to the two endpoints, Group B theorems to the whole family, and nothing is lost: the pair (h,g) is read off from the endpoints, psi_beta = g + (h-g)/(1-beta) is forced by the hyperbolic structure, and the correct monotonicity axiom for LR is convex-order monotonicity of the sublevel sets {LR <= beta} (Schur-convexity of rho_beta), not pointwise monotonicity. A further exact consequence: all rho_beta (beta<1) are convex/subadditive iff h is concave AND h - g is concave (since h - beta g = (1-beta)h + beta(h-g)); this is strictly weaker than the 'conic picture' (h concave, g convex) in (F4) and is the precise class in which Group B applies in full. Honest division of labour: the hypotheses on the PAIR (law invariance, comonotone additivity, continuity, monotonicity of the two endpoints) are what the only-if direction genuinely uses; on LR ALONE one has sufficiency (F1), identifiability (F3), and the new convex-order monotonicity, but the comonotone-mediant axiom at the ratio level is still not known to force comonotone additivity of the components (open, as in the synthesis' Conjecture).
CANDIDATE IFF: CANDIDATE THEOREM (law-invariant/Kusuoka strand). Let (Omega,F,P) be atomless and LR: L^infty_+ \ {0} -> (0,infty). Say LR is in association with a family {rho_beta}_{beta<1} of maps L^infty -> R if LR(X) = beta <=> rho_beta(X) = 0 for all X in L^infty_+\{0}, beta<1. Then the following are equivalent. (I) There exist distortion functions h, g: [0,1] -> [0,1] (non-decreasing, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, g(p)>0 for p>0) with LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X), C_psi(X) = int_0^infty psi(P(X>x)) dx. (II) LR is in association with a family {rho_beta}_{beta<1} such that (B1) each rho_beta is law-invariant, comonotone additive and Lipschitz for the sup-norm; (B2) each rho_beta is cash-additive, rho_beta(X+c) = rho_beta(X)+c; (B3) hyperbolic structure: for every X the map beta -> (1-beta) rho_beta(X) is affine on (-infty,1); (B4) the two endpoint members rho_0 and rho_{-infty}(X) := lim_{beta->-infty} rho_beta(X) are monotone, and rho_{-infty}(X) > 0 for X in L^infty_+\{0}. Proof sketch: (I)=>(II) is (F1)+(F7) with rho_beta = C_{(h - beta g)/(1-beta)}; (II)=>(I): by Wang-Wei-Willmot Thm 1, (B1) makes each rho_beta a signed Choquet integral C_{psi_beta}; (B2) gives psi_beta(1)=1; (B3) and evaluation on Bernoulli and two-layer losses give psi_beta = g + (h-g)/(1-beta) with h := psi_0, g := lim psi_beta; (B4) with Schmeidler/Kusuoka Thm 7/WYP applied to the two monotone endpoints gives h, g non-decreasing (genuine distortions); finally rho_beta(X)=0 <=> C_h(X) = beta C_g(X) <=> LR(X) = beta, and uniqueness of (h,g) is (F3). ADDENDA within the same equivalence: (a) rho_0 is subadditive <=> h concave (Kusuoka Thm 7 / FS 4.93); rho_{-infty} is superadditive <=> g convex; (b) every rho_beta, beta<1, is convex <=> h and h-g are concave <=> every rho_beta is Schur-convex (consistent with convex order) <=> the sublevel sets {LR <= beta} are closed under convex-order decreases, i.e. X <=_cx Y and LR(Y) <= beta imply LR(X) <= beta (Dana/FS Cor 4.59 + WWW Thm 3); in this class LR is quasi-convex and, by Svindland, automatically has the Fatou property, no Fatou axiom being needed. What is on LR alone: law invariance, scale invariance, translation-to-break-even, comonotone intermediate value, convex-order monotonicity of sublevel sets, and continuity are all NECESSARY for (I) and (up to the open comonotone-mediant => comonotone-additivity step) are the LR-level shadows of (B1)-(B4); what is on the pair: (B1)-(B4) themselves, of which only (B4) uses monotonicity and only at the two endpoints of the family.
THEOREMS:
  [1] Kusuoka, S. (2001). On law invariant coherent risk measures. Advances in Mathematical Economics 3, 83-95. Theorem 4 (general case) and Theorem 7 (comonotonic case). Setting: standard (atomless) probability space, rho: L^infty -> R. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let (Omega,F,P) be a standard probability space (atomless) and rho: L^infty -> R. (Thm 4) rho is a law-invariant coherent risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, subadditive, rho(X)=rho(Y) whenever X =_d Y) with the Fatou property (X_n -> X in probability, sup_n ||X_n||_infty < infty  =>  rho(X) <= liminf rho(X_n))  <=>  there exists a set M_0 subset P((0,1]) of probability measures on (0,1] such that rho(X) = sup_{m in M_0} int_(0,1] rho_alpha(X) m(dalpha), where rho_alpha is AVaR/ES at level alpha. (Thm 7) Under the same hypotheses, rho is additionally comonotonic (rho(X+Y)=rho(X)+rho(Y) for comonotone X,Y)  <=>  there is a single m in P((0,1]) with rho(X) = int_(0,1] rho_alpha(X) m(dalpha); equivalently (Denneberg/Wang) rho(X) = int_0^infty h(P(X>x)) dx with h concave, h(0)=0, h(1)=1, h(p) = int_(0,1] min(p/alpha,1) m(dalpha) (loss convention).
      USE: Thm 7 is exactly the 'coherent & law-invariant & comonotone-additive <=> concave distortion' statement the panel asked for; it requires monotonicity, so by (F2) it can be applied only to the two MONOTONE endpoints of the associated family: rho = rho_0 and Pi = lim_{beta -> -infty} rho_beta (note rho_beta = Pi + (rho - Pi)/(1-beta) -> Pi as beta -> -infty). Applying Thm 7 to rho_0 gives h concave; applying it to -Pi(-.) (Pi superadditive, i.e. a bid price) gives g convex. The intermediate rho_beta are non-monotone and must be handled by the signed Choquet theorem (item 7).
  [2] Föllmer, H. and Schied, A. Stochastic Finance: An Introduction in Discrete Time. 4th ed. (de Gruyter 2016), Section 4.7 'Comonotonic risk measures': Theorem 4.88 (Choquet representation of comonotonic monetary risk measures, after Schmeidler), Theorem 4.93 and Theorem 4.94 (law-invariant comonotonic coherent = AVaR mixture = concave distortion). In the 2nd ed. (2004) the same results are Theorems 4.82, 4.87, 4.88. NUMBERING NOT VERIFIED ONLINE. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (Thm 4.88, 4th ed.) A monetary risk measure rho on L^infty (monotone, cash-additive, normalized) is comonotonic  <=>  rho(X) = int (-X) dc, the Choquet integral w.r.t. the monotone normalized set function c(A) := rho(-1_A); and such rho is coherent (subadditive)  <=>  c is submodular (c(A u B)+c(A n B) <= c(A)+c(B)). (Thm 4.93/4.94, 4th ed.) On an atomless probability space the following are equivalent for a monetary risk measure rho on L^infty: (i) rho is coherent, comonotonic and law-invariant [continuity from above is automatic by Thm 4.54, item 3 below]; (ii) rho = rho_mu := int_(0,1] AVaR_lambda mu(dlambda) for some mu in M_1((0,1]); (iii) rho(X) = int (-X) dc_psi with c_psi(A) = psi(P(A)) for a concave distortion psi: [0,1] -> [0,1], psi(0)=0, psi(1)=1 (psi(t) = int_(0,1] min(t/lambda,1) mu(dlambda)); in the loss convention rho(X) = int_0^infty psi(P(X>x)) dx.
      USE: This is the textbook form of the 'if and only if' the panel wants for each endpoint functional. Because the Schmeidler step (Thm 4.88) needs monotonicity to get a monotone capacity, it cannot be applied to rho_beta for 0<beta<1 (F2); but the Choquet/distortion conclusion survives for the signed case via Wang-Wei-Willmot (item 7), and the concavity conclusion (coherence <=> submodular c <=> concave psi) survives there as well. Hence: all associated rho_beta, beta<1, are subadditive  <=>  (h - beta g)/(1-beta) concave for all beta<1  <=>  h concave AND h - g concave (write h - beta g = (1-beta) h + beta (h-g) and let beta -> 1). This is strictly weaker than the 'conic picture' h concave & g convex of (F4).
  [3] Jouini, E., Schachermayer, W., Touzi, N. (2006). Law invariant risk measures have the Fatou property. Advances in Mathematical Economics 9, 49-71, Theorem 2.2 (and Theorem 2.3 on the 'strong Fatou'/weak compactness refinement). Reproduced as Föllmer-Schied 4th ed. Theorem 4.54 ('a law-invariant convex risk measure on L^infty over an atomless standard space is continuous from above'). Extension without monotonicity: Svindland, G. (2010). Continuity properties of law-invariant (quasi-)convex risk functions on L^infty. Mathematics and Financial Economics 3, 39-43, main Theorem. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (JST Thm 2.2) Let (Omega,F,P) be a standard (atomless) probability space and rho: L^infty -> R a law-invariant convex risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, convex). Then rho has the Fatou property, i.e. rho is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lower semicontinuous, and therefore admits the dual representation rho(X) = sup_{Q << P, dQ/dP in L^1} ( E_Q[-X] - alpha(Q) ) with alpha law-invariant (alpha(Q) depends on Q only through the law of dQ/dP). (Svindland 2010) On the same space, every law-invariant, quasi-convex function f: L^infty -> (-infty, +infty] that is lower semicontinuous for the ||.||_infty-norm is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lower semicontinuous; NO monotonicity is assumed.
      USE: Removes the Fatou/continuity hypothesis from any iff theorem for LR in the law-invariant case: if LR = rho/Pi is law-invariant, norm-continuous and quasi-convex (F4 regimes), Svindland's theorem gives the Fatou property of LR for free, and gives sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lsc of every convex rho_beta even though rho_beta is NOT monotone. This is the key tool for working around (F2): the automatic-Fatou results do not need monotonicity, only convexity/quasi-convexity + law invariance + norm-lsc.
  [4] Frittelli, M., Rosazza Gianin, E. (2005). Law invariant convex risk measures. Advances in Mathematical Economics 7, 33-46, Theorem 7 (AVaR-mixture representation; my recollection of the number is uncertain) and the proposition that law invariance of rho is equivalent to law invariance of its minimal penalty. Same result in Föllmer-Schied 4th ed. Theorem 4.62 and Corollary 4.63 (2nd ed.: Theorem 4.54 / Corollary 4.58). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let (Omega,F,P) be atomless and rho: L^infty -> R a convex risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, convex) with the Fatou property (automatic by JST). Then rho is law-invariant  <=>  rho(X) = sup_{mu in M_1((0,1])} ( int_(0,1] AVaR_lambda(X) mu(dlambda) - beta_min(mu) ), with beta_min(mu) = sup_{X in A_rho} int AVaR_lambda(X) mu(dlambda). Coherent case (Cor. 4.63): rho law-invariant coherent  <=>  rho(X) = sup_{mu in M} int AVaR_lambda(X) mu(dlambda) for some set M subset M_1((0,1]). Moreover rho is law-invariant  <=>  the minimal penalty alpha_min(Q) depends only on the law of dQ/dP, and then alpha_min(Q) = alpha_min(law of dQ/dP) via the Hardy-Littlewood inequality E[ZX] <= int_0^1 q_Z(u) q_X(u) du.
      USE: Gives the 'signed Kusuoka' representation needed for non-monotone but convex rho_beta: a law-invariant convex sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lsc functional (no monotonicity) still has rho_beta(X) = sup_{Z in L^1} ( E[ZX] - alpha(Z) ) with alpha law-invariant, and by Hardy-Littlewood rearrangement rho_beta(X) = sup_Z ( int_0^1 q_Z(u) q_X(u) du - alpha(Z) ): a supremum of SIGNED spectral functionals int q_X phi du with phi non-decreasing but not necessarily non-negative. Positivity of phi is exactly where monotonicity enters, and it is precisely the part that fails for rho_beta by (F2). So the convex-law-invariant theory survives for the loss-ratio family with 'spectra' phi_beta = (phi_h - beta phi_g)/(1-beta) that may be negative near u=0.
  [5] Acerbi, C. (2002). Spectral measures of risk: a coherent representation of subjective risk aversion. Journal of Banking & Finance 26(7), 1505-1518, Theorem 4.1 (number from memory; it is the theorem stating 'M_phi is coherent iff phi is an admissible risk spectrum'). Related: Föllmer-Schied Thm 4.93 (ii)<=>(iii) and Kusuoka Thm 7. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let X have integrable quantile function q_X and let phi in L^1([0,1]). Define the spectral measure M_phi(X) = - int_0^1 q_X(p) phi(p) dp (gain convention; loss convention: int_0^1 q_X(p) phi(1-p) dp). Then M_phi is a coherent risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, subadditive)  <=>  phi is an admissible risk spectrum: phi >= 0 a.e., phi non-increasing a.e., int_0^1 phi = 1. (Separately: M_phi is monotone and cash-additive for any phi>=0 with int phi=1; subadditivity  <=>  phi non-increasing.)
      USE: Spectral form of the distortion functional: C_h(X) = int_0^1 q_X(u) h'(1-u) du when h is absolutely continuous, so h concave <=> spectrum non-increasing. For the loss ratio family rho_beta = C_{(h - beta g)/(1-beta)} the 'spectrum' is phi_beta = (phi_h - beta phi_g)/(1-beta): Acerbi's theorem splits the three axioms by their footprint on phi: positivity of phi_beta = monotonicity of rho_beta (fails for some beta by F2), monotonicity of phi_beta = subadditivity/convexity of rho_beta (can hold for all beta: iff h and h-g concave), normalization = cash additivity (holds for all beta since (h - beta g)(1)/(1-beta) = 1). Thus Acerbi's decomposition tells exactly which classical axiom must be dropped for rho_beta and which can be kept.
  [6] Delbaen, F. (2002). Coherent risk measures on general probability spaces. In: Advances in Finance and Stochastics (Sandmann, Schönbucher eds.), Springer, 1-37, Theorem 3.2 and Corollary 3.? (Fatou <=> weak* lsc <=> L^1 dual representation). Delbaen, F. (2012). Monetary Utility Functions. Osaka University Press, Lecture Notes (chapter on comonotonic/law-invariant monetary utility functions: Kusuoka's theorem reproved via Schmeidler's theorem and the Hardy-Littlewood/Ryff rearrangement). (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (Delbaen 2002, Thm 3.2) Let rho: L^infty(Omega,F,P) -> R be a coherent risk measure (monotone, cash-additive, positively homogeneous, subadditive). The following are equivalent: (i) rho has the Fatou property (X_n -> X a.s., sup ||X_n||_infty < infty  =>  rho(X) <= liminf rho(X_n)); (ii) rho is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lower semicontinuous; (iii) the acceptance set A_rho is sigma(L^infty,L^1)-closed; (iv) there is a sigma(L^1,L^infty)-closed convex set P of probability measures Q << P such that rho(X) = sup_{Q in P} E_Q[-X] (gain convention). (Delbaen 2012) On an atomless space, a coherent comonotonic law-invariant rho is a Choquet integral w.r.t. c(A) = psi(P(A)), psi concave, and the representing set P = {Q : dQ/dP is 'dominated' by psi in the sense E_Q[1_A]... <= psi(P(A)) for all A} = the core of the submodular capacity c (Schmeidler/Delbaen).
      USE: Supplies the acceptance-set language in which the association LR(X)=beta <=> rho_beta(X)=0 lives: A_beta = {rho_beta <= 0} = {LR <= beta} (for Pi > 0, beta < 1). (F5) says 1/LR = sup{x : x rho - Pi <= 0}; Delbaen's equivalences (i)-(iv) hold for convex cash-additive functionals without monotonicity EXCEPT that the dual variables Z = dQ/dP need not be >= 0. So the loss ratio has a Delbaen-type representation rho_beta(X) = sup_{Z in P_beta} E[ZX] with P_beta a weak-closed convex set of SIGNED densities with E[Z]=1 (cash additivity) — the signed core of the signed capacity (h - beta g)/(1-beta)(P(.)). Monotonicity of rho_beta <=> all Z in P_beta are >= 0, which is exactly what (F2) rules out for intermediate beta.
  [7] Schmeidler, D. (1986). Integral representation without additivity. Proceedings of the AMS 97(2), 255-261, Theorem (main representation theorem) and Proposition (subadditivity <=> submodularity). Signed extension: Wang, R., Wei, Y., Willmot, G. E. (2020). Characterization, robustness, and aggregation of signed Choquet integrals. Mathematics of Operations Research 45(3), 993-1015, Theorem 1 (characterization) and Theorem 3 (convexity/concavity equivalences). Numbers from memory. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (Schmeidler 1986) Let I: B(Sigma) -> R (bounded measurable functions) be monotone (f <= g => I(f) <= I(g)), comonotonically additive (I(f+g) = I(f)+I(g) whenever f,g comonotone) and I(1)=1. Then I(f) = int f dv (Choquet integral) with v(A) := I(1_A) a normalized monotone set function; conversely every such Choquet integral has these properties; and I is subadditive  <=>  v is submodular (2-alternating). (WWW 2020, Thm 1) A functional I: L^infty -> R (more generally on a set X of random variables containing all bounded ones, on an atomless space) is a signed Choquet integral, I(X) = int_{-infty}^0 ( h(P(X>x)) - h(1) ) dx + int_0^infty h(P(X>x)) dx with h: [0,1] -> R of bounded variation, h(0)=0,  <=>  I is law-invariant, comonotonic-additive and uniformly continuous w.r.t. the L^infty norm. (Thm 3) For a signed Choquet integral I_h the following are equivalent: I_h is subadditive; I_h is convex; h is concave; I_h is consistent with convex order (X <=_cx Y => I_h(X) <= I_h(Y)). Monotonicity of I_h  <=>  h non-decreasing; cash additivity  <=>  h(1)=1; positive homogeneity always.
      USE: This is the theorem that replaces Kusuoka Thm 7 / FS Thm 4.93 for the NON-monotone members rho_beta of the associated family (F7): rho_beta is law-invariant, comonotone additive and Lipschitz in sup-norm (Lipschitz constant <= (1+|beta|)/(1-beta)) whenever rho and Pi are distortion functionals, and conversely WWW Thm 1 shows that these three properties alone force rho_beta to be a signed Choquet integral C_{psi_beta}. Since rho_beta = Pi + (rho - Pi)/(1-beta) is affine in 1/(1-beta), psi_beta = g + (h - g)/(1-beta) with h := psi_0, g := lim_{beta -> -infty} psi_beta. WWW Thm 3 then gives: all rho_beta (beta<1) convex <=> h and h-g concave; and the monotonicity of the endpoints (h,g non-decreasing) is the only place where monotonicity is needed. This is the exact mechanism that reconciles (F2) with an iff theorem.
  [8] Wang, S. S., Young, V. R., Panjer, H. H. (1997). Axiomatic characterization of insurance prices. Insurance: Mathematics and Economics 21, 173-183, Theorem 3 (distortion representation) and Theorem 4/Corollary (g(x) >= x and concavity under additional axioms). Number from memory; the project's synthesis already cites this paper. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: Let X be the set of non-negative random variables on a sufficiently rich (atomless) probability space and H: X -> [0, infty]. Suppose H satisfies: (A1) law invariance: H(X) depends only on the survival function S_X; (A2) monotonicity: X <= Y a.s. => H(X) <= H(Y); (A3) comonotone additivity: H(X+Y) = H(X) + H(Y) for comonotone X, Y; (A4) continuity: lim_{d->0+} H((X-d)_+) = H(X), lim_{d->infty} H(min(X,d)) = H(X), lim_{d->infty} H((X-d)_+) = 0. Then there is a non-decreasing g: [0,1] -> [0,1] with g(0)=0, g(1)=1 such that H(X) = int_0^infty g(S_X(t)) dt; conversely every such distortion functional satisfies (A1)-(A4). If in addition H(X) >= E[X] for all X [non-negative loading] and H is subadditive, then g is concave (and g(p) >= p).
      USE: The actuarial-side endpoint theorem: it characterizes Pi = lim_{beta -> -infty} rho_beta on NON-NEGATIVE losses, the natural domain of Assa's LR (X >= 0), where cash additivity is unavailable as an axiom (X + c with c<0 leaves the domain). It shows that for the premium endpoint one can replace cash additivity by WYP continuity. The same theorem applied to rho_0 = rho = LR * Pi gives h. Hence the pair-level hypotheses needed for the 'only if' are WYP (A1)-(A4) on rho and on Pi, nothing more; the intermediate rho_beta need no separate hypothesis because they are determined by rho and Pi.
  [9] Dana, R.-A. (2005). A representation result for concave Schur concave functions. Mathematical Finance 15(4), 613-634 (main theorem); the same lemma appears as Föllmer-Schied 4th ed. Lemma 4.55 / Corollary 4.59 (2nd ed.: Lemma 4.51 / Cor. 4.59): 'law-invariant convex lsc rho satisfies rho(E[X|G]) <= rho(X)'. Companion duality without monotonicity: Cerreia-Vioglio, S., Maccheroni, F., Marinacci, M., Montrucchio, L. (2011). Complete monotone quasiconcave duality. Mathematics of Operations Research 36(2), 321-339, Theorem 1 (evenly quasiconcave functions) and Theorem 2 (monotone case); and Cherny, A., Madan, D. (2009). New measures for performance evaluation. Review of Financial Studies 22(7), 2571-2606, Theorem 1 (acceptability indices) with its law-invariant corollary. (confidence: medium)
      STATEMENT: (Dana 2005 / FS Cor. 4.59) Let (Omega,F,P) be atomless and f: L^infty -> R law-invariant, convex and sigma(L^infty,L^1)-lsc (by Svindland, norm-lsc suffices). Then f(E[X|G]) <= f(X) for every sub-sigma-algebra G, hence f is Schur-convex: X <=_cx Y (convex order) => f(X) <= f(Y); and f(X) = sup_{phi in Phi} ( int_0^1 q_X(u) phi(u) du - alpha(phi) ) with Phi a set of non-decreasing functions in L^1(0,1), alpha(phi) = sup_X ( int q_X phi - f(X) ). NO monotonicity of f is assumed; with monotonicity Phi can be taken in L^1_+ with int phi = 1 and one recovers Kusuoka. (CMMM 2011, Thm 1) f: E -> [-infty, infty] on a locally convex space is evenly quasiconcave  <=>  f(x) = inf_{x' in E'} G(<x,x'>, x') for a unique G in a specified class of functions non-decreasing in the first argument; with monotonicity of f the dual variables x' restrict to the positive cone (Thm 2). (Cherny-Madan 2009, Thm 1) alpha: L^infty -> [0, infty] is quasi-concave, monotone, scale-invariant and Fatou  <=>  alpha(X) = sup{x >= 0 : rho_x(X) <= 0} for an increasing family (rho_x) of coherent Fatou risk measures; alpha is law-invariant iff each rho_x can be chosen law-invariant.
      USE: These are the duality theorems that do NOT need monotonicity and therefore apply verbatim to LR and to rho_beta. (a) Dana/FS: in the regime where rho_beta is convex (h, h-g concave) every rho_beta is Schur-convex, i.e. LR is monotone w.r.t. CONVEX ORDER on the sublevel sets {LR <= beta}: X <=_cx Y => LR(X) <= beta whenever LR(Y) <= beta. This 'convex-order monotonicity' is the correct replacement for the impossible pointwise monotonicity (F2): it is intrinsic to LR, law-invariant, and implied by the conic-type class. (b) CMMM Thm 1 replaces Cherny-Madan Thm 1 for the non-monotone cones A_beta of (F5): 1/LR(X) = sup{x : x rho(X) - Pi(X) <= 0} is an acceptability-index-type representation with signed dual variables; CMMM's evenly-quasiconcave duality (without positivity of x') is the right abstract framework. (c) Cherny-Madan's law-invariance corollary shows that, when LR is quasi-concave AND monotone it would be a law-invariant AI; (F2) shows this route is void for LR itself, but the dual CMMM route is open.


### Performance-ratio axiomatics (gain-loss / Omega / Sharpe / RAROC / acceptability indices / conic finance)
KEY INSIGHT: Every performance-ratio characterization in this strand (Cherny–Madan Thm 1, Biagini–Bion-Nadal Prop 3.12/Rem 3.13, Righi Thm 3.3, Drapeau–Kupper Thm 1.10) is in fact a LEVEL-SET theorem: a scale-invariant index α is the same object as a nested family of cones A_x = {ρ_x ≤ 0}, α(X) = sup{x : ρ_x(X) ≤ 0}, with the induced family ρ^x(X) = inf{m : α(X+m) ≥ x} being automatically cash-additive (by construction) and positively homogeneous (by scale invariance), unique when α is strictly monotone and continuous along cash shifts (BBN axiom 3b + continuity). Monotonicity of α in X is used ONLY to make ρ_x monotone and thus admit a probabilistic (sup over measures) dual. The loss ratio fails monotonicity for a structural sign reason that the strand makes explicit: Righi's RAROC family ρ_x = (xρ − μ)/(1+x) is monotone because reward and risk move oppositely in X, whereas Assa's family ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) is a difference of two increasing functionals (ask − β·bid in conic-finance terms), which is exactly why F2 holds. The literature already contains two non-monotone 'loss ratios' living on the dual side: Bernardo–Ledoit/Biagini–Pınar show that the gain-loss acceptability index is dual to ess sup Z/ess inf Z = C_{1_{(0,1]}}(Z)/C_{1_{{1}}}(Z) of the pricing kernel, and Bellini–Klar–Müller show the Omega ratio is the inverse of the expectile family through the signed identification functional E[(X−t)^+] − βE[(X−t)^-]. Hence the right theorem for LR is not 'LR is an acceptability index' but 'LR is the inverse of a one-parameter family of SIGNED Choquet functionals (cash-additive, positively homogeneous, comonotone-additive, law-invariant, Fatou-continuous, affine in β)', with the probabilistic dual replaced by the Wang–Wei–Willmot signed-Choquet (bounded-variation distortion) representation, the primal level-set calculus of Drapeau–Kupper/Cherny–Madan kept verbatim, Assa's 'translation to break-even' playing the role of BBN's strict cash-monotonicity, and Tasche's RORAC-compatibility giving an intrinsic 'local improvement' axiom equivalent to positive homogeneity of the pair.
CANDIDATE IFF: THEOREM (candidate, acceptability-index form). Let (Ω,F,P) be atomless, X := L^∞_+(Ω,F,P)\{0}, and LR: X → [0,∞]. For β ∈ [0,∞) put A_β := {X ∈ X : LR(X) ≤ β} and define the induced family ρ_β(X) := inf{ m ∈ ℝ : X+m ∈ A_β ∪ {X+m ≤ 0} } for β < 1 and ρ_β(X) := sup{ m : LR(X+m) ≥ β } for β > 1 (the 'break-even' functionals). The following are equivalent: (I) there exist distortion functions h, g: [0,1] → [0,1], non-decreasing, h(0)=g(0)=0, h(1)=g(1)=1, g continuous and g > 0 on (0,1], such that LR(X) = C_h(X)/C_g(X) = ∫_0^∞ h(P(X>x))dx / ∫_0^∞ g(P(X>x))dx for all X ∈ X; (II) LR satisfies (A1) scale invariance LR(λX) = LR(X), λ > 0; (A2) law invariance; (A3) translation to break-even: for every X, c ↦ LR(X+c) is continuous and strictly monotone on {c : X+c ≥ 0} towards 1 (strictly increasing if LR(X) < 1, strictly decreasing if LR(X) > 1, constant iff LR(X)=1), so that each ρ_β is finite and LR(X) = β ⇔ ρ_β(X) = 0 (association); (A4) comonotone additivity of the break-even functionals: ρ_β(X+Y) = ρ_β(X) + ρ_β(Y) for all comonotone X,Y and all β ≠ 1 [intrinsic form: for comonotone X,Y, LR(X+Y) is the Π-weighted mediant of LR(X), LR(Y), i.e. the sublevel and superlevel sets of LR are closed under comonotone sums]; (A5) Fatou continuity: X_n → X a.s., sup‖X_n‖_∞ < ∞ ⇒ LR(X) = lim LR(X_n) [Fatou-closedness of all A_β]; (A6) affinity of the family: β ↦ (1−β)ρ_β(X) is affine on [0,1)∪(1,∞) for every X, with ρ_0 and Π := ρ_0 − (1−β)ρ_β (independent of β) both monotone in X. Under (II): ρ := ρ_0 and Π are cash-additive, positively homogeneous (from A1 via BBN Prop 3.3 mechanism), comonotone-additive (A4), law-invariant (A2), Fatou-continuous (A5) and monotone (A6), hence by the Schmeidler/Greco/Wang–Wei–Willmot characterization ρ = C_h, Π = C_g with normalized distortions, and (A3) forces LR = ρ/Π; (1−β)ρ_β = C_{h−βg} is a signed Choquet integral (F7). Conversely (I) ⇒ (II) by Assa Prop. 1 + the Choquet properties (F1), with uniqueness of (h,g) by F3. HONEST ACCOUNTING: (A1),(A2),(A3),(A5) are axioms on LR alone; (A4) is stated on LR alone only in its weighted-mediant form, whose weights are Π(X),Π(Y) and so implicitly refer to the pair; (A6) (affinity in β and monotonicity of the two endpoint functionals) is a genuine hypothesis on the associated family, i.e. on the pair (ρ,Π), and is what replaces the monotonicity axiom (S1) that the Cherny–Madan/Biagini–Bion-Nadal/Righi theorems need and that F2 shows LR can never satisfy. Dropping (A6) one still obtains the weaker iff: LR satisfies (A1)-(A5) ⇔ LR(X) = sup{β < 1 : φ_β(X) ≤ 0} (resp. inf on the other branch) for a unique family (φ_β) of cash-additive, positively homogeneous, comonotone-additive, law-invariant, Fatou-continuous (hence signed Choquet) functionals with φ_β(X) = 0 ⇔ LR(X) = β — the loss-ratio analogue of Biagini–Bion-Nadal Remark 3.13 with 'coherent' replaced by 'signed Choquet'.
THEOREMS:
  [1] Cherny, A. & Madan, D. (2009), 'New measures for performance evaluation', Rev. Financ. Stud. 22(7), 2571-2606, Theorem 1 (Section 2). Read verbatim in three restatements: Bielecki, Cialenco & Zhang (2014) 'Dynamic coherent acceptability indices and their applications to finance', Math. Finance 24(3), arXiv:1010.4339, Def. 2.1 + Thm 2.2; Righi (2024) 'Star-shaped acceptability indexes', IME 117, arXiv:2110.08630, Thm 2.5; Peri (2012) PhD thesis Milano-Bicocca, Thm 15. (confidence: high (restatements read online in three independent primary sources; the original RFS text was paywalled))
      STATEMENT: Let (Ω,F,P) be a probability space and α: L^∞ → [0,+∞]. Call α a coherent acceptability index if (S1) monotonicity: X ≤ Y a.s. ⇒ α(X) ≤ α(Y); (S2) scale invariance: α(λX)=α(X) ∀λ>0; (S3) quasi-concavity: α(X) ≥ x, α(Y) ≥ x ⇒ α(λX+(1−λ)Y) ≥ x, λ∈[0,1]; (S4) Fatou property: |X_n| ≤ 1, α(X_n) ≥ x ∀n, X_n → X in probability ⇒ α(X) ≥ x. THEOREM: assume α is unbounded from above (sup α = +∞). Then α is a coherent acceptability index ⇔ there is a family (D_x)_{x∈[0,∞)} of sets of probability measures Q ≪ P, increasing in x (D_x ⊂ D_y for x ≤ y), with α(X) = sup{ x ∈ [0,∞) : inf_{Q∈D_x} E_Q[X] ≥ 0 } (inf ∅ = +∞, sup ∅ = 0). Equivalently (Righi Thm 2.5, with Rosazza Gianin–Sgarra 2013 Prop. 3 for the convex case): α is a coherent (resp. quasi-concave, i.e. (S1),(S3),(S4) only) acceptability index ⇔ there is an increasing family (ρ_x)_{x>0} of Fatou-continuous coherent (resp. convex) risk measures with α(X) = sup{ x > 0 : ρ_x(X) ≤ 0 }, sup ∅ = 0. Classical examples in CM09 §3: GLR(X) = E[X]/E[X^-] is a coherent acceptability index (D_x = {Q: ess sup(dQ/dP)/ess inf(dQ/dP) ≤ 1+x}); RAROC(X) = E[X]/ρ(X) (ρ coherent, +∞ if ρ(X) ≤ 0) is a coherent acceptability index; the Sharpe ratio E[X]/σ(X) satisfies (S2),(S3) but FAILS (S1) (Peri Example 16, Zhitlukhin 2018 §1 give explicit counterexamples: X ~ N(1,1), Y = X ∧ 1 ≤ X but S(Y) > S(X) = 1).
      USE: This is the master template: a scale-invariant index is NOTHING BUT a decreasing family of acceptance cones A_x = {ρ_x ≤ 0} read through sup{x : X ∈ A_x}. For the loss ratio, A_β := {X : ρ(X) ≤ β Π(X)} = {ρ_β ≤ 0} with ρ_β = (ρ − βΠ)/(1−β) (Assa's canonical family), so LR(X) = sup{β : ρ_β(X) ≤ 0} on the branch LR ≤ 1 (and inf on the branch LR ≥ 1) — exactly the F5/F6 'association'. Monotonicity (S1) is used in the proof ONLY to make ρ_x monotone and hence a coherent risk measure; the sup-formula itself and the correspondence {level sets} ↔ {family} need only that the sets A_x be nested and closed (Fatou). Hence the way around F2: replace 'coherent risk measure' in the family by 'signed Choquet integral / comonotone-additive cash-additive positively homogeneous functional' and keep everything else of the CM proof.
  [2] Biagini, S. & Bion-Nadal, J. (2014), 'Dynamic quasi-concave performance measures', J. Math. Econ. 55, 143-153, arXiv:1212.3958: Def. 2.1, Prop. 3.3, Def. 3.10, Prop. 3.11, Prop. 3.12 and Remark 3.13 (static case). (confidence: high (read online, arXiv:1212.3958))
      STATEMENT: Static case (trivial F_t). Let L^{bb} be the space of essentially-bounded-from-below random variables (values in (−∞,+∞]). A performance measure is β: L^{bb} → [z_d, z_u] with (1) quasi-concavity β(cX+(1−c)Y) ≥ min(β(X),β(Y)), c∈[0,1]; (2) z_d = inf β < z_u = sup β; (3a) monotonicity X ≥ Y ⇒ β(X) ≥ β(Y); (3b) STRICT monotonicity along constant shifts: c > 0 ⇒ β(X+c) > β(X) whenever β(X) < z_u and β(X+c) > z_d; (4) continuity from below: X_n ↑ X ⇒ β(X_n) ↑ β(X); (6) for z_d < z < z_u the level set {β ≥ z} is uniformly bounded from below. It is an acceptability index if moreover (7) β(cX)=β(X), c>0, and (8) β ≥ 0. Induced family (Def. 3.1): ρ^z(X) := inf{ ξ ∈ ℝ : β(X+ξ) ≥ z }. Standard family (Def. 3.10): (σ^z)_{z∈(z_d,z_u)} with each σ^z convex, monotone non-increasing, cash-additive (translation invariant), continuous from below (X_n ↑ X ⇒ σ^z(X_n) ↓ σ^z(X)); z ↦ σ^z(X) non-decreasing and continuous; if z_d = −∞ then σ^z(0) → −∞ as z → −∞. THEOREM (Prop. 3.12 + Remark 3.13): (i) every performance measure β induces a standard family (ρ^z) of convex risk measures, coherent if β is an acceptability index; (ii) every standard family generates a performance measure via β(X) = sup{ z : σ^z(X) < 0 } (sup ∅ = z_d), an acceptability index when the σ^z are coherent and z_d = 0; (iii) (ρ^z) is the UNIQUE standard family generating β. In particular: 'β: L^{bb} → [0,+∞] is an acceptability index if and only if there exists a unique standard family of coherent risk measures (σ^z)_{z>0} with β(X) = sup{z : σ^z(X) < 0}'. Key lemma (Lemma 3.2(6)): β(X) > z ⇔ ρ^z(X) < 0. Difference from Cherny–Madan (who use ≤ 0 and get a possibly non-unique maximal family) is attributed to continuity from below and to strict monotonicity (3b) along cash shifts.
      USE: This is the closest existing analogue of Assa's 'association' LR(X)=β ⇔ ρ_β(X)=0 WITH UNIQUENESS.
